# Case 1: swirl first-crossing records under the historical shape condition (A)

Last revised: 2026-10-16T21:02Z.

Status label: UNRESOLVED_NOT_ACCEPTED

First missing implication. No argument yet derives a contradiction from the fixed physical solution, (A) and the Case-1 witnesses (K) and (TAIL), and no fixed-datum counterexample was found. Theorem 13 below splits what remains into two branches; the missing implication is an exclusion of each of them.
(I) Viscous, single-scale branch. At the swirl records the normalized global numbers m_n, d_n, h_n (sec. 1.2, Proposition 8) are bounded, and, after viscosity normalization, the limits taken at the swirl records and at the gradient records belong to the class LL defined in sec. 1.1, with global W^{2,infinity} bounds and, at the gradient records, the shape bound Q <= C_* (Q is the shape ratio of Proposition 8(b)). Proposition 15 selects (existentially, not within a prescribed branch or subsequence) gradient-record limits that are eternal, with the gradient maximum over all space and time attained; their whole-time shape bound is Q <= 18 C_*^2. Every member of LL has finite Q. Consequently, an exclusion using only the ancient limit properties of Theorem 2 and finiteness of Q, uniformly over all finite shape constants, would settle the bounded-circulation case of the Liouville conjecture of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak (Theorem 3); an exclusion for one fixed C_* would not. This reduction does not apply to an argument using the additional eternal selection or information from the fixed physical trajectory. For the eternal swirl-record limits available when all late gradient records are uniformly viscous, the attained swirl maximum adds nothing to a Liouville theorem uniform over the constants: such a theorem is equivalent to one for all eternal bounded mild axisymmetric solutions with bounded circulation and nonzero swirl, steady ones included (sec. 3.7). (A) and the vector Landau inequality D^2 <= 6 M H (Theorem 13(e)) already exclude C_* < 1/6 (a singular-value form of the inequality improves this to C_* < 0.2545..., Proposition 11(v)); no exclusion was obtained for the remaining admissible parameter regime. Sufficient conditions proved here are a large-radius compression envelope with divergent scale integral (Theorem 5) and sup R^3 |eta| < infinity (Theorem 4); neither was derived for the Case-1 limit.
(II) Inviscid branch. At the gradient records the rescaled viscosity kappa = D/M^2 tends to 0, the swirl ratio S/M tends to 0, and the gradient-scale limits are ancient swirl-free Euler flows (axisymmetric about a fixed line, or planar). Backward decay ||grad V(-sigma_k)||_infty = o(sigma_k^{-1/2}) along some sequence sigma_k -> infinity, in particular backward gradient windows, would exclude them (Proposition 12); no such estimate was derived, and Proposition 11 shows that it does not follow from the scalar information used so far. The unconditional bounds of Proposition 8(c) that these limits inherit do not exclude them by themselves when C_* >= sqrt7/8 (= 0.3307...): steady planar radial vortices satisfy all of them (Proposition 11(v)), and steady columnar jets do when C_* > 1/2 (Proposition 11(iv)); for C_* < c_3 = 0.3158... there is no such field (Proposition 11(v)), and the range in between is not decided. If the energy at the gradient scale, M^5/D^3, is bounded below along the records defining a limit, that limit has finite energy and is not planar, and along these records a fixed amount of energy concentrates in balls of radius comparable to M/D (Proposition 11(vi)); planar limits and limits with infinite energy, which include all the examples above, therefore require M^5/D^3 -> 0 along the records. The first alternative forces an atom of the terminal energy measure (the weak-* limit of |u(t)|^2 dx as t -> T_*) at a point of the axis, and hence a failure of energy equality at T_* (Proposition 11(vii)); this is excluded if ||u(t)||_infty = o((T_* - t)^{-3/5}) or ||u||_infty^{5/3} is integrable up to T_*, but neither bound is available, and neither alternative is excluded. In Case 1, (K) and (TAIL) force the gradient records attached to the swirl records in this branch to be separated from them in time (in swirl time units) or in space (on the scale (M^5 D^{-3} r_n^3)^{1/4} when the maximizer is at bounded rescaled distance from the axis), and the gradient maximizers converge to the axis in physical variables (Proposition 11(viii)); no estimate derived here excludes the separation. Alternatively, by Proposition 14 this branch forces the critical potential-vorticity mass N(t) = ||omega^theta/r(t)||_{L^1(dx)} to satisfy N(s_n) >= c_1(C_*)/kappa_n -> infinity at the gradient records, with the constant c_1(C_*) of Proposition 14, so a strict deficit liminf_n kappa_n N(s_n) < c_1(C_*) would exclude it; the available physical balance gives only N(t) = o(S(t)), with S the historical swirl amplitude (when N is finite at some time; Proposition 18(e)), which is too weak: it only forces kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity, and a truncated form of the balance gives this without the finiteness of N (Proposition 18(f)). When moreover M(s_n)^5/D(s_n)^3 >= epsilon_0 > 0, the truncated balance localizes the deficit: the swirl source d_z(q^2) on the superlevel set {|omega^theta/r| > c_eta D(s_n)^2/(2 M(s_n))}, with c_eta > 0 depending only on C_*, E_0 and epsilon_0, at distance r >= c' M(s_n)/D(s_n) from the axis contributes o(1/kappa_n) for every fixed c' > 0, so the required mass of order 1/kappa_n must be produced at distance o(M(s_n)/D(s_n)) from the axis (Proposition 18(f)); no bound on that inner source has been derived. Averaging the truncated balance over a band of levels removes the sign problem of the source at the price of a positive term: in all of branch (II), int int q^4 over the band of levels comparable to D(s_n)^2/(M(s_n) B_n) up to time s_n must be at least c D(s_n) M(s_n)/B_n, B_n being the distance of the gradient maximizer from the axis in units M(s_n)/D(s_n) plus a constant (Proposition 18(h)); no upper bound small enough to contradict this lower bound has been derived.
(Critical functionals.) The Y_p family of critical weighted swirl energies (Proposition 16) has one unsigned term, an inflow term. Other functionals leave additional unsigned terms; the coupled meridional energy has cubic, pressure and axis-flux terms after cancellation of the swirl transfer. The conditional absorption criteria stated in Proposition 16 (a global inflow bound or a smallness of the stream function) were not derived from the Case-1 assumptions, and their global weighted-integral justification is not fully supplied here. In Case 1 the critical inflow integral is necessarily infinite (Proposition 18). No compatible bound on the total has been obtained; spatial or temporal localization is another possible approach.
(Unified sufficient condition.) In both branches it would suffice that, for some constant c_0, the meridional vector potential of the swirl-record limit U satisfies sup_{tau <= 0} ||(psi_U(R, Z, tau) - c_0 R/2) e_theta||_{BMO(R^3)} < infinity (Proposition 19(b), via the Hoelder estimate of Lei and Zhang). This was not derived. Proposition 19 also shows that in Case 1, for every C, some normalized phase tau_C carries liminf_n N(t_n + T_n tau_C) >= C, so the bound sup_tau liminf_n N(t_n + T_n tau) < infinity, which would give the BMO bound, cannot hold.
(Localized physical form.) At every singular point (0, z_*, T_*) the solution is of Type II in the sense of Seregin, and for every rho > 0 the local critical mass int_{C_rho(z_*)} |omega^theta/r| dx is unbounded as t -> T_* (Proposition 20). A contradiction along this line needs an upper bound, uniform as t -> T_*, for that local mass or for the local BMO norm of the meridional vector potential B = psi^theta e_theta (Proposition 19) on one cylinder around the singular point; no estimate derived here gives one. (A) does not enter the available local inequalities; this does not show that it cannot help in combination with other estimates.
(Far-field one-sided form.) The circulation of every Case-1 limit U saturates at large normalized radius, and saturation forces strong inflow there (Proposition 23): for every R_0 >= 1 there are L and n_0 such that, for every index n >= n_0 of the subsequence defining U, the physical solution has a point of the window W_n(R_0, L) := {R_0 r_n < r <= L r_n, |z - z_n| <= L r_n, t_n - L^2 T_n <= t <= t_n} with Gamma > gamma_n (A - m) and -r u^r > 2, where A = sup Gamma_U and m = m(R_0) > 0 is the deficit of Proposition 23(b). Hence the following would exclude Case 1: for some fixed R_0 >= 1 and every finite L, the bound -r u^r <= 2 holds, for all sufficiently large indices of that subsequence (the first index may depend on L), on W_n(R_0, L) intersected with {Gamma > gamma_n (A - m)}. This is a localized sufficient form of critical meridional control. By Proposition 25 the bound is needed only at such points where moreover d_r Gamma > 0, and it would follow from the weighted critical vorticity bound calQ <= 4 there (Biot-Savart); the scalar circulation equation, incompressibility and the record conditions are consistent with strong far-field inflow (an explicit bounded drift model, which fails the vorticity equation); posed for the whole class LL, the same bound is equivalent to the Liouville problem for LL (this is not automatic for the subclass of physical limits); and an exact exterior solution, the Landau meridional flow with a potential swirl, shows that bounded velocity, potential swirl, bounded signed flux and the exterior equations alone allow -r u^r > 2 on a cone reaching infinity (Proposition 25(f)). Averaging the circulation equation over cylinders and long boxes expresses the averaged circulation through the correlation of inflow and circulation at larger radii (Proposition 26); at each fixed radius rho the mean circulation deficit in all sufficiently long boxes is at least 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu) minus any prescribed epsilon, averaged saturation forbids a critical one-sided outflow bound, and averaged saturation was not derived. An averaged exclusion would follow if one fixed family of long boxes and one common normalized positive averaging functional MM (a Banach limit of the box means) satisfied MM[Gamma_U(r_*)^2] > 0 at some radius r_*, together with MM[(Phi_rho - c_rho)^2] <= nu^2 rho^2 C(rho)^2 for every sufficiently large rho, where Phi_rho is the axial flux of U through the disk of radius rho, nu = 1/gamma_* is the viscosity of U, each c_rho is bounded in time and int^infinity d rho/[rho(1 + C(rho)^2)] = infinity (Proposition 26(i)). Neither positive mass nor the required flux bound has been derived for the same family. By Proposition 26(l), positive mass in some family is equivalent to the existence of a shift-invariant measure on the hull of axial-temporal translates of U that charges nonconstant fields; limits without such a measure (density-trivial limits) have zero long-box means for the circulation-weighted quantities and derivative-energy terms considered here, and this includes structures uniformly localized in the axial direction throughout their past; velocity moments need not vanish, and excluding these limits needs information beyond those vanishing averages, for example an unaveraged or Z-integrated argument. The averaged energy budgets (Proposition 26(k)) do not supply the flux bound, and by Proposition 26(j) it would follow from critical averaged decay of the axial velocity fluctuation, which was not derived either. The available flux bound is one power of the radius weaker, and an exact scalar solution with bounded drift and slowly increasing axial wavelength saturates, so the scalar circulation equation alone cannot supply the critical bound (that solution fails the meridional vorticity equation). For axially localized limits, Proposition 26(g) instead requires positive axial-integrated weighted mass, explicit trace integrability, a radial growth bound O(rho^alpha) with alpha < 2, and weighted mean inflow below (2 - alpha) nu. For a physical finite-box argument, the adverse earlier-time inventory and axial boundary errors must be controlled relative to the actual Dirichlet contribution throughout the radial range used. In the near-maximal set the circulation is almost flat, so the instantaneous local swirl source of the meridional vorticity is exponentially small there. This does not locate the vorticity producing the inflow, because existing local vorticity can reflect earlier creation and transport, and the bound was not derived.
Summary. No contradiction or fixed-datum counterexample has been established. Theorem 13 gives two possible subsequential regimes. Proposed sufficient exclusions include an appropriate viscous Liouville theorem, the backward decay of Proposition 12, a strict critical-mass deficit, and suitably quantified BMO or inflow control. None was derived. Proposition 15 selects an eternal limit, but not within an arbitrarily prescribed branch. Propositions 19 to 26 add compatible necessary conditions and local identities. The o(r_n) window-dissipation target is false. These observations leave distinct unproved estimates; they do not establish an equivalent reformulation of the original problem.

## 1. Result

### 1.1 Setting

u is a smooth finite-energy axisymmetric solution of the Navier-Stokes equations on R^3 x [0, T_*) with viscosity 1, smooth decaying data u_0, maximal classical solution with first singular time T_* < infinity, E_0 := ||u_0||_{L^2(R^3)}^2, and circulation Gamma = r u^theta with |Gamma| <= G := ||r u_0^theta||_infty (maximum principle). Historical quantities are suprema over [0, t]:
  M(t) = sup_{s <= t} ||u(s)||_infty, D(t) = sup_{s <= t} ||grad u(s)||_infty, H(t) = sup_{s <= t} ||grad^2 u(s)||_infty, S(t) = sup_{s <= t} ||u^theta(s)||_infty,
with Cartesian (Frobenius) tensor norms. Hypotheses of the case:
 (A) M(t) H(t) <= C_* D(t)^2 for all t in [t_A, T_*), for some t_A < T_*.
 Swirl first-crossing records (r_n, z_n, t_n): S_n := S(t_n) = |u^theta(r_n, z_n, t_n)| -> infinity, t_n first time at which S reaches S_n. Normalization gamma_n = r_n S_n, T_n = r_n/S_n, U_n(y, tau) = u(r_n y', z_n + r_n y_3, t_n + T_n tau)/S_n, which solves NS with viscosity nu_n = 1/gamma_n.
 (K) limsup_n sup_{|y| <= L, -A <= tau <= 0} |U_n| <= K for all L, A < infinity.
 (TAIL) lim_{L -> infinity} limsup_n sup_{-A <= tau <= 0} int_{|y| > L} |U_n|^2 |y|^{-4} dy = 0 for each A.

LL denotes the class of bounded mild ancient solutions W of NS on R^3 x (-infinity, 0] (viscosity 1), axisymmetric about the x_3 axis, with Gamma_W = r W^theta bounded and W^theta not identically 0.

Further notation. Cylindrical coordinates (r, theta, z) refer to the x_3 axis; normalized limits use (R, Theta, Z) and time tau. The meridional velocity is b = u^r e_r + u^z e_z (V or U^b for limits), omega^theta is the angular vorticity, q := u^theta/r and eta := omega^theta/r (potential vorticity). Delta_5 := d_rr + 3 r^{-1} d_r + d_zz is the Laplacian of R^5 acting on functions of (r, z), and L := d_RR - R^{-1} d_R + d_ZZ is the operator of the circulation equation d_t Gamma + b.grad Gamma = L Gamma (nu L Gamma at viscosity nu). For axisymmetric functions with sufficient decay, int phi Delta_5 F dx = int F L phi dx - 4 pi int F(0, z) phi(0, z) dz. The angular stream function psi^theta, with b = curl(psi^theta e_theta), is written psi^theta = r phi (phi is the 5D stream potential); then u^r = -r phi_z, u^z = 2 phi + r phi_r, -Delta_5 phi = eta, and the 5D divergence of b is div_5 b = d_r u^r + 3 u^r/r + d_z u^z = 2 u^r/r. C_P < infinity and t_P < T_* are constants with D(t) <= C_P M(t)^2 for t in [t_P, T_*) (historical mild smoothing; C_P is absolute). A gradient record is a time s with ||grad u(s)||_infty = D(s); kappa(s) := D(s)/M(s)^2 (Theorem 13), N(t) := ||eta(t)||_{L^1(dx)} (Proposition 14), and C_0, C_2 = 1 + C_0(1 + log_+ C_*) are the constants of Theorem 7. The smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions is used repeatedly: a bounded mild ancient solution W with ||W||_infty <= K_0 and viscosity nu satisfies ||d_tau^m grad^k W||_infty <= C(k, m, K_0, nu) on its whole past (proved as the extraction of Theorem 2, without the far field).

### 1.2 What was proved

The numbered results 1 to 26 below are stated with all their hypotheses. Their conditional hypotheses and external inputs (cited at the point of use, listed in sec. 4, item 5, and resolved in the References) are part of the statements. Each item is followed by its proof, or by its key estimates together with an explicit statement of the steps not reproduced, and ends with a Status line recording the extent of the argument given here, its source and its verification. Items whose complete argument is not reproduced say so and are not checkable from the report alone; the report does not rely on any other document for the arguments it presents; the verification record (sec. 5) does not certify every argument or subsequent summary sentence.

Theorem 1 (normalization and record identity). S(t) -> infinity as t -> T_*, so first-crossing records exist, and at each record -r_n u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1. Under (K): K^2 >= 1 + G^{-2} (so K > 1 is a consequence), every limit point gamma_* of gamma_n lies in [(K^2 - 1)^{-1/2}, G], the limit viscosity nu_* = 1/gamma_* lies in [1/G, (K^2 - 1)^{1/2}], r_n -> 0, and the backward depth t_n/T_n tends to infinity.

Proof. Write b = u^r e_r + u^z e_z; the swirl satisfies d_t u^theta + b.grad u^theta + u^r u^theta/r = (Delta - r^{-2}) u^theta with Delta = d_rr + r^{-1} d_r + d_zz. (a) S(t) -> infinity. If S(t) <= S_0 for all t < T_*, then |Gamma| = r |u^theta| <= S_0 r <= C |log r|^{-2} for 0 < r <= 1/4 and all t < T_* (r (log r)^2 is bounded on (0, 1/4]). By [LZ2, Corollary 1.3] (for the strong axisymmetric solution with data in H^{1/2} and Gamma_0 in L^infty, a bound sup_{0 <= t < T} |Gamma(t, r, z)| <= C_1 |log r|^{-2} for r <= delta_0, with any delta_0 in (0, 1/2) and any C_1 > 1, implies global regularity), applied with the smooth decaying data u_0, or after restarting at a positive time t_0 < T_* with data u(t_0), the solution would not break down at T_*, a contradiction. Since S is nondecreasing, S(t) -> infinity. (b) Records. s -> ||u^theta(s)||_infty is continuous on [0, T_*), so for every level lambda > ||u_0^theta||_infty there is a first time t(lambda) with ||u^theta(t(lambda))||_infty = lambda = S(t(lambda)); since u^theta(t(lambda)) is continuous, tends to 0 at spatial infinity and vanishes on the axis, the value lambda is attained at a point with r > 0. Levels S_n -> infinity give records with t_n -> T_*, because S is finite on [0, t] for every t < T_*. (c) Record identity. After the reflection x_2 -> -x_2 if necessary (it maps u^theta to -u^theta and preserves u^r, u^z and the equations), u^theta(r_n, z_n, t_n) = S_n > 0. Then d_t u^theta >= 0 at the point (one-sided derivative; t -> u^theta(r_n, z_n, t) is maximal at t_n on [0, t_n]), and d_r u^theta = d_z u^theta = 0, (d_rr + d_zz) u^theta <= 0 there (global spatial maximum). The equation at this point gives u^r S_n/r_n = -d_t u^theta + (d_rr + d_zz) u^theta - S_n/r_n^2 <= -S_n/r_n^2, i.e. -r_n u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1. Only the classical equation at one point is used. (d) Constants. In normalized variables U_n^theta(e_1, 0) = 1, |U_n^theta| <= 1 on R^3 x [-t_n/T_n, 0] (|u^theta(x, t)| <= S(t) <= S_n for t <= t_n), and -gamma_n U_n^R(e_1, 0) = -r_n u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1. Hence |U_n(e_1, 0)|^2 >= (U_n^theta)^2 + (U_n^R)^2 >= 1 + gamma_n^{-2} >= 1 + G^{-2}, since gamma_n = |Gamma(r_n, z_n, t_n)| <= G. By (K) with L = 2, A = 1, for every eps > 0 and n large |U_n(e_1, 0)| <= K + eps; so K^2 >= 1 + G^{-2} (in particular K > 1), and gamma_n^{-2} <= (K + eps)^2 - 1, whence liminf gamma_n >= (K^2 - 1)^{-1/2}, while gamma_n <= G. Every limit point gamma_* therefore lies in [(K^2 - 1)^{-1/2}, G], and nu_* = 1/gamma_* in [1/G, (K^2 - 1)^{1/2}]. Finally r_n = gamma_n/S_n <= G/S_n -> 0, and t_n/T_n = t_n S_n/r_n = t_n S_n^2/gamma_n >= t_n S_n^2/G -> infinity, because t_n -> T_* > 0.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation (using [LZ2, Corollary 1.3]); verification: adversarial audit rounds 1, 21, 22.

Theorem 2 (extraction through the terminal time). Pass to a subsequence with a fixed sign of the record swirl and, if necessary, reflect the angular coordinate so that u^theta(r_n, z_n, t_n) = S_n > 0. Under (K) and (TAIL), a further subsequence of U_n converges in C^{2,1}_loc(R^3 x (-infinity, 0]), including tau = 0, to a bounded ancient mild axisymmetric solution U of NS with viscosity nu_* and canonical pressure gradient, satisfying |U| <= K, |U^theta| <= 1 = U^theta(e_1, 0), -gamma_* U^R(e_1, 0) >= 1, |Gamma_U| <= G/gamma_*.

Key estimates. Mild with viscosity nu means U(tau) = e^{nu(tau - sigma) Delta} U(sigma) - int_sigma^tau e^{nu(tau - s) Delta} Pi div(U (x) U)(s) ds for sigma < tau <= 0 (Pi the Leray projector); the canonical pressure gradient of a bounded field is grad P = grad R_iR_j(chi U^i U^j) + int grad d_i d_j N(. - z) (1 - chi(z)) U^i U^j(z) dz (N the Newtonian potential, R_i the Riesz transforms, chi a compactly supported cutoff equal to 1 near the point; the result does not depend on chi). By Duhamel's formula for the physical solution (p = R_iR_j(u_i u_j)), U_n is mild with viscosity nu_n on [-t_n/T_n, 0]. (1) Kernels. The kernel O^h of e^{h Delta} Pi div satisfies |d^alpha O^h(x)| <= C_alpha (|x| + sqrt h)^{-4-|alpha|}, int |O^h| = c h^{-1/2}, int |O^h(x + e) - O^h(x)| dx <= C min(h^{-1/2}, |e| h^{-1}), int grad O^h = 0 and int |grad O^h(x)| |x|^a dx = c_a h^{-1+a/2} (0 < a < 1); the far pressure kernel satisfies |grad^m d_j d_k N(x)| <= C |x|^{-3-m}. (2) Tail. J_n(L, A) := sup_{-A <= s <= 0} int_{|z| > L} |U_n(z, s)|^2 |z|^{-4} dz. (TAIL) gives lim_L limsup_n J_n(L, A) = 0, and with (K), limsup_n J_n(L, A) < infinity for fixed L, A (the shell L < |z| < L_1 contributes at most 4 pi (K + 1)^2/L). (3) Local bounds. Fix L, A >= 1, k >= 0 and n_0 with nu_n in [nu_*/2, 2 nu_*] and |U_n| <= K + 1 on B_{64L} x [-A - 3, 0] for n >= n_0. Take radii 16L = rho_0 > rho_1 > ... > rho_{k+4} = 2L, restart times sigma_j = -A - 2 - 2^{-j} and delays delta_j = 2^{-j-2}, so that sigma_j - sigma_{j-1} = 2^{-j} >= delta_{j-1} and sigma_j + delta_j < -A - 2. Step j uses the mild formula from sigma_j, only for tau in [sigma_j + delta_j, 0] (never beyond tau = 0), and splits U_n (x) U_n by a cutoff chi_j equal to 1 on B_{(rho_j + rho_{j-1})/2} with support in B_{rho_{j-1}} (step 0: 1 on B_{32L}, support in B_{64L}); for |y| <= rho_j and z outside {chi_j = 1}, |y - z| >= c_j |z| with c_j > 0. With h = nu_n(tau - sigma_j) in [nu_* delta_j/2, 2 nu_*(A + 3)], the heat part has spatial derivatives of order alpha on B_{rho_j} at most C_alpha (K + 1) + C'_alpha J_n(64L, A + 3)^{1/2} (Cauchy-Schwarz on |z| > 64L); the far part satisfies |d^alpha N^far_n(y, tau)| <= C_{alpha,j} (A + 3) rho_j^{-|alpha|} J_n(rho_j, A + 3); by the kernel bounds of (1), the near part is uniformly C^a in space (a < 1) after step 0 and, inductively, uniformly C^{j, a_j} on B_{rho_j} x [sigma_j + delta_j, 0] for some a_j in (0, 1). After k + 4 steps all spatial derivatives up to order k + 3 are bounded on B_{2L} x [-A - 2, 0], uniformly in n >= n_0. Then d_tau U_n = nu_n Delta U_n - U_n.grad U_n - grad P_n is bounded on B_L x [-A, 0] with its spatial derivatives: the near pressure (cutoff 1 on B_{3L/2}, support in B_{2L}) by the Riesz transform bounds, and |grad^k grad P^far_n| <= C_k J_n(3L/2, A) (kernel of degree -4-k, |y - z| >= |z|/3). (4) Limit, in this order. Arzela-Ascoli for the grad^m U_n (bounded, uniformly Lipschitz in tau) and a diagonal argument give convergence in C_tau C^m_y on every B_L x [-A, 0], through tau = 0. Then the pressure gradients converge locally uniformly: for L > 2l the near parts with cutoff radius L converge on B_l, and the far parts are at most C J_n(L, A) for U_n and C K^2/L for the limit; let n -> infinity, then L -> infinity, using (TAIL). Then d_tau U_n converges locally uniformly, which gives C^{2,1}_loc convergence. In the mild formula the contributions of |z| > L_1 are at most C(tau - sigma, nu_*) J_n(L_1, |sigma|)^{1/2} (heat part) and C (tau - sigma) J_n(L_1, |sigma|) (nonlinear part) for U_n, and C K L_1^{-1/2}, C K^2/L_1 for U; the rest converges by dominated convergence (nu_n -> nu_*). The listed properties are pointwise limits of (K), of the normalized record in the proof of Theorem 1 and of Gamma_{U_n} = Gamma/gamma_n (Gamma_U = R U^theta). For U itself (TAIL) is automatic: sup_tau int_{|y| > L} |U|^2 |y|^{-4} dy <= 4 pi K^2/L.

The Hoelder bootstrap in (3) uses only the kernel bounds of (1), and the passage to the limit in the mild formula in (4) uses only the displayed tail bounds and dominated convergence; these routine steps are indicated rather than written out in full.

Status: proof with key estimates (routine steps indicated, not written out in full); source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 1, 5, 21, 22.

Theorem 3 (reduction). W(y, s) = gamma_* U(y, gamma_* s) belongs to LL. Conversely, for every W in LL some member W' of its closure under translations, parabolic scalings and the reflection x_2 -> -x_2 has, after rescaling to viscosity 1/gamma', every property in Theorem 2 with (K, G, gamma_*) replaced by some admissible (K', ||Gamma_W||_infty, gamma'). Consequently: LL empty implies the exclusion of Case 1, and any exclusion argument using only the properties in Theorem 2 (for all admissible K, G) proves that LL is empty, which is the bounded-circulation case of the Liouville conjecture of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak, open on R^3 to our knowledge.

Proof. We use the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions: a bounded mild ancient solution W with ||W||_infty <= K_0 and viscosity nu satisfies ||d_tau^m grad^k W||_infty <= C(k, m, K_0, nu) ([KNSS, sec. 4]; also proved as in Theorem 2, with no far field). With Arzela-Ascoli and dominated convergence in the mild formula (no tail term, the bound being global), every sequence of such solutions with a common bound has a subsequence converging in C^infty_loc(R^3 x (-infinity, 0]) to a bounded mild ancient solution. LL is invariant under Z-translations, time translations into the past, the reflection x_2 -> -x_2 and the parabolic scalings W -> lambda W(lambda ., lambda^2 .), and Gamma is invariant under the last. (a) W(y, s) = gamma_* U(y, gamma_* s) turns viscosity nu_* = 1/gamma_* into 1 and is a bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with ||W||_infty <= gamma_* K, ||Gamma_W||_infty <= G, sup |W^theta| = W^theta(e_1, 0) = gamma_* > 0 and -W^R(e_1, 0) >= 1; so W is in LL. (b) Let W be in LL, K_0 = ||W||_infty, G_0 = ||Gamma_W||_infty, s_0 = sup |W^theta| > 0 over R^3 x (-infinity, 0], and C_1 := ||grad W||_infty, finite by the smoothing estimate. Since W^theta/R is, up to sign, an entry of grad W in the frame (e_R, e_theta, e_Z), |W^theta| <= C_1 R; also |W^theta| <= G_0/R. Take (R_k, Z_k, tau_k) with |W^theta| -> s_0; eventually |W^theta| > s_0/2 there, so R_k lies in [s_0/(2 C_1), 2 G_0/s_0]. Pass to a subsequence with R_k -> R_* > 0 and a fixed sign (+ after the reflection). The translates W(. + Z_k e_3, . + tau_k) converge to some W'' in LL with |W''^theta| <= s_0 everywhere and W''^theta(R_* e_1, 0) = s_0. Put W'(y, tau) = R_* W''(R_* y, R_*^2 tau) and gamma' := R_* s_0. Then sup |W'^theta| = W'^theta(e_1, 0) = gamma' = Gamma_{W'}(e_1, 0) <= G_0, and the record calculation in the proof of Theorem 1 at this space-time maximum (viscosity 1, R = 1) gives -W'^R(e_1, 0) >= 1. U'(y, tau) := W'(y, tau/gamma')/gamma' is a smooth bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with viscosity 1/gamma' and canonical pressure gradient, with |U'| <= R_* K_0/gamma' = K_0/s_0 =: K', |U'^theta| <= 1 = U'^theta(e_1, 0), -gamma' U'^R(e_1, 0) >= 1 and |Gamma_{U'}| <= G_0/gamma'; moreover K'^2 >= |U'(e_1, 0)|^2 >= (U'^theta)^2 + (U'^R)^2 >= 1 + gamma'^{-2}. Thus (K', G_0, gamma') is admissible, meaning gamma' in (0, G_0] and K'^2 >= 1 + gamma'^{-2}. (c) Consequences. In Case 1, Theorem 2 and (a) produce a member of LL, so LL empty excludes Case 1. Conversely, an argument that derives a contradiction from the properties in Theorem 2 alone, for all admissible parameters, applies to U' for every W in LL and hence proves LL empty. W' is in general a different member of the closure of W, and nothing here realizes W as a limit of a fixed finite-energy datum.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 1, 21, 22.

Theorem 4 (Route A, the critical R^3 eta mechanism). Let W be a bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with viscosity nu and bounded circulation, K := ||W||_infty, V the meridional part of W and eta_W = omega_W^theta/R. If sup R^3 |eta_W| < infinity, then the meridional velocity V is c(tau) e_3 + b_0 with |b_0| <= C min(1, 1/R) (5D Newton potential plus a bounded harmonic remainder), c is constant, and W = c_0 e_3 (Galilean change of frame and [KNSS, Theorem 5.3]). Hence every member of LL has sup R^3 |eta| = infinity. The weighted equation for R^3 eta has zero-order coefficient 3(nu + R V^R)/R^2. The elementary maximum-principle argument requiring a nonpositive zero-order coefficient would require -R V^R >= nu throughout the relevant region, and the vertical-flux identity (Proposition 25(b)) rules out that condition on vertical segments longer than K R^2/nu. This does not exclude every weighted maximum-principle argument; the source term also requires control. The global physical bound sup r^3 |eta| <= C is itself a known regularity criterion ([LP, Corollary 1.1]), so it is a valid but strong target.

Proof. The scaling W(y, tau) -> nu^{-1} W(y, tau/nu) makes the viscosity 1 and multiplies eta by 1/nu, so in (a) to (c) nu = 1. Let K_0 = ||W||_infty, G_0 = ||Gamma_W||_infty and A_eta := sup_{R, Z, tau} R^3 |eta_W| < infinity. By the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions (stated in Theorem 3) all derivatives of W are bounded. omega^theta extends oddly in R, so eta is even and smooth in R and lifts to a smooth bounded function on R^5 = R^4_{y'} x R, with |eta| <= C_eta := ||grad omega||_infty; thus |eta(y)| <= m(|y'|) := min(C_eta, A_eta |y'|^{-3}). (a) Newton potential. Fix tau and let phi_0 = N_5 * eta, N_5(x) = c_5 |x|^{-3}, c_5 = 1/(8 pi^2), so -Delta_5 phi_0 = eta. Integrating the kernels first in the axial variable, int_R (d^2 + s^2)^{-3/2} ds = 2/d^2 and int_R (d^2 + s^2)^{-2} ds = pi/(2 d^3), so |phi_0(x)| <= C int_{R^4} m(|y'|) |x' - y'|^{-2} dy' and |grad phi_0(x)| <= C int_{R^4} m(|y'|) |x' - y'|^{-3} dy', both finite. For R = |x'| >= 2 split R^4 into I = {|y'| < R/2}, where |x' - y'| >= R/2 and int_I m <= C(1 + R); II = {|y' - x'| < R/2}, where m <= 8 A_eta R^{-3}, int |w|^{-2} dw = C R^2 and int |w|^{-3} dw = C R over |w| < R/2; and III, the rest, where |x' - y'| >= |y'|/3 and the integrands are at most C A_eta |y'|^{-5} and C A_eta |y'|^{-6}. Hence |phi_0| <= C/R and |grad phi_0| <= C/R^2 for R >= 2, with C = C(A_eta, C_eta); phi_0 is invariant under rotations of y', so phi_0 = phi_0(R, Z). (b) Harmonic remainder. b_0 := curl(R phi_0 e_theta) = -R d_Z phi_0 e_R + (2 phi_0 + R d_R phi_0) e_Z is smooth and divergence free, |b_0| <= C_1 min(1, 1/R) with C_1 = C(A_eta, C_eta), and curl b_0 = R(-Delta_5 phi_0) e_theta = omega^theta e_theta. V is smooth, div V = 0 and curl V = omega^theta e_theta. So h := V - b_0 is bounded, divergence free and curl free; its Cartesian components are bounded harmonic functions on R^3, hence constant in space, and rotation invariance gives h = c(tau) e_3. (c) c is constant and W = c_0 e_3. Translates W(. + R_k e_1, .), R_k -> infinity, converge along a subsequence (compactness, stated in Theorem 3) to a bounded mild ancient solution which equals c(tau) e_3, since |W^R| = |b_0^R| <= C_1/R, |W^theta| <= G_0/R and W^Z = c(tau) + O(1/R). For a spatially constant mild solution the nonlinear Duhamel term vanishes (the kernel of e^{h Delta} Pi div is integrable with integral 0) and the heat semigroup fixes constants, so c(tau) = c_0. The Galilean transformation W'(x, tau) := W(x + c_0 tau e_3, tau) - c_0 e_3 is a smooth bounded axisymmetric NS solution with bounded derivatives, whose meridional part is b_0 and whose swirl is W^theta, both evaluated at (x + c_0 tau e_3, tau); hence |W'| <= (C_1 + G_0)/R for R >= 1 and |W'| <= (K_0 + |c_0|)/R for R <= 1. By [KNSS, Theorem 5.3] (a bounded weak axisymmetric ancient solution with |u| <= C (x_1^2 + x_2^2)^{-1/2} vanishes identically) W' = 0, i.e. W = c_0 e_3; so W^theta = 0 and no member of LL satisfies sup R^3 |eta| < infinity. (d) Weighted equation (viscosity nu). From (d_tau + V.grad) eta = nu Delta_5 eta + d_Z(q^2), q = W^theta/R, direct differentiation gives for f = R^3 eta: (d_tau + V.grad) f = nu(f_RR - 3 R^{-1} f_R + f_ZZ) + 3 R^{-2}(nu + R V^R) f + R^{-1} d_Z(Gamma^2). The zero-order coefficient is nonpositive exactly where -R V^R >= nu. Vertical flux: the flux of the divergence-free field V (|V| <= K) out of the cylinder {R < rho, |Z - Z_0| < h} vanishes, so 2 pi rho int_{Z_0-h}^{Z_0+h} V^R(rho, Z) dZ = -2 pi int_0^rho [V^Z(R, Z_0 + h) - V^Z(R, Z_0 - h)] R dR, whence |int_{Z_0-h}^{Z_0+h} rho V^R(rho, Z) dZ| <= K rho^2. If -rho V^R >= nu on that segment, then 2 h nu <= K rho^2, which fails when the length 2h exceeds K rho^2/nu. (e) Physical bound. r^3 |eta| = r^2 |omega^theta|, and [LP, Corollary 1.1] states that |omega^theta| <= C r^{-2} gives a bounded angular stream function and regularity; a bound uniform on [t_0, T_*) suffices after restarting at t_0.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation (using [KNSS, Theorem 5.3] and [LP, Corollary 1.1]); verification: adversarial audit rounds 2, 21, 22; checked by a script

Theorem 5 (Route B, radial-compression comparison principle). Let W be a bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with viscosity nu and bounded circulation. If -W^R(R, Z, tau) <= b(R) for R >= R_0, all Z and tau <= 0, with b locally integrable on [R_0, infinity) and int_{R_0}^infinity R exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_0}^R b) dR = infinity, then Gamma_W = 0 and W is constant. The threshold b = 2 nu/R is sharp for the scalar exterior problem (the line sink -(c/R) e_R with c > 2 nu admits the bounded nonconstant steady solution Gamma = C_0 - R^{-(c/nu - 2)}).

Proof. Let V be the meridional part of W. Gamma = R W^theta solves d_tau Gamma + V.grad Gamma = nu L Gamma on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0]. By the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions (stated in Theorem 3) ||grad W||_infty < infinity, so Gamma is uniformly Lipschitz in the spatial variables on each bounded radial strip and |Gamma| <= ||grad W||_infty R^2. Envelope: B(R) := sup_{Z, tau <= 0} (-V^R(R, Z, tau)) satisfies |B| <= ||W||_infty and |B(R) - B(R')| <= ||grad W||_infty |R - R'|, so B is bounded and Lipschitz; B <= b on [R_0, infinity), hence exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_0}^R B) >= exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_0}^R b), and the scale integral with B also diverges. (An upward mollification of b would not do: for b = 2 nu/R the integral diverges, for b + eps it converges.) For R_1 >= R_0 let sigma_{R_1}(R) = int_{R_1}^R rho exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_1}^rho B) d rho, the scale function [Fel] of the radial generator nu d_RR + (B - nu/R) d_R. Then sigma_{R_1} is C^2, sigma_{R_1}(R_1) = 0, sigma_{R_1}' > 0, sigma_{R_1}(R) -> infinity as R -> infinity, and nu(sigma_{R_1}'' - sigma_{R_1}'/R) = -B sigma_{R_1}'. Step 1 (outer comparison). Let m(R_1) = sup_{Z, tau} Gamma(R_1, Z, tau) and, for eps > 0, w = Gamma - m(R_1) - eps sigma_{R_1}(R). Then d_tau w + V.grad w - nu L w = -eps (V^R + B) sigma_{R_1}' <= 0 on {R > R_1}, w <= 0 on {R = R_1}, and w -> -infinity as R -> infinity uniformly in (Z, tau). Suppose sup_{R >= R_1} w = m > 0. Since Gamma - m(R_1) <= C (R - R_1) near R_1 by the gradient bound, near-maximizing points have R in a compact subinterval of (R_1, infinity). Translate them in (Z, tau) to Z = 0, tau = 0 and pass to a limit (compactness, stated in Theorem 3); the limit field satisfies the same envelope (it is preserved under Z, tau translations and locally uniform convergence), so the limit w~ is a subsolution attaining its maximum m at an interior point of {R > R_1} x (-infinity, 0]. The operator d_tau - nu(d_RR + d_ZZ) + (V^R + nu/R) d_R + V^Z d_Z has locally bounded continuous coefficients on {R > 0}, so Nirenberg's strong maximum principle [Nir] gives w~ = m on {R > R_1} x (-infinity, 0], contradicting w~ -> -infinity. Hence Gamma <= m(R_1) + eps sigma_{R_1} on R >= R_1, and letting eps -> 0, sup_{R >= R_1} Gamma = m(R_1). Step 2. Suppose G_+ := sup Gamma > 0. By Step 1 with R_1 = R_0, G_+ = sup_{R <= R_0} Gamma, so near-maximizing points have R in [delta, R_0] for some delta > 0 (|Gamma| <= C R^2). Their translated limits attain G_+ at an interior point and by [Nir] equal G_+ on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0], contradicting Gamma = 0 on the axis. So Gamma <= 0, and the same argument for -Gamma gives Gamma = 0. W then has no swirl and is constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.2 and Remark 6.1]. Sharpness: V = -(c/R) e_R is divergence free on R > 0; with p = c/nu - 2 > 0, Gamma = C_0 - R^{-p} satisfies V.grad Gamma = -c p R^{-p-2} = -nu p (p + 2) R^{-p-2} = nu(Gamma_RR - Gamma_R/R), so it is a bounded nonconstant steady solution on R >= 1, while for b = c/R the scale integrand is a multiple of R^{1 - c/nu}, integrable at infinity exactly when c > 2 nu.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 1, 14, 21, 22.

Proposition 6 (circulation estimates for the limit). |Gamma_U| <= (G/gamma_*)(1 - (1 + aR) e^{-aR}) with a = K gamma_*; sup_{Z, tau} int_0^infinity q_U^2 R dR <= 1 + log((KG)^2/2), and (KG)^2 > 2 holds automatically in Case 1. For every member of LL, sup Gamma (if positive) is not attained and is approached only as R -> infinity; along sequences escaping to radial infinity, Cartesian translates have subsequences converging to constant vectors, and along such sequences with Gamma -> sup Gamma the correspondingly translated circulations converge to sup Gamma locally uniformly.

Proof. Let U be the limit of Theorem 2, V its meridional part (|V^R| <= K), G' = G/gamma_*, a = K gamma_* and q_U = U^theta/R. Gamma = Gamma_U = R U^theta solves d_tau Gamma + V.grad Gamma = nu_* L Gamma on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0], with |Gamma| <= G' and |Gamma| <= R (since |U^theta| <= 1). (a) Barrier. phi := G' phi_a, phi_a(R) = 1 - (1 + aR) e^{-aR}, satisfies phi' = G' a^2 R e^{-aR} >= 0 and phi'' - phi'/R = -a phi', so nu_*(phi'' - phi'/R) = -K phi'. For w = Gamma - phi, d_tau w + V.grad w - nu_* L w = -(V^R + K) phi' <= 0 on {R > 0}. Also w <= G'(1 + aR) e^{-aR} -> 0 as R -> infinity, and w <= Gamma <= R. Suppose m := sup w > 0. Points with w -> m have R in [m/2, R_1], where G'(1 + aR_1) e^{-aR_1} = m/2. Translating U in Z and tau and passing to a limit (compactness, stated in Theorem 3, viscosity nu_*) gives U~ with the same bounds |U~| <= K, |U~^theta| <= 1, |Gamma~| <= G', so w~ = Gamma~ - phi is again a subsolution, w~ <= m, and w~(R_infty, 0, 0) = m for some R_infty in [m/2, R_1]. The operator d_tau - nu_*(d_RR + d_ZZ) + (V~^R + nu_*/R) d_R + V~^Z d_Z has locally bounded continuous coefficients on {R > 0}, so Nirenberg's strong maximum principle [Nir] gives w~ = m on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0], contradicting w~ <= R near the axis. Hence Gamma <= phi, and likewise -Gamma <= phi. (b) Slice integral. q_U^2 R = Gamma^2/R^3, and by (a) with 1 - (1 + x) e^{-x} <= x^2/2, |Gamma| <= min(G' a^2 R^2/2, R, G'); note G' a = KG. If (KG)^2 >= 2, split at rho_1 = 2/(G' a^2) <= rho_2 = G': the regions R < rho_1, rho_1 < R < rho_2 and R > rho_2 contribute at most (G' a^2/2)^2 rho_1^2/2 = 1/2, log(rho_2/rho_1) = log((KG)^2/2) and G'^2/(2 rho_2^2) = 1/2, so the integral is at most 1 + log((KG)^2/2). If (KG)^2 < 2, splitting min(G' a^2 R^2/2, G') at sqrt2/a gives at most (KG)^2/2 < 1. In Case 1 only the first alternative occurs: at the normalized record 1 = Gamma_U(e_1, 0) <= G' phi_a(1) < G' a^2/2, because phi_a(1) = int_0^a s e^{-s} ds < a^2/2, and G' >= 1 because sup |Gamma_U| >= 1; hence (KG)^2 = G'^2 a^2 >= G' a^2 > 2. (c) Members of LL. Let W be in LL, K_0 = ||W||_infty, G_0 = ||Gamma_W||_infty and G_+ = sup Gamma_W > 0. If sup_{R <= R_1, Z, tau} Gamma_W = G_+ for some R_1, near-maximizing points have R bounded below (|Gamma_W| <= ||grad W||_infty R^2); their translated limit in (Z, tau) attains G_+ at an interior point, and since Gamma_W solves the same equation with viscosity 1, [Nir] makes it equal to G_+ on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0], impossible because it vanishes on the axis. So sup_{R <= R_1} Gamma_W < G_+ for every R_1, and G_+ is approached only as R -> infinity. Now let R_k -> infinity, W_k = W(. + R_k e_1 + Z_k e_3, . + tau_k) and rho_k = ((R_k + x_1)^2 + x_2^2)^{1/2}. The second Cartesian component satisfies |W_k^2| <= K_0 |x_2|/rho_k + G_0/rho_k -> 0 locally uniformly; W_k is the rotation by an angle O(1/R_k) of W written in the meridional coordinates (rho_k, x_3), so the uniform gradient bounds make every subsequential limit independent of x_2, of the form (v_1, 0, v_3)(x_1, x_3, tau). Integrating the three-dimensional kernel of e^{h Delta} Pi div in x_2 (absolutely convergent by the kernel bound of Theorem 2) gives the two-dimensional kernel, so (v_1, v_3) is a bounded mild ancient planar solution; [KNSS, Theorem 5.1] makes it constant in space, and the mild formula for a spatial constant makes it constant in time. The limit is a constant vector a_0 e_1 + c_0 e_3. If moreover Gamma_W(R_k, Z_k, tau_k) -> G_+, then Gamma_k(x_1, x_3, tau) := Gamma_W(R_k + x_1, Z_k + x_3, tau_k + tau) solves d_tau Gamma_k + V_k.grad Gamma_k = Delta_{x_1,x_3} Gamma_k - (R_k + x_1)^{-1} d_1 Gamma_k, V_k the translated meridional field, with |Gamma_k| <= G_0 and coefficients bounded in C^m locally uniformly in k (smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions), so interior Schauder estimates give local C^{2+alpha,1+alpha/2} bounds (some alpha in (0, 1)) through the terminal time; every subsequential limit is a bounded ancient solution of d_tau g + (a_0, c_0).grad g = Delta g in the plane attaining its maximum G_+ at the origin, hence equal to G_+ by [Nir].

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 1, 21, 22.

Theorem 7 (pressure Hessian under (A); Riccati bound). For s <= t < T_*, with historical M, D, H at time t and the canonical pressure,
  ||grad^2 p(s)||_infty <= C_0 D(t)^2 [1 + log_+(M(t) H(t)/D(t)^2)],
with an absolute C_0. Under (A) this is at most C_0(1 + log_+ C_*) D(t)^2, and D is locally Lipschitz with 0 <= D' <= C_2 D^2 a.e. for late t, C_2 = 1 + C_0(1 + log_+ C_*); hence D(t) >= 1/(C_2 (T_* - t)) for late t.

Proof. Fix s <= t, write M, D, H for M(t), D(t), H(t), let c denote absolute constants (depending only on the choice of tensor norms), and assume u is not identically 0. Put f := d_i u_j d_j u_i (s); by incompressibility -Delta p = d_i d_j(u_i u_j) = f, and ||f||_infty <= c D^2, ||grad f||_infty <= c D H. Since u(s) is smooth with u(s), grad u(s) in L^2, f lies in L^1, L^infty and C^1, and the canonical pressure p = R_i R_j(u_i u_j) equals N * f with N = 1/(4 pi |x|) (both have Fourier transform -xi_i xi_j (u_i u_j)^(xi)/|xi|^2, so there is no harmonic ambiguity). Hence
  d_k d_l p(x_0) = -(1/3) delta_kl f(x_0) + lim_{eps -> 0} int_{|y| > eps} K_kl(y) f(x_0 - y) dy,   K_kl(y) = (3 y_k y_l - delta_kl |y|^2)/(4 pi |y|^5),
where K_kl has zero mean on spheres and |K| <= c|y|^{-3}, |grad K| <= c|y|^{-4}, |grad^2 K| <= c|y|^{-5}. Split the integral at radii 0 < a <= b. Near field (|y| < a): by the zero mean the integrand can be replaced by K(y)(f(x_0 - y) - f(x_0)), which gives at most c a ||grad f||_infty <= c D H a. Intermediate annulus (a < |y| < b): at most c ||f||_infty log(b/a) <= c D^2 log(b/a). Far field (|y| > b): write f(x_0 - y) = d_{y_i} d_{y_j} F_ij(y) with F_ij(y) = (u_i u_j)(x_0 - y), |F| <= M^2, |grad F| <= 2 M D, and integrate by parts twice on {b < |y| < Lambda}; the terms on |y| = Lambda are at most c(M D/Lambda + M^2/Lambda^2) -> 0, the inner sphere |y| = b contributes at most 4 pi b^2 (c b^{-3} M D + c b^{-4} M^2), and the volume term is at most c M^2 int_{|y| > b} |y|^{-5} dy = c M^2/b^2. Altogether
  ||grad^2 p(s)||_infty <= c (D^2 + D H a + D^2 log(b/a) + M D/b + M^2/b^2),   0 < a <= b.
Choose b = M/D and a = min(D/H, M/D). If a = D/H the bound is c D^2 (1 + log(M H/D^2)). If a = b = M/D < D/H, then M H < D^2, the annulus is empty and the bound is c D^2. This proves the first inequality; under (A), M H/D^2 <= C_* at late t.
Riccati step. Let J = grad u (Frobenius norm) and a(t) := ||J(t)||_infty, continuous in t. From d_t J + u.grad J + J^2 + grad^2 p = Delta J,
  (d_t + u.grad - Delta)|J|^2 = -2|grad J|^2 - 2 J:J^2 - 2 J:grad^2 p.
The spatial maximum of |J(t)|^2 is attained (J(t) decays), and there grad|J|^2 = 0, Delta|J|^2 <= 0 and |J:J^2| <= |J|^3; the maximum-point argument gives for the upper Dini derivative D^+ a(t) <= a(t)^2 + ||grad^2 p(t)||_infty. Passage to the running maximum D: where a(t) < D(t), D is constant near t; at a contact point a(t) = D(t), D(t + h) - D(t) <= max(0, sup_{t <= t' <= t + h} (a(t') - a(t))), so D^+ D(t) <= max(0, D^+ a(t)) <= D(t)^2 + C_0(1 + log_+ C_*) D(t)^2 = C_2 D(t)^2, using the first part with s = t and (A). Thus for late t the continuous nondecreasing function D has upper Dini derivative at most C_2 D^2 at every point; hence it is locally Lipschitz with 0 <= D' <= C_2 D^2 a.e., so d(1/D)/dt >= -C_2 a.e. and 1/D(t) - 1/D(b) <= C_2 (b - t) for late t < b < T_*. Since D(b) -> infinity as b -> T_* [BKM], D(t) >= 1/(C_2 (T_* - t)). The coefficient 1 in C_2 refers to the Frobenius norm.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 2, 21, 22.

Proposition 8 (normalized form of (A); gradient-record rescaling; abstract obstructions).
 (a) At the swirl record, m_n = M(t_n)/S_n, d_n = r_n D(t_n)/S_n, h_n = r_n^2 H(t_n)/S_n satisfy m_n >= (1 + gamma_n^{-2})^{1/2}, d_n >= 1, h_n >= 2, and (A) reads m_n h_n <= C_* d_n^2. These are global historical numbers whose maximizers need not lie near the record; passing (A) with its constant C_* to the limit U has not been justified.
 (b) Every W in LL satisfies the ancient shape inequality Q(W) := sup|W| sup|grad^2 W|/sup|grad W|^2 < infinity. If LL is nonempty, Q is unbounded on LL (axial Galilean transforms increase sup|W| without changing the derivatives).
 (c) Rescaling at a gradient record s (length M/D, time 1/D, velocity M, all at time s) gives fields V with |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0, 0)|, |grad^2 V| <= C_* on the whole past and viscosity kappa = D/M^2 <= C_P (historical mild smoothing). (A) is used only at s. If kappa -> kappa_infinity > 0, the limit is, after the viscosity normalization W(y, tau) = kappa_infinity^{-1} V(y, tau/kappa_infinity), a member of LL when the axis stays at bounded distance, and it is planar, hence constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.1], contradicting |grad V(0, 0)| = 1, when the axis recedes. If kappa -> 0 the limit is an ancient swirl-free Euler field, axisymmetric about a fixed line or planar.
 (d) (O1) A smooth bounded divergence-free axisymmetric drift (stream function R^2 Z (1 + R^2 + Z^2)^{-1/2}) admits a bounded nonconstant steady solution of the circulation equation tending to 0 at the axis; so bounded drift alone does not make the circulation equation rigid. This drift is not a Navier-Stokes field and violates the hypotheses of Theorems 4 and 5.

Key estimates. (a) m_n >= 1 since M >= S. Since u^theta/r is, up to sign, an entry of grad u in the frame (e_r, e_theta, e_z), |u^theta|/r <= |grad u|, so d_n >= 1. With the record point rotated to (r_n, 0, z_n), g(rho) := u_2(rho e_1 + z_n e_3, t_n) has g(0) = 0, |g(r_n)| = S_n and g'(r_n) = d_r u^theta(r_n, z_n, t_n) = 0 (|u^theta(t_n)| is maximal there); Taylor's formula on [0, r_n] gives H(t_n) >= 2 S_n/r_n^2, i.e. h_n >= 2. Theorem 1 gives r_n |u^r| >= 1 at the record point, so M(t_n)^2 >= S_n^2 + r_n^{-2}, i.e. m_n >= (1 + gamma_n^{-2})^{1/2}. Multiplying (A) at t_n by r_n^2/S_n^2 gives m_n h_n <= C_* d_n^2.
(b) sup|grad^2 W| < infinity by the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions (sec. 1.1), and sup|grad W| > 0 since W is not constant. For c in R, W_c(x, tau) := W(x - c tau e_3, tau) + c e_3 is again in LL, and Q(W_c) >= (|c| - ||W||_infty) ||grad^2 W||_infty/||grad W||_infty^2 -> infinity as |c| -> infinity (||grad^2 W||_infty > 0, as a bounded field with vanishing Hessian is constant).
(c) With D = D(s), M = M(s) and x(s) a maximizer of |grad u(s)|, V(y, tau) := u(x(s) + (M/D) y, s + tau/D)/M solves NS with viscosity kappa on R^3 x [-s D, 0], and s D(s) -> infinity. Monotonicity of M, D gives |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0, 0)|; (A) at s alone gives |grad^2 V| <= M H/D^2 <= C_*, and Theorem 7 gives |grad^2 P| <= C_0(1 + log_+ C_*) for the rescaled pressure P; kappa <= C_P for s >= t_P. The rescaled circulation is Gamma D/M^2, so |V^theta| <= min(R, G kappa/R) <= (G kappa)^{1/2}, where R is the rescaled distance to the axis, which passes at distance a := r(x(s)) D/M from the origin. Along records s_n -> T_* take kappa_n -> kappa_infinity in [0, C_P] and a_n bounded or a_n -> infinity. Viscous case (kappa_infinity > 0): the smoothing estimate and the convergence argument of Theorem 2 without its far-field part give C^infinity_loc convergence to a bounded mild ancient V with |grad V(0, 0)| = 1. For bounded a_n, V is axisymmetric about a fixed line, |Gamma_V| <= G kappa_infinity, and V^theta is not identically 0 (otherwise V is constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.2 and Remark 6.1]), so W is in LL. For a_n -> infinity, |V_n^theta| <= G kappa_n/R -> 0 locally and the limit is planar, V = (v_1, 0, v_3)(y_1, y_3, tau), a bounded ancient mild 2D solution (the 3D kernel integrated in the inactive variable is the 2D kernel), hence constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.1 and Remark 6.1]. Inviscid case (kappa_infinity = 0): ||V_n^theta||_infty <= (G kappa_n)^{1/2} -> 0; ||grad P_n||_infty <= C (grad N is integrable on the unit ball, and outside it two derivatives move onto the kernel), so ||d_tau V_n||_infty <= C(1 + C_*), and interpolation with the Hessian bound makes grad V_n equicontinuous in time. A subsequence converges in C^1 in space, locally uniformly in time, to a weak ancient swirl-free Euler flow V with |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0, 0)| and ||grad^2 V||_infty <= C_*, axisymmetric about a fixed line or planar. Off the axis Omega_n := omega^theta(V_n)/R solves d_tau Omega_n + div(B_n Omega_n) = kappa_n (Delta + (2/R) d_R) Omega_n + d_Z(|V_n^theta|^2/R^2), B_n the meridional part; against test functions phi supported in {R >= delta} the viscous term is at most sqrt2 C_* kappa_n int (|Delta phi| + 2 delta^{-1} |grad phi|) (as |Omega_n| <= sqrt2 |grad^2 V_n|) and the source at most G^2 kappa_n^2 delta^{-4} ||d_Z phi||_{L^1}, so the continuous limit Omega solves d_tau Omega + div(B Omega) = 0 weakly off the axis. Characteristics do not reach the axis since |V^R| <= ||grad V||_infty R. In the planar case the planar vorticity is weakly transported.
(d) Psi is the Stokes stream function; with rho := (1 + R^2 + Z^2)^{1/2}, V^R = -R^{-1} d_Z Psi = -R(1 + R^2)/rho^3 and V^Z = R^{-1} d_R Psi = Z(2 + R^2 + 2Z^2)/rho^3, so |V^R| <= 1 and |V^Z| <= 2. The steady circulation equation nu L Gamma = V.grad Gamma reads A Gamma = 0, A := nu(d_RR + d_ZZ) - (nu/R + V^R) d_R - V^Z d_Z. Upper barrier: beta := R/sqrt(nu) has A beta = -(nu/R + V^R)/sqrt(nu) <= 0 for R^2 <= nu (as -V^R <= R). Lower barrier: for eps in (0, 1] and R_1 := max(1, 1.0001 nu (11 + 2/eps^2)(2 + eps^2)^{3/2}) (so R_1 >= sqrt(nu)), w := 1 - R_1/R - Z^2/(eps^2 R^2) is positive only on the horn O_eps := {w > 0}, contained in {R > R_1, |Z| < eps R}; there nu L w = -nu(3 R_1/R^3 + 8 Z^2/(eps^2 R^4) + 2/(eps^2 R^2)) >= -nu(11 + 2/eps^2) R^{-2}, -V^R >= (2 + eps^2)^{-3/2}, w_R >= R_1/R^2 and -V^Z w_Z >= 0, so R^2 A w >= (2 + eps^2)^{-3/2} R_1 - nu(11 + 2/eps^2) > 0. Existence: on Omega_k := (1/k, k) x (-k, k), k > max(R_1, 2), solve A Gamma_k = 0 with boundary values min(1, R/sqrt(nu)) by [GT, Theorem 6.13]. The maximum principle gives 0 <= Gamma_k <= 1, Gamma_k <= beta on {R < sqrt(nu)} and Gamma_k >= w on O_eps (where O_eps meets the boundary of Omega_k, Gamma_k = 1). Interior Schauder estimates uniform in k and a diagonal argument give Gamma in C^infinity({R > 0}) with A Gamma = 0 and max(w, 0) <= Gamma <= min(1, R/sqrt(nu)): bounded, nonconstant, and -> 0 at the axis. Not a Navier-Stokes field: eta = Z(7 + 4R^2 + 4Z^2) rho^{-5}, and along Z = R/2, V.grad eta = (384/125) R^{-3} + o(R^{-3}), while nu Delta_5 eta and d_Z(q^2) = 2 Gamma d_Z Gamma/R^4 are O(R^{-4}) (interior estimates give d_Z Gamma = O(1)), so the steady eta equation fails at large R. Also V^R(R, 0) = -R(1 + R^2)^{-1/2} -> -1, which violates the envelope condition of Theorem 5 and the C/R bound of [KNSS, Theorem 5.3].

In the inviscid case of (c), the time bound for V_n and the Hessian bound give ||grad V_n(t + h) - grad V_n(t)||_infty = O(|h|^{1/2}) by interpolation, which is the time equicontinuity used. The viscous passage is the convergence argument of Theorem 2 (with globally bounded tails), and for receding axes the three-dimensional kernel integrated in the inactive variable is the two-dimensional mild kernel. These routine steps are indicated rather than written out in full.

Status: proof with key estimates (routine steps indicated, not written out in full); source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 2, 21, 22; checked by scripts

Theorem 9 (Type I gradient growth is impossible under (A)). Assume (A). Then limsup_{t -> T_*} (T_* - t) D(t) = infinity. Equivalently, (A) together with D(t) <= C_I/(T_* - t) on late times is contradictory. The proof uses neither (K), (TAIL) nor the swirl records.

Key estimates. Assume (A) and D(t) <= C_I/(T_* - t) on [t_I, T_*); fix t_0 >= max(t_A, t_I) so late that the Riccati bound of Theorem 7 holds on [t_0, T_*). With delta := T_* - t, 1/(C_2 delta) <= D <= C_I/delta, so A_0 := C_2 C_I >= 1. Put l := delta M and f := delta M^2 = l^2/delta. Leray's lower bound [Ler] gives ||u(t)||_infty >= c_L delta^{-1/2} (c_L > 0 absolute), so f >= c_L^2. The interpolation ||g||_infty <= c ||g||_2^{2/5} ||grad g||_infty^{3/5} and ||u||_2^2 <= E_0 give M <= c E_0^{1/5} C_I^{3/5} delta^{-3/5}, so l <= c' delta^{2/5} -> 0. Call tau a running-minimum time if l(tau) = min_{[t_0, tau]} l; the largest minimum point of l on [t_0, t] is one and tends to T_*. For late tau, D(tau) is attained at a gradient record s in [t_0, tau] with delta(s) <= A_0 delta(tau); as M is nondecreasing, l(t) >= l(s)/A_0 on [t_0, s], f(s) >= f(tau)/A_0 and delta(s) D(s) >= 1/C_2. Either (E) f(tau_n) -> infinity along running-minimum times tau_n -> T_*, or (P) f <= F at all late running-minimum times.
Case (E). Local notation (differs from sec. 1.1): s_n the gradient records attached to tau_n, T_n := T_* - s_n, M_n := M(s_n), l_n := T_n M_n, nu_n := T_n/l_n^2 = 1/f(s_n) <= A_0/f(tau_n) -> 0. Rotate the gradient maximizer to x_n = (r_n, 0, z_n); along a subsequence r_n/l_n stays bounded (finite axis, a_n := (0, 0, z_n)) or tends to infinity (receding axis, a_n := x_n). V_n(y, sigma) := u(a_n + l_n y, T_* + T_n sigma)/M_n, sigma in [-(T_* - t_0)/T_n, -1], solves NS with viscosity nu_n. Write V_n = B_n + Z_n with B_n the meridional part and Z_n := V_n^theta e_theta the swirl part with respect to the original axis; R_n(y) := |a_n' + l_n y'|/l_n (y' = (y_1, y_2)) is the rescaled distance to that axis, Theta_n := T_n omega^theta and Omega_n := Theta_n/R_n. With t = T_* + T_n sigma, the historical and Type I bounds and (A) at each t give on R^3: |V_n| <= 1, |grad V_n| <= C_I/|sigma|, |grad^2 V_n| <= T_n l_n C_* D(t)^2/M(t) <= C_* C_I^2 (l_n/l(t))/|sigma| <= A_0 C_* C_I^2/|sigma| (and <= C_H := C_* C_I^2 at sigma = -1), R_n |Z_n| = |Gamma|/(l_n M_n) <= G nu_n, and |grad V_n(y*_n, -1)| = T_n D(s_n) >= 1/C_2 at the bounded points y*_n := (x_n - a_n)/l_n. By [CIV, Lemma 2.1], |grad^j V_n|^2 = |grad^j B_n|^2 + |grad^j Z_n|^2, |Theta_n| <= sqrt2 |grad B_n| and |Omega_n| <= sqrt2 |grad^2 V_n|. Swirl gradient: with rho := nu_n^{1/3}, where R_n >= 2 rho one has |Z_n| <= G nu_n/rho on B(y, rho), and |phi'(0)| <= sup|phi|/rho + (rho/2) sup|phi''| gives |grad Z_n| <= 3(G nu_n/rho^2 + rho C_H/2); closer to the axis one moves by at most 2 rho; hence ||grad Z_n(., -1)||_infty <= 4(G + C_H) nu_n^{1/3} and |grad B_n(y*_n, -1)|^2 >= C_2^{-2} - 16 (G + C_H)^2 nu_n^{2/3}. Compactness: by the meridional equation, d_sigma curl B_n is bounded in L^infty_sigma W^{-2,p}(B) on balls B; with the Lipschitz bounds, interpolation and Arzela-Ascoli give Theta_n -> Theta locally uniformly on R^3 x (-infinity, -1] (and Omega_n -> Omega off the axis for a finite axis), and B_n -> B weak-star with div B = 0, |B| <= 1 and ||grad B(sigma)||_infty <= C_I/|sigma|. Transport: for a finite axis, d_sigma Omega_n + div(B_n Omega_n) = nu_n (Delta + (2/R_n) d_{R_n}) Omega_n + d_Z(|Z_n|^2/R_n^2); against test functions phi supported in {R_n >= delta} the viscous term is O(nu_n) and the source at most G^2 nu_n^2 delta^{-4} ||d_Z phi||_{L^1}, so Omega solves d_sigma Omega + div(B Omega) = 0 weakly off the axis. For a receding axis, d_sigma Theta_n + div(B_n Theta_n) = (B_n)_r Theta_n/R_n + R_n^{-1} d_Z |Z_n|^2 + nu_n (Delta - R_n^{-2}) Theta_n, whose right side tends to 0 since R_n -> infinity on compact sets, so the planar vorticity Theta is weakly transported. Backward decay: the characteristic X' = B(X, sigma), X(-1) = y_0, has |X(sigma)| <= |y_0| + |sigma + 1| and, for a finite axis, distance to the axis at least |y_0'| |sigma|^{-C_I} (as |B^R| <= C_I R/|sigma|). By [CIV, Lemma 2.3] Omega (respectively Theta) is constant along it, so |Omega(y_0, -1)| <= sqrt2 A_0 C_* C_I^2/|sigma| -> 0 (respectively |Theta(y_0, -1)| <= sqrt2 C_I/|sigma| -> 0) as sigma -> -infinity. Thus Theta(., -1) = 0. Since B_n(., -1) is bounded in C^{1,1}, it converges in C^1_loc to B* with div B* = 0, curl B* = 0 and |B*| <= 1; B* is bounded and harmonic, hence constant, contradicting liminf |grad B_n(y*_n, -1)| >= 1/C_2.
Case (P). Excursion lemma: for late t let tau be the largest minimum point of l on [t_0, t], m := l(tau), and tau' := min{s >= t : l(s) = m}; if tau = t then tau' = t, and otherwise l(t) > m and tau' exists by continuity, since l(s) -> 0. Both are running-minimum times, so (T_* - tau)/(T_* - tau') = f(tau')/f(tau) <= F/c_L^2, and M(t) <= M(tau') = m/(T_* - tau') gives f(t) <= f(tau')(T_* - t)/(T_* - tau') <= F^2/c_L^2. Hence ||u(t)||_infty <= (F/c_L)(T_* - t)^{-1/2} on some [t_1, T_*). [CSTY2, Theorem 1.1] applies on (t_1, T_*) (u(t_1) in H^1, contained in H^{1/2}; Gamma(t_1) bounded; pressure in L^{5/3} since u is in L^{10/3}) and bounds u on B_R x [t_1, T_*) for every R. For |x_0| large, u in L^{10/3} and p in L^{5/3} make rho_0^{-2} int_Q (|u|^3 + |p|^{3/2}) small on the parabolic cylinders Q of radius rho_0 (rho_0^2 < (T_* - t_1)/2) centred at (x_0, s), and the epsilon-regularity criterion [SS, Lemma 1.2], in the form quoted in [CIV, proof of Lemma 2.2], gives |u| <= c/rho_0 there. So u stays bounded up to T_*, contradicting Leray's bound.

The spatial Lipschitz bounds and the W^{-2,p} time bound give local uniform compactness of Theta_n by interpolation (as in [CIV, proof of Lemma 3.2, Step 1]); the displayed transported equations and characteristic bounds handle the finite-axis and planar cases; and the L^{10/3} velocity and L^{5/3} pressure tails make the epsilon-regularity quantities at a fixed radius uniformly small outside a large ball. The rescaled vorticity equations follow by the change of variables and are not written out.

Status: proof with key estimates (routine steps indicated, not written out in full); source: own derivation (an adaptation of [CIV, Theorem 1.2]); verification: adversarial audit rounds 3, 5, 21, 22; checked by a script (numerical sanity check of the excursion lemma only).

Proposition 10 (exact 5D identities and the Hardy threshold). With dmu = r^3 dr dz, b the meridional velocity and phi the 5D stream potential: div_5 b = -2 phi_z, div_5(phi b) = 0, and for p >= 2 (for positive classical times, with the integrability needed for the integrations by parts; at an initial endpoint impose the corresponding integrability or use cutoff inequalities)
  (1/p) d/dt int |q|^p dmu = -(p - 1) int |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dmu + 2(1 - 1/p) int |q|^p phi_z dmu;
  (1/2) d/dt int eta^2 dx = -int |grad eta|^2 dx - 2 pi int eta(0, z, t)^2 dz + int eta d_z(q^2) dx.
If c > 0, -r u^r <= c on a time interval and 2 <= p <= 2/c, then int |q|^p dmu is nonincreasing there. At the critical exponent p = 5/2 this needs c <= 4/5, while every swirl record has -r_n u^r >= 1, so this absorption criterion cannot exclude Case 1. Also ||q(t)||_{L^4(dx)}^4 <= 4 E_0 H(t)^2.

Proof. With b = -r phi_z e_r + (2 phi + r phi_r) e_z, div_5 b = d_r u^r + 3 u^r/r + d_z u^z = div_3 b + 2 u^r/r = 2 u^r/r = -2 phi_z, and div_5(phi b) = phi div_5 b + b.grad phi = -2 phi phi_z - r phi_z phi_r + (2 phi + r phi_r) phi_z = 0. Here dmu = r^3 dr dz is the R^5 measure of axisymmetric functions up to the factor 2 pi^2, and q, eta, phi are smooth functions of (r^2, z), hence smooth on R^5. The swirl and vorticity equations read q_t + b.grad q = Delta_5 q + 2 q phi_z and eta_t + b.grad eta = Delta_5 eta + d_z(q^2). Multiply the first by |q|^{p-2} q and integrate in dmu: the transport term is -(1/p) int |q|^p div_5 b dmu = (2/p) int |q|^p phi_z dmu, and the diffusion term gives -(p - 1) int |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dmu; this is the q^p identity, with coefficient 2 - 2/p. At positive classical times the integrals are finite: |q| <= |grad u| gives int |q|^p dmu = (2 pi)^{-1} int |u^theta|^2 |q|^{p-2} dx <= E_0 D(t)^{p-2}/(2 pi), and int |grad q|^2 r^3 dr dz <= C ||grad u||_2^2 since r q_r = d_r u^theta - q and r q_z = d_z u^theta; smoothness and decay of the classical solution justify the integrations by parts (with cutoffs) and the axis trace. Multiply the eta equation by eta and integrate in dx = 2 pi r dr dz: div_3 b = 0 removes the transport term, Delta_5 = Delta_3 + (2/r) d_r, and 2 eta eta_r/r = div_3(eta^2 r^{-1} e_r), whose integral over {r > eps} is -2 pi int eta(eps, z)^2 dz -> -2 pi int eta(0, z)^2 dz. Hardy threshold: if -r u^r <= c then phi_z = -u^r/r <= c/r^2. The Hardy inequality on R^4, int f^2 |x'|^{-2} dx' <= ((4 - 2)/2)^{-2} int |grad' f|^2 dx' = int |grad' f|^2 dx', applied to f = |q|^{p/2} on each slice z = const, gives int |q|^p r^{-2} dmu <= (p^2/4) int |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dmu, hence 2(1 - 1/p) int |q|^p phi_z dmu <= (p - 1)(c p/2) int |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dmu, which the dissipation absorbs when c p <= 2. The exponent 5/2 is critical (int |q|^{5/2} dmu is invariant under the NS scaling, q having dimension length^{-2}), and p = 5/2 <= 2/c means c <= 4/5, while Theorem 1 gives -r_n u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1 at every record. L^4 bound: on the half-line {(r, 0, z): r > 0}, q = -d_{x_2} u_1 and q_r = -d_{x_1} d_{x_2} u_1, so |q_r| <= H(t). For a.e. z, I := int_0^infinity q^4 r dr = -2 int_0^infinity q^3 q_r r^2 dr <= 2 H(t) I^{1/2} (int_0^infinity q^2 r^3 dr)^{1/2}, so I <= 4 H(t)^2 int_0^infinity (u^theta)^2 r dr, and integration in 2 pi dz gives ||q(t)||_{L^4(dx)}^4 <= 4 H(t)^2 ||u^theta(t)||_2^2 <= 4 E_0 H(t)^2 (the boundary terms vanish by smoothness at the axis and decay).

Status: proved here; source: own derivation (the L^4 bound: supplied in adversarial audit round 4 and checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 4, 21, 22; checked by a script

Proposition 11 (the Type II regime is an obstruction for the method of Theorem 9). Let g = 1/D. Say that the backward window W(c, A, s) holds if g(t) >= c(g(s) + s - t) on [s - A g(s), s]; in gradient time this is exactly the historical ratio bound D(s + sigma/D(s))/D(s) <= [c(1 + |sigma|)]^{-1} on [-A, 0]. (i) Type I bounds imply W(c, A, s) for all A and late s, with c = 1/(C_I max(1, C_2)). (ii) For every C_2 > 0 there are continuous nonincreasing histories g -> 0 with |g'| <= C_2 (so D = 1/g is monotone, satisfies D' <= C_2 D^2 and the Riccati lower bound, and is Type II) for which W(c, A, s) fails at every late s once c(1 + A) > 2, while the rescaled histories g(s + sigma g(s))/g(s) stay in [1, 2] on [-A, 0]. This is a scalar construction; it does not show that the Navier-Stokes equations, (A) or the swirl records fail to imply windows. (iii) The class of normalized inviscid limit bounds of Proposition 8(c) contains a nonconstant steady Euler flow: V = e^{-|y'|^2} e_3 has sup|grad V| = sqrt(2/e), sup|grad^2 V| = 2 sqrt2 and shape constant sqrt2 e; after the change of variables y -> V(b y) with b = sqrt(e/2) and a translation of a gradient maximum point to the origin it satisfies all of those bounds, provided C_* >= sqrt2 e. It says nothing about C_* < sqrt2 e, has infinite energy and is not a Navier-Stokes blow-up limit. (iv) For every C_* > 1/2 the same class contains a nonconstant smooth steady columnar jet V = f(R) e_3 whose axis is at finite distance from the origin, and for C_* >= 1/2 a C^{1,1} steady planar shear (the class admits C^{1,1} fields, Proposition 8(c)); for C_* < 1/2 it contains no columnar jet f(R) e_3 and no planar shear (g(y_3), 0, 0). (v) For every C_* >= sqrt7/8 (= 0.3307...) the class contains a nonconstant steady planar radial vortex, C^{1,1} at C_* = sqrt7/8 and smooth for C_* > sqrt7/8; it contains no planar field when C_* < c_2 := 1/B_2 = 0.3234..., where B_2 := 2 + (4 7^{-1/4} - sqrt2)^2, and no swirl-free axisymmetric field when C_* < c_3 := 1/(B_2 + t_0^2) = 0.3158..., where t_0 = 0.2723... is the unique positive root of 144 t^4 - 112 2^{1/4} t^3 + (32 B_2 - 40) t^2 - 3 (B_2 - 4)^2; so it is empty for C_* < c_3. A planar member at whose gradient maximum the gradient matrix has two equal singular values (for instance a radial vortex whose gradient is maximal at its centre) requires C_* >= sqrt7/8. Without any equation, every bounded C^{1,1} field on R^3 satisfies sup|grad V|^2 <= B_3 sup|V| sup|grad^2 V| with B_3 := B_2 + (6 10^{-1/4} - 4 7^{-1/4})^2 = 3.9289..., so (A) by itself forces C_* >= 1/B_3 = 0.2545... (this improves the constant 1/6 of Theorem 13(e), which is used elsewhere as stated). Whether the class contains a nonconstant field for c_3 <= C_* < sqrt7/8 is not decided. The examples of (iii)-(v) have infinite energy and are not known to be Navier-Stokes blow-up limits. (vi) (Energy at the gradient scale.) At a gradient record s put epsilon(s) := M(s)^5/D(s)^3. Then epsilon(s) <= 24 E_0/pi, the rescaled fields of Proposition 8(c) satisfy ||V(tau)||_{L^2(R^3)}^2 <= E_0/epsilon(s) for -s D(s) <= tau <= 0, and int_{|x - x(s)| < M/(2 C_* D)} |u(s)|^2 dx >= c(C_*) epsilon(s) with c(C_*) = pi (2/15 - 1/28) (2 C_*)^{-5}. Consequently, if an inviscid gradient-scale limit V is taken along records with liminf epsilon(s_n) > 0, then V has finite energy at every time and is axisymmetric about a line at finite distance (it is not planar), and a fixed amount of physical energy concentrates in balls of radius M/(2 C_* D) -> 0 along these records; planar limits and limits with infinite energy, such as the examples of (iii)-(v), can only arise as limits along records with epsilon(s_n) -> 0 (apply the preceding statement to a subsequence on which epsilon is bounded below). (vii) (Energy atoms.) As t -> T_*, |u(t)|^2 dx converges weakly-* to a finite measure mu with mu(R^3) = lim_{t -> T_*} ||u(t)||_2^2. If epsilon(s_n) >= delta > 0 along gradient records s_n -> T_*, then mu has an atom of mass at least c(C_*) delta at a point x_* of the axis, u is unbounded in every neighbourhood of (x_*, T_*), and the weak L^2 limit u_* of u(t) satisfies lim_{t -> T_*} ||u(t)||_2^2 - ||u_*||_2^2 >= c(C_*) delta, so energy equality fails at T_*. Conversely, mu has no atom, and therefore epsilon(s_n) -> 0 along every sequence of gradient records s_n -> T_*, if int^{T_*} ||u(t)||_infty^{5/3} dt < infinity or ||u(t)||_infty = o((T_* - t)^{-3/5}); neither condition follows from the hypotheses, and by Theorem 7 epsilon(s_n) >= delta forces M(s_n) >= (delta C_2^{-3})^{1/5} (T_* - s_n)^{-3/5}. (Atom radius.) If mu has an atom of mass at least m > 0, then rho_m(t) := inf{rho > 0 : sup_y int_{B_rho(y)} |u(t)|^2 dx >= m/2} is positive and measurable, tends to 0 as t -> T_*, and satisfies int_{t_m}^{T_*} rho_m(t)^{-2} dt <= C_S E_0/m for some t_m < T_* and an absolute constant C_S; so for every C the set of t > t_m with rho_m(t) <= C (T_* - t)^{1/2} has logarithmic measure int dt/(T_* - t) at most C^2 C_S E_0/m. In the situation above, with m = c(C_*) delta, rho_m(s_n) <= M(s_n)/(2 C_* D(s_n)). If moreover a limit V as in (vi) (with liminf epsilon(s_n) > 0) satisfies ||grad V(-sigma)||_infty <= C (1 + sigma)^{-a} for all sigma >= 0 and some a > 5/13, then V is constant, which is excluded; Proposition 12 needs the exponent 1/2. (viii) (Rotational packing; separation of the two record families.) At every late gradient record s, (1 + a(s)) epsilon(s) <= C(C_*) E_0 and r(x(s)) + M(s)/D(s) = (1 + a(s)) epsilon(s) kappa(s)^2 <= C(C_*) E_0 kappa(s)^2, with C(C_*) = (1 + C_*)/(C_* c(C_*)); so in branch (II) of Theorem 13 the gradient maximizers converge to the axis in physical variables, although a(s) may be unbounded when epsilon(s) -> 0. In Case 1 let s_n be as in Theorem 13(d), x_n := x(s_n), a_n := a(s_n), epsilon_n := epsilon(s_n) and Lambda_n := ((1 + a_n) epsilon_n r_n^3)^{1/4}. Along every sequence of swirl records in branch (II) of Theorem 13(d), max{(t_n - s_n)/T_n, |x_n - z_n e_3|/Lambda_n} -> infinity, and M(s_n)/D(s_n) = o(Lambda_n). If moreover epsilon_n >= epsilon_0 > 0 and (t_n - s_n)/T_n stays bounded, then |z(x_n) - z_n|/Lambda_n -> infinity, where z(x_n) is the axial coordinate of x_n and Lambda_n >= (epsilon_0 r_n^3)^{1/4}. This is a necessary condition, not an exclusion: neither the historical ordering of the records nor energy and Theorem 7 bound (t_n - s_n)/T_n from above (on the plateau D = D(t_n) of [s_n, t_n] the swirl equation and |u^r/r| <= D(t_n) give only log(S_n/S(s_n)) <= D(t_n)(t_n - s_n), which is a lower bound on t_n - s_n), and an exclusion of branch (II) along this route would need an upper bound on (t_n - s_n)/T_n and a bound |x_n - z_n e_3| = O(Lambda_n) for infinitely many n.

Proof. Fix a late time t_0 after which (A) and the Riccati bound of Theorem 7 hold, so g = 1/D is continuous and nonincreasing, g -> 0 and 0 <= -g' <= C_2 a.e.; windows are considered for c in (0, 1], A >= 1 and s - A g(s) >= t_0. With g_s(sigma) := g(s + sigma g(s))/g(s), W(c, A, s) says g_s(sigma) >= c(1 + |sigma|) on [-A, 0], and D(s + sigma/D(s))/D(s) = 1/g_s(sigma), which is the stated equivalence. (i) The Riccati bound gives T_* - s >= g(s)/C_2, so for t <= s, g(t) >= (T_* - t)/C_I = ((T_* - s) + (s - t))/C_I >= (g(s)/C_2 + s - t)/C_I >= (g(s) + s - t)/(C_I max(1, C_2)); the two-sided bound 1/(C_2 (T_* - t)) <= D <= C_I/(T_* - t) gives C_I C_2 >= 1, so c <= 1. (ii) Let a_1 = 0, let g = 2^{-k} on the flat [a_k, b_k], b_k - a_k = k 2^{-k}, and let g decrease linearly with slope -C_2 from 2^{-k} to 2^{-k-1} on the drop [b_k, a_{k+1}], a_{k+1} - b_k = 2^{-k-1}/C_2 (k >= 1). Then T_* = sum_k (k 2^{-k} + 2^{-k-1}/C_2) < infinity, |g'| <= C_2, g(t) <= C_2 (T_* - t) (the Riccati lower bound for D), and (T_* - a_k)/g(a_k) >= (b_k - a_k) 2^k = k, so the history is Type II. Let k_A := ceil(A) + 1 and s >= a_{k_A}. Then s lies in [a_{k+1}, a_{k+2}) for some k >= A, and 2^{-k-2} <= g(s) <= 2^{-k-1}. Since s - a_k >= k 2^{-k} >= A g(s), the point t := s - A g(s) lies in [a_k, s], where g <= 2^{-k}. If s lies on the flat [a_{k+1}, b_{k+1}], then g(s) = 2^{-k-1} and g(t) <= 2 g(s). If s lies on the drop [b_{k+1}, a_{k+2}), then s - a_{k+1} >= (k + 1) 2^{-k-1} >= A g(s), so t lies in [a_{k+1}, s], where g <= 2^{-k-1} <= 2 g(s). In both cases g(t) <= 2 g(s) < c(1 + A) g(s) = c(g(s) + s - t) when c(1 + A) > 2, so the window fails at its left endpoint; the same estimate on [s - A g(s), s] and monotonicity give 1 <= g_s <= 2 on [-A, 0]. (iii) V = e^{-R^2} e_3 (R = |y'|) is divergence free with (V.grad)V = V^3 d_3 V = 0, so it is a steady Euler flow with constant pressure, axisymmetric and swirl free, with omega^theta = 2R e^{-R^2} and potential vorticity 2 e^{-R^2}. |grad V| = 2R e^{-R^2} has maximum D_0 = sqrt(2/e) at R = 2^{-1/2}; |grad^2 V|^2 = e^{-2R^2}(16 R^4 - 16 R^2 + 8) has derivative -32 (x - 1)^2 e^{-2x} <= 0 in x = R^2, so sup|grad^2 V| = 2 sqrt2 (at R = 0), and the shape constant is 1 . 2 sqrt2/(2/e) = sqrt2 e. The field y -> V(b y), b = 1/D_0 = sqrt(e/2), is again a steady Euler flow, with sup|grad| = b D_0 = 1 and sup|grad^2| = b^2 . 2 sqrt2 = sqrt2 e; after a translation moving a maximum point of |grad V| to the origin (the axis is then at distance e^{-1/2}) it satisfies |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0)| and |grad^2 V| <= C_* whenever C_* >= sqrt2 e, and it is eternal.
(iv) Let g be the even C^{1,1} function with g(x) = 1/2 - x^2/2 on [0, 1], g(x) = -1/2 + (x - 2)^2/2 on [1, 2] and g(x) = -1/2 on [2, infinity). Then sup|g| = 1/2, sup|g'| = 1 = |g'(1)|, |g''| <= 1 and g' is 1-Lipschitz. For 0 < epsilon < 1 let g_epsilon = g * rho_epsilon with an even mollifier rho_epsilon supported in [-epsilon, epsilon]; g_epsilon is smooth, constant for |x| >= 2 + epsilon, and sup|g_epsilon| <= 1/2, sup|g_epsilon''| <= 1, sup|g_epsilon'| >= |g_epsilon'(1)| >= 1 - epsilon. For a > 4 put f(R) = g_epsilon(R - a). Since f is constant for R <= a - 3, V = f(R) e_3 is smooth, bounded and swirl free, div V = 0 and (V.grad)V = f d_3(f) e_3 = 0, so V is a steady Euler flow with constant pressure. Its gradient f'(R) e_3 (tensor) e_R has Frobenius norm |f'(R)|, and |grad^2 V|^2 = f''(R)^2 + (f'(R)/R)^2 <= 1 + (a - 3)^{-2} on the support of f'. Hence Q(V) := sup|V| sup|grad^2 V|/sup|grad V|^2 <= (1/2)(1 + (a - 3)^{-2})^{1/2} (1 - epsilon)^{-2}, which is at most C_* for small epsilon and large a when C_* > 1/2. With m = sup|V|, d = sup|grad V| and x_0 a maximum point of |grad V|, the field y -> m^{-1} V(x_0 + (m/d) y) is a steady (so eternal) Euler flow with |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0)| and |grad^2 V| <= Q(V). Conversely, for a columnar jet the even function s -> f(|s|) on a line through the axis has second derivative f''(|s|), and |f''| <= |grad^2 V|; so the one-dimensional Landau inequality sup|f'|^2 <= 2 sup|f| sup|f''| gives Q >= 1/2, and the same argument applies to planar shears. Emptiness for small C_* is proved in (v). The endpoint C_* = 1/2 is attained in the C^{1,1} class (example of the round-32 referee): with g as above, V(y) = (2 g(1 + y_3/2), 0, 0) is a steady planar shear with |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0)| and |grad^2 V| <= 1/2 almost everywhere.
(v) For a planar field V = v(rho) e_theta in the (y_1, y_3)-plane (polar coordinates rho, theta there; V independent of y_2 and without y_2-component) put w = v/rho. Then div V = 0 and (V.grad)V = -(v^2/rho) e_rho = -grad P with P(rho) = int_0^rho v(s)^2 s^{-1} ds, so V is a steady planar Euler flow, and its vorticity v' + w is radial, hence transported. By rotation equivariance it suffices to compute at points of the positive y_1-axis, where a direct Cartesian computation gives the Frobenius norms |grad V|^2 = v'^2 + w^2 and |grad^2 V|^2 = v''^2 + 3 w'^2. Take v(rho) = rho - rho^2/2 for rho <= 1 and v = 1/2 for rho >= 1. Then w = 1 - rho/2 on [0, 1], V = (1 - rho/2)(-y_3, y_1) there, and v, v' are continuous at rho = 1, so grad V is Lipschitz. On (0, 1), |grad V|^2 = (1 - rho)^2 + (1 - rho/2)^2 = (5 rho^2 - 12 rho + 8)/4 decreases from 2 to 1/4 and |grad^2 V|^2 = 1 + 3/4 = 7/4; on (1, infinity), |grad V|^2 = 1/(4 rho^2) <= 1/4 and |grad^2 V|^2 = 3/(4 rho^4) <= 3/4. So sup|V| = 1/2, sup|grad V| = sqrt2 is attained in the (y_1, y_3)-plane only at the origin (in R^3 along the y_2-axis), ess sup|grad^2 V| = sqrt7/2, and Q(V) = sqrt7/8; the field y -> 2 V(y/(2 sqrt2)) satisfies |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0)| and |grad^2 V| <= sqrt7/8. For C_* > sqrt7/8 convolve V with a smooth radial mollifier of the plane supported in the disc of radius epsilon < 1: the result V_epsilon is rotation equivariant and divergence free, hence (being smooth at the origin) again a radial vortex b(rho) e_theta and a steady Euler flow; |V_epsilon| <= 1/2 and |grad^2 V_epsilon| <= sqrt7/2 by convexity of the norms, |grad V_epsilon(0)| -> sqrt2 as epsilon -> 0, and |grad V_epsilon(x)| <= 1/(2(|x| - epsilon)) for |x| >= 1 + epsilon, so |grad V_epsilon| attains its maximum and Q(V_epsilon) <= (sqrt7/4)/|grad V_epsilon(0)|^2 < C_* for small epsilon; normalize at a maximum point as in (iv). Lower bounds. Let U be a bounded C^{1,1} map from R^n to R^n, M = sup|U|, H = ess sup|grad^2 U|, and let sigma_1 >= sigma_2 >= ... be the singular values of grad U(x_0). Then sigma_1 + ... + sigma_k <= 2k (3k + 1)^{-1/4} (M H)^{1/2} for k <= n. By mollification we may assume U smooth. Let P be the k x n matrix whose rows are the first k left singular vectors, Q_k the n x k matrix whose columns are the first k right singular vectors, and F(x) = P U(x_0 + Q_k x) on R^k, so grad F(0) = diag(sigma_1, ..., sigma_k), |F| <= M and |grad^2 F| <= H. The average W(x) = int_{O(k)} R^T F(R x) dR (Haar probability measure) satisfies W(R x) = R W(x) for R in O(k), |W| <= M, |grad^2 W| <= H and grad W(0) = a I with a = (sigma_1 + ... + sigma_k)/k; invariance under the reflections fixing x gives W(x) = g(|x|) x. With r = |x|, b = g' and h = (r g)'' = 2b + r b', a Cartesian computation gives |grad^2 W|^2 = h^2 + 3(k - 1) b^2, so by Cauchy-Schwarz 2 r b' + (3k + 1) b = 2h + 3(k - 1) b >= -(3k + 1)^{1/2} H. Multiplying by r^{(3k - 1)/2} and integrating from 0 gives b >= -(3k + 1)^{-1/2} H, hence M >= r g(r) >= a r - (3k + 1)^{-1/2} H r^2, and r = (3k + 1)^{1/2} a/(2H) gives M H >= (3k + 1)^{1/2} a^2/4, which is the claim. For n = 2 the cases k = 1, 2 give sigma_1 <= 2^{1/2} (M H)^{1/2} and sigma_1 + sigma_2 <= 4 7^{-1/4} (M H)^{1/2}; the decreasing vector (2^{1/2}, 4 7^{-1/4} - 2^{1/2}) (M H)^{1/2} weakly majorizes (sigma_1, sigma_2), and t -> t^2 is convex and increasing on [0, infinity), so sigma_1^2 + sigma_2^2 <= B_2 M H. A planar field has a 2 x 2 active gradient block, so Q >= c_2; for n = 3, k = 1, 2, 3 give B_3 in the same way (the vector (2^{1/2}, 4 7^{-1/4} - 2^{1/2}, 6 10^{-1/4} - 4 7^{-1/4}) is decreasing). If sigma_1 = sigma_2 at a gradient maximum of a planar field, then sup|grad V|^2 = (sigma_1 + sigma_2)^2/2 <= (8/sqrt7) M H, so Q >= sqrt7/8. For a swirl-free axisymmetric divergence-free C^{1,1} field U normalize M = H = 1 and mollify radially in R^3 (this preserves the symmetries and the bounds and gives locally uniform convergence of gradients). At a point (r, 0, z) with r > 0 write U = u e_r + w e_z, a = u_r, b = u_z, c = u/r, d = w_r, e = w_z = -a - c; after a change of sign of U, c >= 0. Then |grad U|^2 = S + c^2 with S = R_1 + T_1, R_1 = a^2 + b^2, T_1 = d^2 + e^2. (1) The planar bound for (y_1, y_3) -> (U_1, U_3)(y_1, 0, y_3) gives S <= B_2. (2) The pointwise Landau inequality |f'(0)|^2 <= 2 L (1 - |f(0)|) for |f| <= 1, |f''| <= L (maximize f(0) + f'(0) t - L t^2/2 <= 1) gives: T_1 <= 2 (for U_3 in the direction of grad U_3); e^2 <= 2^{1/2} (for U_3 on axial lines, since d_3 div U = 0 gives |grad^2 U|^2 >= U_{3,33}^2 + 2 U_{1,13}^2 + 2 U_{2,23}^2 >= 2 U_{3,33}^2, U_{i,jl} = d_j d_l U_i), hence a <= 2^{1/4} - c; and R_1/2 + c r <= 1 (for U_1 in the direction (a, 0, b), along which its derivative is R_1^{1/2}).
