(3) In cylindrical form |grad^2 U|^2 = u_rr^2 + 2 u_rz^2 + u_zz^2 + w_rr^2 + 2 w_rz^2 + w_zz^2 + (3 (a - c)^2 + 2 b^2 + d^2)/r^2, and differentiation of div U = 0 gives u_rr + w_rz = -(a - c)/r and u_rz + w_zz = -b/r; since min{p^2 + 2q^2 : p + q = X} = 2X^2/3, 1 >= G^2/r^2 with G^2 = (11/3)(a - c)^2 + (8/3) b^2 + d^2 = (8/3) R_1 + T_1 - (28/3) a c + (8/3) c^2 >= (8/3) S - 10/3 + 12 c^2 - (28/3) 2^{1/4} c, by T_1 <= 2 and a <= 2^{1/4} - c. With R_1 >= S - 2 and c G <= c r, (2) gives c G <= 2 - S/2, which is positive as S <= B_2 < 4; so f(S) := c^2 [(8/3) S - 10/3 + 12 c^2 - (28/3) 2^{1/4} c] - (2 - S/2)^2 <= 0, and f is increasing in S < 4. If S + c^2 > K := B_2 + t_0^2, then c > t_0 by (1), and 12 f(K - c^2) < 0; but 12 f(K - c^2) = P_K(c) := 109 c^4 - 112 2^{1/4} c^3 + (38 K - 64) c^2 - 3 (K - 4)^2, P_K(t_0) = 0 is the defining equation of t_0, and P_K'(c) = c (436 c^2 - 336 2^{1/4} c + 76 K - 128) > 0 for c > 0 (negative discriminant, as K > 3), a contradiction. So |grad U|^2 <= K off the axis, by continuity everywhere, and Q >= c_3. (The equation of t_0 has exactly one positive root: its value at 0 is negative and its derivative t (576 t^2 - 336 2^{1/4} t + 64 B_2 - 80) is positive for t > 0, as B_2 > 3.) (A numerical search over piecewise polynomial radial profiles found no radial vortex with Q < sqrt7/8; this is not part of the proof.)
(vi) M(s) is attained or approached at some time t <= s; at a point where |u(t)| >= (1 - delta) M(s), |u(t)| >= (1/2 - delta) M(s) on the ball of radius M(s)/(2 D(s)), since D(t) <= D(s), so E_0 >= ||u(t)||_2^2 >= (1/2 - delta)^2 M^2 (4 pi/3) (M/(2 D))^3 and, letting delta -> 0, epsilon(s) <= 24 E_0/pi. The rescaling has ||V(tau)||_2^2 = (D^3/M^5) ||u(s + tau/D)||_2^2 <= E_0/epsilon(s). For the lower bound put rho = 1/(2 C_*) and A = grad V(0, 0), |A| = 1; Taylor's formula gives V(y) - V(-y) = 2 A y + e(y) with |e(y)| <= C_* |y|^2, so int_{B_rho} |V|^2 >= (1/4) int_{B_rho} |V(y) - V(-y)|^2 >= (1/4) int_{B_rho} (2 |A y|^2 - C_*^2 |y|^4) dy, and int_{B_rho} |A y|^2 = (1/3) int_{B_rho} |y|^2 = 4 pi rho^5/15, int_{B_rho} |y|^4 = 4 pi rho^7/7, which gives int_{B_rho} |V|^2 >= pi rho^5 (2/15 - 1/28); undoing the rescaling (lengths M/D, velocity M) gives the physical bound. If liminf epsilon(s_n) > 0, the local uniform convergence of Proposition 8(c) and Fatou's lemma give ||V(tau)||_2^2 <= limsup E_0/epsilon(s_n) < infinity. A planar limit V(y_1, y_3, tau) that is not identically 0 at tau = 0 has int_{B_L} |V(0)|^2 >= (L - 2) int_{|(y_1, y_3)| < 1} |V(0)|^2 for L > 2, which tends to infinity; since |grad V(0, 0)| = 1, V(0) is not identically 0, so the limit is not planar. Finally M/D = 1/(kappa M) -> 0 since D/M >= (pi/(24 E_0))^{1/3} M^{2/3} -> infinity.
(vii) For phi in C_c^infinity, d/dt int phi |u|^2 = int |u|^2 Delta phi + int (|u|^2 + 2p) u.grad phi - 2 int phi |grad u|^2 on [0, T_*), and every term on the right is integrable on (0, T_*), since ||u||_3^3 <= C E_0^{3/4} ||grad u||_2^{3/2}, ||p||_{3/2} <= C ||u||_3^2 and int_0^{T_*} ||grad u||_2^2 dt <= E_0/2. Hence int phi |u(t)|^2 converges as t -> T_*, and as ||u(t)||_2^2 <= E_0, |u(t)|^2 dx converges weakly-* to a finite measure mu. With psi_R = 1 - phi(x/R) (phi = 1 on B_1, supported in B_2) the same identity gives sup_{t < T_*} int_{|x| > 2R} |u(t)|^2 <= int_{|x| > R} |u_0|^2 + C E_0 T_*/R^2 + (C/R) int_0^{T_*} int (|u|^3 + |p| |u|), which tends to 0 as R -> infinity; so the measures are tight and mu(R^3) = lim ||u(t)||_2^2. Similarly int u(t).phi converges for vector test functions, so u(t) converges weakly in L^2 to some u_*, and int phi |u_*|^2 <= int phi d mu for phi >= 0. Suppose epsilon(s_n) >= delta. By (vi) the balls B_n of radius lambda_n/(2 C_*) about x_n = x(s_n), lambda_n = M(s_n)/D(s_n) -> 0, satisfy int_{B_n} |u(s_n)|^2 >= m := c(C_*) delta; tightness keeps x_n bounded, and along a subsequence x_n -> x_*. For r > 0 and phi in C_c(B_{2r}(x_*)) with 0 <= phi <= 1 and phi = 1 on B_r(x_*), int phi d mu = lim int phi |u(s_n)|^2 >= m; so mu({x_*}) >= m. As u is axisymmetric, mu is invariant under rotations about the axis, and an atom off the axis would have infinitely many disjoint rotated copies of mass at least m; so x_* lies on the axis. If |u| <= K on B_r(x_*) x (T_* - r^2, T_*), then mu(B_rho(x_*)) <= K^2 |B_rho| for rho < r, which contradicts mu({x_*}) >= m. Finally mu - |u_*|^2 dx is a nonnegative measure and |u_*|^2 dx has no atoms, so mu(R^3) - ||u_*||_2^2 >= mu({x_*}) >= m. Conversely, let m_u(t) = ||u(t)||_infty. Integrating the identity over [T_* - d, T_*] for phi about a point a as above, with int |u|^3 <= E_0 m_u and ||p||_2 <= C ||u||_4^2 <= C m_u E_0^{1/2}, gives mu(B_r(a)) <= C r^3 m_u(T_* - d)^2 + C E_0 d/r^2 + (C E_0/r) int_{T_* - d}^{T_*} m_u dt. If int^{T_*} m_u^{5/3} < infinity, there are d_k -> 0 with d_k m_u(T_* - d_k)^{5/3} -> 0; with r_k = d_k^{2/5} the three terms are at most C (d_k m_u(T_* - d_k)^{5/3})^{6/5}, C E_0 d_k^{1/5} and, by Hoelder, C E_0 (int_{T_* - d_k}^{T_*} m_u^{5/3})^{3/5}, all tending to 0, so mu has no atoms; the same choice works if m_u(t) = o((T_* - t)^{-3/5}). (The integrability condition is also the case d = 0 at the corner L^{5/3} L^infinity of [LS, Theorem 5.4].) By Theorem 7, D(s) >= 1/(C_2 (T_* - s)), so epsilon(s_n) >= delta gives M(s_n)^5 >= delta D(s_n)^3 >= delta C_2^{-3} (T_* - s_n)^{-3}. For the last statement, the ball argument of (vi) applied to V(tau) gives sup|V(tau)|^5 <= (24/pi) ||V(tau)||_2^2 ||grad V(tau)||_infty^3 <= C (1 + |tau|)^{-3a}, since ||V(tau)||_2^2 <= limsup E_0/epsilon(s_n) < infinity; in the proof of Proposition 12 the axial extent of E_n is then at most Z_0 + int_{-sigma_n}^{0} sup|V(tau)| d tau, and |E_n| <= C g_n^2 (Z_0 + int_0^{sigma_n} (1 + tau)^{-3a/5} d tau) -> 0 when a > 5/13, since g_n^2 <= C (1 + sigma_n)^{-2a} and 1 - 3a/5 - 2a < 0. For the atom radius, let mu({x}) >= m. For r > 0 and phi in C_c(B_r(x)) with 0 <= phi <= 1 and phi = 1 on B_{r/2}(x), int phi |u(t)|^2 -> int phi d mu >= m, so rho_m(t) <= r for t near T_*; rho_m(t) > 0 since int_{B_rho(y)} |u(t)|^2 <= (4 pi/3) rho^3 ||u(t)||_infty^2; and rho_m is measurable, since t -> sup_y int_{B_rho(y)} |u(t)|^2 is continuous on [0, T_*) and {rho_m < c} is the union over rational rho < c of the closed sets on which this function is at least m/2. For rho > rho_m(t) some ball B_rho(y) carries energy at least m/2, and by Hoelder and Sobolev int_{B_rho(y)} |u(t)|^2 <= |B_rho|^{2/3} ||u(t)||_6^2 <= (C_S/2) rho^2 ||grad u(t)||_2^2, C_S := 2 (4 pi/3)^{2/3} K_S^2 with K_S the absolute Sobolev constant of ||f||_6 <= K_S ||grad f||_2 (applied to f = |u(t)|, not the constant K of (K)); letting rho decrease to rho_m(t), ||grad u(t)||_2^2 >= m/(C_S rho_m(t)^2), and int_0^{T_*} ||grad u||_2^2 dt <= E_0/2 gives the integral bound. On the set where rho_m <= C (T_* - t)^{1/2}, rho_m^{-2} >= C^{-2} (T_* - t)^{-1}. Finally, the ball B_n of radius lambda_n/(2 C_*) carries energy at least m at time s_n, so rho_m(s_n) <= lambda_n/(2 C_*).
(viii) Write l := M/D, R := r(x(s)) = a l and rho := 1/(2 C_*) at the record s. By (vi) the ball of radius rho l around x(s) carries energy at least c(C_*) epsilon at time s. If R <= 2 rho l take N := 1; otherwise take N := floor(2R/(rho l)) >= 2; then the rotations of this ball about the axis by the angles 2 pi j/N, 0 <= j < N, are pairwise disjoint, since neighbouring centres are at distance 2R sin(pi/N) >= 4R/N >= 2 rho l. In the first case a <= 1/C_* and N = 1 >= C_* (1 + a)/(1 + C_*); in the second case a > 1/C_*, so N >= 2 C_* a >= 2 C_* (1 + a)/(1 + C_*). Hence E_0 >= N c(C_*) epsilon >= C_* c(C_*) (1 + a) epsilon/(1 + C_*). The identity epsilon kappa^2 = (M^5/D^3)(D^2/M^4) = M/D gives r(x(s)) + M/D = (1 + a) l = (1 + a) epsilon kappa^2, and in branch (II) kappa -> 0. For the separation, write M_n, D_n, l_n, F_n := (1 + a_n) epsilon_n at s_n and e_n := S_n/M_n. Theorem 13(a) at t_n, with M(t_n) >= M_n and D(t_n) = D_n, gives e_n^3 <= (C_* G^2/2) kappa_n^2 -> 0, and l_n^4/Lambda_n^4 <= l_n^4/(epsilon_n r_n^3) = 1/(M_n D_n r_n^3) = e_n^3/(gamma_n^3 kappa_n) <= C_* G^2 kappa_n/(2 gamma_n^3) -> 0, since gamma_n is bounded below (Theorem 1). Suppose that along a subsequence (t_n - s_n)/T_n <= A and |x_n - z_n e_3| <= B Lambda_n. If Lambda_n/r_n <= L_0 along a further subsequence, then for large n the ball of radius rho l_n around x_n lies in B_{L r_n}(z_n e_3), L := (B + 1) L_0, at a time (s_n - t_n)/T_n in [-A, 0] of the swirl window; by (K), |u(s_n)| <= (K + 1) S_n there, so c(C_*) M_n^2 l_n^3 <= (K + 1)^2 S_n^2 (4 pi/3) rho^3 l_n^3, and e_n is bounded below, a contradiction. Otherwise Lambda_n/r_n -> infinity, that is, F_n/r_n -> infinity. For fixed L, (K) bounds the energy of u(s_n) in B_{L r_n}(z_n e_3) by (K + 1)^2 S_n^2 (4 pi/3) L^3 r_n^3 = C_L gamma_n^2 r_n = o(F_n). The N rotated balls carry energy at least c' F_n, c' := C_* c(C_*)/(1 + C_*), and lie in B_{(B + 1) Lambda_n}(z_n e_3), since rotations about the axis fix z_n e_3 and rho l_n <= Lambda_n for large n. So the energy in {L r_n < |x - z_n e_3| < (B + 1) Lambda_n} is at least c' F_n/2 for large n, and with x = z_n e_3 + r_n y and tau_n := (s_n - t_n)/T_n in [-A, 0], int_{|y| > L} |U_n(y, tau_n)|^2 |y|^{-4} dy = (r_n/S_n^2) int_{|x - z_n e_3| > L r_n} |u(s_n)|^2 |x - z_n e_3|^{-4} dx >= (r_n/S_n^2) (c' F_n/2) ((B + 1) Lambda_n)^{-4} = c'/(2 (B + 1)^4 gamma_n^2) >= c'/(2 (B + 1)^4 G^2) for every L, contradicting (TAIL). If epsilon_n >= epsilon_0, the first part gives a_n <= C(C_*) E_0/epsilon_0, so r(x_n) = a_n l_n = o(Lambda_n), and |x_n - z_n e_3| <= |z(x_n) - z_n| + r(x_n) gives the last statement. (The kinematic constructions in research answer res33, which are not Navier-Stokes solutions, remain unverified here and are not used.)

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: (i)-(iii) adversarial audit rounds 4, 5, 6, 21, 22, checked by a script; (iv) checked by the author, by a script and in adversarial audit round 32 (whose referee supplied the endpoint shear); (v) and (vi) added after round 32, checked by the author and, for the vortex formulas and profile, by a script, and audited in round 33 (which corrected the sign of the pressure identity in (v) and the time range in (vi)); the lower bounds c_2, c_3, B_3 and the equal-singular-value bound in (v) are from research answer res32 and (vii) is from research answer res31 (except the remark on Theorem 7, which is the author's, and the last statement of (vii), which follows res31 but avoids its use of energy conservation for the limit); they were checked by the author by hand and, for the Hessian formulas and the algebra of the constants, by a script, and they were audited in round 34. Part (viii) is from research answer res33 (the bound on the number of rotated balls and the explicit constant are the author's), checked by the author by hand, and audited in round 35. The atom-radius statement in (vii) is the author's, checked by hand and audited in round 38.

Proposition 12 (volume rigidity for the inviscid branch). Let V be an ancient swirl-free Euler velocity on R^3 x (-infinity, 0], axisymmetric about a fixed line, bounded by K, continuous, divergence free and spatially C^1 with locally integrable ||grad V(t)||_infty, whose potential vorticity Omega = omega^theta/R is continuous off the axis and constant along characteristics there. If (1 + sigma_n) ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty^2 -> 0 for some sigma_n -> infinity, then V is spatially constant. The same holds for planar limits as soon as ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty -> 0 along a sequence. Consequently, if the windows W(c, A_j, s_j) hold for one fixed c > 0 along gradient records s_j with A_j -> infinity, every inviscid gradient-scale limit at s_j is constant, which contradicts |grad V(0, 0)| = 1.

Proof. Suppose Omega(x_0, t_0) != 0 with R(x_0) > 0. By continuity there are delta > 0, Z_0 and a compact set E off the axis with |E| > 0, |Omega(., t_0)| >= delta on E and |z| <= Z_0 on E. Characteristics exist and are unique since V is Lipschitz in space with locally integrable constant L(t) = ||grad V(t)||_infty; the horizontal velocity vanishes on the axis, so |V^R| <= L(t) R, and |dR/dt| <= L(t) R keeps characteristics that start off the axis away from it on finite time intervals. Let E_n be the image of E under the flow from t_0 back to time -sigma_n. Since Omega is constant along characteristics, |Omega| >= delta on E_n, while with g_n := ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty, delta R <= |omega^theta| = |curl V| <= sqrt2 g_n there; so E_n lies in {R <= sqrt2 g_n/delta}, and also in {|z| <= Z_0 + K sigma_n} since |V| <= K. The flow preserves three-dimensional Lebesgue measure (the Jacobian determinant solves d_t det = (div V) det = 0). Hence
  0 < |E| = |E_n| <= pi (sqrt2 g_n/delta)^2 . 2 (Z_0 + K sigma_n) -> 0,
a contradiction. So Omega = 0 off the axis at every time, curl V = omega^theta e_theta = 0 (on the axis by continuity of grad V), and V(t) is bounded, divergence free and curl free on R^3, hence harmonic and constant. Planar case V = (v_1, 0, v_3)(y_1, y_3, t), with the planar vorticity omega = d_3 v_1 - d_1 v_3 continuous and constant along characteristics: |omega(x_0, t_0)| = |omega(X(-sigma_n), -sigma_n)| <= sqrt2 ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty -> 0 along the characteristic X through (x_0, t_0), so omega = 0 and V is spatially constant as before. Consequence: if W(c, A_j, s_j) holds with A_j -> infinity, the gradient-record rescalings V_j of Proposition 8(c) satisfy |grad V_j(sigma)| <= D(s_j + sigma/D(s_j))/D(s_j) <= [c(1 + |sigma|)]^{-1} on [-A_j, 0], and the locally uniform C^1 convergence of Proposition 8(c) passes this bound to every inviscid limit V on (-infinity, 0]. That limit is bounded by 1, continuous, divergence free and spatially C^1 with ||grad V||_infty <= 1; its potential vorticity (finite axis) or planar vorticity (receding axis) is continuous (grad V is Lipschitz) and weakly transported (Proposition 8(c)), hence constant along characteristics by [CIV, Lemma 2.3]. For sigma_n = n, (1 + n) ||grad V(-n)||_infty^2 <= 1/(c^2 (1 + n)) -> 0, so V is spatially constant, contradicting |grad V(0, 0)| = 1.

Status: proved here (the final consequence uses the inviscid compactness and transport of Proposition 8(c)); source: own derivation (the volume argument: supplied in adversarial audit round 4 and checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 4, 5, 21, 22.

Theorem 13 (scale dichotomy at gradient records). Let S(t) = sup_{tau <= t} ||u^theta(tau)||_infty, and for a gradient record s (a time with ||grad u(s)||_infty = D(s), attained at x(s)) let kappa(s) = D(s)/M(s)^2 and a(s) = r(x(s)) D(s)/M(s).
 (a) For every t: S(t)^2 <= G D(t) and 2 S(t)^3 <= G^2 H(t). Under (A), 2 M(t) S(t)^3 <= C_* G^2 D(t)^2, hence S(s)/M(s) <= (C_* G^2/2)^{1/3} kappa(s)^{2/3} at gradient records.
 (b) Assume (A). For every c > 0 there are C_c < infinity and t_c < T_* such that every gradient record s >= t_c with kappa(s) >= c satisfies a(s) <= C_c and S(s) >= M(s)/C_c. At such records M/C_c <= S <= M, S^2/G <= D <= C_P C_c^2 S^2 and 2 S^3/G^2 <= H <= C_* C_P^2 C_c^3 S^3 (all at time s).
 (c) Along any sequence of gradient records tending to T_*, kappa -> 0 if and only if S/M -> 0.
 (d) In Case 1 let s_n be the last time <= t_n with ||grad u(s_n)||_infty = D(t_n), and kappa_n = kappa(s_n). Every subsequence of the records has a further subsequence in one of two branches.
  (I) kappa_n >= c > 0: d_n <= C_P C_c^2 G, m_n <= C_* d_n^2/2 and 2 <= h_n <= C_* d_n^2. The normalized fields U_n and their first and second spatial derivatives are bounded on their whole past (L^infinity in time, W^{2,infinity} in space), so the bounds in (K) and (TAIL) follow from (A), the record estimates and the branch condition, with K = sup_n m_n. The gradient-record limits are, after viscosity normalization, members of LL with Q <= C_*, axis within distance C_c of the origin, and gradient maximum attained at the terminal time; there are J < infinity and delta > 0 (not explicit) with sup_{B_J x [-J, 0]} |V^theta| >= delta for all of them. The bound Q <= C_* is for the gradient-record limits, not for the swirl-record limits.
  (II) kappa_n -> 0: m_n >= (2/(C_* G^2))^{1/3} kappa_n^{-2/3} -> infinity, d_n^2 >= 2 m_n/C_* -> infinity and S(s_n)/M(s_n) <= (C_* G^2/2)^{1/3} kappa_n^{2/3} -> 0. The gradient-record limits are the swirl-free Euler fields of Proposition 8(c) (weak limits with C^1 spatial convergence; smooth convergence is not established), and in the swirl-record variables the spacetime points (x(s_n), s_n) of the gradient maximizers leave every compact subset of R^3 x (-infinity, 0].
 (e) If s <= t are late, s is a gradient record and D(s) = D(t), then M(s) <= M(t) <= 18 C_* M(s) (Landau inequality at s, (A) at t). Hence kappa(t) := D(t)/M(t)^2 is comparable to kappa(s), the branch of (d) is visible at t_n (kappa(t_n) = d_n/(gamma_n m_n^2)), and (c) extends to arbitrary late sequences of times.

Proof. (a) Since the e_theta-derivative of u is r^{-1}(u^r e_theta - u^theta e_r), |u^theta|/r <= |grad u|, so (u^theta)^2 = |Gamma| |u^theta|/r <= G D(t) at all times tau <= t. Let S(t) = |u^theta(x_0, tau)|, tau <= t, x_0 = (r_0, 0, z_0) (the supremum is attained); then r_0 S(t) <= G. The function f(x_1) = u_2(x_1, 0, z_0, tau) is odd with |f| <= S(t) = |f(r_0)|, so f'(r_0) = 0, and Taylor's formula from r_0 to 0 gives S(t) = (1/2) r_0^2 |f''(xi)| <= (1/2) r_0^2 H(t) <= G^2 H(t)/(2 S(t)^2). Multiplying by M and using (A) gives 2 M S^3 <= C_* G^2 D^2; at a gradient record D = kappa M^2.
(b) Take t_c >= max(t_A, t_P). Otherwise there are gradient records s_j -> T_* with kappa(s_j) >= c and a(s_j) > j or S(s_j) < M(s_j)/j. The rescalings V_j(y, sigma) = M^{-1} u(x(s_j) + l y, s_j + sigma/D), l = M/D (at s_j), satisfy |V_j| <= 1, |grad V_j| <= 1 = |grad V_j(0, 0)|, |grad^2 V_j| <= C_* on their whole past, with viscosity in [c, C_P] and backward depth s_j D(s_j) -> infinity; by the compactness of Proposition 8(c) a subsequence converges in C^infty_loc to a bounded mild ancient V with |grad V(0, 0)| = 1 and viscosity kappa_infinity in [c, C_P]. If a(s_j) is unbounded, V is planar, hence constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.1]. Otherwise S(s_j)/M(s_j) -> 0, so |V_j^theta| -> 0 on the whole past and V is swirl-free, axisymmetric about a line at bounded distance; W(y, tau) = kappa_infinity^{-1} V(y, tau/kappa_infinity) has viscosity 1, so W = (0, 0, b_3(tau)) by [KNSS, Theorem 5.2] and W is constant by [KNSS, Remark 6.1]. Both contradict |grad V(0, 0)| = 1. At such records the lower bounds are (a), D = kappa M^2 <= C_P M^2 <= C_P C_c^2 S^2, and (A) gives H <= C_* D^2/M <= C_* C_P^2 M^3 <= C_* C_P^2 C_c^3 S^3. (C_c may depend on the solution.)
(c) If kappa -> 0, S/M -> 0 by (a); otherwise a subsequence has kappa >= c > 0 and S/M >= 1/C_c by (b).
(d) D(s_n) = D(t_n) -> infinity, so s_n -> T_*. (I) By (b), M(s_n) <= C_c S(s_n) <= C_c S_n, so D(t_n) <= C_P C_c^2 S_n^2 and d_n = r_n D(t_n)/S_n <= C_P C_c^2 gamma_n <= C_P C_c^2 G. (A) at t_n is m_n h_n <= C_* d_n^2; with h_n >= 2 and m_n >= 1 (Proposition 8(a)) this gives the bounds on m_n, h_n. On R^3 x [-t_n/T_n, 0], |U_n| <= m_n, |grad U_n| <= d_n, |grad^2 U_n| <= h_n; so (K) holds with K = sup_n m_n, and int_{|y| > L} |U_n|^2 |y|^{-4} dy <= 4 pi K^2/L gives (TAIL). The gradient-record limits V have |V| <= 1, |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0, 0)|, |grad^2 V| <= C_*, hence Q <= C_*; Q is unchanged by the normalization W = kappa_infinity^{-1} V(y, tau/kappa_infinity), after which the viscosity is 1 and |Gamma_W| <= G (|Gamma_V| = kappa |Gamma|). The axis lies within C_c by (b), and a swirl-free limit is excluded as in (b), so W is in LL. If no (J, delta) existed, limits V_j with sup_{B_j x [-j, 0]} |V_j^theta| < 1/j would, by the same compactness, have a swirl-free limit with |grad V(0, 0)| = 1, viscosity >= c and bounded axis, excluded as in (b). (II) Since kappa(t_n) <= kappa_n, (a) at t_n gives 2 M(t_n) S_n^3 <= C_* G^2 kappa_n^2 M(t_n)^4, i.e. m_n^3 >= 2/(C_* G^2 kappa_n^2); then d_n^2 >= m_n h_n/C_* >= 2 m_n/C_*, and the swirl ratio is (a) at s_n. The limits are those of Proposition 8(c). In swirl-record variables the gradient maximizer at s_n is ((x(s_n) - z_n e_3)/r_n, (s_n - t_n)/T_n), where |grad U_n| = d_n -> infinity, while along a further subsequence U_n converges in C^{2,1}_loc (Theorem 2), so |grad U_n| is bounded on compact sets.
(e) For bounded C^2 g on R, ||g'||^2 <= 2 ||g|| ||g''|| (Landau); applied to each u_i in each coordinate direction and summed over the 9 entries, ||grad u||^2 <= 18 ||u|| ||grad^2 u||. So D(s)^2 <= 18 M(s) H(s) <= 18 M(s) H(t), and (A) at t gives M(t) H(t) <= C_* D(s)^2 <= 18 C_* M(s) H(t); hence M(t) <= 18 C_* M(s) and kappa(s)/(18 C_*)^2 <= kappa(t) <= kappa(s). With D(t_n) = d_n S_n/r_n and M(t_n) = m_n S_n, kappa(t_n) = d_n/(gamma_n m_n^2). For late times t_j let s_j be the last time <= t_j with ||grad u(s_j)||_infty = D(t_j): then kappa(t_j) -> 0 iff kappa(s_j) -> 0, (a) at t_j gives S/M <= (C_* G^2/2)^{1/3} kappa^{2/3}, and kappa(t_j) >= c gives S(t_j)/M(t_j) >= S(s_j)/(18 C_* M(s_j)) >= 1/(18 C_* C_c).
The constant 18 can be lowered to 6. For fixed x and j with d_j u(x) != 0 let a be the unit vector parallel to d_j u(x) and g(s) = a.u(x + s e_j). Then |g| <= ||u||_infty, |g''| = |a.d_jj u| <= ||grad^2 u||_infty and g'(0) = |d_j u(x)|, so |d_j u(x)|^2 <= 2 ||u||_infty ||grad^2 u||_infty; summing over the three columns, ||grad u||_infty^2 <= 6 ||u||_infty ||grad^2 u||_infty. Hence D^2 <= 6 M H, (A) gives M H <= C_* D^2 <= 6 C_* M H, so C_* >= 1/6, and the Landau constant 18 may be replaced by 6 wherever it is used (in (e), Proposition 15, Proposition 17, where 51840 becomes 5760, and sec. 3.7). Only the finiteness of these constants is used below, and the statements keep the constant 18.

Status: proved here; source: own derivation (part (e), including the constant 6: research answers checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 5, 6, 21, 22; the constant-6 remark was checked by hand by the author and in adversarial audit round 31.

Proposition 14 (critical potential-vorticity mass). Let N(t) = ||eta(t)||_{L^1(dx)} = 2 pi int int |omega^theta| dr dz (scale invariant; the value infinity is allowed). There are e_0, c_1 > 0 depending only on C_* such that every late gradient record s with S(s)/M(s) <= e_0 satisfies kappa(s) N(s) >= c_1, i.e. N(s) >= c_1 M(s)^2/D(s); one can take c_1 = pi^2 c_0^3/(128 C_*^2), where c_0(C_*) > 0 is a lower bound for the vorticity of the rescaled field. In branch (II) of Theorem 13(d), N(s_n) >= c_1/kappa_n -> infinity. On the other hand N is not monotone when swirl is present (pure swirl data have eta = 0 initially and d_t eta = d_z(q^2)), and Kato's inequality gives N(b) + 4 pi int_a^b int |eta(0, z, t)| dz dt <= N(a) + int_a^b ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)} dt with ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)} <= 2 ||grad u||_2 ||grad^2 u||_2, which with the enstrophy inequality and finite dissipation yields, when N(a) < infinity for some a (finite energy does not imply this), N(t) = o(D(t)^{1/2}) as t -> T_*. Since M^5 <= C E_0 D^3, the requirement c_1 M^2/D is at most C D^{1/5}, so this bound gives no contradiction. A strict deficit liminf kappa_n N(s_n) < c_1 along one branch-(II) sequence would suffice for a contradiction (in branch (II) the proof even gives liminf kappa_n N(s_n) >= 8 c_1). (L^infty budget.) If moreover epsilon_n := M(s_n)^5/D(s_n)^3 >= epsilon_0 > 0 along a branch-(II) sequence (the alternative of Proposition 11(vi), (vii) in which the energy at the gradient scale is bounded below), then for large n, D(s_n)^2/M(s_n) <= C_4 (||eta(t_A)||_infty + int_{t_A}^{s_n} (D(t)^2/M(t))^{5/3} dt), with C_4 depending only on C_*, G, E_0 and epsilon_0. If D and M are comparable on [t_A, T_*) to (T_* - t)^{-beta} and (T_* - t)^{-3 beta/5} (the relation that epsilon_0 <= M^5/D^3 <= C E_0 forces along such records), this requires beta >= 15/14. This is a necessary condition only. (Endpoint.) The point at which the proof finds |eta| >= c_0 D(s_n)^2/(4 M(s_n)(a(s_n) + R_1)) lies at distance at least c_0 M(s_n)/(4 sqrt2 C_* D(s_n)) from the axis. Everywhere |d_z(q^2)| <= 2 sqrt6 S^{3/2} H^{1/2} r^{-2}, so for t >= t_A, c > 0 and r >= c M(t)/D(t), |d_z(q^2)(r, z, t)| <= 2 sqrt6 c^{-2} C_*^{1/2} (C_* G^2/2)^{1/2} kappa(t) D(t) X(t), X := D^2/M: outside the region r << M/D the source has the rate kappa D X instead of kappa^{1/3} D X.

Proof. A late gradient record is one with s >= t_A. Let M, D, l = M/D be taken at s, e := S(s)/M(s), and U(X) := M^{-1} u(l X, s) in axis-centered coordinates. Then ||U||_infty <= 1, ||grad U||_infty = 1, ||grad^2 U||_infty <= M H/D^2 <= C_* by (A), and div U = 0.
Step 1 (the constant c_0). Since Delta U = -curl omega_U (omega_U = curl U), for 0 < eps < h, grad e^{eps Delta} U = grad e^{h Delta} U + int_eps^h grad e^{t Delta} curl omega_U dt. The heat-kernel bounds ||grad e^{h Delta} f|| <= C h^{-1/2} ||f||, ||grad f - grad e^{eps Delta} f|| <= C eps^{1/2} ||grad^2 f|| and ||grad^2 G_t||_{L^1} <= C/t (G_t the heat kernel, C absolute) give 1 <= C h^{-1/2} + C C_* eps^{1/2} + C ||omega_U||_infty log(h/eps). Fix h with C h^{-1/2} <= 1/4, then eps < h with C C_* eps^{1/2} <= 1/4, and put c_0(C_*) := [2 C log(h/eps)]^{-1}; then ||omega_U||_infty >= c_0.
Step 2. Write U = B + W, with B the meridional part and W = (1/2)(U - P U(P .)) the swirl part, where P = diag(1, -1, 1). Then ||W|| <= e, ||grad^2 W|| <= C_*, and the Landau inequality of Theorem 13(e) gives ||curl W|| <= sqrt2 ||grad W|| <= sqrt2 (18 e C_*)^{1/2} = 6 (e C_*)^{1/2}. For e <= e_0 := c_0^2/(144 C_*) this is at most c_0/2, so m := ||omega^theta_U||_infty = ||curl B||_infty >= c_0/2.
Step 3. The odd extension f of omega^theta_U(R, Z) to the meridional plane (the Cartesian component of curl U normal to {x_2 = 0}) is Lipschitz with constant L_f <= sqrt2 C_*. Around a point with |f| >= a we have |f| >= a/2 on the disc of radius a/(2 L_f), which contributes at least pi a^3/(8 L_f^2) >= pi a^3/(16 C_*^2) to int_{R^2} |f| dR dZ. Since omega^theta_U(R, Z) = D^{-1} omega^theta(l R, l Z, s), int_{R^2} |f| dR dZ = kappa(s) N(s)/pi. Letting a -> m gives kappa N >= pi^2 m^3/(16 C_*^2) >= pi^2 c_0^3/(128 C_*^2) = c_1. In branch (II), e_n -> 0 (Theorem 13(d)), so m >= c_0 - 6 (e_n C_*)^{1/2} -> c_0 and liminf kappa_n N(s_n) >= pi^2 c_0^3/(16 C_*^2) = 8 c_1.
Balance. Pure-swirl data u_0 = q_0(r, z)(-x_2, x_1, 0), smooth and compactly supported with d_z(q_0^2) not identically 0, have eta_0 = 0 and d_t eta = d_z(q_0^2) at t = 0. The function eta satisfies (d_t + b.grad) eta = Delta_5 eta + d_z(q^2), where Delta_5 = Delta + (2/r) d_r and div b = 0. Kato's inequality and int (2/r) d_r |eta| dx = -4 pi int |eta(0, z)| dz give the stated balance. On {x_2 = 0, x_1 > 0}, q = -d_2 u_1 and d_z q = -d_3 d_2 u_1, so by rotation |q| <= |grad u| and |d_z q| <= |grad^2 u|, whence ||d_z(q^2)||_1 <= 2 ||grad u||_2 ||grad^2 u||_2. Put Y(t) = ||grad u(t)||_2^2 and I(a, b) = int_a^b Y dt <= E_0/2. With the enstrophy inequality d/dt Y + 2 ||grad^2 u||_2^2 <= 2 D Y, Cauchy-Schwarz gives N(b) <= N(a) + 2 (I (Y(a)/2 + D(b) I))^{1/2} <= N(a) + (2 I Y(a))^{1/2} + 2 D(b)^{1/2} I. If N(a_0) < infinity, then N is finite on [a_0, T_*) by the same bound. Dividing by D(b)^{1/2}, letting b -> T_* and then a -> T_* (I(a, T_*) -> 0) gives N(t) = o(D(t)^{1/2}). Finite energy does not give N(a) < infinity: take disjoint axial translates of one smooth compactly supported meridional divergence-free field with amplitudes 1/j; the energy and all H^k norms are finite, but N = infinity. Finally, at a time tau <= t with ||u(tau)||_infty = M(t), |u(tau)| >= M/2 on a ball of radius M/(2D), and ||u(tau)||_2^2 <= E_0. So M^5 <= C E_0 D^3, and c_1 M^2/D <= C D^{1/5}.
L^infty budget. (i) For t < T_*, eta(t) is smooth as a function of (|x'|, z) on R^5, x' in R^4, and tends to 0 at infinity: |eta| <= ||omega(t)||_infty/r, and, omega^theta being odd in r, |eta(r, z)| <= sup_{rho <= r} |d_r omega^theta(rho, z)|, while omega(t) and grad omega(t) are continuous and tend to 0 at infinity for the smooth decaying solution. Since Delta_5 is the Laplacian of R^5, the maximum principle, applied to eta and -eta in (d_t + b.grad - Delta_5) eta = d_z(q^2), gives ||eta(t)||_infty <= ||eta(t')||_infty + int_{t'}^t ||d_z(q^2)(tau)||_infty d tau for t' <= t. (ii) Let W = (1/2)(u - P u(P .)), P = diag(1, -1, 1), be the swirl part of u(t). Then |W| = |u^theta| <= S(t) and |grad^2 W(x)| <= (|grad^2 u(x)| + |grad^2 u(P x)|)/2 <= H(t), so ||grad W||_infty^2 <= 6 S(t) H(t) by the vector Landau inequality of Theorem 13(e). On {x_2 = 0, x_1 > 0}, q = -d_2 W_1 and d_z q = -d_3 d_2 W_1, so ||q||_infty <= (6 S H)^{1/2} and ||d_z q||_infty <= H, whence ||d_z(q^2)(t)||_infty <= 2 sqrt6 S(t)^{1/2} H(t)^{3/2}. For t >= t_A, (A) gives H <= C_* D^2/M and Theorem 13(a) gives S^{1/2} <= (C_* G^2/2)^{1/6} (D^2/M)^{1/6}, so ||d_z(q^2)(t)||_infty <= C_5 (D(t)^2/M(t))^{5/3} with C_5 = 2 sqrt6 C_*^{3/2} (C_* G^2/2)^{1/6}. (iii) At s = s_n let U, l, e be as in the proof above and X_0 the rescaled maximizer, |grad U(X_0)| = 1 and R(X_0) = a(s). Split the kernel of Step 1 at |Y - X_0| = R_1: since ||omega_U||_infty <= sqrt2 and int_{|Y| > R_1} |grad^2 G_t| dY <= C t^{-1} e^{-R_1^2/(8t)}, the outer part is at most sqrt2 C log(h/eps) e^{-R_1^2/(8h)}, which is at most 1/4 for R_1 = R_1(C_*) large, while the inner part is at most C log(h/eps) max_{B_{R_1}(X_0)} |omega_U| by ||grad^2 G_t||_{L^1} <= C/t; with the choices of h and eps in Step 1 this gives max_{B_{R_1}(X_0)} |omega_U| >= c_0/2. By Step 2, max_{B_{R_1}(X_0)} |omega^theta_U| >= c_0/2 - 6 (e C_*)^{1/2} >= c_0/4 once e <= c_0^2/(576 C_*), which holds for large n since e_n -> 0. At a point X_1 of the closed ball where |omega^theta_U(X_1)| >= c_0/4 one has 0 < R(X_1) <= a(s) + R_1, so at the physical point l X_1, |eta| = D |omega^theta_U(X_1)|/(l R(X_1)) >= c_0 D^2/(4 M (a(s) + R_1)). By Proposition 11(viii), a(s_n) <= C(C_*) E_0/epsilon_0 when epsilon_n >= epsilon_0. Combining (i) with t' = t_A, (ii) and (iii) gives the inequality with C_4 = 4 (R_1 + C(C_*) E_0/epsilon_0) max(1, C_5)/c_0. (iv) Under the stated comparability the left side is comparable to (T_* - s_n)^{-7 beta/5} and the integrand to (T_* - t)^{-7 beta/3}; since beta >= 1 by Theorem 7, the right side is at most C (T_* - s_n)^{1 - 7 beta/3}, and the inequality for s_n -> T_* needs 7 beta/5 <= 7 beta/3 - 1, that is beta >= 15/14. (v) (Endpoint.) d_z(q^2) = 2 u^theta d_z u^theta/r^2, and on {x_2 = 0, x_1 > 0}, u^theta = W_2 and d_z u^theta = d_3 W_2, so |d_z u^theta| <= ||grad W||_infty <= (6 S H)^{1/2} by (ii); this gives |d_z(q^2)| <= 2 sqrt6 S^{3/2} H^{1/2} r^{-2}. For r >= c M/D, (A) gives H^{1/2} r^{-2} <= C_*^{1/2} c^{-2} X^{1/2} D^2/M^2, so the bound is at most 2 sqrt6 C_*^{1/2} c^{-2} (S/M)^{3/2} D X, and (S/M)^3 <= (C_* G^2/2) kappa^2 by Theorem 13(a). At the physical point l X_1 of (iii), |omega^theta| = D |omega^theta_U(X_1)| >= c_0 D/4, while omega^theta vanishes on the axis and |grad omega| <= sqrt2 |grad^2 u| <= sqrt2 H <= sqrt2 C_* D^2/M pointwise; so c_0 D/4 <= sqrt2 C_* (D^2/M) r(l X_1).

Status: proved here; source: research answer checked by the author (the energy bound M^5 <= C E_0 D^3, used in comparing the critical-mass lower bound with N = o(D^{1/2}) and in the power-law relation of the L^infty budget: own derivation); verification: adversarial audit rounds 6, 21, 22. The L^infty budget is the author's, checked by hand and audited in round 36. The endpoint remark (from research answer res34, checked by the author by hand) was audited in round 38.

Proposition 15 (eternal gradient-record limits). Assume (A). Taking first hitting times s_n of geometric levels of D with ratios lambda_n = 1 + 1/n, there are (existentially; they need not lie in a preassigned sequence or branch) gradient records s_n -> T_* and times b_n > s_n with D(b_n) = lambda_n D(s_n) and D(s_n)(b_n - s_n) -> infinity (by Theorem 9). On the whole interval up to b_n the rescalings at s_n satisfy |V_n| <= 18 C_* lambda_n^2, |grad V_n| <= lambda_n = lambda_n |grad V_n(0, 0)|, |grad^2 V_n| <= C_* lambda_n^2. Their limits are eternal (defined on R^3 x R): bounded mild axisymmetric NS solutions with nonzero swirl and finite axis if kappa_n -> kappa > 0, weak swirl-free (finite axis) or planar Euler fields if kappa_n -> 0, and in both cases |grad V| <= 1 = |grad V(0, 0)| on all of R^3 x R. In the scenario in which all late gradient records are viscous, the same selection at swirl levels gives eternal swirl-record limits with |U^theta| <= 1 = U^theta(e_1, 0) over all space and time. Neither statement excludes a branch: in branch (II) the steady jet of Proposition 11(iii) is an eternal model when C_* >= sqrt2 e, and an appropriate Liouville theorem for eternal viscous limits would exclude an eternal limit selected here if its viscosity is positive; this does not exclude an arbitrary prescribed viscous subsequence, and no such theorem is supplied by the sources we checked. On the whole time line the selected gradient-record limits satisfy |V| <= 18 C_*, |grad V| <= 1 and |grad^2 V| <= C_*, so their whole-time shape bound is Q <= 18 C_*^2; the bound Q <= C_* of Theorem 13(d) refers to the past half-line.

Key estimates. D is continuous and nondecreasing and tends to infinity [BKM]. Fix lambda > 1 and B > D(t_A), put L_k = B lambda^k, and let tau_k be the first time with D(tau_k) = L_k. Then ||grad u(tau_k)||_infty = L_k, so tau_k is a gradient record. With the normalized forward durations delta_k := L_k (tau_{k+1} - tau_k), summing T_* - tau_k = sum_{m >= k} (tau_{m+1} - tau_m) gives the exact identity
  (T_* - tau_k) L_k = sum_{m >= k} lambda^{k-m} delta_m.
If delta_m <= A on a tail, this is at most A lambda/(lambda - 1), and for tau_k <= t < tau_{k+1}, (T_* - t) D(t) <= lambda (T_* - tau_k) L_k stays bounded, contradicting Theorem 9. So delta_k is unbounded on every tail. For lambda = lambda_n choose k_n with tau_{k_n} later than the previous choice and than T_* - 1/n, and with delta_{k_n} > n; set s_n = tau_{k_n} and b_n = tau_{k_n + 1}. Here V_n(X, sigma) = M(s_n)^{-1} u(x_n + l_n X, s_n + sigma/D(s_n)), l_n = M(s_n)/D(s_n), and x_n maximizes |grad u(s_n)|.
Forward bounds. The Landau inequality of Theorem 13(e) at s_n gives D(s_n)^2 <= 18 M(s_n) H(s_n) <= 18 M(s_n) H(b_n). (A) at b_n gives M(b_n) H(b_n) <= C_* D(b_n)^2 = C_* lambda_n^2 D(s_n)^2. Hence M(b_n) <= 18 C_* lambda_n^2 M(s_n) and, since M(b_n) >= M(s_n), H(b_n) <= C_* lambda_n^2 D(s_n)^2/M(s_n). Up to the image of b_n, |V_n| <= M(b_n)/M(s_n), |grad V_n| <= D(b_n)/D(s_n) = lambda_n and |grad^2 V_n| <= H(b_n) M(s_n)/D(s_n)^2, which are the stated bounds. Moreover |Gamma_{V_n}| = kappa_n |Gamma| <= G kappa_n with kappa_n = kappa(s_n) <= C_P, and both depths s_n D(s_n) and D(s_n)(b_n - s_n) tend to infinity.
Limits. If kappa_n -> kappa > 0, apply the compactness of Proposition 8(c) on each [-j, j], restarting the mild formula at sigma = -j - 1, and take a diagonal subsequence. The axis bound is Theorem 13(b) with c = kappa/2, and nonzero swirl follows as in Theorem 13(b) from [KNSS, Theorem 5.2, Remark 6.1] applied to the restriction to sigma <= 0. If kappa_n -> 0, the argument of Proposition 8(c) applies on each [-j, j] with the small-swirl bound |V_n^theta|^2 <= |R V_n^theta| |V_n^theta/R| <= G kappa_n lambda_n -> 0, valid on the whole interval. The whole-time bounds |V| <= 18 C_*, |grad V| <= 1 and |grad^2 V| <= C_* give Q <= 18 C_*^2.
Swirl levels. Suppose all late gradient records have kappa >= c > 0. For late t let s be the last gradient record <= t with D(s) = D(t); then Theorem 13(e), (b) give M(t) <= 18 C_* M(s) <= 18 C_* C_c S(t) and D(t) <= C_P C_c^2 S(t)^2, so limsup (T_* - t) S(t)^2 = infinity by Theorem 9. Run the same selection with levels L_k of S, first hitting times t_k (swirl records) and delta_k = L_k^2 (t_{k+1} - t_k), using the identity (T_* - t_k) L_k^2 = sum_{j >= 0} lambda^{-2j} delta_{k+j}. The resulting swirl-record rescalings satisfy |U_n| <= 18 C_* C_c lambda_n and |U_n^theta| <= lambda_n up to the next level. Their limits satisfy |U^theta| <= 1 = U^theta(e_1, 0) on R^3 x R, -gamma U^R(e_1, 0) >= 1 and d_tau U^theta(e_1, 0) = 0 (gamma the limit of gamma_n); this does not imply stationarity.

The local convergence follows from Proposition 8(c) on each finite interval and a diagonal subsequence, and the displayed geometric-level identities give both selections; the routine compactness steps are indicated rather than written out in full.

Status: proof with key estimates (routine steps indicated, not written out in full); source: research answer checked by the author (swirl-level selection; the gradient-level version is the author's adaptation); verification: adversarial audit rounds 6, 21, 22.

Proposition 16 (critical weighted swirl energies). For p > 3/2 let Y_p(t) = int |q|^p r^{2p-3} dx = int |Gamma|^p r^{-3} dx (scale invariant; for p >= 2 finite at positive classical times, while finiteness at t = 0 requires an additional integrability assumption; for 3/2 < p < 2 it can be infinite for smooth finite-energy fields). Whenever Y_p is finite,
  d/dt Y_p + p(p-1) int r^{2p-3} |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dx + (2p-3)(5-2p) int r^{2p-5} |q|^p dx = -3 int (u^r/r) |q|^p r^{2p-3} dx,
with the same inflow coefficient -3 for every p. By a weighted Hardy inequality the left-hand dissipation is at least 3(2p-3)/p int r^{2p-5}|q|^p dx, so Y_p is nonincreasing on any interval on which -r u^r <= 2 - 3/p everywhere (for p > 3 this threshold exceeds the value 1 forced at the swirl records, so the record identity does not contradict it, but the global inflow bound was not derived), or, for the chosen finite p, on which ||psi^theta||_infty <= P_p := (2/p)((p-1)(2p-3)/3)^{1/2}, where psi^theta is the angular stream function (P_2 = 3^{-1/2} exactly, and P_p increases to (8/3)^{1/2} = 1.632993... as p -> infinity, so every admissible bound is below that limit; it uses the axial part of the dissipation). For p = 2 it gives N(b) <= N(a) + Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b) with the critical inflow integral I_in(a, b) = int_a^b int (u^r)^- q^2 dx dt. The Y_p family has one inflow term. Other functionals leave additional unsigned terms: the point-centered functional int Gamma^2 rho^{-3} dx with rho = |x - x_0| for a point x_0 on the axis (rho^{-3} is harmonic for the 5D Laplacian) and the self-similar Gaussian weight (which closes if -(x - x_0).b <= 2 everywhere) have an analogous inflow term toward the chosen point, and the coupled meridional energy Y_2 + (3/2) int |b|^2/r dx has cubic, pressure and axis-flux terms of indefinite sign after cancellation of the swirl transfer. The conditional absorption criteria stated here were not derived from the Case-1 assumptions, and their global weighted-integral justification is not fully supplied (the proposition is a proof sketch).

Key estimates. Write beta = 2p - 3, A_p = int r^beta |q|^{p-2} |grad q|^2 dx = A_{p,r} + A_{p,z} (the r- and z-derivative parts), B_p = int r^{beta-2} |q|^p dx and A_2 = int r |grad q|^2 dx.
Finiteness. Let p >= 2. For r >= 1, |Gamma|^p r^{-3} <= G^{p-2} (u^theta)^2. For r < 1, |q|^p r^beta <= D(t)^{p-2} q^2, and int q^2 dx <= ||grad u(t)||_2^2 since |q| <= |grad u|. For 3/2 < p < 2 these bounds fail: a smooth pure-swirl field supported in 1 < r < 2, made of disjoint axial translates of one bump with amplitudes j^{-alpha}, 1/2 < alpha <= 1/p, has finite energy, bounded circulation and finite Sobolev norms, but Y_p = infinity.
Identity. Multiply (d_t + b.grad) q = Delta_5 q - 2 (u^r/r) q by p |q|^{p-2} q r^beta and integrate; for p < 2, regularize |q|^p as (q^2 + eps^2)^{p/2} first. Transport gives beta int u^r |q|^p r^{beta-1} dx (div b = 0). Together with the zero-order term this gives (beta - 2p) int u^r |q|^p r^{beta-1} dx = -3 int (u^r/r) |q|^p r^beta dx. Write Delta_5 q = r^{-3} div_{(r,z)}(r^3 grad q); two integrations by parts give -p(p-1) A_p + beta(beta - 2) B_p for the diffusion, and the boundary terms at r = 0 carry the factor r^beta.
Hardy. The inequality int_0^infty r^{2p-2} |f'|^2 dr >= ((2p-3)/2)^2 int_0^infty r^{2p-4} f^2 dr, applied to f = |q|^{p/2} on each slice, gives A_{p,r} >= ((2p-3)/p)^2 B_p. Since p(p-1)((2p-3)/p)^2 + (2p-3)(5-2p) = 3(2p-3)/p, the dissipation is at least 3(2p-3)/p B_p. If -r u^r <= c, the right side is at most 3c B_p, which gives the threshold c <= 2 - 3/p. It exceeds the value 1 forced at the swirl records (Theorem 1) iff p > 3.
Stream function. Since u^r = -d_z psi^theta, int (u^r/r) |q|^p r^beta dx = p int psi^theta |q|^{p-2} q d_z q r^{beta-1} dx. Cauchy-Schwarz with the split (|q|^{(p-2)/2} |d_z q| r^{beta/2})(|q|^{p/2} r^{beta/2-1}) bounds the inflow term by 3 p P A_{p,z}^{1/2} B_p^{1/2} if ||psi^theta||_infty <= P. Hardy uses only A_{p,r}, so the good terms are at least p(p-1) A_{p,z} + (3(2p-3)/p) B_p. The form p(p-1) a^2 - 3pP ab + (3(2p-3)/p) b^2 is nonnegative for a, b >= 0 iff 9 p^2 P^2 <= 12 (p-1)(2p-3), i.e. P <= P_p. P_p increases in p because (p-1)(2p-3)/p^2 = 2 - 5/p + 3/p^2.
Case p = 2. Here ||d_z(q^2)||_1 <= int q^2/r dx + int r |d_z q|^2 dx <= A_2 + B_2, and integrating the identity gives int_a^b (2 A_2 + B_2) dt <= Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b). The Kato balance of Proposition 14 then gives the bound on N(b).
Other functionals. Let x_0 = (0, 0, z_0) lie on the axis and put rho = |x - x_0| = (r^2 + (z - z_0)^2)^{1/2}. Then Delta_5 rho^a = a(a+3) rho^{a-2}, and (x - x_0).b = r u^r + (z - z_0) u^z. Using (d_t + b.grad) Gamma^2 = L Gamma^2 - 2 |grad Gamma|^2, the duality int w L f dx = int f Delta_5 w dx (valid since Gamma^2 = O(r^4) at the axis) and Gamma(x_0) = 0, Phi = int Gamma^2 rho^{-3} dx (finite since Gamma^2 rho^{-3} <= (u^theta)^2/rho) satisfies
  d/dt Phi + 2 int |grad Gamma|^2 rho^{-3} dx = -3 int Gamma^2 rho^{-5} ((x - x_0).b) dx.
With w = (T_* - t)^{-3/2} exp(-rho^2/(4(T_* - t))), d_t w + Delta_5 w = -w/(T_* - t), and Psi = int Gamma^2 w dx satisfies
  d/dt Psi + Psi/(T_* - t) + 2 int |grad Gamma|^2 w dx = (2(T_* - t))^{-1} int Gamma^2 w (-(x - x_0).b) dx,
so Psi is nonincreasing if -(x - x_0).b <= 2. Coupled energy: put Z = int |b|^2/r dx, Q_b = int (|grad u^r|^2 + |grad u^z|^2)/r dx, K_r = int (u^r)^2 r^{-3} dx and J_z = 2 pi int int ((u^z(r, z))^2 - (u^z(0, z))^2) r^{-2} dr dz, and let Pi be the pressure. Then
  (Y_2 + (3/2) Z)' + 2 A_2 + B_2 + 3 Q_b + (3/2) K_r = -(3/2) int |b|^2 u^r r^{-2} dx - 3 int Pi u^r r^{-2} dx + (3/2) J_z.
The swirl transfer +2 int u^r q^2 dx in Z' (the radial momentum equation times 2 u^r/r) cancels the inflow term. J_z must not be split, since int (u^z)^2 r^{-3} dx diverges when u^z does not vanish on the axis.

The global identities require cutoff and regularization limits for the displayed weighted integrals. Those limits, including the coupled axis term (which must be kept unsplit) and the range 3/2 < p < 2, are not fully justified here, and the coupled identity is checked only as a symbolic density computation. This item is therefore incomplete as a whole and not checkable from the report alone.

Status: proof sketch with key estimates; source: own derivation (coupled meridional energy identity: research answer checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 7, 13, 21, 22; checked by scripts

Proposition 17 (the stream function is large at inviscid gradient records). At a gradient record s >= t_A with S(s)/M(s) <= e, epsilon := kappa(s) ||psi^theta(s)||_infty satisfies 1 <= (18 C_*)^{1/2}((10 epsilon)^{1/4} + e^{1/2}). In branch (II), ||psi^theta(s_n)||_infty >= 1/(51840 C_*^2 kappa_n) -> infinity, so the stream-function criterion of Proposition 16 fails there by an unbounded factor. At every swirl record ||psi^theta(t_n)||_infty >= 1/(2 gamma_n d_n).

Proof. Rescale at s as in Proposition 8(c), in axis-centered meridional coordinates, and let psi_V(X) = psi^theta(l X, s)/(M l), l = M/D. Since M l = 1/kappa, ||psi_V||_infty = epsilon. Also |grad V| <= 1, with equality at the image of x(s), and |grad^2 V| <= C_* by (A) at s >= t_A.
(i) For fixed R, g(Z) = psi_V(R, Z) has |g| <= epsilon and |g''| = |d_Z V^R| <= 1 (on {x_2 = 0}, d_Z V^R = d_3 V_1). The Landau inequality ||g'||^2 <= 2 ||g|| ||g''|| on R gives |V^R| = |g'| <= (2 epsilon)^{1/2}.
(ii) R psi_V(R, Z) = int_0^R rho V^Z(rho, Z) d rho and |d_R V^Z| <= 1. For R_0 >= 0 and h > 0: |int_{R_0}^{R_0+h} rho V^Z d rho| <= epsilon (2 R_0 + h), int_{R_0}^{R_0+h} rho d rho = h(2 R_0 + h)/2, and |int_{R_0}^{R_0+h} rho (V^Z(rho) - V^Z(R_0)) d rho| <= (R_0 + h) h^2/2. Hence |V^Z(R_0)| <= 2 epsilon/h + h, and h = (2 epsilon)^{1/2} gives |V^Z| <= 2 (2 epsilon)^{1/2}. So the meridional part B = (V + P V(P .))/2 (P the reflection x_2 -> -x_2) satisfies |B| <= (10 epsilon)^{1/2}.
(iii) Since |grad^2 B| <= C_*, the Landau inequality with constant 18 (Theorem 13(e)) gives ||grad B||_infty <= (18 C_* (10 epsilon)^{1/2})^{1/2}. The swirl part W = V - B has ||W|| <= e and ||grad^2 W|| <= C_*, so ||grad W|| <= (18 C_* e)^{1/2}. Since 1 = ||grad V|| <= ||grad B|| + ||grad W||, we get 1 <= (18 C_*)^{1/2}((10 epsilon)^{1/4} + e^{1/2}).
In branch (II), e_n = S(s_n)/M(s_n) -> 0 (Theorem 13(d)), so (18 C_* e_n)^{1/2} <= 1/2 for large n. Then (10 epsilon)^{1/4} >= (1/2)(18 C_*)^{-1/2}, i.e. epsilon >= 1/(160 * 324 C_*^2) = 1/(51840 C_*^2), and ||psi^theta(s_n)||_infty = epsilon/kappa_n.
At a swirl record, g(z) = psi^theta(r_n, z, t_n) has g'(z_n) = -u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1/r_n (Theorem 1) and |g''| = |d_z u^r| <= D(t_n). The Landau inequality gives ||psi^theta(t_n)||_infty >= 1/(2 r_n^2 D(t_n)) = 1/(2 gamma_n d_n).

Status: proved here; source: own derivation; verification: adversarial audit rounds 7, 21, 22.

Proposition 18 (necessary conditions from the potential-vorticity transport). (a) In Case 1, for every a < T_*, int_a^{T_*} int r |grad q|^2 dx dt = infinity (disjoint record cylinders each carry a fixed positive amount, by the C^{2,1} convergence of Theorem 2), hence I_in(a, T_*) = infinity; the inflow integral of Proposition 16 diverges for every p >= 2 (and for 3/2 < p < 2 when Y_p is finite at a). (b) In branch (II), if N(a) and Y_2(a) are finite, I_in(a, s_n) >= c_1/(3 kappa_n) - (N(a) + Y_2(a))/3. (c) In the gradient units at a branch-(II) record, the exact backward adjoint of the potential-vorticity equation, with absorbing axis condition, satisfies int Omega_n(0) psi_n = int Omega_n(-A_n) phi_n(-A_n) - int int q_V^2 d_Z phi_n, which forces int int sup_R |d_Z phi_n| dZ dsigma >= c kappa_n^{-4/3} for the adjoint solution (without assuming N finite at the initial time, by the L^2 contraction of the adjoint); no upper bound of this kind is known. (d) A frozen swirl field (not an NS solution) satisfying all the stated swirl bounds has source ||d_Z(q^2)||_{L^1} of order kappa, and a log-slow scalar history satisfies the listed scalar inequalities and instantaneous algebraic bounds with kappa N = 1 and no backward decay; so neither the listed swirl bounds nor scalar record selection on windows of normalized length O(1/kappa) gives the missing estimate. (e) Pointwise (u^r)^- q^2 <= (|u^theta|/2)|grad u|^2, so I_in(a, t) <= (1/2) int_a^t ||u^theta||_infty ||grad u||_2^2 ds = o(S(t)); hence, if N(a) < infinity, N(t) = o(S(t)) and in branch (II) kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity. The part of the evolution of J = -int u^r q^2 dx due to the swirl source d_z(q^2) of the potential-vorticity equation is nonpositive (it drives outflow), but the other terms of that evolution have no sign. (f) (Truncated mass.) For lambda > 0 put Q_lambda(t) = int (|eta(t)| - lambda)_+ dx, which is finite for 0 < t < T_*. For 0 < a < b < T_*, Q_lambda(b) + 4 pi int_a^b int_R (|eta(0, z, t)| - lambda)_+ dz dt <= Q_lambda(a) + int_a^b int_{|eta| > lambda} |d_z(q^2)| dx dt, and int_a^b ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)} dt <= Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b). In branch (II), for every fixed lambda > 0, Q_lambda(s_n) >= pi^2 c_0 rho^2/(4 kappa_n) - o(1), with c_0 as in Proposition 14 and rho = c_0/(8 sqrt2 C_*); hence kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity in branch (II) without the assumption N(a) < infinity of (e). If moreover epsilon_n := M(s_n)^5/D(s_n)^3 >= epsilon_0 > 0 for all n, put l_n = M(s_n)/D(s_n), X_n = D(s_n)^2/M(s_n), c_eta = c_0/(8 (R_1 + C(C_*) E_0/epsilon_0 + rho)) (R_1 as in Proposition 14, L^infty budget (iii), and C(C_*) as in Proposition 11(viii)) and lambda_n = c_eta X_n/2. Then Q_{lambda_n}(s_n) >= c_Q/kappa_n for large n, with c_Q = 2 pi^2 c_eta rho^3, and for every fixed a in (0, T_*) and every fixed c > 0, kappa_n int_a^{s_n} int_{|eta| > lambda_n, r >= c l_n} |d_z(q^2)| dx dt -> 0; consequently liminf_n kappa_n int_a^{s_n} int_{|eta| > lambda_n, r < c l_n} |d_z(q^2)| dx dt >= c_Q for every c > 0. There is also a sequence c_n -> 0 for which the exterior integral with c_n in place of c tends to 0, and for this sequence the inner liminf is again at least c_Q (this inner lower bound need not hold for an arbitrary prescribed sequence c_n -> 0: for each fixed n the inner integral tends to 0 as c -> 0, by dominated convergence). So the high-level source that the critical mass requires is generated at distance o(l_n) from the axis. No bound on this inner source has been derived. More generally, let a(s_n) be the distance from the axis of a maximizer of |grad u(s_n)| in units l_n, and B_n = a(s_n) + R_1 + rho. If B_n^2 epsilon_n >= epsilon_0 > 0 for all n (which holds when epsilon_n >= epsilon_0, with (R_1 + rho)^2 epsilon_0 in place of epsilon_0, and also allows a(s_n) -> infinity), then with lambda'_n = c_0 X_n/(16 B_n) one has, for all sufficiently large n, Q_{lambda'_n}(s_n) >= c'_Q/kappa_n, c'_Q = pi^2 c_0 rho^2 min{R_1, rho}/(4 (4 R_1 + rho)), and the conclusions above hold with lambda'_n, c'_Q and B_n l_n in place of lambda_n, c_Q and l_n: the high-level source must be generated at distance o((1 + a(s_n)) l_n) from the axis. (g) (Dynamic source bound.) There is an absolute constant C_P such that at all sufficiently late times |d_z(q^2)(r, z, t)| <= C_P S(t)^2 D(t)^{3/2} min{1, (D(t) r^2)^{-1}}, with S, D historical. Consequently, at a point where |d_z(q^2)| >= beta S(t)^{1/2} H(t)^{3/2} for some beta > 0 (a fixed fraction of the Landau envelope of Proposition 14, L^infty budget (v)), r <= (2 sqrt6/beta)^{1/2} (S(t)/H(t))^{1/2} and S(t) D(t)/H(t) >= (beta/C_P)^{2/3}; if moreover a < t <= s_n and |eta| > lambda_n there (notation of (f)), then H(t) > lambda_n/sqrt2 and S(t) >= (beta/C_P)^{2/3} c_eta/(2 sqrt2 l_n). In the case epsilon_n >= epsilon_0 of (f), M_n^2 S_n/H_n >= epsilon_n kappa_n S_n/C_* -> infinity: in the units of length (S_n/H_n)^{1/2} and viscosity one, in which circulation, swirl velocity and Hessian are bounded by G, G/sqrt2 and G/sqrt2, the historical velocity at s_n is unbounded. (h) (Level-band balance.) For lambda > 0 put Psi_lambda(v) = int_lambda^{2 lambda} (|v| - mu)_+ d mu, a convex C^{1,1} function with Psi_lambda'' = 1_{lambda < |v| < 2 lambda} almost everywhere and lambda (|v| - 2 lambda)_+ <= Psi_lambda(v) <= lambda (|v| - lambda)_+. At positive times, d/dt int Psi_lambda(eta) dx + 4 pi int_R Psi_lambda(eta(0, z, t)) dz = - int_{lambda < |eta| < 2 lambda} (|grad eta|^2 + q^2 d_z eta) dx <= (1/4) int_{lambda < |eta| < 2 lambda} q^4 dx. In branch (II) let B_n, lambda'_n and c'_Q be as in (f); the lower bound Q_{lambda'_n}(s_n) >= c'_Q/kappa_n holds for all sufficiently large n in all of branch (II), the condition B_n^2 epsilon_n >= epsilon_0 being used in (f) only for the exterior estimate. Put a_n = sup{t <= s_n : sqrt2 H(t) <= lambda'_n/2} and e_n = int_{a_n}^{T_*} ||grad u(t)||_2^2 dt. Then a_n -> T_*, e_n -> 0 and, for all sufficiently large n, int_{a_n}^{s_n} int_{lambda'_n/2 < |eta| < lambda'_n} q^4 dx dt >= c_h D_n M_n/B_n, with c_h = c_0 c'_Q/8; the band {lambda'_n/2 < |eta| < lambda'_n} lies in r < 32 sqrt2 B_n l_n/c_0. Consequently sup_{a_n <= t <= s_n} ||q(t)||_infty^2 >= c_h D_n M_n/(B_n e_n); by (g), B_n e_n S_n^2/M_n is bounded below by a positive constant, so B_n S_n^2/M_n -> infinity. For every fixed c > 0 the part of the integral at r >= c B_n l_n is at most (G^2 e_n/(c^4 B_n^3 epsilon_n)) D_n M_n/B_n; so whenever e_n = o(B_n^3 epsilon_n) (in particular when B_n^2 epsilon_n >= epsilon_0), this q^4 cost must be paid on the band at distance o(B_n l_n) from the axis. No upper bound small enough to contradict this lower bound has been derived. With W_4(t) = int r^2 q^4 dx, finite at positive times, the quartic remainder is paid by the radial diffusion of q at the price of an inflow term: for every lambda > 0, d/dt (int Psi_lambda(eta) dx + W_4/12) + 4 pi int_R Psi_lambda(eta(0, z, t)) dz <= (1/2) int (u^r)^- r q^4 dx. Hence in branch (II), for every fixed a in (0, T_*) and all sufficiently large n, int_a^{s_n} int (u^r)^- r q^4 dx dt >= (c_h/2) D_n M_n/B_n - W_4(a)/6, where D_n M_n/B_n -> infinity; since (u^r)^- r q^4 <= S(t) ||q(t)||_infty (u^r)^- q^2 and I_in(a, t) = o(S(t)) by (e), S_n^2 B_n sup_{a <= t <= s_n} ||q(t)||_infty/(D_n M_n) -> infinity. No uniform instantaneous estimate bounds the right side of this corrected balance by the dissipation: research answer res38 gives compact smooth fields with M H <= C D^2 for a fixed C, bounded circulation and energy, B and epsilon bounded above and below, kappa -> 0 and S/M -> 0, at which the Navier-Stokes time derivative of int Psi_lambda(eta) dx + W_4/12, at a level lambda of order D^2/(B M), exceeds every fixed multiple of (D M/(B^4 epsilon)) ||grad u||_2^2 (key estimates).

Key estimates. (a) The Y_2 identity of Proposition 16 gives 2 int_a^b A_2 dt <= Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b), A_2 = int r |grad q|^2 dx, Y_2(a) < infinity. In the swirl-record normalization, int int r |grad q|^2 dx dt over the physical cylinder corresponding to B_2(0) x [-1, 0] equals gamma_n int int_{B_2 x [-1, 0]} R |grad q_{U_n}|^2 dy dtau, q_{U_n} = U_n^theta/R. U_n -> U in C^{2,1}_loc (Theorem 2), and q_U is not constant there: otherwise U^theta = R (q_U(e_1, 0) = 1), contradicting |U^theta| <= 1 at R = 3/2. As gamma_n is bounded below (Theorem 1), each cylinder carries a fixed positive amount for large n; its duration T_n = r_n^2/gamma_n -> 0, so a subsequence of cylinders is disjoint in [a, T_*) and int_a^{T_*} A_2 dt = infinity. For other p, Hardy (Proposition 16) gives p(p-1) A_p + (2p-3)(5-2p) B_p >= theta(p) p(p-1) A_p with theta(p) > 0.
(b) Combine N(s_n) >= c_1/kappa_n (Proposition 14) with N(s_n) <= N(a) + Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, s_n) (Proposition 16).
(c) Notation (local). At s_n let M_n, D_n be M, D at s_n, l_n = M_n/D_n, V_n(y, sigma) the axis-centered rescaling of Proposition 8(c) (velocity M_n, length l_n, time 1/D_n), B_n its meridional part, q_{V_n} = V_n^theta/R and Omega_n(y, sigma) := (M_n/D_n^2) eta(l_n y, s_n + sigma/D_n) = omega^theta_{V_n}/R. Then ||Omega_n(sigma)||_{L^1(dy)} = kappa_n N(s_n + sigma/D_n) and d_sigma Omega_n + B_n.grad Omega_n = kappa_n Delta_5 Omega_n + d_Z(q_{V_n}^2). The terminal test psi_n is smooth, |psi_n| <= 1, supported in a rotated meridional disc of fixed radius around a point at distance R_0 from the axis where |omega^theta_{V_n}| >= m(C_*) > 0 (the point X_1 of Proposition 14, L^infty budget (iii), with m = c_0/4), and int Omega_n(0) psi_n dy >= c > 0. For fixed a in (0, T_*), A_n := (s_n - a) D_n, and phi_n solves d_sigma phi + B_n.grad phi + kappa_n L phi = 0 on R^3 x [-A_n, 0], phi(0) = psi_n, phi = 0 on the axis; |phi_n| <= 1 (maximum principle). All integrals are in dy, dsigma. By div B_n = 0 and the adjoint relation of sec. 1.1 (axis term killed by phi_n = 0), d/dsigma int Omega_n phi_n dy = -int q_{V_n}^2 d_Z phi_n dy, which integrates to the identity. The adjoint is an L^2 contraction (d/dsigma (1/2) ||phi_n||_2^2 = kappa_n ||grad phi_n||_2^2), ||Omega_n(-A_n)||_2 = ||eta(a)||_2 (M_n D_n)^{-1/2} and ||psi_n||_2^2 <= C (1 + R_0) <= C (1 + E_0 D_n^3/M_n^5); so |int Omega_n(-A_n) phi_n(-A_n)| <= C ||eta(a)||_2 ((M_n D_n)^{-1/2} + E_0^{1/2} D_n/M_n^3) -> 0 (D/M^3 = kappa/M). Splitting at R = (||Gamma_V||/||q_V||)^{1/2}, int_0^infty q_{V_n}^2 R dR <= ||q_{V_n}|| ||Gamma_{V_n}|| <= C_q G kappa_n^{4/3}, C_q = 2 C_*^{1/2} (C_* G^2/2)^{1/6}, using ||Gamma_{V_n}|| <= G kappa_n, ||q_{V_n}|| <= 2 (e_n C_*)^{1/2} (V_{n,2} is odd along {y_2 = 0}, with |V_{n,2}| <= e_n and second derivatives at most C_*) and e_n <= (C_* G^2/2)^{1/3} kappa_n^{2/3} (Theorem 13(a)). Hence int int sup_R |d_Z phi_n| dZ dsigma >= c/(4 pi C_q G) kappa_n^{-4/3} for large n; also A_n kappa_n = (s_n - a)/l_n^2 -> infinity. The bound R_0 <= R_1 + C(C_*) E_0 D_n^3/M_n^5 used above holds because R(X_1) <= a(s_n) + R_1 (Proposition 14, L^infty budget (iii)) and (1 + a(s_n)) epsilon(s_n) <= C(C_*) E_0 (Proposition 11(viii)).
(d) Frozen field: h = kappa^{1/3}, Q_1 in C_c^infty((1, 2)) nonzero, chi in C_c^infty((-2, 2)) with 0 <= chi <= 1, chi = 1 on [-1, 1], amplitude alpha with alpha sup rho^2 |Q_1(rho)| <= G, q = alpha h Q_1(R/h) chi(Z) sin(Z/h). Then |q| <= C kappa^{1/3}, |Gamma| = alpha h^3 rho^2 |Q_1 chi sin| <= G kappa (rho = R/h), int q^2 R dR = alpha^2 h^4 chi^2 sin^2(Z/h) int_1^2 rho Q_1^2 d rho <= C kappa^{4/3}, each derivative of order j <= 2 of R q costs h^{-j}, and d_Z(q^2) = alpha^2 h^2 Q_1(R/h)^2 (2 chi chi' sin^2(Z/h) + h^{-1} chi^2 sin(2Z/h)) has L^1(dy) norm between c alpha^2 kappa and C alpha^2 kappa. Log-slow history (T_* = 1, delta = 1 - t, ell = log(1/delta)): D = delta^{-4/3}, M = delta^{-2/3} ell^{1/2}, H = delta^{-2} ell^{-1/2}, S = delta^{-1/2}, N = ell. Then MH = D^2, kappa N = 1, and M^5/D^3 = delta^{2/3} ell^{5/2}, S^2/D = delta^{1/3}, S^3/H = delta^{1/2} ell^{1/2}, (S/M)/kappa^{2/3} = delta^{1/6} ell^{1/6} all tend to 0. For 0 <= a <= C/kappa(s), a/D(s) <= C ell delta^{4/3} = o(delta), so D(s - a/D(s))/D(s) -> 1 uniformly; on much longer windows the ratio can tend to 0.
(e) The e_r, e_theta components of the e_theta-derivative r^{-1}(u^r e_theta - u^theta e_r) give (u^r)^2 + (u^theta)^2 <= r^2 |grad u|^2, so (u^r)^- q^2 <= |u^theta| |u^r| |u^theta| r^{-2} <= (|u^theta|/2) |grad u|^2 and, with int_0^{T_*} ||grad u||_2^2 dt <= E_0/2, I_in(a, t) <= E_0 S(t)/4. Splitting at b in (a, t), dividing by S(t), letting t -> T_* and then b -> T_* gives I_in(a, t) = o(S(t)); then N(t) <= N(a) + Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, t) = o(S(t)), and c_1/kappa_n <= N(s_n) in branch (II). Source sign: with J = -int u^r q^2 dx and f = (-Delta_5)^{-1} d_z(q^2), the source changes u^r = -r phi_z at rate -r d_z f, so its contribution to dJ/dt (q^2 fixed) is int r q^2 d_z f dx = int r f Delta_5 f dx = -int r |grad f|^2 dx - (1/2) int f^2/r dx <= 0. The other terms (stretching, pressure, diffusion) have no sign, and restriction to {u^r < 0} destroys the integration by parts.
(f) Finiteness: the vector field omega^theta e_theta = curl b (b the meridional part of u) satisfies |grad(omega^theta e_theta)| >= |omega^theta|/r, and |grad curl b| <= sqrt2 |grad^2 b| with ||grad^2 b||_2 <= ||grad^2 u||_2 (b = (u + P u(P .))/2 as in Proposition 14), so ||eta(t)||_2 <= sqrt2 ||grad^2 u(t)||_2 < infinity and Q_lambda <= ||eta||_2^2/(4 lambda); positive superlevel sets of eta are bounded at positive times (L^infty budget (i) of Proposition 14). Balance: for convex regularizations of j_lambda(v) = (|v| - lambda)_+, (d_t + b.grad) j_lambda(eta) = Delta_5 j_lambda(eta) - j_lambda''(eta) |grad eta|^2 + j_lambda'(eta) d_z(q^2) <= Delta_5 j_lambda(eta) + 1_{|eta| > lambda} |d_z(q^2)|; integrate with div b = 0 and int Delta_5 f dx = -4 pi int f(0, z) dz (Proposition 14), using spatial cutoffs. Source: |d_z(q^2)| = 2 |q| |d_z q| <= r |d_z q|^2 + q^2/r, so ||d_z(q^2)||_1 <= A_2 + B_2 with B_2 = int q^2/r dx, and the p = 2 identity of Proposition 16, d/dt Y_2 + 2 A_2 + B_2 = -3 int u^r q^2 dx, gives the bound by Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b). Branch (II): for large n, S(s_n)/M(s_n) <= c_0^2/(576 C_*), and in the units of Proposition 14 the point X_1 has |f(X_1)| >= c_0/4, f the odd extension of omega^theta_U, and R(X_1) <= a(s_n) + R_1. Since f is Lipschitz with constant sqrt2 C_* and vanishes on the axis, R(X_1) >= 2 rho, and |f| >= c_0/8 on the disc of radius rho about X_1, on which rho <= R <= a(s_n) + R_1 + rho. The rotated disc contributes 2 pi D l^2 int int |f| dR dZ >= pi^2 c_0 rho^2/(4 kappa_n) to int |eta| dx and has physical volume at most 2 pi^2 (a(s_n) + R_1 + rho) rho^2 l^3, where l = M(s_n)/D(s_n); by Proposition 11(viii), a(s_n) l^3 <= C(C_*) E_0 l^3/epsilon_n = C(C_*) E_0/M(s_n)^2, and l^3 <= C E_0/M(s_n)^2 by M^5 <= C E_0 D^3 (Proposition 14), so lambda times the volume tends to 0 (M(s_n) -> infinity). As Q_lambda(a) < infinity, the balance gives int_a^{s_n} ||d_z(q^2)||_1 dt >= pi^2 c_0 rho^2/(4 kappa_n) - Q_lambda(a) - o(1) -> infinity. By (e), Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, t) <= Y_2(a) + 3 E_0 S(t)/4, so S(s_n) -> infinity, and then I_in(a, t) = o(S(t)) gives kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity. Case epsilon_n >= epsilon_0: now a(s_n) <= C(C_*) E_0/epsilon_0, so on the disc |eta| = X_n |f|/R >= c_eta X_n, and the rotated disc has volume at least 4 pi^2 rho^3 l_n^3; as X_n l_n^3 = 1/kappa_n, Q_{lambda_n}(s_n) >= (c_eta X_n/2) 4 pi^2 rho^3 l_n^3 = c_Q/kappa_n. Since X_n >= c D(s_n)^{7/5} -> infinity (by M^5 <= C E_0 D^3) while eta is bounded on [a, a'] for every a' < T_*, the superlevel set {|eta| > lambda_n} is empty on [a, a'] for large n, so Q_{lambda_n}(a) = 0. Exterior source: for t <= s_n, |d_z(q^2)| <= 2 sqrt6 S^{3/2} H^{1/2} r^{-2} (L^infty budget (v) of Proposition 14) and 2 S^3 <= G^2 H (Theorem 13(a)) give |d_z(q^2)| <= 2 sqrt3 G H(s_n) r^{-2} <= K_0 X_n r^{-2}, K_0 = 2 sqrt3 G C_*, by (A) at s_n. On {|eta| > lambda_n} one has 1 < |omega^theta|^2/(lambda_n^2 r^2), so on {|eta| > lambda_n, r >= c l_n}, |d_z(q^2)| <= 4 K_0 |omega^theta|^2/(c_eta^2 c^4 X_n l_n^4), and X_n l_n^4 = M(s_n)^3/D(s_n)^2 = epsilon_n kappa_n. With ||omega^theta||_2 <= ||grad u||_2, kappa_n times the exterior integral over [a', s_n] is at most 4 K_0/(c_eta^2 c^4 epsilon_0) int_{a'}^{T_*} ||grad u||_2^2 dt for large n, which tends to 0 as a' -> T_*. Subtracting from the balance gives the inner lower bound (the axis term is nonnegative and is dropped); a sequence c_n -> 0 is obtained by choosing a'_n -> T_* such that {|eta| > lambda_n} is empty on [a, a'_n] (possible since eta is bounded on each [a, a'] and lambda_n -> infinity), and then c_n -> 0 slowly enough that c_n^{-4} int_{a'_n}^{T_*} ||grad u||_2^2 dt -> 0. General B_n: R(X_1) >= max{2 rho, a(s_n) - R_1} and R <= B_n on the disc, so |eta| >= c_0 X_n/(8 B_n) = 2 lambda'_n there and Q_{lambda'_n}(s_n) >= lambda'_n 2 pi^2 R(X_1) rho^2 l_n^3 = (pi^2 c_0 rho^2/(8 kappa_n)) R(X_1)/B_n >= c'_Q/kappa_n, because R(X_1)/B_n >= 2 min{R_1, rho}/(4 R_1 + rho) (treat a(s_n) >= 3 R_1 and a(s_n) < 3 R_1 separately). By Proposition 11(viii) and epsilon_n <= C E_0 (M^5 <= C E_0 D^3), B_n epsilon_n <= C, so lambda'_n >= c X_n epsilon_n = c M_n^2/kappa_n -> infinity and early superlevel sets are again empty. On {|eta| > lambda'_n, r >= c B_n l_n}, |d_z(q^2)| <= K_0 X_n |omega^theta|^2/(lambda'_n^2 r^4) <= 256 K_0 |omega^theta|^2/(c_0^2 c^4 B_n^2 epsilon_n kappa_n), and the argument is concluded as before.
(g) If f_t + v.grad f = Delta f + c f on [t - C_1/D, t] with ||grad v||_infty + |c| <= C D, then passing to the frame of a trajectory of v and rescaling length by D^{-1/2} and time by 1/D gives an equation with drift bounded on the unit parabolic cylinder and bounded zeroth-order coefficient, so interior parabolic L^p estimates give |grad f(t)| <= C D^{1/2} sup |f|. Apply this to W = u^theta e_theta, which satisfies W_t + b.grad W = Delta W - (u^r/r) W, with ||grad b||_infty <= D (the entries of grad u in the cylindrical frame split between b and W) and |u^r/r| <= D: ||grad W(t)||_infty <= C S D^{1/2}, so |q| <= C S D^{1/2} (u^theta vanishes on the axis). Fix D_* = D(t) and h = 1/D_*. First apply the estimate for W uniformly for s in [t - h, t], using [s - h, s] contained in [t - 2h, t]. Then apply it on [t - h, t] to q as an axisymmetric function on R^5 = R^4 x R, which satisfies q_t + b.grad_5 q = Delta_5 q - 2 (u^r/r) q with a drift of Lipschitz constant at most C D: |grad_5 q| <= C S D. So |d_z(q^2)| = 2 |q| |d_z q| <= C S^2 D^{3/2}, and |q| <= S/r with |d_z q| = |d_z u^theta|/r <= C S D^{1/2}/r gives the second bound. The consequences follow by comparison with the Landau envelope |d_z(q^2)| <= 2 sqrt6 S^{1/2} H^{3/2} min{1, S/(H r^2)}, with |eta(t)| <= sqrt2 H(t) (omega^theta vanishes on the axis and |grad omega| <= sqrt2 |grad^2 u|), with D(t) <= D_n and with H_n <= C_* D_n^2/M_n.
(h) Psi_lambda'(v) = sgn(v) min{(|v| - lambda)_+, lambda} is Lipschitz and vanishes for |v| <= lambda, and positive superlevel sets of |eta| are bounded at positive times, so multiplying the equation for eta by Psi_lambda'(eta) and integrating is justified (spatial cutoffs as in (f)). Transport integrates to zero (div b = 0); int Psi_lambda'(eta) Delta_5 eta dx = int Delta_5 Psi_lambda(eta) dx - int Psi_lambda''(eta) |grad eta|^2 dx with int Delta_5 g dx = -4 pi int g(0, z) dz; and int Psi_lambda'(eta) d_z(q^2) dx = - int q^2 Psi_lambda''(eta) d_z eta dx. Young's inequality -q^2 d_z eta <= |grad eta|^2 + q^4/4 gives the inequality. Since |eta(t)| <= sqrt2 H(t), H is finite before T_* and lambda'_n -> infinity, a_n is defined for large n and a_n -> T_*; |eta(s_n)| > lambda'_n somewhere, so a_n < s_n, and Psi_{lambda'_n/2}(eta(a_n)) = 0 by continuity. Integrating with lambda = lambda'_n/2 on [a_n, s_n], (lambda'_n/2) Q_{lambda'_n}(s_n) <= int Psi_{lambda'_n/2}(eta(s_n)) dx <= (1/4) int_{a_n}^{s_n} int_{lambda'_n/2 < |eta| < lambda'_n} q^4 dx dt, and lambda'_n c'_Q/(2 kappa_n) = c_0 c'_Q X_n/(32 B_n kappa_n) = c_0 c'_Q D_n M_n/(32 B_n). On the band |omega^theta| = r |eta| > r lambda'_n/2 and |omega^theta| = |d_z u^r - d_r u^z| <= sqrt2 |grad u| <= sqrt2 D_n, so r < 2 sqrt2 D_n/lambda'_n = 32 sqrt2 B_n l_n/c_0. For the consequences: u^theta/r is an entry of grad u in the cylindrical frame, so q^2 <= |grad u|^2 pointwise and int q^2 dx <= ||grad u||_2^2, whence the band integral is at most sup ||q||_infty^2 e_n; |q| <= C S D^{1/2} by the key estimates of (g) (a_n is eventually late enough); and |q| = |Gamma|/r^2 <= G/r^2 gives q^4 <= G^2 q^2/r^4, with l_n^4 D_n M_n = M_n^5/D_n^3 = epsilon_n. Corrected balance: (d_t + b.grad) q = Delta_5 q - 2 (u^r/r) q, and r^2 dx is a multiple of the five-dimensional volume, for which Delta_5 is the Laplacian of axisymmetric functions, while div(r^2 b) = 2 r u^r; hence d/dt W_4 = -12 int r^2 q^2 |grad q|^2 dx - 6 int (u^r/r) r^2 q^4 dx, with no axis term (cutoffs; q is bounded at positive times and |q| <= G/r^2). Integration by parts, int_0^infty r q^4 dr = -2 int_0^infty r^2 q^3 d_r q dr, and Cauchy-Schwarz give int q^4 dx <= 4 int r^2 q^2 (d_r q)^2 dx, so (1/4) int_band q^4 dx <= int r^2 q^2 |grad q|^2 dx, and what remains is -(1/2) int u^r r q^4 dx <= (1/2) int (u^r)^- r q^4 dx. Integrate on [a, s_n] with lambda = lambda'_n/2 (int Psi_{lambda'_n/2}(eta(a)) dx = 0 for large n, eta(a) being bounded) and use int Psi_{lambda'_n/2}(eta(s_n)) dx >= (c_h/4) D_n M_n/B_n and W_4(s_n) >= 0; W_4(a) <= ||q(a)||_infty^2 ||u(a)||_2^2 < infinity. By Proposition 11(viii), (1 + a(s_n)) epsilon_n <= C(C_*) E_0, so B_n <= C/epsilon_n and D_n M_n/B_n >= c D_n M_n epsilon_n = c M_n^2/kappa_n^2 -> infinity. Finally r q^4 = |u^theta| |q| q^2 and S(t) <= S_n. The res38 fields (checked by the author by hand): with zeta a fixed cutoff equal to 1 on [0, 1] and to 0 on [2, infinity), Phi(y) = Z zeta(|y|) and V = (-R d_Z Phi, 2 Phi + R d_R Phi) in cylindrical components, V = (-y_1, -y_2, 2Z) on the unit ball; for sigma -> infinity put h = sigma^{-7}, L = sigma h, A = sigma h^{-2}, b = A L V(x/L) and q = h^{-2} Q_0(r/h) chi_0(z/L), with fixed nonzero Q_0, chi_0 supported in r/h < 1, |z/L| < 1/4. Then u^r/r = -A and eta = 0 on the swirl support, eta = h^{-3} (-Delta_5 Phi)(x/L), M, D and H are of orders sigma^2/h, sigma/h^2 and at most h^{-3}, the energy, the circulation, M H/D^2, B and epsilon stay bounded (epsilon and B fixed), kappa is of order sigma^{-3} and S/M of order sigma^{-2}. At lambda = lambda_0 h^{-3} the source term of the band balance vanishes (the swirl lies where eta = 0), the band and axis losses are O(sigma h^{-5}) (their sum is K sigma h^{-5} with a fixed profile constant K >= 0, which vanishes for instance when lambda_0 >= ||Delta_5 Phi||_infty), and d/dt W_4 = 6 A W_4 - O(sigma h^{-5}) with A W_4 = J_0 sigma^2 h^{-5}, J_0 = 2 pi int rho^3 Q_0^4 chi_0^4 d rho dZ > 0; so the derivative of int Psi_lambda(eta) dx + W_4/12 is h^{-5} (J_0 sigma^2/2 - O(sigma)), while (D M/(B^4 epsilon)) ||grad u||_2^2 is of order sigma^3 h^{-3} sigma^5 h^{-1} = sigma h^{-5}. The swirl lies outside the eta-band, so these fields say nothing about corrections localized on the band, and they are instantaneous data, not a singular solution.

Not reproduced: theta(p) and the case 3/2 < p < 2 in (a); existence and maximum principle for the adjoint and the construction of psi_n in (c); the finite-dissipation scalar constraints of the history in (d); the remaining terms of dJ/dt in (e). The complete argument is not reproduced in this report; this item is not checkable from the report alone.

Status: proof sketch with key estimates; source: own derivation (parts (c), (d), (e): research answer checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 7, 13, 21, 22. Part (f) and the radius bound in (c) (from research answer res35 and Proposition 11(viii), checked by the author by hand) were added after round 38 and audited in adversarial audit round 39; the key estimates of (f) are complete up to the standard cutoff and regularization justifications. Part (g) (from research answer res36, checked by the author by hand) and the extension of (f) to B_n^2 epsilon_n >= epsilon_0 (own derivation) were added after round 39 and audited in adversarial audit round 40. Part (h) (own derivation) was added after round 40 and audited in adversarial audit round 41. The corrected balance with W_4 in (h) (from research answer res38, checked by the author by hand) was added after round 41 and audited in adversarial audit round 42.

Proposition 19 (stream-function BMO form). Let B(t) = psi^theta(t) e_theta be the vector stream function of the meridional field, so that curl B = u^r e_r + u^z e_z, and let psi_U(R, Z, tau) = R^{-1} int_0^R rho U^Z(rho, Z, tau) d rho be the stream function of the Case-1 limit U. (a) For every 0 < t < T_*, ||B(t)||_BMO <= (beta/(2 pi)) N(t), with beta the BMO norm of the vector potential of a unit circular ring (an absolute constant). (b) If for some constant c_0 the field V - c_0 e_Z (V the meridional part of U) can be written as curl B_2 + b_3 with sup_tau ||B_2(tau)||_BMO + sup R |b_3| < infinity, in particular if sup_tau ||(psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta||_BMO < infinity, then U^theta = 0, which contradicts U^theta(e_1, 0) = 1. Hence in Case 1 no such decomposition has both controlling quantities uniformly finite; in particular, for every c_0, sup_tau ||(psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta||_BMO = infinity. (c) Consequently, for every C there is a normalized time tau_C <= 0 with liminf_n N(t_n + T_n tau_C) >= C along the subsequence of Theorem 2 (the order of quantifiers matters: no single phase works for all C), and limsup_{t -> T_*} N(t) = infinity in both branches of Theorem 13. The global statement limsup N = infinity is not new: since the swirl field u^theta e_theta has a vector potential of BMO norm at most 152 G (Proposition 20(b)), a bound on N near T_* would give the full velocity a potential bounded in BMO, which is excluded by [LZ, Theorem 1.4]. The placement inside the normalized windows is the specific content. (d) For every A > 0 the integral of ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)} over the physical window [t_n - A T_n, t_n] is bounded below by a positive constant independent of n (if d_Z(q_U^2) vanished on the window, U^theta would be independent of Z there and would satisfy w_tau = nu_*(w_RR + w_R/R - w/R^2), which is incompatible with the record conditions at (1, 0, 0)); hence int_a^{T_*} ||d_z(q^2)||_1 dt = infinity for every a < T_*. This divergence does not by itself force cancellation in the balance of N, since diffusion can balance a same-signed source (a stationary scalar example in 5D radial variables exists).

Key estimates. ||f||_BMO := sup_Q |Q|^{-1} int_Q |f - f_Q| dx over balls Q (f_Q the mean over Q). It is invariant under translations and dilations and lower semicontinuous under local uniform convergence.
(a) For the circle C_{rho,zeta} = {r = rho, z = zeta} put B_{rho,zeta}(x) := (4 pi)^{-1} int_{C_{rho,zeta}} e_theta(y) |x - y|^{-1} ds(y) (ds arclength). Then B_{rho,zeta}(x) = B_{1,0}((x - zeta e_3)/rho), so every ring potential has BMO norm beta := ||B_{1,0}||_BMO. Let f := B_{1,0} and let d be the distance to C_{1,0}. Then |grad f| <= C_0/d, f = (2 pi)^{-1} log(1/d) e_theta + O(1) near C_{1,0}, and |f| <= C_0 |x|^{-2} for |x| >= 2. On a ball Q = B(x_0, s) the mean oscillation is bounded in three cases. Intermediate case (s <= 1/6, d(x_0) >= 2s): it is at most 2 C_0. Near case (s <= 1/6, d(x_0) < 2s): compare f with (2 pi)^{-1} log(1/s) e_theta(x_0) and use |Q intersected with {d < lambda s}| <= C lambda^2 s^3. Far case (s > 1/6): use int_Q |x|^{-2} dx <= 4 pi s and f in L^1(B(0, 2)). Hence beta < infinity. For omega_k := omega^theta 1_{1/k < rho < k, |zeta| < k}, Fubini gives A_k := (4 pi |x|)^{-1} * (omega_k e_theta) = int int omega_k(rho, zeta) B_{rho,zeta} d rho d zeta. On every ball its mean oscillation is at most beta int int |omega^theta| d rho d zeta = (beta/(2 pi)) N(t). For 0 < t < T_*, curl b = omega^theta e_theta is in L^2, and grad A_k converges in L^6 by Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev. Since -Delta B = curl b, the limit minus grad B is harmonic and lies in L^6 + L^2, hence it is zero. The Poincare inequality passes the bound to B.
(b) Let W(y, tau) := U(y + c_0 tau e_3, tau) - c_0 e_3 (Galilean change of frame). W is a bounded smooth ancient axisymmetric solution with viscosity nu_* and Gamma_W(y, tau) = Gamma_U(y + c_0 tau e_3, tau). Its meridional part is curl B_2 + b_3 at axially translated points, with the same norms; div b_3 = 0 holds automatically. W~(y, s) := gamma_* W(y, gamma_* s) has viscosity 1 and |Gamma_{W~}| <= G. Here the Lei-Zhang norm is ||b||_E := sup_t ||B_2(t)||_BMO + sup_{t,x} r |b_3| (general form in Proposition 20(d)). It is invariant under b -> lambda b(lambda x, lambda^2 t), so [LZ, Theorem 1.1] holds on every Q_L = B(0, L) x [-L^2, 0] with the same C, alpha: |Gamma_{W~}(x, s) - Gamma_{W~}(0, 0)| <= C [(|x| + |s|^{1/2})/L]^alpha G. Letting L -> infinity gives Gamma_{W~} = Gamma_{W~}(0, 0) = 0, so U^theta = 0. In the particular case, curl((c_0 R/2) e_theta) = c_0 e_Z gives B_2 = (psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta and b_3 = 0.
(c) Put psi_{U_n}(R, Z, tau) := gamma_n^{-1} psi^theta(r_n R, z_n + r_n Z, t_n + T_n tau). Then psi_{U_n} e_theta = gamma_n^{-1} B(z_n e_3 + r_n y, t_n + T_n tau), so ||psi_{U_n}(tau) e_theta||_BMO = gamma_n^{-1} ||B(t_n + T_n tau)||_BMO. Along the subsequence of Theorem 2, psi_{U_n} -> psi_U locally uniformly, and lower semicontinuity gives ||psi_U(tau) e_theta||_BMO <= gamma_*^{-1} liminf_n ||B(t_n + T_n tau)||_BMO. If for some C we had liminf_n ||B(t_n + T_n tau)||_BMO < C for every tau <= 0, this would contradict (b) with c_0 = 0. So each C has a tau_C with liminf_n ||B(t_n + T_n tau_C)||_BMO >= C. By (a), liminf_n N(t_n + T_n tau_C) >= 2 pi C/beta, with t_n + T_n tau_C -> T_*. This does not give N(t_n) -> infinity, nor one tau for all C. Global form: u = curl(B + w_s) with w_s := curl (-Delta)^{-1} (u^theta e_theta), so ||B + w_s||_BMO <= (beta/(2 pi)) N + 152 G. [LZ, Theorem 1.4] states that a suitable weak solution with L^2 data, bounded r u_0^theta and a velocity potential bounded in BMO uniformly in time is smooth. It therefore excludes N bounded on [a, T_*).
(d) Since q = gamma_n r_n^{-2} q_{U_n}, dx = r_n^3 dy and dt = r_n^2 gamma_n^{-1} d tau, the window integral equals gamma_n int_{-A}^0 int |d_Z(q_{U_n}^2)| dy d tau. Suppose d_Z(q_U^2) = 0 on R^3 x [-A, 0]. Then w := U^theta is independent of Z there, and incompressibility gives |(2L)^{-1} int_{-L}^{L} U^R dZ| <= K R/(2L). Averaging w_tau + U^R (w_R + w/R) = nu_* (w_RR + R^{-1} w_R - R^{-2} w) over |Z| < L and letting L -> infinity gives w_tau = nu_* (w_RR + R^{-1} w_R - R^{-2} w). At (1, 0, 0) we have w = 1, w_R = 0, w_RR <= 0, and w_tau >= 0 because w <= 1 at earlier times. The equation, however, gives w_tau <= -nu_* < 0. So d_Z(q_U^2) is nonzero on a compact set off the axis. The C^{2,1}_loc convergence with gamma_n -> gamma_* > 0 gives a lower bound uniform in large n, and disjoint windows give the divergence. Example (not a Navier-Stokes solution) for the equation (d_t + b.grad) eta = Delta_5 eta + d_z(q^2) with b = 0: let rho := (r^2 + z^2)^{1/2}, q^2 := e^{-rho^2}, -Delta_5 H = e^{-rho^2} and eta := d_z H. Then H'(rho) = -rho^{-4} int_0^rho s^4 e^{-s^2} ds < 0, so eta = H'(rho) z/rho and d_z(q^2) = -2z e^{-rho^2} have the same sign. Moreover Delta_5 eta + d_z(q^2) = 0, so eta is stationary, and N is finite and constant.

Not reproduced: the value of beta (not computed), the constants of the near-ring expansion and of the logarithmic estimate in (a), the propagation of finite enstrophy, and the convergence of Theorem 2 used in (c), (d). The complete argument is not reproduced in this report; this item is not checkable from the report alone.

Status: proof sketch with key estimates; source: own derivation ((d), the stationary scalar example and the kinematic family of sec. 3.11: supplied in adversarial audit round 8 and checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 8, 21, 22.

Proposition 20 (shrinking physical cylinders). Let C_rho(z_0) = {r < rho, |z - z_0| < rho}. (a) In Case 1 every singular point has the form (0, z_*, T_*), at least one exists, and at each one limsup_{rho -> 0} of each of the scale-invariant quantities A(rho) = sup_t rho^{-1} int_{B_rho} |u|^2, E(rho) = rho^{-1} int int_{Q_rho} |grad u|^2 and C(rho) = rho^{-2} int int_{Q_rho} |u|^3 is infinite (Type II), by Seregin's theorem [Se20, Theorem 2.1]. (b) The swirl field v = u^theta e_theta has the vector potential w_s = curl (-Delta)^{-1} v with ||w_s||_BMO <= 152 G. (c) For every singular point and every rho > 0, limsup_{t -> T_*} int_{C_rho(z_*)} |omega^theta/r| dx = infinity. The proof rescales by lambda = min(rho/3, tau_0^{1/2}, T_*^{1/2}), splits the meridional potential into the potential of the localized vorticity (BMO norm at most an absolute constant times the local mass) and a far part with gradient at most C (E_0/lambda)^{1/2} on C_1, adds (b), and applies [Se20, sec. 3] (a stream function bounded in local BMO makes the origin regular). (d) For the Case-1 limit U and every c_0, the Galilean-transformed, viscosity-normalized limit W_{c_0} has sup_{R >= 1} A_b(W_{c_0}, R) = infinity at the origin, where A_b(W, R) = R^{-1} sup_{-R^2 < s < 0} int_{B_R} |b_W|^2 is the scaled energy of the meridional part alone: otherwise the hollowed scaled energy of b_W is bounded at all scales, and the Lei-Zhang Hoelder estimate [LZ, Theorem 1.1] on cylinders of every size H, with constants independent of H, forces Gamma_W(e_1, 0) = 0. (e) Let (x_k, t_k) be points with t_k -> T_*, r(x_k) -> 0 and |Gamma(x_k, t_k)| >= c > 0, a_k the axis point at the height of x_k, and A_b(s; a, t) = s^{-1} sup_{t - s^2 < t' < t} int_{B_s(a)} |b(t')|^2. Then, for fixed rho_0 with 4 rho_0^2 < inf_k t_k (after discarding early terms), sup_{0 < s <= 2 rho_0} A_b(s; a_k, t_k) -> infinity, and it exceeds M as soon as rho_0/r(x_k) > (2 C_{2M} G/c)^{1/alpha_{2M}}, where C_M, alpha_M are the constants of [LZ, Theorem 1.1] for ||b||_E <= M (apply that theorem at the regular top (a_k, t_k), where Gamma vanishes on the axis).
