At the swirl records the same holds for the scaled dissipation E_u, selected approximate-maximizing scales s_n satisfy r_n/s_n -> 0 and s_n -> 0 (for the shrinking-scale conclusion, after an extraction with a_n converging to a singular axis point), and A_b(rho; a_n, t_n) >= H_n^{1/2} on the interval s_n <= rho <= s_n H_n^{1/2}, where H_n -> infinity are their values; this interval contains the radii 2^j s_n, 0 <= j <= floor((1/2) log_2 H_n). The analogous statement at the singular top (0, z_*, T_*) would need [LZ, Theorem 1.1] with a singular top point and is not claimed.

Key estimates. Notation: x_* := z_* e_3, B_rho := B(x_*, rho) and Q_rho := B_rho x (T_* - rho^2, T_*). The sup in A(rho) is over T_* - rho^2 < t < T_*, and D(rho) := rho^{-2} int int_{Q_rho} |p|^{3/2} is the pressure quantity. All of these are invariant under u -> lambda u(lambda x, lambda^2 t).
(a) The pair (u, p), with p = R_i R_j(u_i u_j), translated and rescaled, is a suitable weak solution on every Q_rho(x_0, T_*) with rho^2 < T_*. Since u is in L^{10/3} and p in L^{5/3} on R^3 x (0, T_*), fix a with a^2 < T_*. Then C + D on Q_a(x_0, T_*) is small for |x_0| >= R_0, and [Se20, Proposition 1.4(1)] (or [SS, Lemma 1.2]) gives |u| <= c/a on Q_{a/2}(x_0, T_*). If all points of {|x| <= R_0} x {T_*} were regular, finitely many regular neighbourhoods would bound M. An off-axis singular point would give a circle of singular points, which [CKN] excludes. Type II: apply [Se20, Theorem 2.1] to lambda u(x_* + lambda x, T_* + lambda^2 t), lambda^2 < T_*.
(b) w_s(x) = int grad N(x - y) x v(y) dy with N(x) = (4 pi |x|)^{-1}, so curl w_s = v and div w_s = 0. For Q = B(x_0, s), split v at B(x_0, 2s), so that w_s = w_1 + w_2, and use |v(y)| <= G/|y'| (y' the horizontal part). Near part: int_Q |x - y|^{-2} dx <= 4 pi s, and B(x_0, 2s) lies in a cylinder of height 4s over a disk of radius 2s. So int_Q |w_1| dx <= 16 pi G s^3, a mean of at most 12 G. Far part: for d := |y - x_0| >= 2s, ||grad^2 N(z)|| = (2 pi)^{-1} |z|^{-3} gives |grad N(x - y) - grad N(x_0 - y)| <= (4/pi) s d^{-3}. Over the shell 2^k s <= d < 2^{k+1} s, int G |y'|^{-1} dy is at most 2^{2k+4} pi G s^2, so |w_2(x) - w_2(x_0)| <= 64 G sum_{k >= 1} 2^{-k} = 64 G. Hence |Q|^{-1} int_Q |w_s - w_2(x_0)| dx <= 76 G, and the mean oscillation is at most 152 G.
(c) Suppose int_{C_rho(z_*)} |eta(t)| dx <= N_0 for t in (T_* - tau_0, T_*), for some tau_0 > 0. Put v(x, t) := lambda u(x_* + lambda x, T_* + lambda^2 t) on R^3 x (-1, 0). Then |r v^theta| <= G, ||v(t)||_2^2 <= E_0/lambda and int_{C_3} |eta_v(t)| dx <= N_0, where C_s := C_s(0). Also v = curl w with w := B_v + w_s. Take an axisymmetric cutoff chi with chi = 1 on C_2, supp chi in C_3 and |grad chi| <= 2. Split B_v = B_1 + B_2 with B_1 := N * (chi omega_v^theta e_theta). By Proposition 19(a), ||B_1(t)||_BMO <= (beta/(2 pi)) N_0. Write (1 - chi) omega_v^theta e_theta = curl((1 - chi) b_v) + grad chi x b_v. One integration by parts and Cauchy-Schwarz then give |grad B_2| <= C_f (E_0/lambda)^{1/2} on C_1, with C_f absolute. Hence ||w(t)||_{BMO(C_1)} <= c' [(beta/(2 pi)) N_0 + 152 G] + 6 C_f (E_0/lambda)^{1/2}, with c' absolute. Here ||.||_{BMO(C_1)} is the sup of mean oscillations over cylinders C(x, s) contained in C_1. By [Se20, sec. 3], through [Se11, Proposition 1.1], such a bound for a.a. t in (-1, 0) makes the origin regular, a contradiction. This use is qualitative, because the bound of [Se11] also depends on the pressure. For example, v_n = sin(nt) e_3, with pressure -n cos(nt) x_3, has potentials bounded in BMO and bounded A, E, C, while D grows like n^{3/2}. [Se11] is used only as quoted in [Se20].
(d) W_{c_0}(y, s) := gamma_* [U(y + c_0 gamma_* s e_3, gamma_* s) - c_0 e_3] (Galilean change of frame, viscosity 1). It has meridional part b_W, |Gamma_W| <= G and Gamma_W(e_1, 0) = gamma_*. For b = b_1 + b_2 + b_3 with divergence-free parts and b_2 = curl B_2, the Lei-Zhang norm is ||b||_E := HSE(b_1) + sup_t ||B_2(t)||_BMO + sup_{t,x} r |b_3|. The hollowed scaled energy is HSE(b_1) := sup_{0 < R < R_0} sup_{-R^2 < s < 0} R^{-1} int_{B_{2R} minus B_{R/8}} |b_1(y, s)|^2 dy. Since A_b(W, R) <= (4 pi/3) ||W||_infty^2 for R < 1, sup_{R >= 1} A_b < infinity would give L' := sup_{R > 0} A_b(W, R) < infinity. Then HSE(b_W) <= 2L' with R_0 = H for every H, since the hollowed integral is at most 2 A_b(W, 2R). [LZ, Theorem 1.1] on Q_H with b_1 = b_W and b_2 = b_3 = 0 is invariant under y -> y/H. It gives gamma_* = |Gamma_W(e_1, 0) - Gamma_W(0, 0)| <= C H^{-alpha} G, which fails as H -> infinity.
(e) Suppose sup_{0 < s <= 2 rho_0} A_b(s; a_k, t_k) <= M. Then the hollowed scaled energy at the top (a_k, t_k) with R_0 = rho_0 is at most 2M. [LZ, Theorem 1.1] with b_1 = b and Gamma(a_k, t_k) = 0 then gives c <= C_{2M} G (r(x_k)/rho_0)^{alpha_{2M}}. Let E_u(s; a, t) := s^{-1} int_{t - s^2}^{t} int_{B_s(a)} |grad u|^2 dx dt'. If a_k -> x_* with x_* singular, then E_u(s; a_k, t_k) -> E_u(s; x_*, T_*) for each s by dominated convergence, so boundedness would contradict (a). At the swirl records a_n := z_n e_3, and z_n -> z_* singular after extraction. Choose s_n in (0, 2 rho_0] with H_n := A_b(s_n; a_n, t_n) >= (1/2) sup_{0 < s <= 2 rho_0} A_b(s; a_n, t_n). Then H_n -> infinity, and A_b(s) <= E_0/s gives s_n <= E_0/H_n. For s <= L r_n and large n, (K) gives A_b(s; a_n, t_n) <= (4 pi/3) G^2 (K + 1)^2 L^2, so s_n/r_n -> infinity. By nesting, A_b(rho) >= (s/rho) A_b(s) for rho >= s, which gives A_b(rho; a_n, t_n) >= H_n^{1/2} for s_n <= rho <= s_n H_n^{1/2} (<= (E_0 s_n)^{1/2}).

Record-scale bound for E_u. For fixed L, Theorem 2 gives |grad U_n| <= C_L on B_{L+1} x [-G L^2, 0] for large n. For s = lambda r_n with lambda <= L, the axis-centred change of variables x = a_n + r_n y, t = t_n + T_n tau of the normalization in sec. 1.1 (a_n = z_n e_3, T_n = r_n/S_n, gamma_n = r_n S_n) gives E_u(s; a_n, t_n) = (gamma_n/lambda) int_{-gamma_n lambda^2}^0 int_{B_lambda(0)} |grad U_n(y, tau)|^2 dy dtau <= (4 pi/3) gamma_n^2 C_L^2 lambda^4 <= (4 pi/3) G^2 C_L^2 L^4, since B_lambda(0) is contained in B_{L+1}. Hence scales s_n with E_u(s_n; a_n, t_n) >= (1/2) sup_{0 < s <= 2 rho_0} E_u(s; a_n, t_n) -> infinity satisfy s_n/r_n -> infinity; the energy inequality E_u(s) <= E_0/(2s) gives s_n -> 0, and nesting (rho E_u(rho) >= s E_u(s) for rho >= s) gives the same interval of large scaled dissipation as for A_b. The [CKN] covering argument is cited, and the absolute constants C_f, c' are not computed.

Status: proof with key estimates (the [CKN] covering argument cited; the record-scale bound for E_u completed after adversarial audit round 22); source: own derivation ((d): proof through [LZ, Theorem 1.1] supplied by the round-9 audit and checked by the author; (e): research answer res6 checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 9, 13, 21, 22.

Proposition 21 (circulation escape). Let a_* = (0, 0, z_*) be a singular point at which the swirl records concentrate (after a subsequence). Let G_0 := lim_{rho, delta -> 0} sup{Gamma(x, t) : |x - a_*| < rho, T_* - delta <= t < T_*} be the local circulation supremum there, and put phi_a(R) = 1 - (1 + aR) e^{-aR}.
(a) Gamma_U <= (G_0/gamma_*) phi_{K gamma_*}(R) on the limit (a one-sided bound). Hence G_0 > gamma_*/phi_{K gamma_*}(1) > gamma_* (1 + (1 + K gamma_*) e^{-K gamma_*}), the first inequality being strict by the strong maximum principle, and the swirl records do not carry the local circulation supremum.
(b) Local circulation gain. Fix 0 < gamma_0 <= G. Let 0 < delta <= 1/2 and B >= 1, let x_0 be a point with r_0 > 0 and gamma = Gamma(x_0, t_0) in [gamma_0, G], put T_0 = r_0^2/gamma, and assume t_0 >= 2 T_0. Suppose the meridional speed satisfies |b| <= B gamma/r_0 on the annulus delta r_0/2 < r < 2 r_0, |z - z_0| < r_0 during (t_0 - 2T_0, t_0], and Gamma <= gamma/2 at one point (delta r_0, z') with |z' - z_0| <= r_0/2 at a time in [t_0 - 3T_0/2, t_0 - T_0/2]. Then the supremum of Gamma over that region is at least (1 + c_1) gamma, where c_1 > 0 depends on delta, B, gamma_0 and G. The proof is a Harnack chain of Krylov and Safonov [KS] for the nonnegative solution sup Gamma - Gamma. Consequently, take any sequence of points converging to (a_*, T_*) along which Gamma -> G_0. For every fixed delta and B the two hypotheses cannot both hold infinitely often: either the normalized meridional speed in the annuli is unbounded, or the circulation stays above half its value (and, if the speed stays bounded, tends to G_0) deeper than every fixed fraction of the radius. A bound on the normalized meridional speed on whole cylinders reaching the axis is excluded.
(c) Let beta be in (0, 2) and Gamma_0 in [0, G_0) (this range contains gamma_*), and put f = (Gamma - Gamma_0) r^{-beta}. It satisfies (d_t + b.grad) f = f_rr + f_zz + (2 beta - 1) r^{-1} f_r + beta[(beta - 2) - r u^r] f/r^2. The first-crossing records of f exist and converge to the axis; there Gamma > Gamma_0 and -r u^r >= 2 - beta. Hence a bound -r u^r <= 2 - beta_0 at late times near the axis on {Gamma > Gamma_0}, for some beta_0 > 0, would exclude Case 1; quantitatively it would give Gamma <= Gamma_0 + C r^beta near the axis for beta < beta_0. For the weighted first-crossing argument applied to f = (Gamma - Gamma_0) e^{-w(r)} with a fixed C^2 radial exponent w, w' > 0 (so that e^{-w} decreases), no such w certifies -r u^r >= 2 at all small scales (this does not rule out every use of fixed radial weights); weights depending on time or on z improve the certificate only at places (r^2 >> T_* - t, or one horizontal plane) where the records are not known to occur. The identities d/dt int Gamma phi dx = int Gamma (b.grad phi + Delta_5 phi) dx and dJ_a/dt = -8 pi^2 q(a, t) + 3 pi int Gamma |x - a|^{-5} (-(x - a).b) dx, with J_a = pi int Gamma |x - a|^{-3} dx and a on the axis, leave the radial and axial fluxes of circulation uncontrolled. For fixed rho the scale-rho average is Lipschitz in time by finite energy; what is missing is a variation that vanishes as rho -> 0 over times of order rho^2.

Key estimates. a_* exists: the singular set at T_* is a compact subset of the axis (Proposition 20(a)), and S_n -> infinity while u is bounded near regular points. Put g(rho, delta) := sup{Gamma(x, t) : |x - a_*| < rho, T_* - delta <= t < T_*}, so G_0 = lim g.
(a) For fixed rho, delta, L, the normalized cylinder {|y| <= L} x [-L^2, 0] at the n-th record lies in that region for large n, so Gamma_U <= G' := G_0/gamma_*. Let a := K gamma_* and phi := G' phi_a. Then phi' = G' a^2 R e^{-aR} > 0 and phi'' - phi'/R = -a phi'. Since |V^R| <= K and (d_tau + V.grad) Gamma_U = nu_* L Gamma_U, w := Gamma_U - phi satisfies (d_tau + V.grad - nu_* L) w = -(V^R + K) phi' <= 0. Moreover limsup_{R -> infinity} sup_{Z, tau <= 0} w(R, Z, tau) <= 0, since Gamma_U <= G' and phi -> G', and w <= Gamma_U <= R. Suppose m := sup w > 0. Then near-maximizing points have R in a compact subset of (0, infinity). Take limits of translates in Z and tau, using the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions (||W||_infty <= K_0 at viscosity nu implies ||d_tau^m grad^k W||_infty <= C(k, m, K_0, nu)). This gives a subsolution with an interior maximum m. By [Nir], w = m on {R > 0} x (-infinity, 0] for the limit, which contradicts w <= R near the axis. So 1 = Gamma_U(e_1, 0) <= G' phi_a(1). Equality would give w = 0 at all earlier times, hence V^R = -K for all R > 0, which contradicts V^R = 0 on the axis. The last inequality of (a) is 1/(1 - y) > 1 + y for y = (1 + a) e^{-a} in (0, 1).
(b) Let Omega := {delta r_0/2 < r < 2 r_0, |z - z_0| < r_0} x (t_0 - 2T_0, t_0]. Put Gamma^(R, Z, tau) := Gamma(r_0 R, z_0 + r_0 Z, t_0 + T_0 tau)/gamma and V^ := (r_0/gamma) b. Then d_tau Gamma^ + V^.grad Gamma^ = nu L Gamma^ on Omega^ = {delta/2 < R < 2, |Z| < 1} x (-2, 0], with nu = 1/gamma in [1/G, 1/gamma_0] and |V^| <= B. Let M^ := sup_{Omega^} Gamma^ >= 1. Then w := M^ - Gamma^ >= 0 solves d_tau w - nu (d_RR + d_ZZ) w + (V^R + nu/R) d_R w + V^Z d_Z w = 0. This equation is in nondivergence form with constant diffusion and drift at most B + 2 nu/delta. Apply the [KS] Harnack inequality on cylinders of radius delta/8 in {3 delta/4 <= R <= 3/2, |Z| <= 3/4} x (-2, 0], along a finite chain with increasing times. The chain runs from P_0 = (delta, Z', tau') (|Z'| <= 1/2, tau' in [-3/2, -1/2]) to (1, 0, 0) and gives w(P_0) <= C_H w(1, 0, 0), with C_H = C_H(delta, B, gamma_0, G). As w(P_0) >= M^ - 1/2 >= 1/2, M^ - 1 >= 1/(2 C_H). So c_1 = 1/(2 C_H), which is not explicit.
Escape: take points (x_k, t_k) -> (a_*, T_*) with Gamma(x_k, t_k) -> G_0 (near-maximizers for g(rho_k, delta_k) with rho_k, delta_k -> 0), and put r_k := r(x_k). Suppose both hypotheses held infinitely often. Then the gain with gamma_0 = G_0/2 would give sup_{Omega_k} Gamma >= (1 + c_1) Gamma(x_k, t_k). But Omega_k lies in {|x - a_*| < 4|x_k - a_*|} x [t_k - 2T_k, t_k] with T_k = r_k^2/Gamma(x_k, t_k) -> 0, so sup_{Omega_k} Gamma <= g(4|x_k - a_*|, T_* - t_k + 2T_k) -> G_0, a contradiction. With bounded speed, the same chain gives Gamma^ >= M^_k - C_H (M^_k - 1) at the deeper points, with M^_k -> 1.
Whole cylinders: suppose |b| <= B Gamma(x_k, t_k)/r_k on {r < 2 r_k, |z - z_k| < r_k} x (t_k - 2T_k, t_k]. This gives r|b| <= 2BG there. Put c_* := min{1/4, 1/(2 G^{1/2})}. Since T_k = r_k^2/gamma_k and gamma_k <= G, c_*^2 r_k^2 <= T_k, so the backward parabolic cylinder of radius c_* r_k with top (0, z_k, t_k - T_k) lies in the assumed cylinder. On it r|b| <= 2BG, a scale-invariant bound, so [LZ, Theorem 1.1] with b_3 = b, applied after rescaling by c_* r_k, gives |Gamma(delta r_k, z_k, t_k - T_k)| <= C G (delta/c_*)^alpha for delta < c_*/2, with C and alpha depending only on BG and not on k. A small fixed delta therefore gives the second hypothesis.
(c) The f-equation follows by inserting Gamma = Gamma_0 + r^beta f in (d_t + b.grad) Gamma = L Gamma. Fix t' < T_* and m > 0. On [0, t'], Gamma <= D(t') r^2, since |u^theta|/r <= |grad u|. So f > m forces (m/D(t'))^{1/(2 - beta)} < r < (G/m)^{1/beta} and |u^theta| > m r^{beta - 1}. As u is continuous in time with values in the continuous functions vanishing at infinity, {f > m} x [0, t'] lies in a compact subset of {r > 0}. Hence F(t) := sup_x f(x, t) is attained when F(t) > m and is continuous where F > 0. At circulation records, F(t_k) >= (Gamma(x_k, t_k) - Gamma_0) r_k^{-beta} -> infinity (Gamma_0 < G_0). For m_j increasing to infinity with m_j > F(0), let t_j be the first time with F(t_j) = m_j and x_j a maximizer. There d_t f >= 0, grad f = 0 and f_rr + f_zz <= 0, so -r_j u^r >= 2 - beta + r_j^2 (-(f_rr + f_zz))/(beta f) >= 2 - beta. Also Gamma > Gamma_0 and r_j^beta <= G/m_j -> 0.
Quantitative form: suppose -r u^r <= 2 - beta_0 on {r < rho, t' <= t < T_*, Gamma > Gamma_0} and 0 < beta < beta_0. Then Gamma <= Gamma_0 + C r^beta on {r < rho} x [t', T_*), with C := max{F(t'), (G - Gamma_0)_+ rho^{-beta}}. Indeed, a first crossing above C would occur at r < rho, where the zero-order coefficient beta[(beta - 2) - r u^r]/r^2 is negative. This gives G_0 <= Gamma_0, a contradiction.
Certificate: for f = (Gamma - Gamma_0) e^{-w(r)}, (d_t + b.grad) f = f_rr + f_zz + (2w' - 1/r) f_r + [w'' + w'^2 - w'/r - u^r w'] f. A first-crossing record gives -r u^r >= 1 - r w''/w' - r w' = 2 - W_ss/W_s - W_s, where W(s) := w(e^s); this lower bound is the certificate (it equals 2 - beta for w = beta log r). Certifying 2 at all small scales requires W_ss <= -W_s^2 and W_s > 0 on some (-infinity, s_1). Then (1/W_s)_s >= 1, and 1/W_s(s) <= 1/W_s(s_1) - (s_1 - s) < 0 for s < s_1 - 1/W_s(s_1), which is impossible. For w = beta log(r/(T_* - t)^{1/2}) the certificate is 2 - beta + r^2/(2(T_* - t)), but unbounded records of this weight force r_j^2/(T_* - t_j) -> 0. For w = beta log r - a(z - z_0)^2/(r^2 + (z - z_0)^2) it is 2 - beta + 2a/beta on the plane z = z_0 (above 2 if a > beta^2/2). However, nothing places records on that plane, and off it the term u^z w_z has no sign.
Fluxes: the first identity follows from (d_t + b.grad) Gamma = L Gamma, div b = 0 and the adjoint relation; the axis term vanishes because Gamma(0, z) = 0. Let A_rho(t) := rho^{-3} int Gamma(x, t) phi((x - a_*)/rho) dx with supp phi in B_2. The identity gives |A_rho(t_2) - A_rho(t_1)| <= G (t_2 - t_1) rho^{-2} [||Delta_5 phi||_{L^1} + ||grad phi||_{L^2} sup_t (rho^{-1} int_{B_{2 rho}(a_*)} |b|^2)^{1/2}]. With int |b|^2 <= E_0, A_rho is Lipschitz with constant G [rho^{-2} ||Delta_5 phi||_{L^1} + E_0^{1/2} rho^{-5/2} ||grad phi||_{L^2}]. Over t_2 - t_1 <= rho^2 the variation is O(G) under a scale-invariant meridional energy bound, and that bound fails on intermediate scales at the record tops (Proposition 20(e)). For J_a, write J_a = int_{R^5} q |X - a|^{-3} dX (X = (X', z), |X'| = r) and test with pi(|x - a|^2 + eps^2)^{-3/2}. Use Delta_5 (|X|^2 + eps^2)^{-3/2} = -15 eps^2 (|X|^2 + eps^2)^{-7/2}, an approximate identity of total mass 8 pi^2 on R^5, and let eps -> 0. Under |b| <= C|x - a|^{-1}, the part of the second term from {|x - a| > rho} is at most 6 pi^2 G C rho^{-2}. Its time integral diverges logarithmically for rho of order (T_* - t)^{1/2}.

The numerical value of the Harnack constant C_H is not computed; the compactness in (a) is the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions quoted there.

Status: proof with key estimates; source: own derivation (strictness in (a), the range Gamma_0 < G_0 and the quantitative form in (c): supplied by the round-10 audit and checked by the author; the exclusion in (b) of meridional bounds on cylinders reaching the axis: supplied by the round-10 audit, with the cylinder and the uniformity of its constants written out after adversarial audit round 22); verification: adversarial audit rounds 10, 21, 22; checked by scripts

Proposition 22 (localized pressure and strain). Use the Frobenius norm, write w = u^theta, and let A_n = -d_rr w, B_n = -d_zz w at the n-th swirl record (both nonnegative).
(a) The pressure source is -Delta p = (d_r u^r)^2 + (u^r/r)^2 + (d_z u^z)^2 + 2 d_z u^r d_r u^z - 2 w d_r w/r, with swirl part -div((w^2/r) e_r). On the axis Delta p = 2c^2 - (3/2) sigma^2, where c = d_r w and sigma = d_z u^z there. The swirl part p_sw of the canonical pressure satisfies p_sw(0, z, t) = -(1/(4 pi)) int w(y, t)^2 |y - z e_3|^{-3} dy, so -G D(t) <= p_sw <= 0 on the axis for 0 < t < T_*. With spherical principal values its axial curvature is d_zz p_sw(0, z, t) = (2/5) c^2 - (1/2) PV int int w^2 k_zz dz' dr, k_zz = 3r(4 zeta^2 - r^2)(r^2 + zeta^2)^{-7/2}, zeta = z' - z, w = w(r, z', t); it has no sign.
(b) At the record: -u^r >= (1/r_n)(1 + r_n^2 (A_n + B_n)/S_n); the material derivative of Gamma equals -r_n(A_n + B_n) - S_n/r_n <= -gamma_n/r_n^2; the area-weighted mean of d_z u^z over the disk {r < r_n, z = z_n} is at least 2/r_n^2; with beta_n := -r_n u^r at the record, D(t_n)^2 >= 6 beta_n^2/r_n^4 + 2 S_n^2/r_n^2, i.e. d_n^2 >= 2 + 6 beta_n^2/gamma_n^2 (in particular d_n >= sqrt6/gamma_n). The material angular velocity q = w/r increases at the record, and Delta p <= (omega^theta)^2/2 - 3 beta_n^2/(2 r_n^4) there. The radial momentum balance gives |A - S_n^2/r_n| <= Lambda r_n + sqrt3 H(t_n) at the record, with A here (local notation) the meridional material derivative d_t u^r + b.grad u^r of u^r and Lambda := C_0(1 + log_+ C_*) D(t_n)^2 the pressure-Hessian bound of Theorem 7. It is two-sided and fixes no sign of d_t u^r.
(c) Along axial trajectories, d sigma/dt = -sigma^2 - p_zz + nu d_z(Delta u^z) and d sigma/dt = sigma^2/2 + 2(p_rr - c^2) + (a viscous term). In an ancient limit, long-time averages along an axial trajectory (common subsequence) satisfy <p_zz> = -<sigma^2> + nu <d_z(Delta u^z)>. These are trajectory averages, not instantaneous signs.
(d) The Burgers vortex with viscosity nu, normalized so that its swirl maximum is 1 at R = 1, satisfies the displayed local differential identities of (a)-(c) and the weak terminal-maximum conditions (U^theta <= 1 on the past, with equality at the record point) at every time, with bounded circulation and derivatives. It is an exact local model, not a solution satisfying the global hypotheses used for the canonical-pressure statements. With k = 1.2564312... the positive root of e^k = 1 + 2k: -U^R(1) = 2 k nu = 2.5128624... nu, the circulation supremum is 1.3979525... < 1.398, and |U| <= K on the ellipsoid alpha^2 (R^2/4 + Z^2) <= K^2 - 1, alpha = 4 k nu = 5.0257248... nu, which contains the cylinder {R, |Z| <= (2 sqrt5 k)^{-1} (K^2 - 1)^{1/2} gamma_*} centred on the axis ((2 sqrt5 k)^{-1} = 0.1779698...). It matches the Case-1 normalization only for compatible constants (for example the ellipsoid contains the record point only if K^2 - 1 >= (2 k nu)^2), and its maximum is stationary, not a sequence of first crossings of increasing levels. Its stretching is sustained by the meridional stagnation pressure -alpha^2 Z^2/2. It is not admissible for Case 1: besides the global velocity bound it fails finite energy, the tail condition and the BMO pressure control available for bounded mild limits.

Key estimates. Notation: w = u^theta, b = (u^r, u^z), viscosity nu (1 for the physical solution, nu_* in a limit). At the record w = S_n is the maximum of |w| over R^3 x [0, t_n], so w_r = w_z = 0, A_n, B_n >= 0 and d_t w >= 0; put beta_n := -r_n u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) (>= 1, Theorem 1), k^curv_n := r_n^2 (A_n + B_n)/S_n, d_n = r_n D(t_n)/S_n (Proposition 8).
(a) -Delta p = tr((grad u)^2) in the frame (e_r, e_theta, e_z); the swirl term is -2 w w_r/r = -div(F e_r), F := w^2/r, and the meridional part is |S_b|^2 - (omega^theta)^2/2, S_b the symmetric meridional gradient. On the axis (u^r = a r + O(r^3), w = c r + O(r^3), sigma = -2a) the gradient is [[a, -c, 0], [c, a, 0], [0, 0, -2a]], with squared trace 6a^2 - 2c^2 = -(2 p_rr + p_zz). For 0 < t < T_*, F <= M D (|w/r| <= D, -w/r being a Cartesian gradient entry) and int F dx <= ||w||_2 ||w/r||_2 <= ||w||_2 ||grad u||_2, so F e_r is in L^1 and L^infty; the canonical pressure is the Newtonian potential of its source (as for Theorem 7), and p_sw := N * (-div(F e_r)), N = 1/(4 pi |x|). One integration by parts with (grad N)(z e_3 - y).e_r(y) = r(y)/(4 pi |y - z e_3|^3) gives the axis formula, i.e. p_sw(0, z) = -(1/2) int int w^2 r (r^2 + zeta^2)^{-3/2} dz' dr. With m(r) := sup_z |w(r, z, t)| <= min(D r, G/r) and int r (r^2 + zeta^2)^{-3/2} d zeta = 2/r, |p_sw| <= int_0^infty m^2 r^{-1} dr <= int_0^rho D^2 r dr + int_rho^infty G^2 r^{-3} dr = G D, rho^2 = G/D. Curvature: k_zz = d_z^2 [r (r^2 + zeta^2)^{-3/2}]. As w(r, z + zeta)^2 = c^2 r^2 + O((r^2 + zeta^2)^{3/2}) and int_{-1}^1 (1 - mu^2)(5 mu^2 - 1) d mu = 0, k_zz has zero mean against r^2 on spheres, so the spherical principal value exists. The local term comes from the model c(z)^2 r^2 e^{-(r^2 + zeta^2)}: for w = r e^{-(r^2 + z^2)/2} (c = 1) the principal value vanishes on every shell, while moving the axial derivatives onto w^2 gives d_zz p_sw(0, 0) = -(1/(4 pi)) int (4 z'^2 - 2) r^2 e^{-|y|^2} |y|^{-3} dy = 2/5 (the integrals are 4 pi/15 and 4 pi/3); the remainder is O(|y - z e_3|^3), its truncation boundary terms vanish, and it gives no local term. (Cylindrical truncation r > eps, axial integral first, gives the formula without 2c^2/5.) No sign: k_zz < 0 for |zeta| < r/2 and k_zz > 0 for |zeta| > r/2; with phi >= 0 a smooth bump on (-1, 1) of maximum 1 and 0 < delta < 1/10, w = phi((r - 1)/eps) phi(z/eps) + (1/2) phi((r - 1)/delta) phi((z - 2)/delta) has c = 0, a positive contribution tending to 0 with eps and a fixed negative one, so d_zz p_sw(0, 0) < 0 for small eps while w(1, 0) = 1 is the maximum of w (an instantaneous configuration).
(b) The swirl equation d_t w = Delta w - w/r^2 - u^r w/r with Delta w = -(A_n + B_n) gives beta_n = 1 + k^curv_n + r_n^2 d_t w/S_n >= 1 + k^curv_n. From Gamma_r = S_n, Gamma_z = 0, Gamma_rr = -r_n A_n, Gamma_zz = -r_n B_n, (d_t + b.grad) Gamma = L Gamma = -r_n (A_n + B_n) - S_n/r_n = -(gamma_n/r_n^2)(1 + k^curv_n). Integrating d_r(r u^r) + r d_z u^z = 0 over [0, r_n] gives int_0^{r_n} s d_z u^z(s, z_n, t_n) ds = -r_n u^r = beta_n, so the disk mean is 2 beta_n/r_n^2. Gradient: |grad u|^2 = |grad b|^2 + w_r^2 + w_z^2 + w^2/r^2, |grad b|^2 >= (3/2)(d_z u^z)^2 (grad b is trace free), Jensen gives int_0^{r_n} s (d_z u^z)^2 ds >= 2 beta_n^2/r_n^2, and int_0^{r_n} (w_s^2 + w^2/s^2) s ds = w(r_n)^2 + int_0^{r_n} s (w_s - w/s)^2 ds >= S_n^2; integrating D(t_n)^2 >= |grad u|^2 against s ds over [0, r_n] gives D(t_n)^2 >= 6 beta_n^2/r_n^4 + 2 S_n^2/r_n^2. As q = Gamma/r^2, D_t q = r_n^{-2} L Gamma + 2 beta_n S_n/r_n^3 = (S_n/r_n^3)(2 beta_n - 1 - k^curv_n) >= (S_n/r_n^3)(1 + k^curv_n). As w_r = 0 the swirl source vanishes, and minimizing the trace-free diagonal of S_b with u^r/r = -beta_n/r_n^2 fixed gives |S_b|^2 >= 3 beta_n^2/(2 r_n^4), hence the Delta p bound. Radial momentum: with A := d_t u^r + u^r d_r u^r + u^z d_z u^r, A - w^2/r = -p_r + (Delta - r^{-2}) u^r; p_r = 0 on the axis, |p_rr| <= Lambda (Theorem 7 under (A), t_n late) and |(Delta - r^{-2}) u^r| = |(Delta u).e_r| <= sqrt3 H(t_n), so |A - S_n^2/r_n| <= Lambda r_n + sqrt3 H(t_n) at the record.
(c) Differentiating the axial momentum equation on the axis in z and dividing the radial one by r at r = 0 give, along axial trajectories, d sigma/dt = -sigma^2 - p_zz + nu d_z(Delta u^z) and da/dt = -a^2 + c^2 - p_rr + nu Q, Q := [r^{-1}(Delta - r^{-2}) u^r]_{r=0}. Incompressibility gives d_z(Delta u^z)(0, z, t) = -2Q, so with sigma = -2a, d sigma/dt = sigma^2/2 + 2(p_rr - c^2) - 2 nu Q: the viscous term of (c) is -2 nu Q = nu d_z(Delta u^z)(0, z, t). In an ancient limit sigma is bounded and axial trajectories exist for all tau <= 0 (invariant axis, |dz/dtau| <= K); averaging over [-S, 0], (sigma(0) - sigma(-S))/S -> 0 gives the identity along a common subsequence S_j -> infinity, and <p_rr - c^2> = -<sigma^2>/4 + nu <Q>.
(d) With alpha = 4 k nu and Gamma_inf := 1/(1 - e^{-k}) = (1 + 2k)/(2k) = 1.3979525..., the field is U^R = -alpha R/2, U^Z = alpha Z, Gamma_U = Gamma_inf (1 - e^{-k R^2}) (k R^2 = alpha R^2/(4 nu)), pressure P = -alpha^2 (R^2/8 + Z^2/2) - int_R^infty (U^theta)^2 s^{-1} ds. It is a smooth steady solution: the meridional field is a divergence-free linear strain (zero Laplacian), U^R d_R Gamma_U = nu L Gamma_U = -4 nu k^2 R^2 Gamma_inf e^{-k R^2}, and P balances both meridional momentum equations. U^theta = Gamma_U/R is maximal on R > 0 where 2k R^2 e^{-kR^2} = 1 - e^{-kR^2}, i.e. only at R = 1 (e^k = 1 + 2k), with value 1, and d_tau U^theta = 0; -U^R(1) = 2 k nu = nu(1 + a + b) with a = -U^theta_RR(1) = 2k - 1, b = 0 (equality in the rescaled inflow bound of (b)), and the disk strain is alpha. On the axis c = Gamma_inf k = k + 1/2, p_zz = -alpha^2, p_rr - c^2 = -alpha^2/4 (steady balances, zero viscous terms). As |U|^2 = alpha^2 (R^2/4 + Z^2) + (U^theta)^2 and 0 <= U^theta <= 1, |U| <= K on the ellipsoid, which contains {R, |Z| <= L_B} for (5/4) alpha^2 L_B^2 = K^2 - 1, i.e. L_B = (2 sqrt5 k)^{-1} (K^2 - 1)^{1/2} gamma_* when nu = 1/gamma_*, and contains (1, 0) iff (2 k nu)^2 <= K^2 - 1. Since |U|^2 >= alpha^2 |y|^2/4, the energy and int |U|^2 |y|^{-4} dy are infinite.

Boundary terms in (a): fix the evaluation point z e_3, put rho := |y - z e_3| and subtract the model c(z)^2 r^2 e^{-rho^2} from w^2. The remainder h satisfies h = O(rho^3) and grad h = O(rho^2), so the boundary terms of the spherical integrations by parts are O(eps) and the differentiated remainder integral is locally absolutely integrable; the Gaussian calculation therefore gives the universal local term 2c^2/5. The identification of the canonical pressure with the Newtonian potential of its source is the argument given for Theorem 7.

Status: proof with key estimates (routine steps indicated, not written out in full); source: own derivation (parts of (a), (b) also in research answer res7, checked by the author; the local term 2c^2/5 and the bound d_n^2 >= 2 + 6 beta_n^2/gamma_n^2 supplied in adversarial audit round 11 and checked by the author); verification: adversarial audit rounds 11, 21, 22; checked by scripts

Proposition 23 (radial saturation and a far-field inflow threshold). Let U be a Case-1 limit, nu = 1/gamma_*, A := sup Gamma_U, phi_a(R) = 1 - (1 + aR) e^{-aR} with a = K gamma_*, and for L > 0 put rho := min{A/(8K), 1/8}, B_L := K + nu/rho + L + 2 rho, lambda_L := 8 nu/rho^2 + 4 B_L/rho + B_L^2/(2 nu).
(a) 1/phi_a(1) < A <= G_0/gamma_*; sup{Gamma_U : R <= L} <= A - (A/2) e^{-lambda_L}; and sup{Gamma_U : R >= R_0} = A for every R_0. Saturation rules out uniform decay at radial infinity. Consequently Gamma_U is not in L^infinity_tau L^p_x for any p < infinity and does not tend to 0 as R -> infinity, so the hypotheses of the Liouville theorems [LZZ, Theorem 1.3 and Remark 1.4] fail for every Case-1 limit; the rate hypothesis of [LRZ2, Theorem 1.2] fails as well, by their theorem applied to the nonconstant limit (saturation alone does not contradict it). These integrability and quantitative-tail exclusions use the cited Liouville results and the nonzero terminal swirl. Qualitative radial saturation is already in [LRZ2] and in Proposition 6; the new content is the explicit deficit. The proof of the deficit is a comparison for A - Gamma_U with the subsolution (A/2) e^{-lambda_L (s - s_0)} (1 - |xi|^2/rho^2)^2 on a disk of radius rho moving from near the axis to the target point in unit time.
(b) Let U be any smooth bounded ancient axisymmetric field on R^3 x (-infinity, 0] with |U| <= K whose circulation solves d_tau Gamma_U + U^b.grad Gamma_U = nu L Gamma_U with nu > 0, and suppose A := sup Gamma_U lies in (0, infinity). For every R_0 > 0, with m := (A/2) e^{-lambda_{R_0}}, where rho, B_{R_0} and lambda_{R_0} are given by the formulas above with this K, nu and A (rho = min{A/(8K), 1/8}, B_{R_0} = K + nu/rho + R_0 + 2 rho, lambda_{R_0} = 8 nu/rho^2 + 4 B_{R_0}/rho + B_{R_0}^2/(2 nu)), there is a point with R > R_0, Gamma_U > A - m and R U^R < -2 nu. The constant 2 nu is critical for the exterior comparison used in the proof: for beta > 2 nu the exterior line sink u^r = -beta/r, u^z = 0, Gamma = A(1 - (R_0/r)^{beta/nu - 2}), p_r = (Gamma^2 + beta^2)/r^3 is an exact steady solution on r > R_0 with Gamma < A and Gamma -> A. It is singular on the axis, so optimality of 2 nu among globally smooth bounded ancient solutions is not established. More generally, the conclusion holds with -U^R > b(R) in place of R U^R < -2 nu for every b in C^1 with divergent Feller integral int^infinity R exp(-(1/nu) int^R b) dR, for example b = nu (2 + 1/log R)/R with R_0 >= e. The Liouville consequence (a bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with bounded circulation and R U^R >= -2 nu on all of {R >= R_0} is a constant multiple of e_3) is the case b = 2 nu/R of Theorem 5 and is not new; the new content of (b) is that the inflow bound is needed only on the near-maximal set {R > R_0, Gamma_U > A - m}. The proof compares A - Gamma_U on the near-maximal set with m Psi, where Psi solves the two-dimensional radial heat equation on an annulus R_0 < R < R_1 with boundary value 1 at R_0 (switched on smoothly) and 0 at R_1; Psi_R <= 0, so the inflow condition R U^R >= -2 nu makes m Psi a subsolution, a barrier eps e^{M tau} cosh Z handles |Z| -> infinity, and letting the initial time tend to -infinity and then R_1 -> infinity gives A - Gamma_U >= m everywhere. In probabilistic terms the backward radial motion under inflow beta/R is a Bessel process of dimension beta/nu, which hits each inner radius almost surely exactly when beta <= 2 nu (the proof does not use this).
(c) (physical form) In Case 1, along the subsequence defining U, for every R_0 >= 1 there are L and n_0 such that for every index n >= n_0 of that subsequence there is a point (x, t) with R_0 r_n < r(x) <= L r_n, |z - z_n| <= L r_n, t_n - L^2 r_n^2/gamma_n <= t <= t_n, Gamma(x, t) > gamma_n (A - m) and -r u^r(x, t) > 2. Hence the following would exclude Case 1: for some fixed R_0 and every finite L, the bound -r u^r <= 2 (or -r u^r <= 2 + 1/log(r/r_n) when R_0 >= e) holds, for all sufficiently large indices of that subsequence, on the intersection of the windows with {Gamma > gamma_n (A - m)}. (On the whole windows this is Theorem 5 in physical form.)
(d) If u^r >= -c/sqrt(T_* - t) holds as a space-time bound for the physical solution (the hypothesis of Q. S. Zhang's criterion), the records satisfy T_* - t_n <= c^2 r_n^2.
(e) Where 0 <= A - Gamma_U <= m at radius R, |grad Gamma_U|^2 <= 4 m (R h + 2 d), where d and h bound the first and second derivatives of U on the meridional ball of radius R/2 around the point. So the source of the meridional vorticity equation is at most 2 A (4 m (R h + 2 d))^{1/2}/R^3 on the near-maximal set. The estimate bounds the instantaneous local swirl source. It does not locate the vorticity producing the inflow, because existing local vorticity can reflect earlier creation and transport.
(f) (instantaneous supremum) Put a(tau) := sup_x Gamma_U(x, tau) and B(tau) := lim_{rho -> infinity} sup{Gamma_U(x, tau) : R(x) >= rho}. Then a is nonincreasing, a(0) >= 1, a(tau) -> A as tau -> -infinity, B(tau) = B does not depend on tau, and a >= B. Either B = A, and then a(tau) = A for every tau <= 0, or B < A, and then a(tau) < A for every tau <= 0, so every sequence of points along which Gamma_U tends to A has tau -> -infinity. Neither alternative is excluded here. Moreover, for each fixed i, j >= 0 with i + j >= 1, d_R^i d_Z^j Gamma_U, and also d_tau Gamma_U, tend to 0 as R -> infinity, uniformly in Z and tau <= 0; no rate and no bound uniform in the order is asserted, and Gamma_U itself does not tend to 0 uniformly in Z and tau <= 0 (by (a), sup{Gamma_U(x, tau) : R(x) >= rho, tau <= 0} = A for every rho); B > 0 and decay on a fixed time slice are neither asserted nor excluded. By (a), gamma_* < G_0 phi_{K G_0}(1).
(g) (refinements of (f)) Let a, B be as in (f) and omega_t(rho) := sup{|d_tau Gamma_U(x, tau)| : R(x) >= rho, tau <= 0}, which tends to 0 by (f). (i) a(0) > 1: at (e_1, 0) the swirl U^theta has a spatial maximum, so d_R U^theta = 0 and d_R Gamma_U = U^theta + R d_R U^theta = 1 there. (ii) (radius and deficit) If 0 < m < A and A - Gamma_U(x, tau) <= m, then K gamma_* R(x) >= X(m/A), where, for 0 < y < 1, X(y) is the positive root of (1 + X) e^{-X} = y; X(y) = log(1/y) + log log(1/y) + o(1) as y -> 0 (write X - log(1 + X) = log(1/y)). (iii) (strict decrease above B) If s < tau <= 0 and a(tau) > B, then a(s) > a(tau). (iv) (implicit bound on backward escape) If B < A, tau_0 <= 0 and points x(tau) satisfy Gamma_U(x(tau), tau) >= a(tau) - eps(tau) with eps(tau) -> 0 as tau -> -infinity, then for tau < tau_0, (tau_0 - tau) omega_t(R(x(tau))) >= a(tau) - a(tau_0) - eps(tau), whose right side tends to d_0 := A - a(tau_0) > 0. So, with the generalized inverse calR(q) := sup({0} union {rho >= 0 : omega_t(rho) >= q}) (finite for q > 0, since omega_t(rho) -> 0), R(x(tau)) <= calR(d_0/(2(tau_0 - tau))) for very negative tau; a rate omega_t(R) <= C R^{-p}, which is not proved, would give R(x(tau)) <= (2 C (tau_0 - tau)/d_0)^{1/p}. By (ii) these radii also tend to infinity; the two bounds are compatible. (v) a is Lipschitz, and ess sup{|a'(tau)| : tau <= -T} -> 0 as T -> infinity. (vi) (Case 1) For each tau, a(tau) <= liminf_n sup_y Gamma_{U_n}(y, tau) = G_+/gamma_*, where G_+ := lim_{t -> T_*} sup_x Gamma(x, t) exists by the maximum principle; hence A <= G_+/gamma_*. Local convergence proves only this inequality, and equality is not established. Equality is compatible with escape of the near-maximizers of Gamma_{U_n}(., tau), which need not be radial (as a test of the limit operation, not a Navier-Stokes example: with chi smooth, 0 <= chi <= 1, equal to 1 on [0, 1] and to 0 on [2, infinity), the functions chi(|y|/n) 2R^2/(1 + R^2) converge locally smoothly to 2R^2/(1 + R^2), and their suprema tend to its supremum 2, which is approached only as R -> infinity), while near-maximizers in a fixed compact set are impossible here (a limit point y would give Gamma_U(y, tau) = G_+/gamma_* >= A, an attained space-time maximum, excluded as in (f)). If equality holds at one time tau, then A <= G_+/gamma_* = a(tau) <= A, so by (f) B = A, a = A at every time and A = G_+/gamma_*. Neither (i)-(vi) nor (f) selects or excludes a branch.
The constant lambda_L is explicit but very large (lambda_1 = 120143521/100352 = 1197.22... for K = 2, gamma_* = 1 and the admissible value A = 1.75; (a) requires A > 1.6835 for these K, gamma_*), so the explicit deficit is extremely small for these parameters. Part (b) localizes Theorem 5 to the near-maximal set; we did not find the localized form in the literature we checked, it is a short consequence of (a) and two-dimensional recurrence, and no novelty is claimed. Their quantitative hypotheses differ: the one-sided criterion controls radial inflow with a specified coefficient, whereas the theorem of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak controls the whole velocity by C/r. No comparison of the estimates is established here.

Proof. Throughout, Gamma_U <= K R (|U^theta| <= |U| <= K), and v := A - Gamma_U >= 0 solves v_tau - nu(v_RR + v_ZZ) + (U^b + (nu/R) e_R).grad v = 0.
(a) The comparison proving the upper bound of Proposition 6 uses only an upper bound G' for Gamma_U and Gamma_U <= R; with G' = A it gives Gamma_U <= A phi_a(R), strictly at (e_1, 0) by the strong maximum principle (U^R = 0 on the axis), so 1 = Gamma_U(e_1, 0) < A phi_a(1); A <= G_0/gamma_* is Proposition 21(a). Deficit: fix a target (R_t, Z_t, tau_t) with R_t <= L, tau_t <= 0, and put s_0 := tau_t - 1. If Gamma_U <= A/2 there, v >= A/2. Otherwise R_t > A/(2K) >= 4 rho. Let x_c(s) := (2 rho + (R_t - 2 rho)(s - s_0), Z_t), s in [s_0, tau_t], and xi := (R, Z) - x_c(s). On the moving disk |xi| < rho, R >= rho, so the relative drift U^b + (nu/R) e_R - x_c'(s) has size at most K + nu/rho + L <= B_L. Put p(xi) := (1 - |xi|^2/rho^2)^2 and psi := (A/2) e^{-lambda_L (s - s_0)} p. With ell := |xi|^2/rho^2, Delta p = (16 ell - 8)/rho^2 and |grad p| = 4 ell^{1/2}(1 - ell)/rho, so (-nu Delta p + B_L |grad p|)/p <= -8 nu/(rho^2 (1 - ell)^2) + (16 nu/rho^2 + 4 B_L/rho)/(1 - ell) <= lambda_L (maximize -a_1 x^2 + a_2 x over x = 1/(1 - ell): the maximum is a_2^2/(4 a_1)); hence psi is a subsolution on the disk. At s = s_0 the disk lies in R <= 3 rho, where Gamma_U <= 3 K rho <= 3A/8, so v >= 5A/8 >= psi; on |xi| = rho, psi = 0 <= v. Comparison on the fixed disk in the variable xi (bounded coefficients, uniform ellipticity) gives v(R_t, Z_t, tau_t) >= (A/2) e^{-lambda_L}. With L = R_0, sup{Gamma_U : R <= R_0} < A, so sup{Gamma_U : R >= R_0} = A. If Gamma_U -> A along (R_k, Z_k, tau_k) (so R_k -> infinity), the translated-limit argument of Proposition 6 (after the rescaling of Theorem 3) gives Gamma_U(R_k + x_1, Z_k + x_3, tau_k + s) -> A locally uniformly, so Gamma_U >= A/2 on a solid torus of volume 2 pi^2 R_k at every time in [tau_k - 1, tau_k] for large k, and int |Gamma_U|^p dx >= (A/2)^p 2 pi^2 R_k -> infinity. As U^theta(e_1, 0) = 1, U is not a constant multiple of e_3, so the hypotheses of [LZZ, Theorem 1.3, Remark 1.4] fail and [LRZ2, Theorem 1.2] shows that its rate condition fails. Sample: K = 2, gamma_* = 1, A = 1.75 gives rho = 7/64, lambda_1 = 1197.2 and lambda_10 = 1678, so (A/2) e^{-lambda_L} < 10^{-520} A.
(b) The constants are those of the header formed with the K, nu and A of the given field: rho = min{A/(8K), 1/8}, B_{R_0} = K + nu/rho + R_0 + 2 rho, lambda_{R_0} = 8 nu/rho^2 + 4 B_{R_0}/rho + B_{R_0}^2/(2 nu). The deficit argument of (a) uses only the scalar equation, |U| <= K and Gamma_U <= K R, so v >= m on {R <= R_0}. Suppose U^R >= -2 nu/R on D_* := {R > R_0, v < m}; with calL := d_tau + U^R d_R + U^Z d_Z - nu L, calL v = 0. Step 1. For R_1 > R_0 and tau_1 < 0, let h be smooth and nondecreasing, h = 0 on (-infinity, 0], h = 1 on [1, infinity), and let Psi solve Psi_t = Psi_RR + Psi_R/R on (R_0, R_1), Psi(., 0) = 0, Psi(R_0, t) = h(t), Psi(R_1, t) = 0. The maximum principle gives 0 <= Psi <= h, so Psi_R <= 0 at both ends; Psi_R solves f_t = f_RR + f_R/R - f/R^2 with zero initial data, so Psi_R <= 0. For t >= 1, Psi - Psi_inf, Psi_inf := log(R_1/R)/log(R_1/R_0), solves the homogeneous Dirichlet problem on the annulus and tends to 0 uniformly. Step 2. phi := m Psi(R, nu(tau - tau_1)) satisfies calL phi = (U^R + 2 nu/R) phi_R <= 0 where U^R >= -2 nu/R. Step 3. On D' := D_* intersected with {R < R_1, tau_1 < tau <= 0}, let M := K + nu + 1 and chi := phi - v - eps e^{M(tau - tau_1)} cosh Z. Then calL chi <= -eps e^{M(tau - tau_1)} cosh Z (M + U^Z tanh Z - nu) < 0, chi -> -infinity as |Z| -> infinity, and chi < 0 on the parabolic boundary: phi = 0 <= v at tau = tau_1 and at R = R_1, phi <= m = v where v = m, and boundary points of D' with R = R_0 have v = m (v <= m by continuity, v >= m by the deficit). At a positive maximum d_tau chi >= 0, grad chi = 0 and d_RR chi, d_ZZ chi <= 0, so calL chi >= 0, a contradiction. Letting eps -> 0, v >= phi on D', and v >= m >= phi elsewhere in (R_0, R_1) x R x (tau_1, 0]. Step 4. Let first tau_1 -> -infinity (v >= m Psi_inf), then R_1 -> infinity: v >= m on {R > R_0}, so sup Gamma_U <= A - m < A, a contradiction. Feller form: take Psi_t = Psi_RR + c(R) Psi_R, c := b/nu - 1/R, so calL phi = (b + U^R) phi_R; Psi_R solves f_t = f_RR + c f_R + c' f, and the substitution f = e^{lambda t} f~ with lambda > sup c' gives Psi_R <= 0 on the bounded interval; the steady state is (sigma(R_1) - sigma(R))/(sigma(R_1) - sigma(R_0)), sigma' = R exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_0}^R b), and sigma(R_1) -> infinity by divergence of the Feller integral (for b = nu(2 + 1/log R)/R, sigma' = R_0^2 log R_0/(R log R)). The line sink is checked by substitution ((Delta - r^{-2}) u^r = 0).
(c) By (b) for U (nu = nu_*) there is (y_0, tau_0), R(y_0) > R_0, with Gamma_U > A - m and -R U^R > 2 nu_*. By locally uniform C^1 convergence (Theorem 2) and gamma_n -> gamma_*, the strict inequalities give, for large n, Gamma_{U_n}(y_0, tau_0) > A - m and -R U_n^R(y_0, tau_0) > 2/gamma_n; at the physical point Gamma = gamma_n Gamma_{U_n} > gamma_n (A - m) and -r u^r = gamma_n (-R U_n^R) > 2. Any L > max{|y_0|, |tau_0|^{1/2}, R_0} puts it in the window (t = t_n + r_n^2 tau_0/gamma_n). The logarithmic version follows in the same way from the Feller form with b = nu_*(2 + 1/log R)/R, since log(r/r_n) = log R.
(d) At the record 1/r_n <= -u^r <= c (T_* - t_n)^{-1/2} (Theorem 1), hence T_* - t_n <= c^2 r_n^2.
(e) Put H' := 2(R h + 2 d). Since -U^theta/R is an entry of the Cartesian gradient, |U^theta| <= R d, so g := |grad Gamma_U| <= |U^theta| + R |grad U^theta| <= 2 R d and g/H' <= R/2. On the meridional segment of length g/H' from the point in the direction of grad Gamma_U, R' <= 3R/2 and D^2 Gamma_U = R' D^2 U^theta + e_R (x) grad U^theta + grad U^theta (x) e_R has norm at most R' h + 2 d <= H'. Taylor's formula for v >= 0 at the end of the segment gives 0 <= v - g^2/H' + g^2/(2H'), so g^2 <= 2 H' v <= 2 H' m = 4 m (R h + 2 d). Since m < A/2, 0 < Gamma_U <= A there and |d_Z (U^theta)^2/R| = 2 |Gamma_U d_Z Gamma_U|/R^3 gives the source bound.
(f) For s < tau_1 <= 0 let H := e^{c(tau - s)}(1 + R^2 + Z^2) with c := K + 2 nu + 1. Since L(1 + R^2 + Z^2) = 2 and |U| <= K, (d_tau + U^b.grad - nu L) H >= e^{c(tau - s)}((c - K)(1 + R^2 + Z^2) - 2 nu) > 0. For eps > 0, Gamma_U - a(s) - eps H is negative for large |x| and at the axis, and is at most 0 at tau = s, so a positive maximum on [s, tau_1] would be attained at a point with R > 0, where the operator gives a contradiction; letting eps -> 0 gives a(tau_1) <= a(s). Then a(tau) -> sup_tau a(tau) = A, and a(0) >= Gamma_U(e_1, 0) = 1. Derivatives at radial infinity: on unit cylinders in R >= 2 the circulation solves a parabolic equation in (R, Z) with smooth coefficients bounded with their derivatives (the drift U^b + (nu/R) e_R), so interior estimates bound all its derivatives by C G'. Translates Gamma_U(R_k + x_1, Z_k + x_3, tau_k + s) with R_k -> infinity have subsequences converging with their derivatives; by Proposition 6 the translated meridional velocities converge to a constant c, so the limit is a bounded ancient solution of d_s g + c.grad g = nu Delta g in the plane, hence constant (after removing the drift, the heat kernel gives |grad g| <= C G'/(nu T)^{1/2} for every T). So, for each fixed order at least one, the derivatives of Gamma_U, and d_tau Gamma_U = nu L Gamma_U - U^b.grad Gamma_U, tend to 0 uniformly (a sequence violating this would contradict the smooth convergence of the translates to a constant). For tau, tau' <= 0, |sup_{R >= rho} Gamma_U(., tau) - sup_{R >= rho} Gamma_U(., tau')| <= |tau - tau'| sup{|d_tau Gamma_U| : R >= rho}, which tends to 0 as rho -> infinity, so B does not depend on tau; clearly a >= B. If a(tau_0) = A, take x_j with Gamma_U(x_j, tau_0) -> A. If R(x_j) were bounded along a subsequence, then, since |Gamma_U| <= ||grad U||_infty R^2 bounds R(x_j) below, the axial translates of U by z(x_j) would converge (compactness as in Proposition 6) to a solution whose circulation, bounded by A, equals A at a point with R > 0 at time tau_0; the strong maximum principle [Nir] would make it equal to A on {R > 0} at earlier times, contradicting its zero axis value. So R(x_j) -> infinity, B >= A, and a(tau) >= B = A for every tau. If B < A, then a(tau) < A for every tau, and since a is nonincreasing, a sequence with Gamma_U -> A cannot have tau bounded below. Finally (a) gives 1 < A phi_{K gamma_*}(1) <= (G_0/gamma_*) phi_{K gamma_*}(1); this forces gamma_* < G_0, and phi_a(1) = 1 - (1 + a) e^{-a} is increasing in a.
(g) (i) U^theta <= 1 = U^theta(e_1, 0) at tau = 0. (ii) By (a), A - Gamma_U >= A (1 - phi_a(R)) = A (1 + aR) e^{-aR}, and (1 + X) e^{-X} is decreasing in X >= 0. (iii) Suppose a(s) = a(tau) = m > B. Take x_j with Gamma_U(x_j, tau) -> m. Since m > B, R(x_j) is bounded above, and since m >= 1 and |Gamma_U| <= ||grad U||_infty R^2, it is bounded below by a positive constant. The axial translates of U by z(x_j) converge (compactness as in Proposition 6) to a solution whose circulation is at most a(s') <= m at times s' in [s, tau] and equals m at a point with R > 0 at time tau; the strong maximum principle [Nir] makes it equal to m on {R > 0} x [s, tau], contradicting its zero axis value. (iv) At the spatial point x(tau), Gamma_U(x(tau), tau_0) <= a(tau_0) and Gamma_U(x(tau), tau_0) >= Gamma_U(x(tau), tau) - (tau_0 - tau) omega_t(R(x(tau))). If B < A then a(tau_0) < A, since a(tau_0) = A gives B = A by (f). (v) |d_tau Gamma_U| is bounded (by (f) at large R, and by the derivative bounds of the bounded ancient mild solution at bounded R), so a is Lipschitz. Fix rho; by (a), sup{Gamma_U : R <= rho} <= A phi_a(rho) < A, and since a(tau) -> A there is T(rho) such that for tau <= -T(rho), a(tau) is the supremum over R >= rho, a function of tau with Lipschitz constant omega_t(rho). Let rho -> infinity. (vi) Gamma_{U_n} = Gamma(a_n + r_n y, t_n + T_n tau)/gamma_n converges to Gamma_U locally uniformly (Theorem 2), so Gamma_U(y, tau) <= liminf_n sup Gamma_{U_n}(., tau); sup_x Gamma(x, t) is nonincreasing in t, t_n + T_n tau -> T_* and gamma_n -> gamma_*. Compact near-maximizers: if Gamma_{U_n}(y_n, tau) - sup Gamma_{U_n}(., tau) -> 0 with y_n -> y, local uniform convergence gives Gamma_U(y, tau) = G_+/gamma_* >= A >= Gamma_U, so the space-time supremum A > 0 is attained at R(y) > 0, which the strong maximum principle and the zero axis value exclude as in (f).

Status: proved here; source: own derivation for (a)-(e), research answer res21 for (f) and research answer res22 for (g), both checked by the author; verification: (a)-(e) adversarial audit rounds 12, 14 (Feller form), 21, 22, checked by scripts; (f) adversarial audit rounds 23, 24, with a script; (g) adversarial audit rounds 24 (which found the error in (vi) and the missing quantifiers of (ii) and (iv)) and 25 (which checked the repaired text and added the convention for calR and the condition 0 <= chi <= 1) (the explicit examples of res22 that bear on (f), (g) are in sec. 3.12 and are checked by a script).

Proposition 24 (material lines and vorticity creation at the records). Physical variables, viscosity 1, T_n = r_n^2/gamma_n, q = u^theta/r, eta = omega^theta/r.
(a) (backward paths) There is delta > 0, depending only on the limit and G, such that for 0 <= h <= delta and all large n the particle path X ending at the record satisfies r(X(t_n - h T_n)) >= r_n (1 + h/(2 gamma_n)) and Gamma(X(t_n - h T_n), t_n - h T_n) >= gamma_n + h/2. In the normalized frame, a backward path with R = 1 at tau = 0 along which -R U^R >= c > 0 on [-A, 0] has R(-A)^2 >= 1 + 2 c A, so residence in R <= L forces A <= (L^2 - 1)/(2c); similar bounds hold under persistent compression -U^R/R >= kappa, or under persistent axial strain between two ordered axial particles at every time. The statement that the record particle carried circulation at least gamma_n at a fixed earlier time is not proved.
(b) (vortex lines and material volume) omega . grad Gamma = 0, so instantaneous vortex lines lie in level sets of the circulation; at the record omega^r = 0 and omega^z = S_n/r_n; the instantaneous flat disk of radius r at height z carries axial vorticity flux 2 pi Gamma(r, z); r J_2 is constant along particle paths, J_2 the meridional Jacobian; the area-weighted mean of d_z u^z over the disk below the record equals -2 u^r/r_n >= 2/r_n^2. A local divergence-free field with the record inflow has axial strain of either sign at the record particle; incompressibility and the value of the record inflow alone give no positive pointwise lower bound for axial strain at that particle.
(c) (axial stretching) Writing u^theta = c r + d r^3 + O(r^5) and sigma = d_z u^z on the axis, along axial particles c' = sigma c + c_zz + 8 d, and the axial vorticity Omega = 2c satisfies Omega' = sigma Omega + 2(c_zz + 8 d). The radial diffusion term 8 d cannot be dropped: in the Burgers vortex it balances the stretching exactly.
(d) (weighted balances) For smooth axisymmetric test weights phi with compact support or sufficient decay, d/dt int eta phi dx = int eta (phi_t + b.grad phi + L phi) dx - 4 pi int eta(0, z) phi(0, z) dz - int q^2 phi_z dx, using int phi Delta_5 F dx = int F L phi dx - 4 pi int F(0, z) phi(0, z) dz; analogous balances hold for convex functions F of eta (the gradient term -int F''(eta) |grad eta|^2 phi is favourable when F'' >= 0 and phi >= 0) and for eta + lambda q^2. The source pairing -int q^2 phi_z is unsigned; nonlinear or coupled choices can leave additional unsigned terms.
(e) (source per window) For every A > 0, int_{t_n - A T_n}^{t_n} ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)} dt >= c_A > 0 for large n (this is Proposition 19(d)). The window dissipation satisfies int_{t_n - A T_n}^{t_n} ||grad u||_2^2 dt >= c r_n for large n, because it is at least gamma_n r_n times the normalized dissipation on a fixed compact set, whose limit is positive since U(., 0) is not constant. Precisely, for every A > 0 and L > 2 let D_n(A, L) := int_{t_n - A T_n}^{t_n} int_{C_n(L)} |grad u|^2 dx dt, where C_n(L) = {r < L r_n, |z - z_n| < L r_n}, and I(A, L) := int_{-A}^0 int_{R < L, |Z| < L} |grad U|^2 dy dtau; then D_n(A, L)/r_n -> gamma_* I(A, L) > 0. So the bound o(r_n) on the window dissipation, a natural sufficient estimate for small source pairing against record-scale weights, is false. This unweighted dissipation-smallness criterion is impossible in Case 1; signed cancellation, localization or a different weighted estimate is not excluded.
(f) The Burgers vortex exhibits the listed local phenomena and satisfies the local identities, including short backward circulation gain and exact balance of axis stretching by radial diffusion (with inward strain and straight axial vortex lines), with identically vanishing source d_z(q^2). It is unbounded and has infinite energy, while the proof of (e) uses the velocity bound at large distances.

Proof. Notation: w = u^theta, D_t = d_t + b.grad, V_n := U_n^R e_R + U_n^Z e_Z, Gamma_{U_n} = R U_n^theta.
(a) Let Y_n solve Y_n' = -V_n(Y_n, -h), Y_n(0) = (1, 0), and g_n(h) := Gamma_{U_n}(Y_n(h), -h). Since D_tau Gamma_{U_n} = nu_n L Gamma_{U_n}, g_n' = -nu_n L Gamma_{U_n}(Y_n(h), -h). At h = 0 the record conditions (Proposition 22(b), rescaled) give -L Gamma_{U_n}(e_1, 0) = 1 + a_n + b_n >= 1, a_n = -d_RR U_n^theta(e_1, 0) >= 0, b_n = -d_ZZ U_n^theta(e_1, 0) >= 0. By C^{2,1}_loc convergence (Theorem 2) the second derivatives of Gamma_{U_n} are equicontinuous near (e_1, 0), and |V_n| <= K + 1 nearby for large n, so |Y_n(h) - (1, 0)| <= (K + 1) h and there is delta > 0 with -L Gamma_{U_n} >= 1/2 along Y_n on [0, delta] for all large n. Hence g_n(h) >= 1 + h/(2 gamma_n), and R(Y_n(h)) >= g_n(h) because U_n^theta <= 1 on the past (first crossing). In physical variables r = r_n R, Gamma = gamma_n Gamma_{U_n}, t = t_n - h T_n. Residence: if -R U^R >= c along a backward path, d(R^2)/dh = -2 R U^R >= 2c, so R(-A)^2 >= 1 + 2cA and R(-A) <= L forces A <= (L^2 - 1)/(2c). If -U^R/R >= kappa, d(log R)/dh >= kappa and kappa R <= K, so A <= kappa^{-1} log min{L, K/kappa}. If d_Z U^Z >= sigma_0 > 0 between two ordered axis particles at every time of [-A, 0], their separation l satisfies sigma_0 l <= l' <= 2K and l <= 2L, so A <= sigma_0^{-1} log(min{2L, 2K/sigma_0}/l(-A)).
(b) omega^r = -Gamma_z/r, omega^z = Gamma_r/r and omega^theta = d_z u^r - d_r u^z, so omega.grad Gamma = omega^r Gamma_r + omega^z Gamma_z = 0; at the record Gamma_r = S_n and Gamma_z = 0. The flux is int_0^r (Gamma_s/s) 2 pi s ds = 2 pi Gamma(r, z), as Gamma = 0 on the axis. Along paths (log J_2)' = d_r u^r + d_z u^z = -u^r/r and (log r)' = u^r/r, so r J_2 is constant. The disk identity follows from int_0^{r_n} s d_z u^z ds = -r_n u^r (incompressibility) and -r_n u^r >= 1 (Theorem 1). Example: with f(r) := -1/r - N(r - 1), the field u^r = f(r), u^z = -(f' + f/r) z is divergence free near (1, 0), u^r(1, 0) = -1 and d_z u^z(1, 0) = N, of either sign.
(c) q satisfies D_t q = Delta_5 q - 2 (u^r/r) q (from D_t Gamma = L Gamma and r^{-2} L(r^2 q) = Delta_5 q). On the axis u^r/r = a_0 = -sigma/2 and q = c + d r^2 + O(r^4), so Delta_5 q = c_zz + 8 d there, which gives c' = sigma c + c_zz + 8 d; Omega = omega^z(0, z) = 2c. In the Burgers vortex of Proposition 22(d), c = Gamma_inf k and d = -Gamma_inf k^2/2, so sigma c = 4 nu k^2 Gamma_inf = -8 nu d.
(d) Multiply eta_t + b.grad eta = Delta_5 eta + d_z(q^2) by phi and integrate: div b = 0 moves b.grad onto phi, the adjoint relation of sec. 1.1 treats the Delta_5 term, and int phi d_z(q^2) dx = -int q^2 phi_z dx. For F(eta), Delta_5 F(eta) = F'(eta) Delta_5 eta + F''(eta) |grad eta|^2 gives the same terms with F(eta) and F(eta(0, z)), plus -int F''(eta) |grad eta|^2 phi dx and the source -int q^2 (F''(eta) eta_z phi + F'(eta) phi_z) dx. For eta + lambda q^2, D_t(eta + lambda q^2) = Delta_5 (eta + lambda q^2) + d_z(q^2) - 2 lambda |grad q|^2 - 4 lambda (u^r/r) q^2.
(e) The first claim is Proposition 19(d). Since grad u(x, t) = (S_n/r_n) grad U_n(y, tau), dx = r_n^3 dy and dt = T_n d tau, D_n(A, L) = gamma_n r_n int_{-A}^0 int_{R < L, |Z| < L} |grad U_n|^2 dy d tau, and C^{2,1}_loc convergence gives D_n/r_n -> gamma_* I(A, L). If I(A, L) = 0, grad U would vanish on the cylinder and, by continuity, at (e_1, 0), which lies in it since L > 2; but the rotation of the cylindrical basis gives d_{e_theta} U . e_R = -U^theta/R = -1 there. The o(r_n) bound had been proposed because q^2 <= |grad u|^2 (q = -d_{x_2} u_1 on the meridian x_2 = 0) gives |int int d_z(q^2) phi dx dt| <= ||phi_z||_infty int ||grad u||_2^2 dt.
(f) In the notation of Proposition 22(d), the backward path from (1, 0) is R(-h) = e^{alpha h/2}, Z = 0, with Gamma_U = Gamma_inf (1 - e^{-k e^{alpha h}}) > Gamma_U(1) there; omega^theta = 0 and omega^r = 0, so omega = 2 Gamma_inf k e^{-k R^2} e_Z and the vortex lines are straight axial lines; U^R < 0; q is independent of Z, so d_Z(q^2) = 0; the axis balance is that of (c). The proof of Proposition 19(d) averages in Z using |U| <= K, which fails for this unbounded field.

Status: proved here; source: research answer checked by the author (res8; the falsity of the o(r_n) target in (e): own derivation); verification: adversarial audit rounds 13, 21, 22; checked by a script

Proposition 25 (the far-field inflow arrow: refinement, flux, Biot-Savart, scalar models). Notation of Proposition 23; Q_R(Z, tau) := -R U^R(R, Z, tau).
(a) (refinement) Under the hypotheses of Proposition 23(b), for every R_0 > 0 and every 0 < eps <= m(R_0), m(R_0) = (A/2) e^{-lambda_{R_0}}, there is a point with R > R_0, Gamma_U > A - eps, d_R Gamma_U > 0 and -R U^R > 2 nu (for larger eps apply this with min{eps, m(R_0)}). The proof is that of 23(b): at a positive interior maximum of the comparison function the radial derivative of Gamma_U equals minus the radial derivative of the barrier, which is nonnegative, and where it vanishes the drift term of the barrier vanishes too. If U is in LL after normalization, the Cartesian translates along such points with R -> infinity converge along a subsequence to a_0 e_1 + c_0 e_3 with a_0 <= 0 (a_0 < 0 is not obtained).
(b) (vertical flux) Assume in addition that U is divergence-free (true for Case-1 limits). For all Z_1 < Z_2 and tau, |int_{Z_1}^{Z_2} Q_R dZ| <= K R^2, because F_R = int_0^R s U^Z(s, Z, tau) ds satisfies |F_R| <= K R^2/2 and d_Z F_R = Q_R. So each connected Z-interval on which Q_R > 2 nu has length less than K R^2/(2 nu). The identity bounds neither the total measure nor the duration of the strong-inflow set (stream function chi(R) sin Z; steady drifts), and it forces every long signed axial average of Q_R at fixed radius to vanish, so smallness of long signed averages at fixed radius cannot replace the pointwise hypothesis of 23(b) (it does not control positive-part averages, and the convergence is not uniform in R).
(c) (Biot-Savart) If the meridional field b is the Biot-Savart integral of omega^theta e_theta (true for the finite-energy physical field at positive classical times), then r |b(r, z)| <= (1/2) calQ_omega(r, z), with calQ_omega(r, z) := int_0^infinity int_R r R (d D)^{-1} |omega^theta(R, Z)| dZ dR, where d^2 = (r - R)^2 + (z - Z)^2 and D^2 = (r + R)^2 + (z - Z)^2 (from |b(x)| <= (4 pi)^{-1} int |omega(y)| |x - y|^{-2} dy and int_0^{2 pi} d theta/(d^2 + 2 r R (1 - cos theta)) = 2 pi/(d D)). calQ is invariant under the Navier-Stokes scaling. Hence calQ_omega <= 4 nu at a point gives -r b^r <= 2 nu there, and in Case 1 the physical bound calQ_{omega^theta(t)} <= 4 (viscosity one) at the points of the windows of Proposition 23(c) that lie in the near-maximal circulation set, and by (a) only where d_r Gamma > 0, would exclude Case 1; this is a stronger sufficient condition, not an equivalent form. The critical (scale-invariant) vorticity Morrey norm sup_{x in R^3, s > 0} s^{-1} int_{B(x, s)} |omega| dy and the unweighted meridional mass int int |omega^theta| dR dZ, used alone, do not control r|b|: a smooth compactly supported stream-function bump of width delta gives a divergence-free field with |b| <= 20 nu, -b^R(1, 0) = 3 nu and vorticity Morrey norm at most 28080 pi nu delta (9360 pi eps for amplitude eps = 3 nu delta) (a kinematic example, not a Navier-Stokes solution; it refutes estimates from these two norms alone, not an implication using all the ancient Navier-Stokes hypotheses).
(d) (scalar models) (i) Exterior: for p > 0 the steady field U^R = -nu(p + 2)/R, U^Z = 0 with Gamma = A - R^{-p}/p, A = (p + 1)/p, on R > (p + 1)^{-1/p}, is an exact steady exterior Navier-Stokes solution with 0 <= U^theta <= 1 = U^theta(1) and -R U^R = nu(p + 2) > 2 nu everywhere; it has no smooth axis extension. (ii) Whole space: the smooth bounded divergence-free drift V with Stokes stream function beta R^2 xi (1 + xi^2)^{-1/2}, xi = Z/(1 + R^2), beta = 64 nu, has vertical flux int Q_R dZ = 2 beta R^2, so the axial scale R^2 allowed by (b) is attained. It carries a steady circulation with max{0, 1 - 1/R - Z^2/R^4} <= Gamma <= 1 - e^{-32 R^2} (monotone exhaustion of Dirichlet problems with boundary data 1 - e^{-32 R^2}; the barrier signs have an analytic proof). The circulation of each axial translation limit vanishes by 23(b) applied to the constant axial drift (Theorem 5 assumes the Navier-Stokes equations and does not apply to a prescribed drift), so Gamma/R attains a positive maximum. After normalization at that maximum it satisfies the scalar record conditions, every far-radial translation limit of V is an axial constant, and, before normalization, for fixed 0 < eps <= 1 the inflow exceeds 2 nu on {Gamma > 1 - eps} in a region of axial extent comparable to R^2 at every large R. It is not a Navier-Stokes field: along Z = R^2, R^4 V.grad eta -> 3 beta^2 and R^4 nu Delta_5 eta -> -(21/sqrt2) nu beta while R^4 d_Z(q^2) -> 0, so R^4 times the residual of the steady equation for eta = omega^theta/r tends to 3 beta^2 + 21 nu beta/sqrt2 > 0 (the source limit uses that bounded entire solutions of a constant-drift heat equation are constant); it also has infinite energy. The model shows that the listed scalar and kinematic properties are insufficient; it does not show which further estimates a successful argument must use.
(e) (limit-level equivalence) Consider the assertion: for every W in LL (viscosity one) with A_W := sup Gamma_W > 0 there is R_0(W) > 0 such that -R W^R <= 2 on {R > R_0(W), Gamma_W > A_W - m_W(R_0(W))}, with m_W the deficit of Proposition 23 computed for W. By 23(b) it rules out every member with A_W > 0; a member with nonzero circulation and sup Gamma_W <= 0 is ruled out by applying it to the reflection W^#(x, t) = P W(P x, t), P = diag(1, -1, 1), which preserves the equations and the meridional components and reverses the swirl; and a member with Gamma_W = 0 is excluded by the definition of LL. So the assertion implies that LL is empty, and conversely LL empty makes it vacuous: posed for the whole class LL, the far-field bound is equivalent to the Liouville problem for LL (Theorem 3). The contradiction is obtained member by member, so no uniformity of m_W is needed. The equivalence is not automatic when the bound is asserted only for a proper subclass, such as the limits of one physical trajectory; information that distinguishes physically obtainable limits from unrestricted members of LL, for example a uniform bound inherited by the physical limits, is one possible route.
(f) (slender asymptotics and an exact exterior solution) For the Stokes stream function Psi = beta R^2 g(Z/R^2) one has exactly eta = R^{-2} E + R^{-4} F, E = -4 beta k^2 g'', F = -beta g'', k = Z/R^2, and the steady swirl-free vorticity residual W.grad eta - nu Delta_5 eta (W the field of Psi) is R^{-4} C_4 + R^{-6} C_6 + R^{-8} C_8 with C_4 = 2 beta (g E)' - 4 nu (k^2 E')', C_6 = 2 beta (2 g' F + g F') - nu (4 k^2 F'' + 16 k F' + 8 F + E'') and C_8 = -nu F'' (primes are d/dk); the reduced equation C_4 = 0 is not uniform in the core |Z| <~ R, and the unsteady leading equation keeps a time derivative on the time scale R^2/nu. The odd reduced profiles with bounded axial velocity, nonzero vorticity and a finite slope at k = 0 all have a nonzero linear tail (classification from a research answer, partly rechecked and audited), so none satisfies the whole-space flux bound (b); oddness is needed, since a constant profile (constant axial velocity) satisfies it. This excludes only global steady slender similarity profiles of this class. With s = (R^2 + Z^2)^{1/2}, mu = Z/s, a > 1, the field with Stokes stream function 2 nu R^2/(a s - Z) (the Landau jet), swirl A/R and pressure 4 nu^2 (a Z - s)/(s (a s - Z)^2) - A^2/(2 R^2) is an exact steady Navier-Stokes solution off the axis, smooth and bounded on {R > R_1}, with |int_{Z_1}^{Z_2} Q_{R_1} dZ| <= 2 nu R_1 (a^2 - 1)^{-1/2} and Q_R = 2 nu (1 - mu^2)(1 - a mu)/(a - mu)^2, which equals 20 nu/9 > 2 nu on the ray Z = R/sqrt3 for a = 17/16. So an inequality derived only from bounded velocity, potential swirl, bounded signed inner flux and the exterior equations cannot give the far-field bound. The example has Gamma = A and d_R Gamma = 0 everywhere, so it does not satisfy the whole-space conditions A - Gamma > 0 and d_R Gamma > 0 at the witnesses of (a), and it has no smooth extension through the axis.

Key estimates. Conventions: Psi is the Stokes stream function, b^R = -Psi_Z/R, b^Z = Psi_R/R, so Q_R = Psi_Z; w := A - Gamma_U. The comparison behind the deficit of Proposition 23 uses only the circulation equation, |U| <= K and |Gamma_U| <= K R, and gives w >= m(R_0) on {R <= R_0} under the hypotheses of Proposition 23(b).
(a) Suppose -R U^R <= 2 nu on {R > R_0, w < eps, d_R Gamma_U > 0} for some 0 < eps <= m(R_0). Put calL := d_tau + U^R d_R + U^Z d_Z - nu L, so calL w = 0. Let Y(R, t) solve Y_t = Y_RR + Y_R/R on R_0 < R < R_1 with Y = 0 at t = 0 and at R = R_1 and Y = h(t) at R = R_0 (h smooth, nondecreasing from 0 to 1, a smooth switch-on); then 0 <= Y <= 1, Y_R <= 0, and Y_eps := eps Y(R, nu(tau - tau_1)) satisfies calL Y_eps = (U^R + 2 nu/R) Y_eps_R. With c_1 := K + nu + 1 and sigma > 0 let v := Y_eps - w - sigma e^{c_1 (tau - tau_1)} cosh Z on {R_0 < R < R_1, tau_1 < tau <= 0, w < eps}; v < 0 on the parabolic boundary and for large |Z|. At a positive interior maximum d_R v = 0, so d_R Gamma_U = -Y_eps_R >= 0. If d_R Gamma_U > 0 the hypothesis gives calL Y_eps <= 0; if d_R Gamma_U = 0 then Y_eps_R = 0 and calL Y_eps = 0. In both cases calL v <= -sigma e^{c_1 (tau - tau_1)} cosh Z (c_1 + U^Z tanh Z - nu) < 0, whereas calL v >= 0 at a maximum. Hence w >= Y_eps; letting sigma -> 0, tau_1 -> -infinity (Y_eps -> eps log(R_1/R)/log(R_1/R_0)) and R_1 -> infinity gives w >= eps everywhere, contradicting sup Gamma_U = A. For U in LL take R_0 = k and eps = min{m(k), 1/k}: the witnesses have R_k -> infinity, Gamma_U -> A and U^R < -2 nu/R_k, so the translate limits of Proposition 6 have a_0 <= 0.
(b) Incompressibility gives d_s(s U^R) = -s d_Z U^Z, and s U^R -> 0 at the axis, so R U^R = -d_Z F_R, i.e. d_Z F_R = Q_R; |F_R| <= K R^2/2 gives the flux bound, and on an interval I with Q_R > 2 nu, 2 nu |I| < int_I Q_R dZ <= K R^2. For the steady stream function chi(R) sin Z one has Q_R = chi(R) cos Z, so where chi > 2 nu the set {Q_R > 2 nu} has infinite axial measure and persists for all time, while |I|^{-1} |int_I Q_R dZ| <= K R^2/|I|.
(c) With y = (R cos theta, R sin theta, Z), |x - y|^2 = d^2 + 2 r R (1 - cos theta) and int_0^{2 pi} d theta/(a - b cos theta) = 2 pi (a^2 - b^2)^{-1/2} with a = d^2 + 2 r R, b = 2 r R, a^2 - b^2 = d^2 D^2; inserting this into |b(x)| <= (4 pi)^{-1} int |omega| |x - y|^{-2} dy gives |b| <= (1/2) int int R |omega^theta| (d D)^{-1} dR dZ. For omega_lambda(x) = lambda^2 omega(lambda x), calQ_{omega_lambda}(r, z) = calQ_omega(lambda r, lambda z); hence calQ_{omega^theta(t)}(r_n R, z_n + r_n Z) = gamma_n calQ_{omega_{U_n}(tau)}(R, Z), and the physical criterion follows from (a) as Proposition 23(c) follows from 23(b). Bump: zeta(t) = exp(1 - 1/(1 - t^2)) for |t| < 1 (0 otherwise), f(p, q) = q zeta(p) zeta(q), Psi = eps f((R - 1)/delta, Z/delta), 0 < delta <= 1/4. Since f_q(0, 0) = 1, b^R(1, 0) = -eps/delta. From |f_p| <= 3, |f_q| <= 4, |f_pp| <= 84, |f_qq| <= 90 and R >= 3/4 on the support: |b| <= (20/3) eps/delta; omega^theta = -(eps/(R delta^2))(f_pp + f_qq) + (eps/(R^2 delta)) f_p, so |omega^theta| <= 234 eps/delta^2 and int int |omega^theta| dR dZ <= 936 eps. The Morrey quotient is at most 312 pi eps for s <= delta (ball volume) and at most 9360 pi eps for s >= delta (the support meets B(x, s) in volume at most 40 pi delta^2 s). With eps = 3 nu delta: |b| <= 20 nu, -b^R(1, 0) = 3 nu, Morrey norm at most 28080 pi nu delta.
(d) (i) Gamma_R = R^{-p-1} and L Gamma = -(p + 2) R^{-p-2} = U^R Gamma_R/nu; (Gamma/R)_R = ((p + 1)/p) R^{-p-2} (1 - R^p), so U^theta has its maximum 1 at R = 1; the viscous term of -c/R vanishes, (R U^R)_R = 0, and the pressure has P_R = (nu^2 (p + 2)^2 + Gamma^2)/R^3.
(ii) With D_V := (1 + R^2)^2 + Z^2: V^R = -beta R (1 + R^2)^2 D_V^{-3/2}, V^Z = 2 beta Z (1 + R^2 + Z^2) D_V^{-3/2}; V^R/R and V^Z are smooth in (R^2, Z), |V^R| <= beta R/(1 + R^2) <= beta/2, |V^Z| <= 2 beta, -V^R <= beta R, Z V^Z >= 0, and Q_R = beta R^2 (1 + R^2)^{-1} (1 + xi^2)^{-3/2}, whose Z-integral is 2 beta R^2. Put calA := nu L - V.grad, B := 1 - e^{-32 R^2}, l := 1 - R^{-1} - Z^2 R^{-4}. Since nu L B = -beta R B_R, calA B = (-beta R - V^R) B_R <= 0. On {l > 0} (so R > 1, |Z| < R^2): -V^R >= 8 sqrt2 nu/R, -V^Z l_Z >= 0, and calA l >= nu [(8 sqrt2 - 3) R^{-3} - 2 R^{-4} + (32 sqrt2 - 24) Z^2 R^{-6}] > 0. The Dirichlet solutions Gamma_k on (1/k, k) x (-k, k) with data B satisfy max(0, l) <= Gamma_k <= B and decrease in k (Gamma_{k+1} <= B = Gamma_k on the boundary of the k-th domain); the limit keeps both barriers, so sup Gamma = 1. Axis: q = Gamma/R^2 lies in [0, 32] and solves nu Delta_5 q - V.grad q - 2 (V^R/R) q = 0 with coefficients smooth across the axis; the axis has codimension four in R^5, so cutoffs around it have gradient energy O(eps^2) and q extends smoothly; 0 <= Gamma/R <= min(32 R, 1/R) <= sqrt32. Axial translation limits have drift +-2 beta e_z and no inflow, so their circulation vanishes by 23(b); hence Gamma/R attains its maximum S_* at some (R_*, Z_*), where -R_* V^R = nu [1 - (R_*^2/S_*)(d_RR + d_ZZ)(Gamma/R)] >= nu. Normalization: V^(R, Z) := R_* V(R_* R, Z_* + R_* Z), Gamma^ := Gamma(R_* R, Z_* + R_* Z)/(R_* S_*), which preserves the scalar equation. Before normalization, R > 2/eps and |Z| < R^2 (eps/2)^{1/2} give Gamma >= l > 1 - eps and Q_R > 2 nu. Residual: eta = beta Z (11 R^4 + 12 R^2 Z^2 + 22 R^2 + 8 Z^2 + 11) D_V^{-5/2}; in k = Z/R^2, V^R = -beta R^{-1} (1 + k^2)^{-3/2} + O(R^{-3}), V^Z = 2 beta k^3 (1 + k^2)^{-3/2} + O(R^{-2}), eta = R^{-2} E_V(k) + O(R^{-4}) with E_V = 12 beta k^3 (1 + k^2)^{-5/2} and (E_V, E_V', E_V'')(1) = (3 beta/sqrt2, 3 beta/(2 sqrt2), -33 beta/(4 sqrt2)), which gives the two limits along Z = R^2. Far-radial translates of Gamma converge to bounded entire solutions of a constant-drift heat equation, which are constant (|grad| <= C ||.||_infty (nu T)^{-1/2} for every T), so R^4 d_Z(q^2) = 2 Gamma Gamma_Z -> 0; V^Z does not decay, so the energy is infinite.
(e) If A_W > 0, Proposition 23(b) with nu = 1 and K = ||W||_infty gives a point with R > R_0(W), Gamma_W > A_W - m_W(R_0(W)) and -R W^R > 2, contradicting the assertion. P is orthogonal, so W^# is a bounded mild ancient axisymmetric solution with the same meridional components and Gamma_{W^#} = -Gamma_W; if sup Gamma_W <= 0 and Gamma_W is not identically 0, then A_{W^#} > 0 and W^# is in LL.
(f) For Psi = beta R^2 g(k): -R W^R = beta g'(k), W^Z = 2 beta (g - k g'). The residual follows from W.grad(R^{-p} J(k)) = beta R^{-p-2} (p g' J + 2 g J') and Delta_5(R^{-p} J(k)) = R^{-p-2} (4 k^2 J'' + 4 p k J' + p (p - 2) J) + R^{-p-4} J'' (p = 2, 4). Core: (R^{-4} F)/(R^{-2} E) = 1/(4 R^2 k^2) = R^2/(4 Z^2). Unsteady scaling R = L r, Z = L^2 z, t = t_0 + L^2 s/nu, Psi = beta L^2 psi: e := -beta r^{-2} (psi_rr - psi_r/r) solves nu e_s - (beta psi_z/r) e_r + (beta psi_r/r) e_z = nu (e_rr + 3 e_r/r) at leading order. C_4 = 0 integrates to beta g E = 2 nu k^2 E' + c; with g_0 := beta g/(2 nu), alpha := g_0'(0) = Q_0/(2 nu) (Q_0 the inflow at k = 0) and H_0 := k^2 g_0'' it reads k^2 H_0' - g_0 H_0 = c/(16 nu^2), and W^Z = 4 nu (g_0 - k g_0'). Classification (odd profiles, C^1 at 0, C^3 off 0, globally defined, bounded W^Z): a finite slope at 0 forces c = 0; linear profiles have zero vorticity; nonzero-vorticity profiles exist exactly for alpha > alpha_*, a constant in (1, 2) (the terminal slope l(alpha) is continuous and strictly decreasing, l(alpha) -> 1 as alpha -> 1, l(2) = -1, and l(alpha_*) = 0; the decimal alpha_* = 1.6334... comes from uncertified shooting); for each such alpha there is one family under g(k) -> g(lambda k)/lambda, strictly concave on k > 0, with g_0 = l k + B_infinity + O(k^l), l = l(alpha) < 0, as k -> infinity; smooth nonzero-vorticity profiles exist exactly for alpha in {3, 5, 7, ...}; none has alpha <= 1, i.e. Q_0 <= 2 nu. Only two cases were rechecked: the alpha = 2 profile g_0 = k (2 - a|k|)/(1 + a|k|), a > 0 (C^1 but not C^2 at 0), and the series g_0 = 3k + a_1 k^3 + (3/20) a_1^2 k^5 + (13/560) a_1^3 k^7 + ... for alpha = 3. Flux: int_{Z_1}^{Z_2} Q_R dZ = beta R^2 [g(Z_2/R^2) - g(Z_1/R^2)], so (b) forces g bounded, which excludes linear tails and nonzero linear profiles. Landau jet (h := a s - Z, Psi = 2 nu R^2/h): the swirl is potential (Gamma = A), the centrifugal term is balanced by -A^2/(2 R^2), and the swirl-free jet solves the meridional equations, with eta = 4 nu (a^2 - 1)/h^3. Since h >= (a - 1) s >= (a - 1) R_1, the field is smooth and bounded on {R > R_1}; min_Z h = R (a^2 - 1)^{1/2} (at Z = R (a^2 - 1)^{-1/2}), so 0 < Psi <= 2 nu R (a^2 - 1)^{-1/2}, which is the flux bound. For a = 17/16 and mu = 1/2, Q_R = 20 nu/9. A smooth extension through the axis would require Gamma = O(R^2) there, which is incompatible with Gamma = A != 0 on {R > R_1} by real analyticity of smooth steady Navier-Stokes solutions.

Not reproduced: the existence and regularity of the exhaustion limit and the axis removability in (d)(ii), and the proof of the profile classification in (f). The argument for these steps is incomplete: the exhaustion limit and the classification were read in a research answer and not written out, and the classification was rechecked only for alpha = 2 and alpha = 3. The complete argument is not reproduced in this report; this item is not checkable from the report alone.

Status: proof sketch with key estimates; source: research answer checked by the author (res9 for (a)-(e), res10 for (f)); verification: adversarial audit rounds 14, 15, 21, 22; checked by scripts (exhaustion limit in (d)(ii) and profile classification in (f): read only).

Proposition 26 (averaged circulation flux through cylinders). Let U be as in Proposition 23(b), and in addition divergence-free, with bounded first and second derivatives (D_1, D_2) and G' := sup |Gamma_U| < infinity (Proposition 23(b) does not assume incompressibility, and without it (a) and (d) fail: U^R = -4 nu R/(1 + R^2), U^Z = 0, Gamma_U = A R^2/(1 + R^2) solves the scalar equation, has R U^R <= 0 and d(rho) = A/(1 + rho^2)); for rho > 0 let N_rho(Z, tau) = int_0^rho R Gamma_U dR and Q_rho = -rho U^R(rho, Z, tau).
(a) d_tau N_rho + d_Z int_0^rho R U^Z Gamma_U dR - nu d_ZZ N_rho = Q_rho Gamma_U(rho) + nu rho d_R Gamma_U(rho) - 2 nu Gamma_U(rho) (the axis contributes nothing because Gamma_U = O(R^2)). For the exterior line sink of Proposition 23(b) the same expression is a nonzero constant at every radius; for that singular field only the annulus form (difference of the right sides at two radii) applies.
(b) For a box B of axial half-length Lambda and duration T in the past and every constant c, the box means satisfy |2 nu <Gamma_U(rho)> - nu rho <d_R Gamma_U(rho)> + <Q_rho (c - Gamma_U(rho))>| <= G' rho^2/T + (K G' rho^2/2 + nu D_1 rho^3/3 + |c| K rho^2/2)/Lambda, using the flux bound of Proposition 25(b).
(c) For boxes B_j with Lambda_j, T_j -> infinity and a Banach limit, used at every radius, write MM[X] := lim <X>_{B_j} for the resulting normalized positive linear functional on bounded functions of (Z, tau) (the same MM at every radius; lim <X> and MM[X] denote the same quantity below). The averaged profile a(rho) = MM[Gamma_U(rho)] is differentiable and a(rho) = (rho^2/nu) int_rho^infinity lim <Q_s Gamma_U(s)> s^{-3} ds: the averaged circulation at radius rho is supplied only by the correlation of inflow and circulation at larger radii.
(d) Let A = sup Gamma_U > 0 and d(s) := A - a(s) >= 0. Then d(rho) >= 2 A rho^2 e^{K rho/nu} int_rho^infinity e^{-K s/nu} s^{-3} ds >= 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu) for every rho > 0 and every box family; hence for every sequence of boxes with Lambda_j, T_j -> infinity, liminf_j <A - Gamma_U(rho)>_{B_j} >= 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu), and d(s) <= eps A/s for all large s forces eps >= 2 nu/K. This averaged rate condition is implied by the uniform rate hypothesis of [LRZ2, Theorem 1.2] and is weaker than it, so (d) is a stronger necessary condition, with an explicit constant whose optimality is not known. If R U^R <= C (C >= 0) on {R >= R_1}, then d(rho) >= 2 nu A/(C + 2 nu) for all rho >= R_1. So if d(s) -> 0 for some box family (averaged saturation), R U^R is unbounded above on {R >= R_1, A - Gamma_U > 0} for every R_1: averaged saturation and a critical one-sided outflow bound at large radii are incompatible for every C.
(e) (convex weights) Let f be convex and C^2 with f(0) = 0 and f >= 0, and put a_f(rho) = lim <f(Gamma_U(rho))>, F_f(rho) = lim <Q_rho f(Gamma_U(rho))>, D_f(rho) = lim <int_0^rho R f''(Gamma_U) |grad Gamma_U|^2 dR> >= 0. Then a_f(rho) = (rho^2/nu) int_rho^infinity (F_f - nu D_f) s^{-3} ds. If F_f(s) <= 2 nu a_f(s) for all s >= rho_0, then a_f = 0 at every radius. If a_f(r_*) > 0 at one radius, then limsup_{s -> infinity} F_f(s)/a_f(s) >= 2 nu + nu D_f(r_*)/sup f(Gamma_U), with D_f(r_*) > 0; for f = (gamma - theta)_+^3, 0 < theta < A, the excess is at least 8 nu a_f(r_*)/(3 (A - theta)^3 log(r_* (D_1/theta)^{1/2})). So nonzero weighted mass at one radius forces the weighted mean inflow strictly above 2 nu at arbitrarily large radii, the critical value of Proposition 23(b) for weighted box means. For the displayed cubic weight, and more generally for an admissible f with f(A) > 0, averaged saturation gives positive weighted mass by Jensen's inequality. The pointwise special case (-R U^R <= 2 nu on {R >= rho_0, Gamma_U > theta}) is already excluded by Proposition 23(b) when theta < A. For f(gamma) = gamma^2 one gets int_0^infinity lim <Q_s Gamma_U(s)^2> s^{-3} ds = nu int_0^infinity lim <|grad Gamma_U(R)|^2> R^{-1} dR, whose left side equals the averaged correlation of the outflow s U^R with the nonnegative quantity (G')^2 - Gamma_U^2. Under averaged saturation the averaged outflow on the deficit set, O(s) = lim <s U^R(s) (A - Gamma_U(s))>, satisfies limsup O(s)/d(s) = infinity, and its log-average over [r_0, L] tends to 2 nu A.
(f) (scalar and kinematic models) If sup_tau int |Q_R| dZ < infinity at every radius, then a = 0 for every box family. A smooth bounded divergence-free drift with Stokes stream function 64 nu R^2 xi/(1 + xi^2), xi = Z/(1 + R^2)^{1/2}, carries a steady solution of the scalar circulation equation with smooth axis and sup Gamma = 1 attained only at infinity, has near-maximal points with d_R Gamma > 0 and -R U^R > 2 nu at arbitrarily large radii and unbounded critical outflow, satisfies the flux bound of Proposition 25(b), and has a = 0 for every box family; it fails the meridional vorticity equation at large radius (a numerical observation at R = 10^3, 10^5, 10^7). A second explicit smooth bounded field, with stream function 8 nu R^2 (1 + R^2)^{-1/2} cos Z, saturates uniformly (A - Gamma comparable to A/R), satisfies the averaged relation of (c), the flux bound, both radial signs and the linear averaged vorticity identities, and fails the unaveraged circulation equation. It also violates the convex identity of (e) for f = gamma^2: there D_f(R) = (A^2/4) log R + O(1), while the identity with a_f -> A^2 and F_f -> 4 nu A^2 would force D_f -> 2 A^2. So it excludes only arguments from the linear averaged identities. Other radial weights, the equation for u^theta/r and the averaged equation for omega^theta/r (which needs the meridional vorticity equation as an additional hypothesis) give either consequences of (c) or identities with unsigned terms. In every sequence of long boxes the set {A - Gamma_U(rho) >= nu A/(K rho + 3 nu)} has lower density at least eps_rho/(2(A + G') - eps_rho), eps_rho = 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu).
(g) (axially localized fields) If Gamma_U(rho, Z, tau) -> 0 as |Z| -> infinity uniformly in tau at every radius, all circulation-weighted long-box means in (c)-(e) vanish (the deficit mean is A) and (d), (e) say nothing.
