Let f be as in (e) and write h_f := f(Gamma_U). Assume, for every rho > 0, sup_tau int_R int_0^rho R (|h_f| + |U^Z h_f| + |d_Z h_f|) dR dZ < infinity and sup_tau int_R int_0^rho R f''(Gamma_U) |grad Gamma_U|^2 dR dZ < infinity, and, for every compact I subset (0, infinity), sup_{tau, rho in I} int_R (|h_f| + |d_R h_f| + |d_RR h_f|)(rho, Z, tau) dZ < infinity (a bulk integrability condition alone does not control these traces). Let MM_tau be a Banach limit of time means over [tau_j - T_j, tau_j], T_j -> infinity, applied after the axial integration (axial cutoffs are removed before the time average), and put abar_f(rho) := MM_tau int_R h_f(rho, Z, tau) dZ, Fbar_f(rho) := MM_tau int_R Q_rho h_f(rho, Z, tau) dZ and Dbar_f(rho) := MM_tau int_R int_0^rho R f''(Gamma_U) |grad Gamma_U|^2 dR dZ >= 0. Then 2 nu abar_f - nu rho abar_f' + nu Dbar_f = Fbar_f. If abar_f(s) <= C s^alpha for s >= rho_0 with 0 <= alpha < 2, and Fbar_f(s) <= kappa abar_f(s) on [rho_0, infinity) for a constant kappa < (2 - alpha) nu (local notation), then abar_f = 0 on [rho_0, infinity). The threshold therefore degrades with the axial extent of the superlevel sets, and no growth bound was derived for Case-1 limits.
(h) (convex hierarchy, momentum balances, positive mass) Write w = A - Gamma_U, W = A + G', and <X>_log for the limit of (log(L/rho_0))^{-1} int_{rho_0}^L X(s) ds/s. Assume averaged saturation. For g in C^2[0, W] convex with g(0) = g'(0) = 0 and f(gamma) = g(A - gamma) - g(A), the following holds. The cylinder identity underlying (e) holds for every C^2 function f with f(0) = 0; its nonnegativity assumption is needed only for the vanishing consequences. Applying that identity to the present f gives <MM[Q_s g(w(s))]>_log = -2 nu g(A) + nu D_g(infinity), where D_g(infinity) = int g'' d mu and mu is the averaged Dirichlet energy carried by the level sets of w, constructed as follows. For r > 0 put mu_{r,j}(E) := <int_0^r R |grad w|^2 1_{w in E} dR>_{B_j} for Borel sets E in [0, W]. The thin-strip bound mu_{r,j}((v - eps, v + eps)) <= C_r eps (integrate the divergence of R T_eps(w - v) grad w, T_eps the truncation of the identity at level eps; the axis term vanishes and the axial boundary average carries a factor 1/Lambda_j) gives mu_{r,j}(E) <= C'_r |E|, so the Banach limit mu_r is a countably additive measure, absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure; mu := lim_{r -> infinity} mu_r is a countably additive extended measure without atoms (possibly infinite), and D_g(r) = int g'' d mu_r (C^1 approximation of g at fixed radius first, radial limit second); together with the linear relation <lim_L <Q_s w(s)>>_log = -2 nu A and the zero mean of Q. These relations are consistent: for 0 < W_2 < A the inflow distribution (2 nu A/W_2)(delta_0 - delta_{W_2}) over deficit levels and the nonnegative measure mu with density 2 v (A - W_2)/W_2 on [0, W_2] and 2 (A - v) on [W_2, A] satisfy all of them, so excluding them needs additional constraints, such as the flux-energy estimate of (i); no field realizing these data is constructed, and the flux ratio 2 nu A/W_2 they give is a logarithmic-mean value, not a pointwise limit. For steady fields of fixed axial period P with |U^R| <= B, the Wirtinger inequality gives D_{gamma^2}(L) >= (32 pi^2 nu^2 A^2/(B^2 P^2)) log L (1 - o(1)), and then the gamma^2 identity forces <rho d(rho)>_log = infinity; the second field of (f) has rho d -> A, which is why it fails. Assume in addition that U solves the momentum equations with a pressure P such that MM P_R and MM P_Z are defined at every radius and the long-box means of d_Z P_R tend to zero, locally uniformly in R (true when P_R is bounded, and when the pressure gradient is Lipschitz and bounded in BMO; sublinear axial growth |P_R| = o(|Z|) suffices when the box centres satisfy |Z_0| = O(Lambda)). Averaging the radial, angular and axial momentum equations with MM gives (1/R)(R MM (U^R)^2)' + MM P_R = MM Gamma_U^2/R^3, the relation of (c) again, MM(U^R U^Z) = nu (MM U^Z)' - R MM P_Z/2 with MM P_Z constant in R (since the box means of d_Z P_R tend to zero), hence MM P_Z = 0 because MM(U^R U^Z) and MM U^Z are bounded, so MM(U^R U^Z) = nu (MM U^Z)', and a vanishing axis value of the averaged meridional vorticity; a steady periodic field satisfies all averaged momentum and vorticity balances, the linear relation of (c), uniform saturation and the full pressure Poisson equation with bounded pressure, with weighted mean inflow ratio 6 nu A/(A - theta) > 2 nu for f = (gamma - theta)_+^3, while failing the unaveraged equations and the convex hierarchy. Nonzero weighted mass at a fixed radius is equivalent to positive upper density of a separated superlevel set {Gamma_U(r_*) > theta + delta} in some long-box family; if it fails for every family and every radius, every limiting law of sampled translates of U is carried by ancient solutions with Gamma <= theta, which says nothing about the record orbit itself. At scale R the effective viscosity is nu/R, and the endpoint hypothesis of (e) becomes a first-order statement in nu/R that weak limits do not see.
(i) (critical axial flux; sharpness) Let Phi_rho(Z, tau) := int_0^rho s U^Z(s, Z, tau) ds, the axial flux through the disk of radius rho, so d_Z Phi_rho = Q_rho and |Phi_rho| <= K rho^2/2. If for one box family and its functional MM there are rho_0, a measurable function C(rho) >= 0 and bounded functions c_rho(tau) with
  (H) MM[(Phi_rho - c_rho)^2] <= C(rho)^2 nu^2 rho^2 for all rho >= rho_0,
and int^infinity d rho/(rho (1 + C(rho)^2)) = infinity (for example C constant), then MM[Gamma_U(rho)^2] = 0 at every radius. Proof. Put a := MM[Gamma_U(rho)^2], delta_R := MM[(d_R Gamma_U(rho))^2], delta_Z := MM[(d_Z Gamma_U(rho))^2], D := 2 int_0^rho R (delta_R + delta_Z) dR (the dissipation D_{gamma^2} of (e)), F_2 := MM[Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)^2] and s = log rho, and write dots for d/ds. Since Q_rho = d_Z (Phi_rho - c_rho), integration by parts in Z (the box boundary term vanishes in the limit) and Cauchy-Schwarz give |F_2| <= 2 G' nu rho C(rho) delta_Z^{1/2}. Also a-dot = rho MM[2 Gamma_U d_R Gamma_U] <= 2 G' rho delta_R^{1/2} and D-dot = 2 rho^2 (delta_R + delta_Z). The gamma^2 identity of (e), 2 a - a-dot + D = F_2/nu, therefore yields D <= 2 a + D = a-dot + F_2/nu <= 2^{1/2} G' (1 + C(rho)^2)^{1/2} (D-dot)^{1/2}, hence D-dot >= D^2/(2 G'^2 (1 + C(e^s)^2)). If D(s_1) > 0, integrating d(1/D)/ds <= -1/(2 G'^2 (1 + C(e^s)^2)) over [s_1, infinity) contradicts the divergence of int^infinity d rho/(rho (1 + C(rho)^2)). Thus D = 0, so F_2 = 0, a = c rho^2 by the identity, and boundedness of a gives a = 0. No monotonicity of C is needed and C = 0 is allowed. So C may grow, for example C(rho) = (log rho)^{1/2}, whereas a bound only along an arbitrary unbounded sequence of radii does not suffice (a scalar counterexample). Fixed axial period P with |U^R| <= B gives the hypothesis with C = P B/nu. The hypothesis is one power of rho stronger than the available bound, and the growth condition is sharp at the scalar level: for C(rho) = (log rho)^p with p in (1/2, 1] there are exact steady scalar solutions with bounded drift that saturate uniformly in Z (for p = 1 with root-mean-square flux oscillation exactly nu rho log rho/sqrt2; for each fixed p in (1/2, 1] the construction includes the required corrector estimates, and no uniformity of its constants or starting radius as p down 1/2 is asserted), and 1 + C^2 cannot be replaced by C^2 (an example with narrow dips of C). The first construction of this kind uses a bounded smooth divergence-free drift with Stokes stream function Psi = nu R sum_j (v_j(R)/k_j) sin(k_j Z), fixed frequencies k_j = 2^{-j} and smooth radial partition weights v_j, at most two active at each radius and with active k_j comparable to R^{-1/8}, normalized by sum_j v_j^2/k_j^2 = R^{1/4} (so |b^R| <= 3 nu, |b^Z| < nu, |Psi| <= 2 nu R^{9/8} and MM[Phi_R^2] = (nu^2/2) R^{9/4} in every long-box family), and an exact steady circulation with 0 <= A - Gamma <= 6 A (R_0/R)^{1/8} for R >= R_0 = 10^100 and a smooth axis, so exact scalar solutions saturate uniformly, satisfy every scalar convex identity and have Q > 2 nu at near-maximal points with d_R Gamma > 0 at arbitrarily large radius; its axial flux oscillation is of size nu R^{9/8}. It fails the meridional vorticity equation: at R_n = 2^{8n}, Z_n = pi 2^{n-1} the residual times R_n^{11/8} tends to nu^2. For the physical solution, the energy inequality gives the hypothesis with constant C on boxes of physical axial half-length at least ||u_0||_2^2/(8 pi C^2), but such boxes are not seen by the Case-1 limit, and on them the energy inequality bounds the mean Dirichlet energy only by the same order as the available bound for the time-boundary error, so the box version of the argument does not close. A scaling test (not a proof) detects a quasi-steady imbalance in the meridional vorticity equation for short supercritical flux patterns, which is why the first construction above fails it, and gives no dimensional exclusion of axially long patterns (axial wavelength much larger than R at inflow of order nu). For the maximal long pattern psi = nu beta R^2 f(Z/R^2) (Stokes stream function, x = Z/R^2) the leading-order steady vorticity equation is E_1(f) = 2 (x^3 f'')'' - beta [ (x f f'' + f f')' - f'^2 ] = 0. Every bounded C^4 solution of it on (0, infinity) or on (-infinity, 0) is constant. Proof: I := 2 x^2 (x^2 f'')' - beta f x^2 f'' satisfies I' = x E_1(f), so I is constant, and in t = 1/x the function Y := x^2 f'' solves Y' + (beta/2) f(1/t) Y = -I/2. Writing F(t) = f(1/t) (local notation), boundedness near t = 0 forces both Y(0) = 0 and lim_{t down 0} t^2 F'(t) = 0. If I != 0, the integrating-factor formula gives Y a fixed sign and |Y(t)| >= c > 0 for t >= 1; hence F' has a fixed sign and |F'(t)| >= c/(2t) for t >= 2, contradicting boundedness. With I = 0 one gets Y = 0, so f is affine and, being bounded, constant. So bounded steady leading-order outer patterns of this form are uniform axial flows with zero inflow. For the particular steady inner ansatz Psi = nu R^2 h(y), y = Z/R, the leading equation is (h[(1 + y^2) h'' - y h'])' = 0. Under boundedness of h and y h', this equation forces h to be constant. This does not classify general inner matching. Proof: put A_h := (1 + y^2) h'' - y h', so h A_h = k is constant. If k != 0, h has a fixed sign and (h'/(1 + y^2)^{1/2})' = A_h/(1 + y^2)^{3/2} = k/(h (1 + y^2)^{3/2}); since h'/(1 + y^2)^{1/2} -> 0 as y -> infinity (y h' is bounded) and |h| is bounded, h' has a fixed sign and |h'(y)| >= c/y for large y, contradicting boundedness of h. Hence k = 0; continuity gives A_h = 0 everywhere, so h' = b (1 + y^2)^{1/2} for a constant b, and boundedness forces b = 0. This excludes one quadratic-flux mechanism in a restricted class; it does not prove (H) or any o(R^2) bound on the flux oscillation, since compactness on the outer scale, time dependence, general inner matching and intermediate scales are not treated.
(j) (axial velocity) Let m(tau) be bounded measurable with |m| <= K and e(s) := MM[(U^Z(s, ., .) - m)^2]^{1/2}. Then MM[(Phi_rho - rho^2 m/2)^2]^{1/2} <= int_0^rho s e(s) ds. Hence e(s) <= nu C_1/s for s >= s_0 implies (H) with c_rho = rho^2 m/2, rho_0 = s_0 and C = C_1 + K s_0/nu, and then MM[Gamma_U(rho)^2] = 0 at every radius by (i); a nondecreasing C_1(s) with int^infinity d rho/(rho (1 + C_1(rho)^2)) = infinity also suffices. So in a family with positive circulation-square mass, limsup_{s -> infinity} s e(s)/nu = infinity for every such m. This rephrases the missing bound; it is not a route.
(k) (averaged energy budgets) Assume in addition that U is a bounded ancient mild solution of the full Navier-Stokes equations with canonical pressure P. Write u = U^R, v = U^theta, w = U^Z, E_b = (u^2 + w^2)/2, E_theta = v^2/2, d_b = u_R^2 + u_Z^2 + w_R^2 + w_Z^2 + u^2/R^2 and d_theta = v_R^2 + v_Z^2 + v^2/R^2, and put D_b(R) = int_0^R s MM[d_b] ds, D_theta(R) = int_0^R s MM[d_theta] ds, J_b = R MM[u E_b - nu d_R E_b] + MM[Phi_R P_Z], J_theta = R MM[u E_theta - nu d_R E_theta] (Phi_R the disk flux of (i), not a radial derivative) and W(R) := int_0^R MM[U^R Gamma_U^2] s^{-2} ds (centrifugal work). The pressure is centred at (0, Z_0, tau) in each box; the canonical pressure is BMO with bounded gradient, so it grows at most logarithmically relative to this centre, the axial pressure boundary terms are O_R(log Lambda/Lambda), and axial integration by parts turns the radial pressure work R MM[u P] into MM[Phi_R P_Z] (R u = -d_Z Phi_R). Then J_b + nu D_b = W and J_theta + nu D_theta = -W. Moreover W(R) = nu a(R)/R^2 - nu int_0^R D s^{-3} ds with a = MM[Gamma_U^2] and D as in (i), J_theta = -nu D/(2 R^2), W(infinity) = -nu D_theta(infinity) <= 0 and nu D_theta(infinity) <= 2 K^2 G'. These budgets do not imply (H): the pressure work and the meridional transport are unsigned, and the smooth bounded divergence-free kinematic field psi = (V/2) R^2 tanh y, Gamma = A R^2 (L^2 + R^2)^{-1} sech^2 y, y = L Z/(L^2 + R^2), V L = 8 nu, has, for symmetric axial boxes centred at Z = 0 and with the pressure set to zero in these kinematic budget identities, all averaged dissipations, radial energy fluxes and averaged circulation quantities equal to zero, while oddness in Z gives MM[(psi - c_R(tau))^2] >= V^2 R^4/4 for every bounded time-only gauge c_R, so (H) fails for this family. It fails both the circulation equation (residual A L^2 R^2 (10 nu - V L)/(L^2 + R^2)^3 at Z = 0) and the vorticity equation (d_Z R_eta(L, 0) = (A^2 + 168 nu^2)/(4 L^6) > 0 for R_eta = b.grad eta - nu Delta_5 eta - d_Z(q^2)).
(l) (invariant measures; the density-trivial case) Assume in addition that U is a bounded ancient mild axisymmetric solution with |grad^2 U| <= D_2 (for example a Case-1 limit). Let X be the closure of the translates U(. + zeta e_3, . + s), zeta in R, s <= 0, in the topology of local uniform convergence of the field and its first spatial derivatives on R^3 x (-infinity, 0]. Then X is compact and consists of bounded ancient mild axisymmetric solutions with the same bounds (and W^theta <= 1 for a Case-1 limit). For every box family and Banach limit there is a Borel probability measure mu on X, invariant under the axial-temporal shifts, such that MM of every continuous local functional of U is its mu-expectation; in particular a(rho) = int Gamma_W(rho, 0, 0)^2 d mu(W). The mass a vanishes at every radius if and only if mu is concentrated on the constant fields c e_3, |c| <= K. Every box family has a = 0 at every radius if and only if, for all rho, eps > 0, the set of (Z, tau) at which |grad U| > eps somewhere in {R <= rho, |Z' - Z| <= rho, tau - rho^2 <= tau' <= tau} has zero upper Banach density in R x (-infinity, 0]. Here the upper Banach density of a set E is inf_{L, H > 0} sup |E cap B|/|B| over boxes B = [Z_0 - Lambda, Z_0 + Lambda] x [t_0 - T, t_0] with Lambda >= L, T >= H, t_0 <= 0. The record equality, the record inflow and the value of sup Gamma need not pass to members of X. The support of an invariant measure consists of histories that extend uniquely to bounded eternal mild solutions with the same bounds. Hence either (S1) some invariant measure on X charges nonconstant fields, and then an ergodic one does, or (S2) every invariant measure on X is concentrated on constant fields, equivalently U is density trivial in this sense. In case (S1), with V(R) := E|Phi_R - Pi_Z Phi_R|^2 for a nontrivial ergodic measure, where Pi_Z is the projection onto axially invariant random variables, one has int_{R_*}^infinity dR/(R (1 + V(R)/(nu^2 R^2))) < infinity for some R_* > 0; only V(R) <= K^2 R^4/4 is known, and a bound V(R) <= C nu^2 R^2 log R at all large R, for every nontrivial ergodic measure, would exclude (S1), while no fixed power excess V(R) of order R^{2 + 2 alpha}, alpha > 0, would suffice. By (i), the flux bound (H) puts its family in the trivial case, so the averaged route can only exclude (S1). (S2) contains every pattern uniformly localized in the axial direction throughout its past, in the sense used in (g), for example a structure that stays near one height for all past times. All circulation-weighted and derivative-energy balances of this proposition then reduce to 0 = 0, while velocity moments need not vanish, and an exclusion must use unaveraged or Z-integrated information. For a Case-1 limit, in either case, the backward diffusion of the circulation equation from the record, dR_s = (2 nu)^{1/2} dB_s + ((Q - nu)/R)(R_s, Z_s, -s) ds, dZ_s = (2 nu)^{1/2} dB'_s - U^Z(R_s, Z_s, -s) ds, killed at the axis at time zeta, satisfies 1 = E[Gamma(R_s, Z_s, -s) 1_{zeta > s}], so it survives with probability at least 1/A and sup_x Gamma(x, t) >= 1 for every t <= 0; moreover R_s -> infinity almost surely on survival, and E int_0^zeta 1_{R_s <= rho} ds <= h/p_{rho, h} for every h > 0, where p_{rho, h} := P(N(0, 1) < -(rho + K h)/(2 nu h)^{1/2}). This occupation measure is not axial-temporal measure and order-one circulation at radius R forces only |grad U| >= c/R^2, so no lower bound for the density of the nontrivial set follows. Along almost every surviving path, moreover, int_0^infinity (Q - 2 nu)_+ R^{-2} ds = infinity: by Ito, log R_s = log R_0 + int_0^s (Q - 2 nu) R^{-2} ds' + N_s with a martingale N of quadratic variation int_0^s 2 nu R^{-2} ds', and log R_s -> infinity forces the drift integral to be unbounded above (if the quadratic variation stays finite, N converges; otherwise liminf N_s = -infinity). This is a pathwise form of the inflow threshold 2 nu; the magnitude bound (Q - 2 nu)_+ R^{-2} <= K/R is compatible with it.
For Case-1 limits neither averaged saturation nor its failure was established; Proposition 23 gives saturation only along some points.

Key estimates. Notation: D_1, D_2 bound the first and second derivatives of U; w := A - Gamma_U; B = [Z_0 - Lambda, Z_0 + Lambda] x [tau_0 - T, tau_0] with tau_0 <= 0 is a box and <X>_B the mean over B; J_rho := int_0^rho R U^Z Gamma_U dR. MM is a Banach limit (a positive linear functional on bounded sequences, equal to 1 on constants and to the limit on convergent sequences) of <X>_{B_j}, so |MM[X]| <= sup |X|.
(a) R L Gamma = (R Gamma_R - 2 Gamma)_R + R Gamma_ZZ, so int_0^rho R L Gamma_U dR = rho d_R Gamma_U(rho) - 2 Gamma_U(rho) + d_ZZ N_rho (Gamma_U = O(R^2), d_R Gamma_U = O(R)); incompressibility, d_R(R U^R) = -R d_Z U^Z, gives R U^b.grad Gamma_U = (R U^R Gamma_U)_R + (R U^Z Gamma_U)_Z, whose integral is -Q_rho Gamma_U(rho) + d_Z J_rho. For the line sink, Q Gamma + nu rho Gamma_R - 2 nu Gamma = nu p A with p = beta/nu - 2.
(b) Average (a) over B: |N_rho| <= G' rho^2/2 gives |<d_tau N_rho>| <= G' rho^2/T; |J_rho| <= K G' rho^2/2 gives |<d_Z J_rho>| <= K G' rho^2/(2 Lambda); d_Z Gamma_U = R d_Z U^theta gives |d_Z N_rho| <= D_1 rho^3/3 and nu |<d_ZZ N_rho>| <= nu D_1 rho^3/(3 Lambda); 25(b) gives |c <Q_rho>| <= |c| K rho^2/(2 Lambda). Subtract c <Q_rho>.
(c) |d_RR Gamma_U| <= 2 D_1 + rho_1 D_2 on {R <= rho_1}, so radial difference quotients converge uniformly in (Z, tau), a is differentiable and a' = MM[d_R Gamma_U]. By (b), MM[Q_rho] = 0 and 2 nu a - nu rho a' = F, F(rho) := MM[Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)]. Hence (a/rho^2)' = -F/(nu rho^3); |F(s)| <= K G' s and a/rho^2 -> 0 give the formula; U^R = 0 forces a = 0.
(d) By (b) with c = A, F_A := MM[Q_rho w(rho)] = -nu rho d' - 2 nu A + 2 nu d, and Q_rho >= -K rho, w >= 0 give F_A >= -K rho d, i.e. d' <= (K/nu + 2/rho) d - 2A/rho. Then (e^{-K rho/nu} rho^{-2} d)' <= -2A e^{-K rho/nu} rho^{-3}; integrating to infinity (d bounded) gives the first bound, and s = rho + t with (1 + t/rho)^{-3} >= e^{-3t/rho} gives int_rho^infinity e^{-Ks/nu} s^{-3} ds >= e^{-K rho/nu} rho^{-3}/(K/nu + 3/rho), hence d(rho) >= 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu). Taking MM along a subsequence that realizes liminf_j <w(rho)>_{B_j} (the same MM at all radii) gives the liminf statement; it concerns limits, so on a finite box it holds only up to an error that tends to 0 as Lambda_j, T_j -> infinity. Since rho d(rho) -> 2 nu A/K or more, d <= eps A/s forces eps >= 2 nu/K. Comparison with [LRZ2, Theorem 1.2], whose hypothesis is |Gamma^2 - ell| <= eps_0 ell/R uniformly with ell := limsup_R sup Gamma^2: if ell > 0, Gamma has one sign at large R, and after the reflection of 25(e), 0 <= A - Gamma_U <= (eps_0/2 + o(1)) A/R, which implies the averaged rate for every box family; the converse fails because box means allow exceptional axial and temporal sets. Outflow: if s U^R <= C on {s >= R_1, w > 0} (only the deficit set is needed), then Q w >= -C w, F_A >= -C d and d' <= (p/rho) d - 2A/rho with p = 2 + C/nu, so (rho^{-p} d)' <= -2A rho^{-p-1} and d >= 2A/p = 2 nu A/(C + 2 nu) for every box family. Density: w <= W := A + G', so <w>_B <= (eps_rho/2)(1 - p_B) + W p_B with p_B the fraction of B where w >= eps_rho/2, eps_rho := 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu); with liminf_j <w>_{B_j} >= eps_rho this gives liminf_j p_{B_j} >= eps_rho/(2W - eps_rho) (the last sentence of 26(f)).
(e) From L f(Gamma) = f'(Gamma) L Gamma + f''(Gamma) |grad Gamma|^2 and f(Gamma_U(0)) = 0, the computation of (a) gives, for every C^2 function f with f(0) = 0 (with N^f_rho := int_0^rho R f(Gamma_U) dR, J^f_rho := int_0^rho R U^Z f(Gamma_U) dR),
  d_tau N^f_rho + d_Z J^f_rho - nu d_ZZ N^f_rho = Q_rho f(Gamma_U(rho)) + nu rho d_R[f(Gamma_U)](rho) - 2 nu f(Gamma_U(rho)) - nu int_0^rho R f''(Gamma_U) |grad Gamma_U|^2 dR.
The box errors of (b) hold with G' replaced by sup |f(Gamma_U)| and D_1 by sup |f'(Gamma_U)| D_1, so 2 nu a_f - nu rho a_f' + nu D_f = F_f, (a_f/rho^2)' = (nu D_f - F_f)/(nu rho^3), |F_f(s)| <= K s sup f(Gamma_U), and the representation follows by monotone convergence (D_f >= 0). Since f >= 0 is convex with f(0) = 0, f'(0) = 0 and D_f is nondecreasing. Threshold below 2 nu: if F_f <= kappa a_f on [rho_0, infinity) with kappa < 2 nu, put y(rho) := int_rho^infinity a_f s^{-3} ds; then 0 <= y <= sup f(Gamma_U)/(2 rho^2) and a_f/rho^2 = -rho y' <= (kappa/nu) y, so rho^{kappa/nu} y is nondecreasing and bounded by (sup f(Gamma_U)/2) rho^{kappa/nu - 2} -> 0, hence y(rho_0) = 0 (for kappa <= 0 directly). Endpoint kappa = 2 nu: rho a_f' >= D_f >= D_f(r_1) on [r_1, infinity), r_1 >= rho_0, so D_f(r_1) > 0 would give a_f(r) >= a_f(r_1) + D_f(r_1) log(r/r_1) -> infinity; thus D_f = 0 at every radius. With H(x) := int_0^x f''(y)^{1/2} dy, weighted Cauchy-Schwarz gives |H(Gamma_U(r)) - H(Gamma_U(eps))|^2 <= log(r/eps) int_eps^r R f''(Gamma_U) (d_R Gamma_U)^2 dR, and |Gamma_U(eps)| <= D_1 eps^2, so MM[H(Gamma_U(r))^2] <= 2 sup_{|x| <= D_1 eps^2} H(x)^2 + 2 log(r/eps) D_f(r). If H(x) = 0 then f'' = 0 between 0 and x and f(x) = 0; by compactness f <= delta + C_delta H^2 on the range of Gamma_U for every delta > 0, so D_f = 0 gives a_f = 0 at every radius. Gap: if a_f(r_*) > 0, the same bound gives D_* := D_f(r_*) > 0; a_f > 0 on [r_*, infinity), since at a zero s_0 one has a_f'(s_0) = 0 and |F_f(s_0)| <= K s_0 a_f(s_0) = 0, so the identity gives D_f(s_0) = 0. If F_f <= (2 nu + c) a_f on a tail with c < nu D_*/f_max, f_max := sup f(Gamma_U), then rho a_f' >= D_* - c f_max/nu > 0 there and a_f is unbounded. Cubic weight: H^2 = (8/3) f; with r_a := (theta/D_1)^{1/2} one has Gamma_U(r_a) <= theta, so r_a < r_*, H(Gamma_U(r_a)) = 0 and D_f(r_*) >= 8 a_f(r_*)/(3 log(r_*/r_a)), while f_max <= (A - theta)^3. Jensen: a_f(s) >= f(MM[Gamma_U(s)]) = f(A - d(s)), positive once d(s) < A - theta for the cubic weight, and positive for large s under averaged saturation when f(A) > 0. Pointwise case: choose R_1 >= rho_0 with m(R_1) <= A - theta and apply 23(b). For f(gamma) = gamma^2: a_2/rho^2 -> 0 as rho -> 0 (Gamma_U^2 <= D_1^2 rho^4) and as rho -> infinity; MM commutes with the radial integral over finite intervals (uniform continuity in R, Riemann sums), so D_2(s) = 2 int_0^s R MM[|grad Gamma_U(R)|^2] dR, and Tonelli gives the displayed identity; MM[Q_s] = 0 turns its left side into the outflow correlation with (G')^2 - Gamma_U^2. Finally O(s) = -F_A = 2 nu (A - d) + nu s d'; O <= C d on a tail would give d >= 2 nu A/(C + 2 nu) as in (d), and the log-average equals (log(L/r_0))^{-1} [2 nu int_{r_0}^L (A - d) ds/s + nu (d(L) - d(r_0))] -> 2 nu A.
(f) Write b = (U^R, U^Z) for the meridional drift and Psi for its Stokes stream function (b^R = -Psi_Z/R, b^Z = Psi_R/R, Q_R = Psi_Z), and put calA := nu L - b.grad and H_1 := (1 + R^2)^{1/2} (local notation); steady scalar solutions satisfy calA Gamma = 0. Zero averages: |<Q_rho (c - Gamma_U(rho))>_B| <= (|c| + G') sup_tau int |Q_rho| dZ/(2 Lambda) -> 0 for every c, so MM[Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)] = 0 and a = 0 by (c). Scalar model: xi = Z/H_1, beta = 64 nu, Psi = beta R^2 xi/(1 + xi^2), so int |Q_R| dZ = 2 beta R^2 and |int_{Z_1}^{Z_2} Q_R dZ| <= beta R^2. The upper barrier B_+ := 1 - e^{-32 R^2} satisfies calA B_+ = (-beta R - b^R) d_R B_+ <= 0, since -b^R <= beta R. For l := 1 - 1/R - 4 Z^2/R^2, on {l > 0} one has R > 1, |xi| < 1/2, -b^R >= 12 beta/(25 sqrt2), d_R l >= R^{-2}, Z b^Z >= 0 and L l >= -19 R^{-2}, so calA l >= (384 sqrt2/25 - 19) nu R^{-2} > 0, and l_+ <= B_+ is a weak subsolution. Dirichlet problems calA g_k = 0 on (1/k, k) x (-k, k) with boundary values B_+, comparison and interior estimates give a decreasing sequence with a smooth limit Gamma, l_+ <= Gamma <= B_+. The axis is removable: q = Gamma/R^2 takes values in [0, 32] and solves nu Delta_5 q - b.grad q - 2 (b^R/R) q = 0, whose coefficients are smooth across the codimension-four axis (Caccioppoli inequality; cutting out a tube of radius delta costs gradient energy O(delta^2)); derivative bounds follow from the Cartesian swirl equation nu Delta v_i - b.grad v_i - (b^R/R) v_i = 0. On Z = 0, Q_R = beta R^2/H_1 and Gamma >= 1 - 1/R, so the mean value theorem gives near-maximal points with d_R Gamma > 0 and Q_R > 2 nu at arbitrarily large radii; on Z = sqrt3 H_1, R b^R = beta R^2/(8 H_1). For the vorticity equation, R^3 b.grad eta at (R, R) equals -(5/2) beta^2 for R = 10^3, 10^5, 10^7 (a numerical observation), while R^3 Delta_5 eta = O(1/R) and d_Z(q^2) = O(R^{-4}). With R_j = j^2 and boxes [-j, j] x [-j, 0], <Gamma(R_j)> >= 1 - 5/j^2, although a = 0 at every fixed radius. Kinematic model: beta = 8 nu, f_0 = R^2/H_1, a_0 = A (1 - 1/H_1), eps = A (H_1 - 1)(2 H_1 + 1)/(4 H_1^2 (H_1 + 1)), Psi = beta f_0 cos Z, Gamma = a_0 - eps sin Z (local notation). Here f_0/R^2, f_0'/R, a_0/R^2 and eps/R^2 are smooth functions of R^2, |b| <= 2 beta, eps/a_0 <= 3/8 and eps H_1/A < 1/2, so A/(2 H_1) < A - Gamma < 3A/(2 H_1). Long-box means are period means: MM Q = 0, MM[Q Gamma] = beta f_0 eps/2 = 2 nu a_0 - nu R a_0' and MM eta = MM[Q eta] = 0; on Z = -pi/2, Q = beta f_0 > 2 nu (R >= 1) and d(a_0 + eps)/dH_1 = A (H_1^3 + 5 H_1^2 + 4 H_1 + 1)/(2 H_1^3 (H_1 + 1)^2) > 0; on Z = pi/2, R b^R = beta f_0; and R^2 calA Gamma(R, 0) -> 4 nu A. For f = gamma^2, a_f = a_0^2 + eps^2/2 -> A^2, R a_f' -> 0 and F_f = 8 nu f_0 a_0 eps -> 4 nu A^2, while MM|grad Gamma|^2 = (a_0')^2 + ((eps')^2 + eps^2)/2. Other weights: a radial weight phi turns the integrand into nu (R phi' a)'; the equation for q gives nu (a/R^2)' = -MM[Q_R Gamma_U(R)]/R^3; under the meridional vorticity equation, e := MM eta and h_eta := MM[Q_rho eta(rho)] (local notation) satisfy h_eta + nu rho e' + 2 nu (e - e(0)) = 0 with h_eta and e(0) unsigned. These three identities were checked only at the level of the integrations by parts. The density statement at the end of (f) is derived at the end of (d).
(g) Uniform axial decay puts {f(Gamma_U(rho)) > delta} in a bounded Z-interval for all tau, so its density in B_j is at most C_delta/Lambda_j; hence a_f = 0, F_f = 0 (|F_f| <= K rho a_f), D_f = 0 by the identity of (e), and MM[Gamma_U(rho)] = 0, i.e. d = A. Under the trace hypotheses, multiply the identity of (e) by chi(Z/L) and integrate in Z: the axial terms (1/L) int J^f_rho chi'(Z/L) dZ and (nu/L) int d_Z N^f_rho chi'(Z/L) dZ tend to 0 as L -> infinity by the first hypothesis, the time mean of d_tau int N^f_rho dZ over [tau_j - T_j, tau_j] is O(1/T_j), the dissipation term is controlled by the second hypothesis, and the third permits differentiating the traces in rho under MM_tau. This gives the balance with |Fbar_f(s)| <= K s abar_f(s), where abar_f may be unbounded. If Fbar_f <= kappa abar_f on [rho_0, infinity), then nu rho abar_f' = 2 nu abar_f + nu Dbar_f - Fbar_f >= (2 nu - kappa) abar_f, so abar_f(r) >= abar_f(r_1) (r/r_1)^{2 - kappa/nu} for r >= r_1 >= rho_0, which contradicts abar_f = O(s^alpha) unless abar_f(r_1) = 0, since 2 - kappa/nu > alpha. Bulk integrability alone does not control the traces: Gamma = sum_n c_n zeta_1((R - 1)/eps_n) zeta_2((Z - Z_n)/L_n) with eps_n = 2^{-n}, c_n = eps_n^3, L_n = c_n^{-2} (zeta_1, zeta_2 bumps, Z_n separated) has finite bulk integrals and int Gamma(1, Z)^2 dZ = infinity (a kinematic example, not a solution). The lower barriers of the first drift of 26(f) and of 25(d)(ii) give near-maximal sets of axial extent at least comparable to s and to s^2 at radius s (alpha >= 1, threshold at most nu; alpha >= 2, no threshold); they give no upper extent bound. Finite energy does not give the hypotheses: int int Gamma^2 dr dz/r <= E_0/(2 pi) becomes int int Gamma_U^2 dR dZ/R <= E_0/(2 pi gamma_n^2 r_n), which diverges.
(h) Level form: f(gamma) = g(A - gamma) - g(A) has f(0) = 0, f'' = g''(w), a_f = MM[g(w)] - g(A) and F_f = MM[Q g(w)] (MM Q = 0). Average 2 nu a_f - nu rho a_f' + nu D_f = F_f in d rho/rho over [rho_0, L]: |g(w)| <= sup|g'| w gives MM[g(w)] -> 0 under saturation, int_{rho_0}^L rho (MM[g(w)])' d rho/rho = [MM g(w)]_{rho_0}^L is bounded, and D_f increases to D_g(infinity); the relation of (c) gives <MM[Q w]>_log = -2 nu A. The measure: mu_r(E) := lim mu_{r,j}(E) is finitely additive, and the thin-strip bound, extended from intervals to Borel sets by outer regularity of each mu_{r,j}, gives mu_r(E) <= C'_r |E| (|E| the Lebesgue measure); for disjoint E_k, mu_r(union_{k > n} E_k) <= C'_r |union_{k > n} E_k| -> 0, so mu_r is countably additive and atomless, and int phi d mu_r = MM[int_0^r R |grad w|^2 phi(w) dR] for continuous phi (uniform approximation by step functions). Since mu_r increases with r, mu := lim mu_r is a countably additive extended measure; D_f(r) = int g'' d mu_r, D_g(infinity) = int g'' d mu by monotone convergence, and for g_v(w) := (w - v)_+^2/2, C^1 approximation at fixed r and the absence of atoms give D_{g_v}(r) = mu_r((v, W]). So the level balance reads <MM[Q (w - v)_+^2/2]>_log = nu mu((v, W]) - nu (A - v)_+^2. Two-level data: int g dm = (2 nu A/W_2)(g(0) - g(W_2)), and by g(x) = int_0^x (x - v) g''(v) dv, -2 nu g(A) + nu int g'' d mu = -(2 nu A/W_2) int_0^{W_2} (W_2 - v) g''(v) dv = -(2 nu A/W_2) g(W_2); for f = (gamma - theta)_+^3 with A - W_2 < theta < A, int f(A - v) dm = (2 nu A/W_2) f(A). Fixed period: with Gammabar the period mean, MM[Q Gamma] = MM[Q (Gamma - Gammabar)], so |Q| <= B rho and the Wirtinger inequality give MM[Q Gamma]^2 <= B^2 rho^2 (P/(2 pi))^2 MM[(d_Z Gamma)^2] <= (B^2 P^2/(8 pi^2)) rho D'(rho), D := D_{gamma^2}; Cauchy-Schwarz in d rho/rho and <MM[Q Gamma]>_log = 2 nu A give D(L) >= (32 pi^2 nu^2 A^2/(B^2 P^2)) log(L/rho_0)(1 - o(1)); then the gamma^2 identity gives <F_{gamma^2}>_log = infinity, and |F_{gamma^2}| = |MM[Q (Gamma^2 - A^2)]| <= B rho (A + G') d(rho). Momentum: assume P_R and P_Z bounded at each radius, box means of d_Z P_R tending to zero (true when P_R is bounded), and derivative bounds uniform on compact radial ranges, so that MM can be differentiated in R. With C_m := MM[U^R U^Z], B_z := MM U^Z and k := MM P_Z (local notation): radial momentum and U^R d_R U^R + U^Z d_Z U^R = R^{-1} d_R(R (U^R)^2) + d_Z(U^R U^Z) give the radial balance; angular momentum gives R^{-2}(R^2 MM[U^R U^theta])' = nu ((MM U^theta)'' + (MM U^theta)'/R - MM U^theta/R^2) with MM U^theta = a/R and R^2 MM[U^R U^theta] = -MM[Q Gamma_U], so (MM[Q Gamma_U])' = -nu (R a'' - a'), and a = O(R^2), MM[Q Gamma_U] = O(R^4) give (c); k' = MM[d_Z P_R] = 0, and averaged axial momentum integrated from the axis gives C_m = nu B_z' - k R/2, so |k| R/2 <= K^2 + nu D_1 for all R and k = 0; then e = MM eta = -B_z'/R and, by integration by parts in Z and incompressibility, h_eta = MM[U^R d_R U^Z] = C_m' + C_m/R = -nu (R e' + 2 e), and h_eta = O(R^2) at the axis gives e(0) = 0. Periodic momentum field (redefining a_0, eps): t_1 := (1 + R^2)^{1/4}, beta = 16 nu, Psi = beta R^2 t_1^{-3} cos Z, a_0 = A (1 - 1/t_1), eps = A nu (t_1 - 1)(4 t_1^3 + 3 t_1^2 + 2 t_1 + 1)/(beta t_1^2 (t_1 + 1)(t_1^2 + 1)), Gamma = a_0 - eps sin Z; then 3A/(4 t_1) <= A - Gamma <= 5A/(4 t_1), ||P||_infty <= (13/4) beta^2 + 7 A^2, ||grad P||_infty <= (37/2) beta^2 + 90 A^2, MM[Q Gamma] -> 2 nu A, F_f/a_f -> 6 nu A/(A - theta), R^2 times the circulation residual at Z = 0 tends to 2 nu A and R^{3/2} times the vorticity residual at Z = 0 to nu beta. Recurrence: for 0 < delta < A - theta, delta^3 1_{Gamma_U > theta + delta} <= (Gamma_U - theta)_+^3 <= delta^3 + (A - theta)^3 1_{Gamma_U > theta + delta}; with alpha := a_f(r_*) and delta^3 = alpha/2 the superlevel set has Banach density at least alpha/(2 (A - theta)^3), and for theta = 0, D_f(r_*) >= 4 alpha/log(4 D_1^3 r_*^6/alpha). Scale: U(lambda y, lambda s) has viscosity nu/lambda, so the endpoint hypothesis of (e) is of first order in nu/lambda; an additive o(nu/lambda) error in the correlation would suffice once positive mass is known, an unspecified o(1) error would not.
(i) Details of the proof of the main theorem (notation of the statement). (1) In each box Q_rho = d_Z(Phi_rho - c_rho), and integration by parts in Z gives <Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)^2>_B = -<2 (Phi_rho - c_rho) Gamma_U d_Z Gamma_U>_B + e_B with |e_B| <= (K rho^2/2 + sup|c_rho|) G'^2/Lambda. For bounded x_j, y_j >= 0, positivity of the Banach limit and x^{1/2} y^{1/2} <= (t x + y/t)/2, minimized over t > 0, give lim (x_j y_j)^{1/2} <= (lim x_j)^{1/2} (lim y_j)^{1/2}. With Cauchy-Schwarz in each box and (H) this yields |F_2| <= 2 G' (MM[(Phi_rho - c_rho)^2])^{1/2} delta_Z^{1/2} <= 2 G' nu rho C(rho) delta_Z^{1/2} for rho >= rho_0, and a' = MM[2 Gamma_U d_R Gamma_U] (difference quotients converge uniformly by the second-derivative bounds) with |a'| <= 2 G' delta_R^{1/2}. (2) R -> |grad Gamma_U(R, Z, tau)|^2 is Lipschitz on [0, rho] uniformly in (Z, tau), so MM commutes with the Riemann sums of the radial integral: delta_R + delta_Z is continuous, D is C^1 and nondecreasing, D-dot = 2 rho^2 (delta_R + delta_Z), and D(rho) <= rho^2 sup_{R <= rho} |grad Gamma_U|^2. (3) Since a >= 0, the gamma^2 identity gives, for s >= log rho_0, D <= 2a + D = a-dot + F_2/nu <= 2 G' rho (delta_R^{1/2} + C delta_Z^{1/2}) <= 2 G' rho (1 + C^2)^{1/2} (delta_R + delta_Z)^{1/2} = 2^{1/2} G' (1 + C^2)^{1/2} (D-dot)^{1/2}. (4) If D(s_1) > 0 for some s_1 >= log rho_0, then D > 0 on [s_1, infinity) and d(1/D)/ds = -D-dot/D^2 <= -1/(2 G'^2 (1 + C(e^s)^2)), so 0 < 1/D(s) <= 1/D(s_1) - (2 G'^2)^{-1} int_{s_1}^s du/(1 + C(e^u)^2), whose right side tends to -infinity. Hence D = 0 on [rho_0, infinity), and on [0, infinity) by monotonicity; so delta_Z = 0, (1) with c_rho = 0 and |Phi_rho| <= K rho^2/2 gives F_2 = 0 at every radius, the identity gives (a/rho^2)' = 0, and 0 <= a <= G'^2 forces a = 0. For theta >= 0, 0 <= (gamma - theta)_+^2 <= gamma^2, so that weight has zero mass as well. Variants: for one fixed axial period P with |U^R| <= B, the period mean c_rho(tau) of Phi_rho(., tau) gives |Phi_rho - c_rho| <= P sup|Q_rho| <= P B rho, so C = P B/nu; if (H) holds on radii with bounded gaps, |Phi_r - Phi_R| <= K |r^2 - R^2|/2 gives it at all large radii with a larger constant. A bound along an arbitrary unbounded sequence of radii does not suffice: the two-mode construction below with exponent 1/64 in place of 1/8, its weights multiplied on |R - R_j| < R_j^{3/4}, R_j = 2^{64 j}, by a smooth factor equal to R_j^{-1/64} near R_j, has MM[Phi_{R_j}^2] = nu^2 R_j^2/2 and an exact saturating circulation; this example was read, not rechecked. The kinematic model of (f) and the periodic momentum field of (h) satisfy (H) (the latter with C^2 = 256 sqrt3/9) and have MM[Gamma^2] -> A^2 > 0; this is possible only because both violate the gamma^2 identity, through which the circulation equation enters. Physical boxes: Cauchy-Schwarz and the energy inequality give int psi(r, z, t)^2 dz <= r^2 E_0/(4 pi) for psi := int_0^r s u^z ds, hence, in the normalized variables of U_n, <Phi_{n,rho}^2>_B <= nu_n^2 rho^2 E_0/(8 pi Lambda r_n), which is (H) on boxes of physical half-length Lambda r_n >= E_0/(8 pi C^2). The argument on such boxes needs the box errors below D_b/4 up to radius rho_1 exp(c G'^2 (C^2 + 1)/D_b), D_b the box-mean Dirichlet energy at rho_1 (local notation), while |grad Gamma|^2 <= 2 r^2 |grad u|^2 and the energy inequality give only D_b <= 4 C^2 rho_1^2/(gamma_n T), the order of the time-boundary error G'^2 rho^2/T; for the heat example Gamma = r^2 h_t^{-5/2} e^{-(r^2 + z^2)/(4 h_t)}, h_t = 1 + t, Phi = 0 and the initial inventory carries all the dissipation.
Constructions (exact steady solutions of the scalar circulation equation only). Let lambda be the flat step from 0 to 1 obtained by normalizing int_0^t e^{-1/[u(1-u)]} du on [0, 1], and k_j = 2^{-j} (local notation). Power construction: for x = (1/8) log_2 R, n = floor(x), t = x - n, v_n = R^{1/8} k_n cos((pi/2) lambda(t)), v_{n+1} = R^{1/8} k_{n+1} sin((pi/2) lambda(t)), all other v_j = 0, so sum_j |v_j| <= 3 and sum_j v_j^2/k_j^2 = R^{1/4}; Psi = nu lambda(2R/R_0 - 1) R sum_j (v_j/k_j) sin(k_j Z), so b = 0 for R <= R_0/2 and b^R = -nu V, V := sum_j v_j cos(k_j Z), for R >= R_0. With H := R^{-1/8}, chi := sum_j (v_j/k_j^2) cos(k_j Z) (so chi_ZZ = -V) and zeta the cosine polynomial whose Z-second derivative is minus the deviation from its period mean of V (chi H'' + chi_R H') - nu^{-1} b^Z chi_Z H' (all its frequencies lie between R^{-1/8}/2 and 4 R^{-1/8}, so there are no small divisors), the function sigma := H + chi H' + zeta (local notation) satisfies exactly nu^{-1} calA sigma = (H/R^2)(17/64 - R^{1/4}/128) + E, the main term being the period-averaged operator (two-mode orthogonality), with |E| <= 10^42 H R^{-5/2}; hence, for R >= R_0, H/2 <= sigma <= 3H/2 and nu^{-1} calA sigma <= -H R^{-7/4}/256. The barriers 1 - e^{-2 R^2} (calA of it is (-4 nu R - b^R) times its R-derivative, hence <= 0) and (1 - c sigma)_+ on R >= R_0, 0 below, with c = 4 R_0^{1/8} (so c sigma >= 2 at R_0), the exhaustion of (f) on (1/j, j) x (-j, j), and the axis argument (q_0 = g/R^2 in [0, 2] is Delta_5-harmonic where b = 0; tube errors O(eps^2), O(eps^3)) give Gamma = A g and the bounds of the statement; at each radius the active modes are commensurable, so MM is the period mean, and Q(R_n, 0) = nu R_n at R_n = 2^{8n}. Logarithmic construction: s = log R >= 4, x = log_2 s, v_n = s k_n cos((pi/2) lambda(t)), v_{n+1} = s k_{n+1} sin((pi/2) lambda(t)) (so 1/(2s) <= k_j <= 2/s, sum_j |v_j| <= 3, sum_j v_j^2/k_j^2 = s^2), and the same correctors with H = H(s); the averaged operator becomes R^{-2} [(1 + d) H_ss + (d_s - 2) H_s] with d := s^2/2 (local notation), equal to (H/R^2)(-1/8 + 1/s + 3/(4 s^2)) <= -H/(16 R^2) for H = s^{-1/2} and s >= 20, and |sigma - H| <= K_1 H s^{29}/R, |E| <= K_1 H s^{35}/R^3 for a fixed constant K_1. The same barriers (c = 4/H(s_0), s_0 = log R_0) and exhaustion give 0 <= A - Gamma <= 6A (log R_0/log R)^{1/2} and MM[Phi_R^2] = nu^2 R^2 (log R)^2/2. At R_n = exp(2^n), Z_n = pi log R_n/2, eta = nu (k^2 R^2 + 1)(log R) sin(kZ)/R^3 with k = 1/log R_n, the transport vanishes and R_n (log R_n)^3 times the vorticity residual tends to nu^2; the residual divided by nu k^2 is comparable to eta, so no perturbative conclusion follows. General p in (1/2, 1]: v_j = s^p k_j times the same weights (bounded drift because p <= 1), d := s^{2p}/2, J := int_4^s dt/(1 + d), w_p := e^{4J}/(1 + d) and H := int_s^infinity w_p = (e^{4J(infinity)} - e^{4J(s)})/4. From w_p' = w_p (4 - d_s)/(1 + d) one gets (1 + d) H_ss + (d_s - 2) H_s = -2 w_p exactly; H is finite and tends to 0 iff int^infinity ds/(1 + d) < infinity, i.e. p > 1/2, and then H ~ (2 e^{4J(infinity)}/(2p - 1)) s^{1 - 2p} and w_p/H ~ (2p - 1)/s. For fixed p, sum_j |d_R^m v_j| <= C_p R^{-m} (m <= 3), |d_R^m H| <= C_p H R^{-m} (m <= 4), |sigma - H| <= C_p H s^{29}/R and |E| <= C_p H s^{35}/R^3, which is dominated by 2 w_p/R^2 for R >= R_0(p); MM[Phi_R^2] = nu^2 R^2 s^{2p}/2, i.e. (H) with C(rho) = (log rho)^p. Dips: with s_j = 2^j, delta_j = s_j^{-2}, a smooth bump vartheta supported in [-1, 1], equal to 1 on [-1/2, 1/2], with values in [0, 1], and beta_d(s) := 1 - sum_{j >= 3} vartheta((s - s_j)/delta_j) (local notation), multiply the v_j of the logarithmic construction by beta_d(s) and take d := beta_d^2 s^2/2 with the same J, w_p, H: the dips have total length 1/24, J(infinity) <= 13/24, 1/s <= H <= 60/s and -2 w_p <= -H/(30 s), and the exact saturating solution has A - Gamma <= 6A H(s)/H(s_0); C(R) := (log R) beta_d(log R) + R^{-1} satisfies MM[Phi_R^2] <= nu^2 R^2 C(R)^2 and int^infinity C^{-2} dR/R = infinity, while int_4^infinity ds/(1 + C(e^s)^2) <= 7/24.
Profiles. For psi = nu beta R^2 f(x), x = Z/R^2, the steady vorticity equation without swirl forcing has residual 8 beta nu^2 x E_1(f) R^{-4} + O(R^{-6}) at fixed x, and the swirl forcing is O(R^{-6}) when Gamma is a function of x to leading order. Rigidity: I' = x E_1(f) by direct differentiation. With t = 1/x and F(t) = f(1/t), t^2 F'(t) = -f'(1/t), Y = (t^2 F')' = x^2 f'', and d/dx = -t^2 d/dt turns I into -2 Y' - beta F Y. On (0, 1] the coefficient of Y' + (beta/2) F Y = -I/2 is bounded, so Y = Y(0) + O(t), t^2 F' = V_0 + Y(0) t + O(t^2) and F = -V_0/t + Y(0) log t + O(1); boundedness gives V_0 = Y(0) = 0, i.e. Y(0) = 0 and lim_{t down 0} t^2 F'(t) = 0. Then Y(t) = -(I/2) int_0^t exp(-(beta/2) int_u^t F) du, and the part u in [t - 1, t] gives |Y(t)| >= c := |I| e^{-B_F}/2 for t >= 1, B_F := |beta| sup|F|/2 (local notation); t^2 F'(t) = int_0^t Y gives |F'(t)| >= c (t - 1)/t^2 >= c/(2t) for t >= 2, with a fixed sign, so |F(t) - F(2)| >= (c/2) log(t/2). If I = 0, uniqueness for the linear equation with Y(0) = 0 gives Y = 0, so f'' = 0 and the bounded affine f is constant. For x < 0 apply this to f(-x), which solves the equation with beta replaced by -beta. Inner ansatz: for Psi = nu R^2 h(y), U^R = -nu h', U^Z = nu (2h - y h') and eta = -nu R^{-2} A_h, and for Gamma a function of y the full steady vorticity equation is -2 nu^2 R^{-3} (h A_h)' + nu^2 R^{-4} [(1 + y^2) A_h'' + 3 y A_h'] - R^{-5} (Gamma^2)' = 0. If h A_h = k is not 0, then h'(y)/(1 + y^2)^{1/2} = -int_y^infinity k h(u)^{-1} (1 + u^2)^{-3/2} du has a fixed sign and modulus at least (|k|/sup|h|) int_y^infinity (1 + u^2)^{-3/2} du, which gives |h'(y)| >= c/y for large y. If k = 0, A_h = 0 on the open set {h != 0} and on the interior of {h = 0}, whose union is dense, hence everywhere by continuity.

(j) Phi_rho - rho^2 m/2 = int_0^rho s f_s ds with f_s := U^Z(s, ., .) - m. Since |d_s U^Z| <= D_1, the Riemann sums sum_i w_i f_{s_i} (w_i = s_i Delta s) converge to it uniformly in (Z, tau), and |x^2 - y^2| <= |x - y| (|x| + |y|) shows that MM of the squares converges. For each finite sum, Cauchy-Schwarz in each box and MM[x^{1/2} y^{1/2}] <= MM[x]^{1/2} MM[y]^{1/2} for box means x, y >= 0 give MM[(sum_i w_i f_{s_i})^2] = sum_{i,k} w_i w_k MM[f_{s_i} f_{s_k}] <= (sum_i w_i e(s_i))^2. The map s -> <f_s^2>_B is Lipschitz uniformly in B (|f_s| <= 2K, |d_s f_s| <= D_1), so e is continuous and the Riemann sums of int_0^rho s e(s) ds converge. With e <= 2K on [0, s_0]: int_0^rho s e ds <= K s_0^2 + nu C_1 rho <= nu (C_1 + K s_0/nu) rho for rho >= s_0.
(k) The two balances are the long-box means of the local energy identities for E_b = ((U^R)^2 + (U^Z)^2)/2 and E_theta = (U^theta)^2/2, integrated over the cylinder of radius R; the exchange term between them is U^R (U^theta)^2/R = U^R Gamma_U^2/R^3, whose cylinder integral per unit axial length is W. With F = MM[Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)^2] and the gamma^2 identity 2 nu a - nu rho a' + nu D = F of (e), W(R) = -int_0^R F s^{-3} ds = nu a(R)/R^2 - nu int_0^R D s^{-3} ds. Expanding |grad(Gamma_U/R)|^2 + Gamma_U^2/R^4 and integrating the cross term gives D_theta(R) = D(R)/(2R^2) + int_0^R D s^{-3} ds - a(R)/R^2. Since |F(s)| <= K G'^2 s, int^infinity D s^{-3} ds < infinity, and int_R^{2R} D s^{-3} ds >= 3 D(R)/(8 R^2) gives D(R)/R^2 -> 0; hence W(infinity) = -nu D_theta(infinity), and |U^R Gamma_U^2| s^{-2} <= K min(K^2, G'^2 s^{-2}) gives nu D_theta(infinity) <= 2 K^2 G'. The kinematic field was checked symbolically (divergence, averages, residuals).
(l) Compactness: the translates are bounded in W^{2,infinity} in space, and the mild formula gives time moduli C h^{1/2} for the field and its gradient on unit intervals (|(e^{nu h Delta} - 1) f| <= C (nu h)^{1/2} ||grad f||_infty, and the kernel of grad e^{nu sigma Delta} P has L^1 norm at most C (nu sigma)^{-1/2}, applied to W.grad W = div(W x W)); Arzela-Ascoli applies, and the mild formulation passes to such limits by the near/far splitting used for Theorem 2. The functional l(f) := MM[f(U(. + zeta e_3, . + s))] on C(X) is positive with l(1) = 1, so Riesz gives mu; invariance follows from |l(f o T) - l(f)| <= 2 ||f||_infty lim |B_j sym. diff. (B_j + (zeta', s'))|/|B_j| = 0. If a = 0 at every radius, invariance gives int Gamma_W(rho, zeta, s)^2 d mu = 0 for all rational rho, zeta and s <= 0, so mu-almost every W has Gamma_W = 0; such W is a bounded ancient mild axisymmetric solution without swirl, hence W = (0, 0, b_3(tau)) by Theorem 5.2 of [KNSS], and a spatially constant mild solution is constant in time (the nonlinear term vanishes). For the density statement use the continuous functional g(W) := min{1, (sup_window |grad W| - eps/2)_+/(eps/2)}, which equals 1 on the set in question and vanishes on constants, in both directions; a nonconstant W in X has grad W != 0 somewhere. The ergodic clause uses Krein-Milman (if all extreme invariant measures were concentrated on the closed set of constants, every invariant measure would be) and, for generic members, the pointwise ergodic theorem along tempered Folner sequences [Lin], used as stated. Mild closedness: drop the last eps of the Duhamel integral (error C K^2 (eps/nu)^{1/2}) and split the convolution into a large ball, where the convergence is local, and its complement, where the Oseen tails are integrable; this excludes a parasitic time-dependent constant. The dichotomy concerns all invariant probabilities: if one gives positive integral to a nonnegative continuous f vanishing on constants, some finite-box average of f at a member of X, and hence at a translate of U, is positive, and expanding boxes give a family with positive mean. The backward shifts are injective on X by forward mild uniqueness, so on the (invariant) support of an invariant measure they are homeomorphisms, which gives the eternal extension and an R^2 action, to which the pointwise ergodic theorem applies along the dyadic boxes [-2^j, 2^j] x [-2^j, 0]. For the necessary condition in (S1): stationarity of p_Z and logarithmic axial growth of the canonical pressure give E p_Z = 0; the axial spectral measures satisfy d sigma_Q = k^2 d sigma_Phi, so V(R) = int_{k != 0} k^{-2} d sigma_{Q, R}(k); axial integration by parts gives E(Q Gamma^2) = -E[(Phi_R - Pi_Z Phi_R) 2 Gamma Gamma_Z], and the Riccati argument of (i) with C(R)^2 = V(R)/(nu^2 R^2) gives the integral condition (positive mass forces positive Dirichlet integral D(R_*)). A stationary axially ergodic kinematic field (annular stream-function bumps with independent phases and incommensurable frequencies k_n = l_n/L_n^2) has bounded variance of Q and flux variance of order R^4, and satisfies the mean axial momentum relation and the mean meridional-vorticity relation (but not every averaged energy budget), so these relations, stationarity and boundedness do not control V; it is not a Navier-Stokes solution. For the radial escape: the radial drift (Q - nu)/R = -U^R - nu/R is at most K, so from R <= rho the comparison R_h <= rho + K h + (2 nu)^{1/2}(B_h - B_0) gives absorption within time h with probability at least p_{rho, h}, uniformly in the starting point; at stopping times of returns to {R <= rho} separated by h the conditional survival probability is at most 1 - p_{rho, h}, so infinitely many returns have probability zero on survival, and the same comparison gives p_{rho, h} P(zeta > s, R_s <= rho) <= P(zeta > s) - P(zeta > s + h), which integrates to the occupation bound. A three-dimensional stationary energy argument (nu E|grad W|^2 = 0) is not available because homogeneity holds only along the axis and the radial energy flux of (k) can feed the dissipation.
Not reproduced: the elliptic existence, comparison and interior estimates behind the monotone exhaustions and the axis removability (only the barriers and their inequalities are given), the derivation of the corrector error bounds and of the vorticity residuals in (f) and (i), the pressure bounds of the periodic momentum field in (h) (checked only by the script of the research answer that supplied the field), the expansions of the vorticity equation for the long pattern and the inner ansatz, the convergence in law of sampled translates in (h), and the sequence example in (i); the error constant 10^42 of the power construction and the sequence example were only read, not rechecked, and the momentum, recurrence and periodic-field parts of (h) were not independently reviewed. The complete argument is not reproduced in this report; this item is not checkable from the report alone.

Status: proof sketch with key estimates (the main theorem of (i), the long-pattern rigidity and the inner-ansatz statement of (i) proved here); source: own derivation ((a)-(e) and (g), with the sharper lower bounds of (d) and the endpoint and gap statements of (e) supplied in adversarial audit rounds 15 and 16 and rechecked by the author; in (h) the level form, the two-level data and the fixed-period bound; in (i) the main theorem with its variants and the physical-box remark, and the general-p extension), research answer checked by the author ((f): research answer res11; (h) momentum balances, recurrence and periodic momentum field: research answer res12, with the pressure bounds of that field checked only by the script of that answer; (i) power construction: research answer res13; logarithmic construction and dip example: research answer res14; long-pattern rigidity and inner ansatz: research answer res15); verification: adversarial audit rounds 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 21, 22; checked by scripts; read only (the momentum, recurrence and periodic-field parts of (h), not audited; the error constant 10^42 of the power construction and the sequence example in (i)). Item (j): own derivation, checked in adversarial audit round 22. Item (l): own derivation, audited in research answer res18, whose corrections (topology, mild closedness, precise density, dichotomy over all invariant measures, eternal extension on supports), whose necessary condition in case (S1) and whose radial-escape and occupation statements were checked by the author by hand (the pathwise inflow statement is the author's addition, checked in adversarial audit round 22); the kinematic field was checked by rerunning the symbolic script of res18; the pointwise ergodic theorem [Lin] is used as stated. Item (k): research answer res16 checked by the author by hand and with a script; the full Navier-Stokes hypothesis, the definitions of the densities and fluxes and the pressure-centring step were supplied in adversarial audit round 22 and checked by the author (the centring through R u = -d_Z Phi_R), and the residuals of the kinematic field were checked with a script

### 1.3 What was not proved

The exclusion of Case 1 was not proved, and no fixed-datum counterexample was found. Theorems 4 and 5 are conditional Liouville theorems whose hypotheses were not derived. Theorem 9 removes Type I gradient growth under (A) but does not reach the swirl records: it gives a limsup statement for the global gradient, does not locate the large gradients, and does not relate them to (r_n, z_n, t_n). Proposition 12 is a rigidity statement under a backward-decay hypothesis that was not derived. Theorem 13 splits the remaining scenario into two branches and excludes neither; Proposition 14 proves a necessary condition for branch (II); no incompatible upper bound was established; and Proposition 15 refines the limits without excluding them. Propositions 16 to 18 are exact identities and necessary conditions; the absorption criteria of Proposition 16 are conditional on a global inflow bound or a stream-function smallness that was not derived (the stream-function smallness is false at inviscid gradient records by Proposition 17). Proposition 19(b) is a conditional exclusion whose hypothesis (for some constant c_0, sup_{tau <= 0} ||(psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta||_{BMO(R^3)} < infinity for the meridional vector potential of the limit) fails for every member of LL and was not derived from the fixed trajectory; Proposition 19(c), (d) and Proposition 20 are necessary conditions. Proposition 21(a), (b) are necessary conditions. Proposition 21(c) is a conditional exclusion whose compression hypothesis was not derived; its physical threshold 2 - beta_0 is strictly below 2 and approaches the exterior-comparison endpoint of Theorem 5 as beta_0 decreases to 0. Proposition 22 consists of identities and necessary conditions. Its part (d) gives an exact local Navier-Stokes model that satisfies the weak normalized terminal-maximum conditions, bounded circulation and derivatives, and the velocity bound on the ellipsoid of part (d), for compatible constants; it does not realize first crossings of increasing levels or the global Case-1 hypotheses, so an exclusion needs some information not shared by this local model (examples are the velocity bound elsewhere, finite energy, the canonical pressure, the tail or the growth of the records, with compatible constants). Proposition 23(a) is a saturation estimate, 23(b) a conditional Liouville statement together with an inflow witness, and 23(c) a physical necessary condition; the inflow bound that would exclude Case 1 was not derived. Proposition 24 consists of identities, necessary conditions and conditional residence or growth statements; it shows that the local material phenomena at a record are consistent with the local identities, and that one proposed target (small unweighted window dissipation) is false. Proposition 25 refines the far-field inflow witness, gives a flux bound and a stronger sufficient weighted Biot-Savart criterion, shows by explicit scalar models that the listed scalar information is consistent with strong far-field inflow, shows that the far-field bound, posed for the whole class LL, is equivalent to the Liouville problem for LL, and gives an exact exterior solution with strong far-field inflow; it excludes nothing. Proposition 26 consists of identities, explicit lower bounds for the averaged circulation deficit, an averaged threshold theorem at the critical value 2 nu whose conclusion is only vanishing of weighted box means, and a conditional exclusion (averaged saturation together with a critical one-sided outflow bound) whose hypotheses were not derived. The work was done in five work periods. The third added necessary conditions, tests and reformulations (Proposition 23(f), (g), the later entries of sec. 3.12 and the whole-line structure of the eternal limits and the necessary conditions of the uniformly viscous scenario in sec. 3.7) but no new viable route. The fourth added Proposition 11(iv)-(viii), the L^infty budget of Proposition 14 and Proposition 18(f)-(h), which localize and quantify, under the hypotheses and at the scales stated there, what branch (II) requires (a high-level potential-vorticity source near the axis, a q^4 cost on a level band, and a weighted inflow), again without a new viable route. The fifth added a literature update (sec. 3.9) and the tests of fixed-trajectory information in branch (I) and of the time-integrated inflow in branch (II) described in sec. 4(6)(xix) and 4(7), and first-step tests of mechanisms of other types (sec. 4(9)), again without a new viable route. None of the last three periods produced an estimate excluding either branch of Theorem 13 or a qualifying counterexample; the first missing implication stated at the beginning of this report is unchanged in kind and sharper in branch (II).

## 2. Method

1. Re-derivation. The coupled system for Gamma, q = u^theta/r, eta = omega^theta/r and the 5D stream potential phi, the record identity, and the scaling identities were re-derived and checked symbolically (sympy). The 5D divergence div_5 b = 2u^r/r (not zero) was retained throughout.
2. Extraction. Mild formulation with Oseen kernel bounds |grad e^{nu h Delta} P|(y) <= C(|y| + sqrt(nu h))^{-4}, near/far splitting of the pressure with the far part controlled by (TAIL), a restart-time bootstrap for higher derivatives, and Arzela-Ascoli in the order C_tau C^m_y, then pressure gradients, then time derivatives, to pass through tau = 0.
3. Reduction. Translation-scaling compactness of bounded ancient solutions and the strong maximum principle, to identify exactly what the listed limit properties can give.
4. Route A. Five-dimensional Newton potential of eta with an axial-integration kernel bound, a Liouville theorem for bounded harmonic remainders, and the KNSS theorem for solutions with |u| <= C/r.
5. Route B. Feller scale function for the radial part of the circulation operator L = d_rr - (1/r) d_r + d_zz with a Lipschitz envelope of the inward radial velocity, and a two-step strong maximum principle argument with translated limits.
6. Pressure and (A). Calderon-Zygmund decomposition of grad^2 p at the three scales D/H, M/D and infinity using only historical norms at the same time t; the Riccati inequality for the running maximum of |grad u|.
7. Theorem 9. Selection of running minima of the length l = (T_* - t) M and of gradient record times; rescaling at the Euler length; transport of the potential vorticity omega^theta/r along characteristics with backward decay and a harmonic terminal field, following Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol; in the complementary case an excursion lemma for monotone histories gives global Type I velocity, which is excluded by Chen, Strain, Tsai and Yau together with far-field epsilon-regularity.
8. Obstructions. Explicit model histories and explicit steady Euler and drift fields, used only to test whether an implication can hold in the generality in which it was attempted; none is a fixed-datum NS counterexample.
9. Computations. python3 (sympy, numpy, scipy), single-threaded: symbolic verification of identities and of the explicit examples and numerical sanity checks. No computation is part of a proof.
10. Literature. Statements of cited theorems were read in the original sources where retrievable; items only known through summaries are marked as such.
11. Inviscid rigidity (Proposition 12). Backward transport of a set where the potential vorticity is bounded below, by a flow that preserves three-dimensional volume, against the pointwise bound |omega^theta| <= sqrt2 |grad V| and the linear growth of the axial extent.
12. Scale dichotomy (Theorem 13). Taylor expansion of the swirl component along a line through the axis (where it is odd) at the point where the historical swirl maximum is attained, combined with (A) and the circulation bound; a compactness-contradiction argument at viscous gradient records using [KNSS, Theorem 5.2 and Remark 6.1] for swirl-free limits and [KNSS, Theorem 5.1] for receding-axis limits.
13. Critical mass (Proposition 14). A heat-semigroup argument gives a lower bound c_0(C_*) for the vorticity of the rescaled field at a gradient record (the gradient equals 1 and the Hessian is at most C_*); the swirl part (U - P U(P .))/2, P the reflection x_2 -> -x_2, has small vorticity by the Landau inequality when S/M is small; the azimuthal vorticity is then at least c_0/2 at a point and, being Lipschitz with constant sqrt2 C_*, on a disc, and an exact change of variables converts the meridional L^1 norm into kappa N.
14. Level selection (Proposition 15). An exact identity for (T_* - tau_k) L_k along first hitting times of geometric levels L_k shows that the normalized forward durations are unbounded when (T_* - t) D(t) is; the forward bounds come from the Landau inequality at s_n and (A) at b_n. The swirl-level version of the selection is from a research answer (sec. 5).
15. Critical weighted energies (Propositions 16 to 18). Multiplication of the q equation by p |q|^{p-2} q r^{2p-3}, integration in the 3D measure with the adjoint relation between the circulation operator and the 5D Laplacian, the weighted Hardy inequality on (0, infinity) with weight r^{2p-2}, and integration by parts of the inflow term through the stream function; symbolic check of the density identity and numerical evaluation of the stream-function threshold. The stream-function lower bound uses one-dimensional Landau inequalities along axial lines and the relation r psi^theta = int_0^r rho u^z d rho. The divergence of the inflow integral uses the C^{2,1} convergence of Theorem 2 on disjoint record cylinders.
16. Stream-function BMO form (Proposition 19). Superposition of circular ring potentials (all dilates and translates of one), a direct estimate of the mean oscillation of the unit ring potential near the ring (logarithmic), at intermediate distance (gradient bound) and at large scales (dipole decay), identification with the stream function through the Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality and a harmonic Liouville argument, lower semicontinuity of BMO under local uniform convergence, a Galilean change of frame, and the scale-invariant Hoelder estimate of Lei and Zhang applied on cylinders of every size.
17. Shrinking cylinders (Proposition 20). Seregin's exclusion of Type I singular points for axisymmetric suitable weak solutions and his local BMO criterion [Se20], applied at the singular point after a rescaling chosen from the assumed local bound; a near/far dyadic estimate for the BMO norm of the swirl potential using |u^theta| <= G/r; a cutoff decomposition of the meridional potential with the far part estimated from the energy; at the limit level and at the record tops, the Lei-Zhang Hoelder estimate for the circulation with the meridional field as the hollowed-energy part, applied at every scale (limit) or at the regular tops (records), and nesting of cylinders for the many-scale statement.
18. Circulation escape (Proposition 21). The one-sided form of the circulation barrier, applied with the local circulation supremum at the singular point. A Krylov-Safonov Harnack chain for sup Gamma - Gamma, running from a point near the axis to the point considered. First-crossing records of weighted circulations, with the zero-order coefficient of their equation. Flux identities using that the dx-adjoint of L is the 5D Laplacian Delta_5, and the 5D fundamental solution |X|^{-3}.
19. Localized pressure and strain (Proposition 22). The axisymmetric pressure Poisson equation with its swirl source in divergence form and the Newton kernel restricted to the axis; second-order conditions at the swirl maximum; the incompressibility identity for the disk below the record; the radial and axial momentum equations expanded at the axis; the Burgers vortex as an exact local model.
20. Ancient Liouville hypotheses (Proposition 23). Scaling tests for each published hypothesis (scale invariance, and whether the fixed trajectory supplies it at scales between r_n and 1); a comparison on a moving disk with an explicit quartic subsolution for the circulation deficit; a two-dimensional radial heat barrier on annuli with a smooth ramp, the maximum principle for its radial derivative, and a cosh barrier in Z; the exterior line sink as an exact sharpness example; an arXiv API search on 2026-10-16 for further Liouville theorems (a script, including an explicit finite-difference solution of the radial heat problem as a numerical sanity check).
21. Material lines and vorticity creation (Proposition 24). Integration of the particle and circulation equations along backward paths with C^{2,1} convergence on a short window; Stokes' theorem on instantaneous flat disks; the meridional Jacobian; Taylor expansion on the axis; the adjoint identity between L and Delta_5 with its axis term; Z-averaging of the angular equation for the nonvanishing of the source; scaling of the dissipation.
22. Far-field inflow (Proposition 25). The comparison of Proposition 23(b) with the sign of the radial derivative at the maximum; the axial integral of the incompressibility relation; the Biot-Savart kernel averaged over the angle; explicit exterior and whole-space drift models with sub- and supersolution barriers and monotone exhaustion; asymptotic evaluation of the vorticity equation for the drift model; the reduction of the limit-level statement to Theorem 3; the exact slender residual, and symbolic verification of the Landau flow with potential swirl.
23. Averaged circulation flux (Proposition 26). Integration of the circulation equation over cylinders, box averages with explicit errors, a Banach limit, and a first-order radial equation for the averaged profile, integrating factors, and the same for convex functions of the circulation, and explicit scalar and kinematic models with symbolic and high-precision numerical evaluation of the equation residuals, and the level-set form of the convex hierarchy with an explicit two-level solution, the Wirtinger bound for periodic fields and the averaged momentum balances, and the critical axial-flux iteration, its fixed-data scalings and the averaged supersolution identity and vorticity residual of the first saturating construction of Proposition 26(i), and the leading-order vorticity and circulation equations for axially long far-field patterns with their linearization, and the first integral and reciprocal-variable form of the long-pattern equation used in the rigidity proof, and the averaged energy budgets and the kinematic field of Proposition 26(k), the kinematic stationary field of Proposition 26(l), the exponent bookkeeping of the pre-limit tests in sec. 4, and the vorticity identity behind the steady head equation and the steady test field of sec. 4.


## 3. Findings

### 3.1 The limit problem is the bounded-circulation Liouville problem

The record identity is a pointwise statement at a space-time maximum of u^theta: d_t u^theta >= 0, vanishing first derivatives and nonpositive Laplacian give u^r S_n/r_n <= -S_n/r_n^2. Under (K) and (TAIL) the normalized fields converge through the terminal time to a bounded ancient mild solution with viscosity nu_* in [1/G, (K^2 - 1)^{1/2}] that retains the record (Theorems 1 and 2). (TAIL) is used only for the approximants; for the limit it is automatic, since sup_tau int_{|y| > L} |U|^2 |y|^{-4} dy <= 4 pi K^2/L. Finite physical energy gives int |U_n(tau)|^2 dy <= E_0/(gamma_n^2 r_n), which tends to infinity, so it gives no uniform global L^2 bound on U.

Theorem 3 shows that every extracted limit gives an LL solution after normalization and that, conversely, every LL solution has a member of its translation-scaling closure with the listed normalized properties for some admissible parameters (this is not a realization theorem for the fixed physical datum). The swirl maximum of any W in LL can be moved, by translation compactness and the strong maximum principle applied to the angular equation, to a normalized point where the record conditions hold. Hence an exclusion argument that uses only the listed limit properties would establish that LL is empty. Information that is not visibly retained by the abstract limit class includes (A) with its constant C_*, finite energy of the fixed trajectory, and the approximants themselves.

### 3.2 Two conditional Liouville theorems for LL

Theorem 4 (Route A). The proof writes eta_W as a bounded function on R^5 with |eta| <= min(C, A_eta |y'|^{-3}). Its 5D Newton potential phi_0 = N_5 * eta is bounded with |phi_0| <= C/R and |grad phi_0| <= C/R^2 for R >= 2 (axial integration of the kernel, int (d^2 + s^2)^{-3/2} ds = 2/d^2, reduces to R^4 integrals that converge thanks to the R^{-3} decay). The field b_0 = curl(r phi_0 e_theta) has the same vorticity as the meridional velocity V, so V - b_0 is bounded, divergence free and curl free, hence c(tau) e_3. Translates to radial infinity converge to spatially constant mild solutions, so c is constant; after the Galilean change of frame by c_0 the solution satisfies |W'| <= C/R and [KNSS, Theorem 5.3] gives W' = 0. This is unconditional apart from sup R^3 |eta| < infinity, and Corollary: every member of LL violates that bound. A derivation of the bound from properties shared by all of LL would settle the Liouville problem. The local physical target limsup_n sup_{Q_n(L)} r^3 |eta|/gamma_n <= A, uniform in L, is sufficient and weaker than the global criterion of Li and Pan. The elementary weighted maximum-principle argument for R^3 eta is ruled out (Theorem 4, last part); other weighted arguments are not excluded.

Theorem 5 (Route B). The circulation solves d_tau Gamma + V.grad Gamma = nu L Gamma with L = d_RR - (1/R) d_R + d_ZZ, the generator of a diffusion that hits the axis. Replacing the given b by the envelope B(R) = sup_{Z, tau} (-V^R), which is bounded and Lipschitz by the smoothing estimate for bounded mild ancient solutions (sec. 1.1), the scale function sigma(R) = int_{R_0}^R rho exp(-(1/nu) int_{R_0}^rho B) d rho satisfies nu(sigma'' - sigma'/R) = -B sigma' and tends to infinity. Comparison with Gamma - m(R_1) - eps sigma on {R > R_1} and translated limits show that sup_{R >= R_1} Gamma = sup_{R = R_1} Gamma, so a positive supremum would be approached at bounded radius, which the strong maximum principle and Gamma = 0 on the axis exclude. An upward mollification of b is not safe (for b = 2 nu/R the integral diverges, for b + eps it converges); the envelope avoids this. The record identity gives compression -R V^R >= nu only at R = 1; the vertical-flux identity (Proposition 25(b)) shows that the vertical mean of rho V^R over long segments is O(rho^2/h), so an averaged envelope holds trivially but does not feed the comparison argument. O1 (Proposition 8(d)) shows that bounded smooth divergence-free drift alone allows a bounded nonconstant circulation with sup approached at radial infinity.

### 3.3 What (A) gives

Theorem 7 bounds the pressure Hessian at every earlier time s <= t by historical norms at time t only. Under (A) this yields the Riccati inequality for the running maximum D and the lower blow-up rate D(t) >= 1/(C_2 (T_* - t)). It gives magnitude information only: no sign of u^r and no upper bound on D.

At the swirl record, (A) becomes m_n h_n <= C_* d_n^2 between global historical numbers (Proposition 8(a)). If d_n is bounded, the limit U satisfies sup|U| <= m, sup|grad U| <= d, sup|grad^2 U| <= h with m h <= C_* d^2, but the actual sup|grad U| may be strictly smaller than d because the gradient maxima of the approximants can escape. So the denominator of (A) is not retained in the limit, and passing (A) with its constant to U has not been justified. Proposition 8(b) shows that a prescribed constant would be a genuine restriction on LL if LL is nonempty, so a quantitative use of C_* is not excluded by the reduction; we did not find one.

Rescaling at gradient records instead of swirl records (Proposition 8(c)) keeps (A) as a global bound, |grad^2 V| <= C_*, but in general loses the swirl record. Theorem 13 shows what (A) gives about the relation between the two kinds of records; see sec. 3.6.

### 3.4 Theorem 9: (A) excludes Type I gradient growth

Assume (A) and the Type I hypothesis (TI): D(t) <= C_I/(T_* - t) on [t_I, T_*). Let l(t) = (T_* - t) M(t), f(t) = l(t)^2/(T_* - t) and nu(t) = 1/f(t).
 Basic facts. Leray's lower bound gives f >= c_L^2. The interpolation ||u||_infty <= c ||u||_2^{2/5} ||grad u||_infty^{3/5} with the energy bound gives l(t) <= c (T_* - t)^{2/5} -> 0, so the last minimum point of l on [t_0, t] is a running-minimum time tending to T_*. Combining (TI) with the Riccati bound of Theorem 7, every running-minimum time tau has a gradient record time s <= tau with T_* - s <= A_0 (T_* - tau), A_0 = C_2 C_I.
 Case (E): f(tau_n) -> infinity along running minima. At the associated gradient record times s_n, rescale at the Euler length l_n = (T_* - s_n) M(s_n) and time T_n = T_* - s_n, with viscosity nu_n = T_n/l_n^2 (local notation: these T_n, nu_n differ from the record quantities of sec. 1.1). The rescaled fields satisfy, on all of R^3 and all rescaled times sigma <= -1 back to t_0: |V_n| <= 1, |grad V_n| <= C_I/|sigma|, |grad^2 V_n| <= A_0 C_* C_I^2/|sigma| (by (A) at each earlier time and the running-minimum property l(t) >= l_n/A_0), R_n |Z_n| <= G nu_n, where R_n is the rescaled distance to the original symmetry axis and Z_n := V_n^theta e_theta the swirl part of V_n with respect to that axis, and |grad V_n(y*_n, -1)| >= 1/C_2, with nu_n -> 0. The swirl gradient at sigma = -1 is O(nu_n^{1/3}), so the meridional gradient stays of size 1/C_2. Adapting the scheme of [CIV, Lemmas 3.2-3.3] with these global bounds (the estimates are given after Theorem 9), the potential vorticity (for a receding axis, the planar vorticity) converges off the axis and is weakly transported by the limiting meridional field; characteristics starting off the axis stay off it on finite backward intervals, and the backward decay along them forces the azimuthal vorticity to vanish at sigma = -1, so the limit meridional field at sigma = -1 is bounded, divergence free and curl free, hence constant, contradicting the lower bound on its gradient.
 Case (P): f <= F at all late running minima. The excursion lemma (between consecutive returns of l to its running minimum, monotonicity of M and f >= c_L^2 give f <= F^2/c_L^2 at all late times) yields ||u(t)||_infty <= (F/c_L)(T_* - t)^{-1/2}. [CSTY2, Theorem 1.1] (data in H^{1/2}, r v^theta(0) bounded, pressure in L^{5/3}) gives local boundedness, and the far field is bounded by the epsilon-regularity criterion [SS, Lemma 1.2], in the form quoted in [CIV], because the energy class gives vanishing scaled L^3 and L^{3/2} norms of u and p at spatial infinity. So u stays bounded up to T_*, a contradiction.
Remarks. The theorem uses (A) twice: in the Riccati lower bound, which makes gradient records dense in logarithmic time, and in the bound H(t) <= C_* D(t)^2/M(t) at every earlier time t, which gives the backward decay of the transported potential vorticity. No historical quantity is replaced by an instantaneous one. Theorem 9 is an adaptation of [CIV, Theorem 1.2]: there the Euler length is prescribed, nonincreasing, with (T_* - t)/l(t)^2 -> 0 at all late times; here l is built from historical norms, is not monotone, and case (E) gives nu -> 0 only along selected times. This adaptation uses the historical-norm selection and the excursion lemma.

### 3.5 Why Theorem 9 does not reach the Case-1 limit

Write g = 1/D and theta(t) = (T_* - t) D(t). Theorem 9 gives limsup theta = infinity. The decay used in Step 4 of the proof of Theorem 9 is, on the gradient time scale 1/D(s), supplied by backward windows W(c, A, s). A window is exactly the historical ratio bound D(s + sigma/D(s))/D(s) <= [c(1 + |sigma|)]^{-1}; it implies an upper bound for the rescaled gradient, but it is not equivalent to actual decay of the rescaled gradient, nor to decay of the transported potential vorticity. Type I implies windows (Proposition 11(i)). Proposition 11(ii) constructs admissible scalar histories (levels 2^{-k} on flats of length k 2^{-k}, joined by drops of slope -C_2) with no windows at late times; there theta(s) is unbounded and the rescaled history is between 1 and 2 on [-A, 0], so the scalar information gives no backward decay of the historical gradient bound. Under (A) the two lengths M/D (gradient scale) and (T_* - t) M (Euler length) have ratio theta, which is unbounded along a sequence; the running-minimum control of Theorem 9 is for the second, while (A) gives Hessian control in the first.

Windows are a sufficient condition for excluding inviscid gradient-scale limits (Proposition 12); they are not shown to be necessary. The proof of Proposition 12 is a volume argument: a set E of positive volume on which |Omega| >= delta is transported back to time -sigma_n, where it lies in {R <= sqrt2 ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty/delta} and in a slab of height O(sigma_n), so its volume tends to 0 under the stated decay. In the inviscid branch the steady example e^{-|y'|^2} e_3 satisfies every normalized bound when C_* >= sqrt2 e (Proposition 11(iii)). In the viscous branch the limits are in LL, which returns to sec. 3.1. Thus under (A) the remaining scenario is Type II gradient growth (in the limsup sense of Theorem 9) whose gradient-scale ancient limits are either finite-axis viscous limits in LL after viscosity normalization, or finite-axis or planar inviscid limits for which no sufficient backward-decay estimate has been established.

### 3.6 Scale dichotomy (Theorem 13)

Theorem 13(a) is elementary. Along the line through the point where the historical swirl maximum S is attained and perpendicular to the axis, the swirl component is odd, vanishes on the axis, and has a critical point at distance r_0 <= G/S; Taylor's formula gives S <= (1/2) r_0^2 H, hence 2 S^3 <= G^2 H, and (A) turns this into 2 M S^3 <= C_* G^2 D^2.

Theorem 13(b) is a quantitative form of Proposition 8(c). If a sequence of viscous gradient records had S/M -> 0 or the gradient maximizer at unbounded rescaled distance from the axis, the gradient-scale limit would be either a swirl-free axisymmetric NS solution with positive viscosity, constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.2 and Remark 6.1], or planar and constant by [KNSS, Theorem 5.1]; both contradict |grad V(0, 0)| = 1. So at viscous gradient records the historical norms M, D, H are comparable to powers of the historical swirl maximum: M ~ S, D ~ S^2, H ~ S^3, and the gradient maximizer lies within O(1/S) of the axis.

Theorem 13(c) identifies the two branches of Proposition 8(c) with the swirl ratio, and Theorem 13(d) transfers this to the swirl records of Case 1. In branch (I) the normalized global numbers m_n, d_n, h_n are bounded, so (K) and (TAIL) follow from (A) together with the branch condition and the record estimates, and, after viscosity normalization, the swirl-record limits and the gradient-record limits belong to LL. Every member of LL has finite Q. Consequently, an exclusion of branch (I) using only the ancient limit properties of Theorem 2 and finiteness of Q, uniformly over all finite shape constants, would settle the bounded-circulation Liouville problem; an exclusion for one fixed C_* would not, and none is known to us. This reduction does not apply to an argument using the additional eternal selection of Proposition 15 or information from the fixed physical trajectory.

Branch (II) is a high-Reynolds-number regime: the local Reynolds number at a gradient record is 1/kappa -> infinity, the swirl is small relative to M, and the gradient maximizers escape every swirl-record observation cylinder. The gradient-scale limits are swirl-free Euler flows, so Proposition 12 applies once a backward decay o(sigma_n^{-1/2}) of ||grad V(-sigma_n)||_infty is known along one sequence.
