In the rescaled variables the swirl forcing d_Z(q^2) of the potential vorticity is pointwise O(kappa^{1/3}), so it tends to zero on fixed Euler-time intervals; a pointwise estimate for the full material derivative is unavailable because the viscous term is uncontrolled, and transport is asserted for the weak Euler limit; over an interval of length A the forcing integrates to O(A kappa^{1/3}), which is not small for A of order 1/kappa, and the viscous term kappa Delta_5 Omega is not controlled by the available Hessian bound. The following is therefore heuristic, and only the displayed estimates are claimed. An exact unidirectional jet f(R, sigma) e_3 evolves by the heat equation d_sigma f = kappa Delta f (the Gaussian terminal profile e^{-b^2 R^2} has the backward continuation (1 + 4 b^2 kappa sigma)^{-1} exp(-b^2 R^2/(1 + 4 b^2 kappa sigma)), whose gradient supremum exceeds the terminal one by the factor (1 + 4 b^2 kappa sigma)^{-3/2}), so an exact jet cannot sit at a terminal historical gradient record. Quantitatively, Duhamel's formula shows that the nonlinear and pressure acceleration F = -V.grad V - grad P satisfies sup ||F||_infty >= kappa/(8 C_h^2) on [-4 C_h^2/kappa, 0], C_h the heat-gradient constant; but an acceleration of order kappa vanishes in the Euler limit, so convergence on compact sets to a steady jet is not excluded. Also int_0^infinity q_V^2 R dR = O(kappa^{4/3}) at every height and time.

A single-profile field lambda(t) W(lambda(t) x, tau(t)) with W in LL and tau' = lambda^2 is not a model of branch (I): it leaves the residual lambda'(t)(W + X.grad_X W), X = lambda(t) x, in the NS equations, which does not vanish in general.

### 3.7 Critical mass and eternal limits (Propositions 14 and 15)

The critical-mass lower bound in Proposition 14 is a single-time statement. At a gradient record the rescaled field U has |U| <= 1, |grad U| <= 1 attained at the origin and |grad^2 U| <= C_*. If the vorticity of U were uniformly small, heat-semigroup smoothing would make its gradient small, so ||curl U||_infty >= c_0(C_*). If S/M is small, the swirl part of U has small vorticity by the Landau inequality, so the azimuthal vorticity is at least c_0/2 somewhere, hence on a disc of radius of order c_0/C_*, which gives a lower bound for its meridional L^1 norm; in physical variables this is N(s) >= c_1 M(s)^2/D(s). The quantity N is critical, and in branch (II) M^2/D = 1/kappa -> infinity, so this branch forces the global potential-vorticity mass to diverge at the gradient records. The lower bound comes from a disc of physical radius comparable to M/D centred at a point where the heat-semigroup argument finds substantial azimuthal vorticity (not necessarily the gradient maximizer), and |omega^theta| <= sqrt2 D there, so the mass on such a disc is of order M^2/D. A global bound N(s_n) = o(M(s_n)^2/D(s_n)) would suffice; the strict deficit of Proposition 14 would already suffice. When N(a) < infinity, the balances give N = o(D^{1/2}) and N = o(S); neither contradicts the established lower bound. A truncated balance (Proposition 18(f)) removes the assumption N(a) < infinity from the consequence kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity and, when M^5/D^3 >= epsilon_0, shows that the source on the high superlevel set at distance at least a fixed multiple of M(s_n)/D(s_n) from the axis is o(1/kappa_n). The swirl source d_z(q^2) of the eta equation is critical in L^1_t L^1_x but was not controlled critically under (A). The supremum of eta obeys a pointwise budget instead: the maximum principle for eta and the swirl-part bound ||d_z(q^2)||_infty <= 2 sqrt6 S^{1/2} H^{3/2} give, along branch-(II) records s_n with M(s_n)^5/D(s_n)^3 >= epsilon_0 > 0 uniformly in n, D^2/M <= C (1 + int (D^2/M)^{5/3} dt) (Proposition 14, L^infty budget). Since (D^2/M)^{5/3} = kappa^{1/3} D (D^2/M), this is a budget with rate kappa^alpha D (D^2/M), alpha = 1/3. If D(t) and M(t) are comparable to (T_* - t)^{-beta} and (T_* - t)^{-3 beta/5} throughout a fixed late interval, a budget with rate kappa^alpha D (D^2/M), a fixed alpha < 5 and a constant independent of n requires beta >= 5/(5 - alpha), by comparison of exponents; for alpha = 1/3 this is beta >= 15/14, and in the case of equality the conclusion also depends on the constants. These are necessary conditions only, and such a budget does not exclude sufficiently fast power-law growth. Two further proved facts locate the difficulty. The point where the budget finds |eta| of order D^2/M lies at r >= c_0 M/(4 sqrt2 C_* D), and at r >= c M/D the source is at most C kappa D (D^2/M) (Proposition 14, endpoint remark), so the rate kappa^{1/3} is reached only at r << M/D. And an energy atom of mass m has a concentration radius rho_m(t) with int^{T_*} rho_m^{-2} dt < infinity (Proposition 11(vii)). For the power laws above, a budget with rate kappa D (D^2/M) (alpha = 1) would need beta >= 5/4, while a core that stays at the gradient scale, rho_m(t) <= C M(t)/D(t) for all late t, needs int (D/M)^2 dt < infinity, that is beta < 5/4. Under these power-law assumptions and the persistent-core assumption, for every fixed c > 0 the source from r >= c M(t)/D(t) contributes o(X(s_n)) to the parabolic (backward-diffusion) representation of eta at the budget point, so a contribution of order X(s_n) must come from r < c M(t)/D(t); no corresponding statement about deterministic particle paths has been proved. Research answer res34 gives formal power-law orders of this kind (beta = 6/5, S ~ (T_* - t)^{-14/25}, eta generated in an axial tube of radius 1/S and length (T_* - t)^{11/25}, cores of size M/D at axial distance (T_* - t)^{7/25} from the singular point) that satisfy the scalar budgets it checks; it is not a Navier-Stokes solution, the author checked its exponents but not its profiles, and it does not verify every necessary condition of this report (Proposition 18(c) in particular). Neither an o(X(s_n)) bound on this inner-region contribution nor the all-late-time bound rho_m(t) <= C M(t)/D(t) has been derived from the Case-1 hypotheses.

Proposition 15 adds eternality and an attained spacetime gradient maximum to the limits. Its forward bounds use only (A) and the Landau inequality: if D(b) <= lambda D(s) and s is a gradient record, then M(b) <= 18 C_* lambda^2 M(s). It does not exclude either branch; an appropriate Liouville theorem for eternal members of LL with a shape bound would exclude an eternal limit selected by Proposition 15 if its viscosity is positive, but not an arbitrary prescribed viscous subsequence (steady members would be eternal, and we know of no theorem excluding bounded steady axisymmetric solutions with bounded circulation and nonzero swirl on R^3 without further decay). In the scenario in which all late gradient records are uniformly viscous (kappa >= c for one c > 0 and all sufficiently late gradient records; a viscous subsequence does not suffice), the solution is single-scale at all late times (M(t) <= 18 C_* C_c S(t)), so limsup (T_* - t) S(t)^2 = infinity by Theorem 9, and along suitable swirl records ||(u^r)^-|| >= S/G and ||grad^2 u|| >= 2 S^3/G^2; hence the global hypotheses of Zhang's one-sided Type I criterion, of the paired Euler-length bounds of [CIV] (their product would bound (T_* - t)^2 ||u|| ||grad^2 u||, which is at least (2/G^2)((T_* - t) S^2)^2 along these records) and of the radial bound in [CIV2] fail there. No application of the local versions of these criteria is obtained from these estimates. For the eternal swirl-record limits W of this scenario (velocity bound C_0 := 18 C_* C_c, circulation bound G/gamma_*, viscosity nu_* = 1/gamma_*, |W^theta| <= 1 = W^theta(e_1, 0) on R^3 x R) the circulation structure of Proposition 23(f) extends to the whole time line: a(tau) = sup_x Gamma_W(x, tau) is nonincreasing and Lipschitz on R with limits A_- >= A_+ >= max{B, 0} as tau -> -infinity and tau -> +infinity, where the tail supremum B is time independent; a(tau_0) = A_- for one finite tau_0 holds if and only if B = A_-, and then a = A_- identically; a(s) > a(t) whenever s < t and a(t) > max{B, 0}; and ess sup |a'| over tau >= T and over tau <= -T tends to 0 as T -> infinity (a + C tau^2/2 is convex, C = sup |d_tau^2 Gamma_W|). Whether A_+ = max{B, 0} is not known (B >= 0 is not established either): it would require interchanging tau -> +infinity with radial infinity, and the available estimate |sup_{R >= rho} Gamma_W(., t) - sup_{R >= rho} Gamma_W(., s)| <= |t - s| sup_{R >= rho} |d_tau Gamma_W| does not permit it. The attained normalization adds nothing at the level of Liouville theorems: if W is any eternal bounded mild axisymmetric solution with |Gamma_W| <= G' and m := sup |W^theta| > 0 over space and time, then near-maximizers of |W^theta| have radii in [m/(2 ||grad W||_infty), 2 G'/m] (since |W^theta| <= ||grad W||_infty R and |W^theta| <= G'/R), so a subsequence of axial and temporal translates converges to an eternal mild solution V attaining |V^theta| = m at some radius r_* > 0; after the reflection V(x, t) -> P V(P x, t), P = diag(1, -1, 1), if needed (it reverses the swirl and preserves the class and the bounds), the rescaled field Wt(y, tau) := m^{-1} V(r_* y, (r_*/m) tau) is a solution with viscosity nu/(m r_*), velocity bound K/m, circulation bound G'/(m r_*) and |Wt^theta| <= 1 = Wt^theta(e_1, 0); a Liouville theorem for eternal solutions with an attained swirl maximum, uniform over all finite constants and positive viscosities, is therefore equivalent to one for all eternal bounded mild axisymmetric solutions with bounded circulation and nonzero swirl, the steady ones included. (The equivalence does not preserve prescribed constants and says nothing about realizability by one physical trajectory. These statements are from a research answer; they were checked by the author and in adversarial audit round 26.)

In the same uniformly viscous scenario the following necessary conditions hold; none of them excludes either branch of Theorem 13, although they exclude certain growth histories and exact shrinking copies of steady profiles. Fix C_1 with D(t) <= C_1 S(t)^2 at late times (the restart bound D(t) <= C M(t)^2 of sec. 3.9 and M(t) <= C_0 S(t)). The field u(t) is Lipschitz with finite energy, hence tends to 0 at infinity, so a nonzero extremum of u^theta(t) is attained off the axis; at a positive spatial maximum of u^theta (or of -u^theta) the transport term of the swirl equation vanishes and (Delta - r^{-2}) u^theta <= 0 (respectively >= 0); moreover ||d_t u^theta||_infty <= sqrt(3) H + M D, the axisymmetric pressure having no angular gradient, so ||u^theta(t)||_infty is locally Lipschitz in time and its upper Dini derivative is at most ||u^r/r||_infty ||u^theta||_infty <= D S; as the running maximum S grows only where it is attained, S' <= C_1 S^3 almost everywhere, and integration of (S^{-2})' >= -2 C_1 gives (T_* - t) S(t)^2 >= 1/(2 C_1) for late t. In the normalized variables of the swirl records, int over t_n + T_n I of int over z_n e_3 + r_n Omega of |grad u|^2 dx dt equals gamma_n r_n times the normalized Dirichlet integral of U_n over Omega x I; with eps(t) := int_t^{T_*} ||grad u(s)||_2^2 ds, lower semicontinuity gives liminf_n eps(t_n)/(gamma_n r_n) >= int_0^A int_{B_L} |grad W|^2 for all fixed A, L, so a steady nonconstant eternal limit W forces eps(t_n)/(gamma_n r_n) -> infinity, which is compatible with eps(t_n) -> 0. With m(t) := ||u(t)||_infty and C_S the constant in ||f||_6 <= C_S ||grad f||_2, the bound |u| >= m/2 on the ball of radius m/(2 D) about a maximizer gives ||grad u(t)||_2^2 >= c_S m(t)^3/D(t), c_S = (4 pi/3)^{1/3}/(8 C_S^2); hence, for 0 < eta <= 1, the integral of S dt over {t >= t_0 : ||u^theta(t)||_infty >= eta S(t)} is at most C_1 eps(t_0)/(c_S eta^3). Conversely, ||d_t u^theta||_infty <= C_2 S^3 at late times with C_2 = sqrt(3) C_* C_1^2 + C_0 C_1 (by (A), H <= C_* D^2/M <= C_* C_1^2 S^3); with c_2 = min{(4 C_1)^{-1}, (16 C_2)^{-1}} and a first crossing t_n of a level S_n, the interval [t_n, t_n + c_2 S_n^{-2}] lies before T_*, carries S <= 2 S_n and ||u^theta(t)||_infty >= S_n/2 >= S(t)/4, so it lies in the set with eta = 1/4 and contributes between c_2/S_n and 2 c_2/S_n to the integral; for a fixed geometric ladder S_n = S_0 lambda^n, lambda > 1, these contributions have a finite sum, so the occupation bound is consistent with such records (this interval estimate, with its constants, was supplied in adversarial audit round 27 and checked by the author). On the counterexample side, write u = (gamma/rho) V(y, s) and p = (gamma^2/rho^2) P(y, s) (up to a function of time), y = (x - z e_3)/rho, ds/dt = gamma/rho^2, with C^1 functions rho, gamma > 0 and z; then V_s + V.grad V + grad P = gamma^{-1} Delta V + a (V + y.grad V) - b V + c d_Z V with a = rho rho'/gamma, b = rho^2 gamma'/gamma^2, c = rho z'/gamma, and the integral of a ds equals log(rho(t)/rho(t_0)), which tends to -infinity as rho -> 0. An exactly shrinking copy of a bounded steady profile (W smooth, bounded, divergence-free and steady with viscosity 1/gamma on R^3, gamma and z constant, rho' not identically 0) is impossible unless the profile is constant: taking the curl of grad(P - P_W) = a (W + y.grad W) at a time where a is not 0 gives 2 omega + y.grad omega = 0, so lambda^2 omega(lambda y) does not depend on lambda > 0 and omega = 0 by continuity at the origin; a bounded divergence-free and curl-free field is constant, and an axisymmetric constant has no swirl. This excludes exact shrinking copies, not steady subsequential limits or approximate modulations. A qualifying counterexample in the uniformly viscous scenario would, by Proposition 15, yield a bounded eternal mild axisymmetric solution with bounded circulation and nonzero swirl; no such solution is supplied here or known to us (constant axial fields are bounded eternal solutions without swirl). A construction around a chosen profile would further need a modulation law with rho -> 0 in finite time, stability under the secular dilation term and matching to finite energy with (A) for one fixed datum; the change of variables alone does not make V an eternal solution or prove convergence to a profile. (These statements are from research answer res26, except the interval estimate noted above; they were checked by the author and in adversarial audit round 27, and the precision repairs requested there were made afterwards.)

### 3.8 Energy-type identities

Proposition 10 gives exact identities in the 5D measure. The q^p identity shows that the compression term 2(1 - 1/p) int |q|^p phi_z dmu is the only possible source; by the Hardy inequality in R^4 it is dominated by the dissipation when -r u^r <= 2/p. At the critical exponent 5/2 the threshold 4/5 is below the value 1 forced at every swirl record, so this particular global absorption criterion cannot exclude the records; this does not show that every refinement of the energy method fails. The eta^2 identity contains the absorbing axis term -2 pi int eta(0, z)^2 dz, but its source int eta d_z(q^2) dx is controlled by ||q||_{L^4(dx)}^2 ||grad eta||_{L^2(dx)}. The norm ||q||_{L^4(dx)} is subcritical (it scales like lambda^{5/4}); it satisfies ||q(t)||_{L^4(dx)}^4 <= 4 E_0 H(t)^2 <= 4 E_0 C_*^2 D(t)^4/M(t)^2 under (A), which may diverge as t -> T_*. No adequate uniform or time-integrated bound was obtained.

### 3.9 Literature relevant to the remaining regime

 Q. S. Zhang [QZ, Theorem 1.1]: v_r >= -c (T - t)^{-1/2} implies regularity, so at T_* the inward radial velocity is not Type I in the sense limsup (T_* - t)^{1/2} ||(u^r)^-(t)||_infty = infinity. The swirl records give -u^r(r_n, z_n, t_n) >= 1/r_n, which is consistent with this. The proof uses a one-dimensional auxiliary equation and a circulation barrier with a time-only drift g(t) >= ||(u^r)^-(t)||_infty; the published time envelope g = c (T - t)^{-1/2} has the interval-integral consequence int_a^b g dt <= 2c (b - a)^{1/2}; extending the argument to arbitrary envelopes satisfying that consequence is not proved here. We have ||(u^r)^-(t)||_infty <= M(t) <= c E_0^{1/5} D(t)^{3/5}, but no bound of the required temporal strength.
 Pan [Pan] and Zujin Zhang [ZZ], as summarized in [QZ]: r u^r >= -1 (respectively -M with M in (1, 2)) everywhere implies regularity. The record identity -r_n u^r >= 1 at the record point does not contradict these global hypotheses, which were not established here. These papers were not retrieved.
 Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol [CIV2, Theorem 1.3]: local regularity under anisotropic Type II pointwise bounds on u and its derivatives up to order 2 near the singular point; these bounds were not derived from (A), (K), (TAIL).
 Seregin [SeII]: reductions and exclusions of Type II scenarios (in the sense of those papers) under additional quantitative hypotheses, including bounds on second spatial and time derivatives and spatial envelopes; these were not derived from (A), (K), (TAIL).
 Lei, Ren and Zhang [LRZ2, Theorem 1.2] prove triviality for bounded ancient solutions when Gamma^2 approaches L = limsup_{r -> infinity} sup_{z, t} Gamma^2 (which the source identifies with the global supremum) at the quantitative rate eps_0 L/r, uniformly in z and t for sufficiently large r, with eps_0 depending on the velocity bound in viscosity-one units; the z-periodic case is in [LRZ1]; Lei, Zhang and Zhao [LZZ] treat Gamma in L^infty_t L^p_x and Gamma -> 0 uniformly at radial infinity. None of these hypotheses was derived for the Case-1 limit.
 Shahmurov [Sh] proves critical signed-action continuation criteria: with Y_q = r^{1-3/q} u^theta and Ubar_q = int (u^r/r) |Y_q|^q dx / int |Y_q|^q dx, defined as zero when the denominator vanishes, the integrating factor is exp(3 int Ubar_q dt), and a hypothetical singularity with smooth H^2 data and bounded circulation must have unbounded signed compression action for every finite q >= 2. The paper also proves continuation under the sliding condition int_{sigma-h}^{sigma} ||(u^r)_-||_infty dt <= 2 sqrt(nu h l_{h_0}(h)), where l_{h_0}(h) = log log(e^e h_0/h), on all sufficiently small windows specified in its Theorem 5.3, and proves non-amplification of the instantaneous source-only meridional response for the weighted functionals of its Theorem 6.1. These are regularity criteria and necessary conditions, not a Liouville theorem; neither their signed-action nor sliding-compression hypotheses have been derived here from (A), (K), (TAIL) and the fixed data. The last step of that regularity argument uses the near-axis logarithmic circulation criterion of [Wei] (not retrieved here). For axis regularity, [LZ2, Corollary 1.3] uses a bound |Gamma| <= C_1 |log r|^{-2} near the axis, uniform up to the terminal time; the improvement attributed to [Wei] in [QZ] has order |log r|^{-3/2}. Smoothness of each normalized limit at its own axis does not supply a uniform physical modulus as t -> T_*. Further papers of the same author [Sh2], [Sh3], [Sh4] give a conditional reduction of axis regularity to scale-uniform closure statements in a five-dimensional framework, the sharp coercivity range of power-weighted swirl energies, and structural obstructions. Among the latter is the elementary bound S_R <= 2 pi G^2 log(1 + R D_{theta, R}/(2 pi G^2)) for the scaled swirl energy S_R = R^{-1} int_{B_R} |u^theta|^2 dx in terms of the swirl dissipation D_{theta, R} = int_{B_R} |u^theta|^2 r^{-2} dx [Sh4, Proposition 10.1], which with the energy inequality controls the time spent at large scaled swirl energy [Sh4, Theorem 10.2]. At the swirl records the scaled swirl energy at scale L r_n is at most of order gamma_n^2 + G^2 log L (from |u^theta| <= S_n and |Gamma| <= G), which is consistent with that bound. A further paper [Sh5] compares record-selection frameworks for the general and the axisymmetric problem and states that it claims no unconditional regularity theorem and no singularity construction. These papers state that they do not prove regularity for arbitrary swirl, and none of them is a Liouville theorem for LL.
 Hou [Hou1] reports numerical evidence of potentially singular, nearly self-similar behaviour, at a point of the symmetry axis, of axisymmetric Navier-Stokes solutions with swirl and smooth data, driven by a potential Euler singularity in the interior reported in [Hou2]; no singularity is proved there. The computation is posed in the periodic cylinder {r < 1}, with a no-slip, no-flow condition at r = 1, period 1 in z, and a viscosity that is increased once at an early time and is fixed on the late fitted interval; because of the bounded domain it is not a finite-energy solution on R^3. The tracked point (R(t), Z(t)) maximizes u_1 = u^theta/r (other maxima are also tracked), not u^theta (these points were read in the text of [Hou1]). Whether such a scenario, transplanted to R^3, would satisfy (A), (K) and (TAIL) is not known to us. [Hou3] studies generalized axisymmetric equations with a dynamically varied space dimension and a rescaled model with two viscosity coefficients, which are different equations. Chen and Hou [CH] prove finite-time, nearly self-similar blowup of the 2D Boussinesq and the 3D axisymmetric Euler equations with smooth initial data of finite energy in the presence of a boundary; it does not concern the Navier-Stokes equations on R^3. Hou, Wang and Yang [HWY, Theorem 1] give a computer-assisted proof of nonuniqueness of suitable Leray-Hopf solutions on R^3 with compactly supported initial data in C^infinity(R^3 \ {0}) intersected with L^q, q < 3, which are singular at the origin; this is not singularity formation from smooth data. Petrillo and Glimm [PG] report that a forced Navier-Stokes singularity was produced in September 2026 and state that the unforced problem remains open; a forced solution does not solve the equations considered here. These works bear only on the counterexample alternative and supply no fixed-datum Navier-Stokes singularity.

Two elementary consequences bear on power-law fits of numerical data; both are proved here. First, a finite-energy axisymmetric solution with singular time T_* cannot satisfy ||grad u(t)||_2^2 <= C (T_* - t)^{-1/2} for t near T_*: at every point of the singular time this gives E(rho) <= rho^{-1} int_{T_* - rho^2}^{T_*} ||grad u(t)||_2^2 dt <= 2C, so every singular point would be of Type I, contradicting [Se20, Theorem 2.1], while a singular point exists (Proposition 20(a)). Second, suppose M, S, D, H (running maxima) and the record radii satisfy two-sided power laws M ~ delta^{-a}, S ~ delta^{-s}, D ~ delta^{-d}, H ~ delta^{-h} and r_n ~ delta_n^b, with delta = T_* - t and constants independent of time. Then M >= S gives a >= s; at a swirl record, |u^theta|/r <= |grad u| gives D(t_n) >= S_n/r_n, and the swirl on the radial segment through the record vanishes on the axis and has a critical point at the record, so S_n <= H(t_n) r_n^2/2; hence d >= s + b and h >= s + 2b. Since gamma_n = r_n S_n is at most G and, for large n, at least a positive constant (by (K) and the record inflow), b = s. Hypothesis (A) gives a + h <= 2d, and ||grad u||_infty^2 <= C ||u||_infty ||grad^2 u||_infty gives a + h >= 2d, so a + h = 2d. Fix an early time t_0 > 0; for late t put h_0 := c M(t)^{-2} < t_0/2 and restart the mild solution at q - h_0 for every q in [t_0, t]; the smoothing estimate gives ||grad u(q)||_infty <= C M(t) h_0^{-1/2} <= C' M(t)^2, and D(t_0) is absorbed as M(t) -> infinity, so D(t) <= C M(t)^2 at fixed viscosity and d <= 2a. Finally a > 1/2, since a velocity bound M <= C delta^{-1/2} excludes a singularity [CSTY2]. These are necessary tests on exponents, not criteria: genuine two-sided power bounds with a + h = 2d give some finite shape constant, but exponent fits alone establish neither those bounds nor a prescribed value of C_*, and (K) and (TAIL) concern whole rescaled fields on backward intervals. A ratio bound M(t_n)/S_n <= C would give both witnesses (it bounds the rescaled velocity by C, so the rescaled tail integral over |y| > L is at most 4 pi C^2/L), but (K) does not imply it.
 A further search of arXiv listings on 2026-10-16 (abstracts read) found nothing that applies here: Liouville theorems for stationary D-solutions under additional decay conditions [WY] and related stationary results; a regularity criterion for axisymmetric solutions in a finite cylinder with boundary conditions, which requires ||u^theta||_{L^infty_t L^p_x}/||u^theta||_{L^infty_t L^infty_x} to stay bounded below for some p in (6, infinity) [OZ] (on R^3 finite energy gives ||u^theta(t)||_p <= E_0^{1/p} S(t)^{1-2/p}, so this ratio tends to zero whenever S -> infinity, as in Case 1, and the criterion gives no information); and a revised version of a global regularity claim whose current version states that no unconditional global regularity claim is made [GZ].
 The papers above exclude particular classes. We know no theorem excluding the unrestricted remaining regime under the assumptions stated here, and to our knowledge the bounded-circulation case of the ancient Liouville conjecture is open on R^3. This is based on the cited sources and an arXiv search, not on an exhaustive survey.

### 3.10 Critical weighted energies and the inflow integral (Propositions 16 to 18)

The circulation Gamma is dimensionless in viscous units, so the critical weighted norms of Gamma are int |Gamma|^p r^{-3} dx. In the variable q = Gamma/r^2 these are the functionals Y_p of Proposition 16. Their evolution has a single term without sign, -3 int (u^r/r)|q|^p r^{2p-3} dx, which is an inflow term: it is harmful exactly where u^r < 0. Every way we found to absorb it requires a global hypothesis on the solution. One option is a bound -r u^r <= 2 - 3/p, which interpolates between the Hardy threshold 4/5 of Proposition 10 (p = 5/2) and the value 2 as p -> infinity; the latter matches the critical value 2 nu of Route B. The other is smallness of the angular stream function: for a chosen finite p, ||psi^theta||_infty <= P_p (P_2 = 3^{-1/2}; P_p increases to (8/3)^{1/2} = 1.632993...). Neither hypothesis was derived. Proposition 17 shows that the second is false at the gradient records of branch (II), where ||psi^theta|| is at least of order 1/kappa. Weights centered at a point of the axis, including the self-similar Gaussian weight, have an analogous inflow term toward the chosen point, and other functionals leave additional unsigned terms. At a swirl record that inflow is at least nu_* = 1/gamma_* in the record normalization (1 in viscosity-one units), below the threshold 2 nu_* of the self-similar weight. In Case 1 the time-integrated inflow is infinite (Proposition 18(a)), because each record contributes a fixed amount of critical dissipation of q in its own cylinder. No compatible bound on the total has been obtained; spatial or temporal localization is another possible approach. Branch (II) needs the inflow to be at least of order 1/kappa_n before the gradient record (Proposition 18(b)). A separate necessary condition, from the exact backward adjoint identity, is that the adjoint test functions develop axial gradients of total size at least of order kappa_n^{-4/3} (Proposition 18(c)). When M^5/D^3 >= epsilon_0, the truncated balance of Proposition 18(f) places the source that branch (II) needs at distance o(M(s_n)/D(s_n)) from the axis.

### 3.11 The stream-function BMO form (Proposition 19)

Lei and Zhang [LZ, Theorem 1.1] prove that for an axisymmetric Navier-Stokes solution on a parabolic cylinder Q_L of radius L whose meridional field is curl B_2 + b_3 with B_2 bounded in BMO and r|b_3| bounded, the circulation is Hoelder continuous at the axis with constants depending only on these two norms. Both norms are invariant under the Navier-Stokes scaling, so for an ancient solution with bounded circulation the estimate holds on every Q_L with the same constants, and letting L -> infinity gives Gamma = Gamma(0, 0) = 0. We apply this to the Case-1 limit after the Galilean change of frame that removes a uniform axial flow c_0 e_Z. Since curl((c_0 R/2) e_theta) = c_0 e_Z, the natural choice is B_2 = (psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta. This contains Route A (Theorem 4 gives R|V - c_0 e_Z| bounded, which is the b_3 part) and replaces the smallness of Proposition 16 by a bound, in a weaker norm, and only for the limit. Like the hypotheses of Theorems 4 and 5, it fails for every member of LL. Deriving the BMO condition from the abstract LL properties would itself prove a Liouville theorem. The fixed trajectory may supply additional information, but its use is not proved necessary.

On the physical side, the stream function is a superposition of ring potentials weighted by omega^theta dr dz, and all ring potentials are dilates and translates of one, so the BMO norm of B(t) is at most an absolute constant times N(t) = 2 pi int int |omega^theta| dr dz. The BMO norm of the normalized stream function equals the physical one divided by gamma_n, and BMO is lower semicontinuous at the limit, so a bound sup_tau liminf_n N(t_n + T_n tau) < infinity would give the hypothesis of Proposition 19(b) (bounds that depend on the window length or on the radius of the balls would not suffice, because the Lei-Zhang constants must be independent of the cylinder size). Proposition 19(c) shows that no such bound holds. The global statement limsup N = infinity is a known consequence of [LZ, Theorem 1.4]; what is specific here is that large N appears at fixed normalized phases of the record windows. The balance of N has the source ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)}, whose integral over each normalized swirl window has a positive lower bound (Proposition 19(d)), so the integrated absolute source diverges, like the inflow integral. This does not show that a bound on N would need a cancellation between the signed source and eta: the diffusion loss in the balance of N can absorb a same-signed source, as a stationary scalar example in 5D radial variables shows. But no estimate controlling either the diffusion term or the signed source was found. An explicit kinematic family (not an NS solution: a record-scale swirl core plus a meridional field at the intermediate scale S^{-2/3}) satisfies the listed energy, circulation and shape inequalities and the instantaneous record conditions while the normalized BMO norm and N diverge, so these instantaneous constraints do not by themselves give a bound.

### 3.12 Limitations of the tested arguments

Elementary weighted maximum principle for R^3 eta (needs compression -R V^R >= nu on long vertical segments, which the vertical-flux identity rules out; other weighted arguments are not excluded); vertical averaging of Route B (no closed equation for averages); energy and dissipation counting over records (gives only sum r_n <= C E_0); gradient-record rescaling alone (loses the swirl record and leaves the NS class in the inviscid branch); bounded drift rigidity (false by O1); Hardy-threshold energy criterion (threshold 4/5 below the record value 1); Euler-length transport without windows (no backward decay from the scalar information); Zhang's time-only drift mechanism combined with (A) (no bound of the required temporal strength on the inward velocity); the scale dichotomy as an exclusion (both branches survive); the L^1 balance for the critical mass N (when N(a) < infinity it gives N = o(D^{1/2}) and N = o(S), neither of which contradicts the lower bound); record selection for windows from scalar information (smooth power-law histories D = delta^{-4/3}, M = delta^{-3/4}, H = delta^{-23/12}, S = delta^{-1/2} satisfy the listed scalar inequalities and instantaneous algebraic bounds and are flat on gradient time scales); the viscous-time jet comparison (an acceleration of order kappa is invisible in the Euler limit); eternal limits (no Liouville theorem for eternal members of LL is known to us); critical weighted energies of the circulation (one inflow term, absorbed only under a global inflow bound or a stream-function smallness that fails in branch (II)); the backward adjoint of the potential-vorticity equation (reduces branch (II) to an unproved gradient bound for the adjoint); the coupled critical meridional energy Y_2 + (3/2) int |b|^2/r dx (the swirl transfer cancels exactly, but the cubic and pressure-head fluxes have no sign, the viscous axis term is not absorbable, and smooth swirl-free data with increasing meridional energy exist); the BMO form of the stream function (Proposition 19; a uniform N bound is one sufficient way to obtain BMO control, and that proposed bound contradicts Proposition 19(c); BMO control is a separate sufficient hypothesis, not derived); maximum-principle rigidity at attained interior swirl maxima (needs a global compression bound -r u^r <= nu; the record gives the opposite inequality, and if the additional global upper bound held, equality would be forced at the record); scale-invariant energy and the local critical mass in shrinking cylinders around the singular point (Proposition 20; necessary conditions only, the local energy inequality is circular in Type II quantities); material circles and the instantaneous geometry of vortex lines (an explicit local configuration has record-compatible inward strain, straight vortex lines and zero instantaneous source); circulation escape (Proposition 21; necessary conditions and a compression criterion with threshold 2 - beta_0, strictly below 2 and approaching the exterior-comparison endpoint of Theorem 5 as beta_0 decreases to 0, whose hypothesis was not derived); localized pressure and strain (Proposition 22; identities and necessary conditions; the Burgers vortex satisfies the displayed local record, pressure and strain identities for compatible constants, so an exclusion needs some information not shared by this local model); published Liouville hypotheses (Proposition 23; the integrability and tail classes are violated by every Case-1 limit, and the others need a critical meridional bound or upper rates; the one-sided far-field form -r u^r <= 2, sharp for the exterior comparison, was not derived); material lines and vorticity creation (Proposition 24; short-window and conditional statements only, the source pairing is unsigned, and the unweighted small-dissipation target is false); the far-field inflow arrow (Proposition 25; (1) Finite-time path entropy for the circulation. Fix t_0 < T_* with D_0 = D(t_0) > 0 and a subsequence of swirl records with gamma_n >= gamma_low > 0 and M(t_n) <= C_0 S_n, and put rho^2 = gamma_low/(2 D_0), p_0 = gamma_low/(2G). Then, for large n, 2 p_0 log(rho/r_n) - log 2 <= C_0^2 gamma_n^2/4 + (C_H^{1/3} C_S^2/4) int_{r_n^2}^{t_n - t_0} ||grad u(t_n - s)||_2^2 s^{-1/2} ds, where C_S is the constant of ||f||_6 <= C_S ||grad f||_2 and C_H is absolute. The argument, checked step by step by the author: with sigma_n = +-1 chosen so that sigma_n Gamma(x_n, t_n) = gamma_n, the stopped representation gives gamma_n as the expectation of sigma_n Gamma at the stopping point of the backward diffusion with generator Delta - (b + 2 e_r/r).grad stopped at r = e (at time t_0 if the path has not reached r = e, on r = e otherwise); since |Gamma(t_0)| <= D_0 r^2, |Gamma| <= G and |Gamma| <= D(t_n) e^2 on r = e, the probability of surviving to t_0 at radius at least rho is at least p_0 in the limit e -> 0; for the reference diffusion without b the radius solves dR = -R^{-1} ds + sqrt2 dB, so R^2 stopped at the exit from the annulus e < R < rho is a bounded martingale and that probability is at most (r_n^2 - e^2)/(rho^2 - e^2) <= r_n^2/rho^2; the relative entropy of the two stopped path laws is (1/4) E int_0^{min(t_n - t_0, zeta_e)} |b(X_s, t_n - s)|^2 ds, zeta_e the exit time from e < R < rho, with the same stopping rule for both laws, and binary data processing gives the left side; the total drift b + 2 e_r/r is divergence free in three dimensions on {r > e}, so the killed transition density k satisfies ||k(s)||_1 <= 1 and the Nash bound ||k(s)||_infty <= C_H s^{-3/2}, and E|b|^2 <= ||k||_{3/2} ||b||_6^2 <= C_H^{1/3} C_S^2 s^{-1/2} ||grad u||_2^2 for s >= r_n^2, while |b| <= M(t_n) for s < r_n^2; the bound is uniform in e, and e -> 0 is taken at fixed n using only the lower bound for the escape probability. The estimate contradicts nothing known: Leray's lower bound ||grad u(t)||_2^2 >= c (T_* - t)^{-1/2}, with c universal and valid at every t < T_* (from d/dt ||grad u||_2^2 <= C ||grad u||_2^6), makes the right side at least c' log(1/(T_* - t_n)) - C' whenever r_n^2 <= K (T_* - t_n) for a fixed K (as in the uniformly viscous scenario, where r_n^2 <= G^2/S_n^2 <= 2 C_1 G^2 (T_* - t_n)); this lower bound gives no upper bound, the inequality bounds log(1/r_n) by the weighted enstrophy integral, and these estimates alone yield no contradiction; the scalar history S = (T_* - t)^{-alpha}, ||grad u||_2^2 = Lambda S with 1/2 < alpha < 1, which meets every scalar constraint of sec. 3.7 for compatible constants (for example M = 2S, D = 2S^2, H = 4S^3, gamma = 1, C_* >= 2, Lambda large), makes it of order (T_* - t_n)^{1/2 - alpha}. (2) Accelerating Burgers vortex. For g > 0, 1/2 < alpha < 1, h = (T - t)^{2 alpha} and a = (4 - h')/h = 4/h + 2 alpha/(T - t), the field u^r = -a r/2, u^z = a z, u^theta = (g/r)(1 - e^{-r^2/h}) with pressure (a'/4 - a^2/8) r^2 - (a' + a^2) z^2/2 + int_0^r (u^theta(s, t))^2 s^{-1} ds is an exact smooth solution of infinite energy; its swirl maximum is at r = (xi h)^{1/2}, where xi is the unique positive root of e^xi = 1 + 2 xi (xi lies in (1, 2)), so the records are genuine, increasing, with gamma_n = gamma := g(1 - e^{-xi}) and (T - t_n) S_n^2 -> infinity. A finite-energy modification that keeps these records and the affine meridional field on balls B_{l_n} with l_n/r_n -> infinity violates (K) (the normalized axial velocity at (0, 0, L) is A_n L with A_n = a(t_n) r_n^2/gamma -> 4 xi/gamma) and (TAIL) (the weighted shell integral of |U_n|^2 over L < |y| < l_n/r_n is at least that of the meridional part, which equals 2 pi A_n^2 (l_n/r_n - L)), and (A) with D(t_n) <= C_1 S_n^2 alone gives limsup l_n/r_n <= C_* C_1^2 gamma^3/(8 xi), from H(t_n) >= 2 S_n/r_n^2 and M(t_n) >= a(t_n) l_n. Matching within O(r_n) is not excluded, and no modification that solves the equations is known. A third test comes from research answer res28 (checked by the author by hand; audited in round 29, with the repairs requested there made afterwards): the Gaussian frequency of q = u^theta/r in the five-dimensional lift, where q_t + b.grad q = Delta_5 q - c q with c = 2 u^r/r = div_5 b. For N = tau int |grad q|^2 G_tau dy / int q^2 G_tau dy, with G_tau the five-dimensional backward heat kernel of tau = T_* - t centred at an axis point, completing the square in the exact identity gives N_t <= M^2 N + 4 (T_* - t) D^2, whose majorants int M^2 dt and int (T_* - t) D^2 dt diverge for the power histories S = (T_* - t)^{-alpha}, 1/2 < alpha < 1 (this concerns the majorants, not the signed errors). More decisively, a frequency bound has nothing to contradict: in the uniformly viscous scenario, with the kernel of scale r_n centred at (0, z_n) at time t_n, the rescaled Q_n(x) = (r_n/S_n) q(r_n x', z_n + r_n x_3, t_n) has |Q_n(e_1)| = 1 (Q_n(e_1) = 1 with the sign convention of the records; the lifted coordinates are (x', x_3) in R^4 x R) and |grad Q_n| <= r_n^2 H(t_n)/S_n <= L_0 := C_* C_1^2 G^2 (because q = -d_2 u_1 on {x_2 = 0}), so N <= L_0^2/kappa with kappa = mu(B_eps(e_1))/4, eps = min{1/2, 1/(2 L_0)}, mu the Gaussian probability measure (4 pi)^{-5/2} e^{-|x|^2/4} dx on R^5 (this record-scale frequency differs from the frequency with pole T_* unless the two radii coincide); q need not vanish on the axis.

Four further mechanisms were tested after round 30; none currently supplies an exclusion, and none is used elsewhere in this report. (a) Generalized helicity Casimirs (the author's; the identity R^2 Delta_5(R^{-2} g) = L g was checked by a script). For F in C^2 with F(0) = 0 and enough decay, the critical functional C_F(t) = int eta F(Gamma) dx has no swirl source (int d_z(q^2) F(Gamma) dx = -int r^{-4} Gamma^2 F'(Gamma) d_z Gamma dx = 0) and, by the integration by parts of sec. 1.1 (the axis term vanishes since F(0) = 0), dC_F/dt = int eta [2 F'(Gamma) L Gamma + F''(Gamma) |grad Gamma|^2] dx. The right side has no sign, and C_F = 0 at every time for data with u(Qx) = Q u(x), Q = diag(1, 1, -1), for which eta is odd and Gamma even in z. (b) Product barriers for the eta equation (the author's; checked by the same script). For the operator calL = d_t + b.grad - Delta_5 of the eta equation, calL(r^{-alpha} g(Gamma)) = r^{-alpha-2} { -g''(Gamma) r^2 |grad Gamma|^2 + (2 alpha - 4) g'(Gamma) r d_r Gamma + g(Gamma) [ -alpha r u^r - alpha (alpha - 2) ] } for r > 0. For g > 0 and alpha >= 2 (the critical weight is alpha = 3) the zero-order coefficient is negative wherever r u^r > 2 - alpha, in particular on every outflow region, and at points where grad Gamma = 0 the whole expression is then negative. So this ansatz supplies no supersolution for arbitrary data; where grad Gamma != 0 the derivative terms can compensate (round 31: alpha = 3, g(c) = e^{-4 c^2}, u^r = r, u^z = -2z, Gamma = r^2 (r^2 - 1)/2 near (r, z) = (1, 0) give the value 8 - 6 = 2 > 0 in the braces), and compensation along a particular trajectory is not excluded; a barrier for the forced eta equation must also dominate the source d_z(q^2). For alpha < 2 the weight is subcritical. (c) A swirl-normalized dynamically rescaled trajectory (research answer res29, checked by the author by hand and, for the test field, with a script). With V(y, s) = S^{-1} u(y/S, t), P(y, s) = S^{-2} p(y/S, t) and ds = S^2 dt, V_s + V.grad V + grad P = Delta V - a (V + y.grad V), where a = S'/S^3 >= 0 (S is locally Lipschitz with S' <= D S a.e.) is supported on the contact set {||u^theta(t)||_infty = S(t)}, a <= C_1 in the uniformly viscous scenario of sec. 3.7, and int_{s_0}^{s} a = log(S(t(s))/S(t(s_0))), which diverges as s -> infinity. At an active record (a contact time at which S is differentiable) the angular equation gives -R V^R >= 1 + a R^2. The normalized energy E = ||V||_2^2 obeys E_s + 2 ||grad V||_2^2 = a E, every dilation-invariant functional receives no contribution from a, and J_4 = int (V^theta/R)^2 dy receives -a J_4 but has a positive rate from the equation itself at an attained record of a Gaussian test field: for V^theta/R = e^{(1 - R^2 - Z^2)/2} and the Stokes stream function (b^R = -Psi_Z/R, b^Z = Psi_R/R) Psi = beta R^2 Z chi((R^2 + Z^2)/9) with beta > 4, 0 <= chi <= 1, chi = 1 on [0, 1], chi = 0 on [4, infinity) and |chi'| <= 1, one has a = beta - 4 and (J_4)_s >= (66 beta/25 - 3) J_4 > 0 (with zero meridional velocity the rate would be -7 J_4). A finite-dimensional example (w_s = w x - a w, x_s = (2 - 1/k) x^2 - 2 a x with the exact solution w = 1, x = a = k/(s + s_0), k > 1) shows that scaling neutrality, compactness and O(a) proximity to an equilibrium do not give a bounded adjoint mode with a positive projection on the dilation direction (at W = (1, 0) the adjoint solutions are (A, B - A s), bounded only for A = 0; the unbounded (1, -s) has projection one). (d) A localized axial impulse in branch (II) (research answer res30, checked as in (c)). For I_chi = (1/2) int chi r omega^theta dx = int u.Xi_chi dx with Xi_chi = curl(chi e_3 x (x - a)/2), a fixed smooth compactly supported axisymmetric cutoff chi and a fixed point a on the axis, the balance dI_chi/dt = int u_i u_j d_j (Xi_chi)_i dx + int u.Delta Xi_chi dx contains no pressure. The resulting exclusion of finite-axis branch-(II) limits assumes that, in the units of the gradient scale, the flux integrated from a fixed smooth time to s_n tends to 0 for every axisymmetric chi; by the balance itself and the smallness of the initial impulse in these units, this is equivalent to omega^theta = 0 for the terminal limit, so the balance supplies no estimate.

### 3.13 Shrinking physical cylinders (Proposition 20)

This section uses the fixed solution near its singular point, where the finite energy, the circulation bound and (A) are all available at once, instead of the limit U. Seregin [Se20] proved that axisymmetric suitable weak solutions have no Type I singular points: at a singular point the scale-invariant energy quantities A, E and C are all unbounded as the radius tends to 0. He also proved that a stream function bounded in local BMO gives regularity. Since the swirl part has a potential of BMO norm C G for every bounded circulation (Proposition 20(b)), the BMO criterion reduces to the meridional potential, whose local part is controlled by the local critical mass and whose far part is controlled by the energy at the chosen scale. This gives Proposition 20(c): the local critical mass is unbounded near every singular point, at every scale. These are fixed-data necessary conditions. The first unproved arrow is an upper bound, uniform as t -> T_*, for int_{C_rho(z_*)} |eta| on one cylinder, or for the local BMO norm of the meridional vector potential B = psi^theta e_theta. The local balance of this mass, d/dt int chi |eta| + 4 pi int chi(0, z) |eta(0, z)| dz <= int |eta| (b.grad chi + L chi) + int chi |d_z(q^2)|, has an axis drain, and its uncontrolled terms are the source and the cutoff transport. The local energy inequality bounds A + E at scale rho/2 by C and the pressure quantity D at scale rho; C is a Type II quantity and D is not controlled. (A) relates global sup norms that all diverge and does not enter these inequalities; this does not show that (A) cannot be useful in combination with other estimates. At the record scale, the scale-invariant quantities A, E, C and the mean-subtracted pressure quantity on cylinders of radius L r_n around the records are bounded for each fixed L. Proposition 20(e) shows where the unbounded quantities sit: the scaled energy of the meridional field alone, centered at the record tops, exceeds any M on scales between a multiple of r_n that depends on M and a scale tending to 0, with explicit heights and dyadic counts; the same holds for the scaled dissipation. This is again a necessary condition. The physical dissipation of disjoint record cylinders is summable, nested cylinders cannot be summed (a finite measure can have divergent scaled mass at every scale), and no universal instantaneous bound on the local mass, for this kinematic class, can depend only on E_0, G, C_* and rho (the dilates eps^{-3/2} b_0(x/eps) of one swirl-free field keep energy and shape ratio fixed while the local mass grows like eps^{-1/2}). The example does not rule out an estimate using the evolution of the fixed solution and all Case-1 hypotheses.

### 3.14 Circulation escape (Proposition 21)

This section tests whether the circulation must leave the neighbourhood of the singular point by diffusion into the axis, where Gamma = 0 and the radial part of L is absorbing, against the inflow. The local spacetime limsup G_0 of the circulation at the singular point exceeds gamma_* (Proposition 21(a); the circulation vanishes on the axis at every regular time), so the swirl records do not carry the local circulation supremum. Near the points that do, the Harnack chain of Proposition 21(b) shows that the normalized meridional speed is unbounded, or the circulation reaches deeper toward the axis than every fixed fraction of the radius. In the ancient limit the circulation supremum is approached only at infinite normalized radius; this does not by itself locate large meridional speeds there. Records of (Gamma - Gamma_0) r^{-beta} carry compression -r u^r >= 2 - beta (Proposition 21(c)). In the frame normalized at such a record this is -R V^R >= (2 - beta) nu at normalized radius 1, just below the threshold 2 nu of Theorem 5, which is needed with the opposite inequality uniformly at large radius. No fixed radial weight improves the threshold at all scales, and the weights depending on time or height that do improve it locally do so only where records are not known to occur. For a nonlinear function Phi(Gamma) in place of Gamma, with Phi' > 0, Phi(Gamma_0) = 0 and Phi/Phi' -> 0 at Gamma_0, put calR = Phi Phi''/Phi'^2. The certificate exceeds 2 only where calR > 1, and int_{Gamma_0}^{Gamma} (calR - 1) d xi = -Phi(Gamma)/Phi'(Gamma) < 0, so calR averages below 1 from the zero of Phi (a positive calR < 1 can still improve 2 - beta). The flux identity for scale averages of Gamma allows a change of order G over a time of order rho^2 at radius rho, even under bounded scale-invariant meridional energy, and by Proposition 20(e) that energy is unbounded on intermediate scales at the record tops. A proposed further estimate here is critical control of the meridional field at scales at and above the record scale, related to, but not shown equivalent to, the estimates proposed in Route B and Propositions 19 and 20. Here it appears as a near-axis compression bound -r u^r <= 2 - beta_0 on {Gamma > Gamma_0}, or as bounded meridional speed near circulation maxima together with a drop of the circulation at a fixed fraction of the radius, or (not yet a precise sufficient condition) as smallness of the circulation fluxes. Neither was derived from (A), (K), (TAIL), finite energy or the circulation bound.

### 3.15 Localized pressure and strain (Proposition 22)

This section tests whether the pressure field near a swirl record, or the axial strain that the record forces, is incompatible with the bounds of Case 1. The record forces inflow -u^r >= 1/r_n and therefore, by incompressibility, a mean axial strain at least 2/r_n^2 on the disk below the record (Proposition 22(b)). The record also forces d_n^2 >= 2 + 6 beta_n^2/gamma_n^2 for the normalized gradient, where beta_n = -r_n u^r >= 1. The swirl lowers the pressure on the axis, by at most G D(t), but the sign of its axial curvature is not fixed (the curvature formula contains a local term 2c^2/5 and has no sign). On the axis the strain obeys a Riccati equation forced by the axial pressure curvature. Its long-time average along an axial trajectory says that strain of size sigma is accompanied, up to viscous terms, by an averaged axial pressure curvature -sigma^2. For Burgers, the displayed axial stagnation pressure is meridional. The general identity does not separate the origins of p_zz, and the swirl contribution has no fixed sign. The radial momentum balance at the record fixes the material radial acceleration only up to the pressure Hessian bound, so it gives no sign. The Burgers vortex satisfies these local identities and the weak record conditions exactly, for compatible constants, with bounded circulation and derivatives and the velocity bound on an ellipsoid around the axis (Proposition 22(d)). It is not a Case-1 solution (no finite energy, unbounded velocity, divergent tail), so an exclusion needs some information not shared by this local model (examples are the velocity bound outside the ellipsoid, finite energy, the canonical pressure or the tail). Here two proposed targets are a signed bound on the meridional part of the radial momentum balance and a lower bound on the averaged axial pressure curvature near the record height; they are not shown to be necessary or equivalent. Neither was derived. The second would not suffice alone, because the averaged identity turns it into a lower bound on a third-order viscous term.

### 3.16 Ancient Liouville hypotheses (Proposition 23)

This section checks whether a published Liouville theorem for bounded ancient axisymmetric solutions with swirl has a hypothesis that every Case-1 limit satisfies. The hypotheses fall into five classes: critical pointwise bounds on the velocity or vorticity (Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak; Chen, Strain, Tsai and Yau; Li and Pan; Q. S. Zhang), critical integral bounds on the meridional drift (Lei and Zhang; Seregin), integrability of the circulation (Lei, Zhang and Zhao) or a near-axis modulus of the circulation (Lei and Zhang), tail or periodicity conditions (Lei, Zhang and Zhao; Lei, Ren and Zhang), and conditional rate packages (Seregin; Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol). Saturation of the circulation at large normalized radius (Proposition 23(a)) rules out uniform tail decay for every Case-1 limit, and the integrability, periodicity and rate hypotheses of the third and fourth classes fail as well, by the corresponding Liouville theorems and the nonzero terminal swirl; a modulus for the physical circulation near the axis is violated by the trajectory itself, while a modulus for the limit near its own axis holds trivially and is no obstruction. This shows only that those theorems do not apply to U directly. The fifth class consists of hypothesis packages that include upper rates in T_* - t, which (A) does not supply. The first two need a critical bound on the meridional field at scales between r_n and 1, which was not derived. Proposition 23(b) gives a localized sufficient form of that bound; it is Theorem 5 with the inflow bound required only on the near-maximal circulation set. Saturation of the circulation at large radius forces, by two-dimensional recurrence, inflow stronger than 2 nu/R somewhere in the near-maximal circulation set, and the threshold is critical for the exterior comparison. In physical variables (Proposition 23(c)) the near-maximal circulation at radii R_0 r_n < r <= L r_n must coexist with -r u^r > 2 somewhere near every sufficiently late record of the subsequence defining U. Compare Proposition 21(c), which needs -r u^r < 2 - beta_0 near the axis: the threshold rises to 2 and the region moves outward. On the near-maximal set the circulation is nearly flat (Proposition 23(e)), so the instantaneous source of the meridional vorticity equation is exponentially small there; this does not control the accumulated vorticity or the inflow it induces. The global energy bound alone does not exclude the inflow (if inflow of size 1/r occupied a region with all relevant lengths comparable to r, its scaled energy would be of order one against an allowance E_0/r), but a pointwise violation gives no such region, so this does not show that every energy argument fails. A local smallness condition on omega^theta cannot by itself give the bound: a compactly supported vector potential (alpha/2) R Z chi e_theta produces the inflow -alpha R/2 with zero vorticity on the region where chi = 1, so the far-field and harmonic parts must be controlled as well. Proposition 25(c) gives one specified sufficient form, the weighted critical bound calQ_omega <= 4 (viscosity-one units) through the Biot-Savart law, which controls local and far contributions for a field given by the Biot-Savart integral. That hypothesis was not derived.

### 3.17 Material lines and vorticity creation (Proposition 24)

This section tests whether following the fluid from the record, or the creation of meridional vorticity by the swirl, gives a quantity that must blow up or a residence time that must be infinite. Backward from the record the particle comes from a slightly larger radius with more circulation (Proposition 24(a)), but only on a short normalized window: the maximum principle controls the extrema of Gamma, not its monotonicity along particle paths. The vortex lines lie in circulation level sets, and the displayed derivative estimate does not establish the required coherence of the vorticity direction above the record scale. The record forces a positive mean axial strain on the disk below it, not a pointwise strain at the record particle. On the axis, stretching competes with the radial diffusion term 8 d, which balances it exactly in the Burgers vortex. In the weighted vorticity balances the source pairing of q^2 with the derivative of the weight is unsigned; nonlinear or coupled choices can leave additional unsigned terms. The source cannot vanish on any window of a Case-1 limit, so the accumulated absolute source diverges, but this is a necessary condition and signs nothing. Small window dissipation, o(r_n), would make the pairing small against record-scale weights. That estimate is false in Case 1: by scaling, the window dissipation is at least a constant times r_n. This unweighted dissipation-smallness criterion is impossible in Case 1; signed cancellation, localization or a different weighted estimate is not excluded. Proposed further estimates are the propagation of a signed circulation loss along paths beyond the record window, and a sign or gain for a weighted vorticity quantity whose unsigned pairing is controlled at intermediate scales. They have not been proved sufficient as complete strategies, and no equivalence with the other proposed meridional estimates is established.

### 3.18 The far-field inflow arrow (Proposition 25)

This section tests whether the one-sided far-field bound of Proposition 23(c) follows from the structure of the equations. Proposition 25(a) sharpens the witness: strong inflow is needed only at near-maximal points where the circulation still increases outward. The vertical-flux identity (b) limits each connected strong-inflow interval to axial length K R^2/(2 nu), but bounds neither their number nor their duration. The scalar models (d) show that the scalar circulation equation, incompressibility and the record conditions are consistent with strong far-field inflow: a smooth bounded divergence-free drift with the largest axial flux allowed by (b) carries a bounded steady circulation with the record normalization and inflow above 2 nu on a near-maximal region of axial extent comparable to R^2 at every large radius. The model fails the vorticity equation at large radius, so it shows only that the listed scalar and kinematic properties are insufficient; further physical or drift estimates might exclude it. The Biot-Savart estimate (c) gives the weighted critical vorticity bound calQ <= 4 in viscosity-one units as a stronger sufficient condition, and the kernel weight r R/(d D) cannot be replaced by the critical Morrey norm alone. (e) shows that the bound, posed for the whole class LL, is equivalent to the Liouville problem for LL; the equivalence is not automatic for the subclass of physical limits, and information that distinguishes physically obtainable limits from unrestricted members of LL is one possible route. Candidates are finite energy, (A) at the intermediate scales R_0 r_n < r <= L r_n, or a fixed-data bound on calQ. Part (f) tests the far field directly. The slender reduction is exact up to explicit lower-order terms but not uniform near Z ~ R, and its global profiles violate the whole-space flux bound. The Landau flow with potential swirl satisfies the exterior equations exactly, with bounded velocity and bounded signed inner flux, and has -R U^R = 20 nu/9 along a ray, so an exclusion must use information beyond these exterior conditions, such as the strict deficit A - Gamma > 0 with d_R Gamma > 0 at the witnesses, or the axis. Finite energy bounds the normalized energy only by E_0/(gamma_n^2 r_n), which diverges, (A) gives no small normalized derivatives, and (TAIL) controls a velocity-square tail rather than vorticity concentration. None of these gave the bound.

### 3.19 Averaged circulation flux (Proposition 26)

This section tests whether the conservation form of the circulation equation, together with ancientness and the flux bound, constrains the far field. Integrating over a cylinder of radius rho gives an exact flux identity in which the axis contributes nothing. Averaging over long boxes in Z and in the past kills the time derivative, the axial flux and the mean of the inflow, and a Banach limit MM (Proposition 26(c)) gives the averaged radial equation 2 nu a(rho) - nu rho a'(rho) = F(rho) for the averaged circulation a(rho) := MM[Gamma_U(rho)], where F(rho) := MM[Q_rho Gamma_U(rho)] and Q_rho = -rho U^R(rho). Its bounded solution is a(rho) = (rho^2/nu) int_rho^infinity F(s) s^{-3} ds. In the exterior line sink the flux through every cylinder is the same nonzero constant, which is possible only because there is no axis. Two consequences were proved. The averaged deficit cannot decay faster than order nu A/(K rho), which gives an explicit constant 2 nu/K in a box-averaged form of the rate theorem of Lei, Ren and Zhang. Averaged saturation and a critical one-sided outflow bound R U^R <= C at large radii cannot hold together for any C. Proposition 23(b) constrains the inflow on the near-maximal set; this constrains the outflow on the deficit set. Neither averaged saturation nor the outflow bound was derived for Case-1 limits. Proposition 23 gives saturation only along some points, and the box averages can stay below the supremum. In fact they must: an integrating factor in the radial equation shows that at each fixed radius rho the mean deficit in all sufficiently long boxes is at least 2 nu A/(K rho + 3 nu) minus any prescribed epsilon, and an outflow bound C would raise this to 2 nu A/(C + 2 nu) at all large radii. The same averaging applied to convex functions of the circulation (Proposition 26(e)) recovers the critical value 2 nu for weighted box means of the inflow at every circulation level, but its conclusion is only that the corresponding superlevel sets have zero density. At the endpoint, a weighted mean inflow at most 2 nu at all large radii forces the weighted mass to vanish at every radius, and nonzero weighted mass at one radius forces the weighted mean inflow strictly above 2 nu at arbitrarily large radii. One sufficient contradiction would therefore follow from nonzero weighted mass at one radius, for some level below A, together with weighted mean inflow at most 2 nu at all large radii, for the same box family. Two explicit models (Proposition 26(f)) locate the obstruction. In the first, a bounded drift with finite vertical flux carries an exact steady circulation that approaches its supremum at large radius with strong inflow and unbounded outflow, yet every box average vanishes; near-maximal plateaux at growing radii do not produce saturation at a fixed radius. In the second, the circulation saturates and the linear averaged identities hold, but the unaveraged circulation equation fails, and so does the convex identity for the square of the circulation. The scalar example prevents deriving averaged saturation from the scalar hypotheses alone, and the periodic example prevents a contradiction from the linear averaged identities alone; neither rules out nonlinear averaged estimates. All convex identities together form a balance, at each deficit level, between the inflow and the averaged Dirichlet energy carried by that level (Proposition 26(h)). In logarithmic means this hierarchy is consistent with saturation, by an explicit two-level solution; these logarithmic-mean constraints alone give no contradiction, and additional information could be a flux-energy estimate or information retained at finite radii. For steady fields of fixed axial period the hierarchy forces the logarithmic mean of R d(R) to diverge under saturation, and Proposition 26(i) excludes nonzero steady periodic circulation altogether, which is why the periodic example fails. The averaged momentum and pressure balances add no sign: a periodic field satisfies them, the full pressure Poisson equation with bounded pressure, and saturation. Nonzero weighted mass at a fixed radius is a positive upper density of a separated superlevel set in a sampled-translate family (it does not establish recurrence of the particular record configuration), and at scale R the needed flux bound is first order in the effective viscosity nu/R. One sufficient target is positive mass and the critical flux bound for the same box family, potentially obtained from the coupled dynamics or compatible fixed-data estimates (the coupled equations are not claimed to be the only possible source). A critical bound on the oscillation of the axial flux through disks suffices (Proposition 26(i)); the available bound is one power of the radius short, and an exact scalar solution with slowly increasing axial wavelength saturates, so the scalar equation alone does not supply it; bounded steady long patterns of the maximal form psi = nu beta R^2 f(Z/R^2) carry no leading-order inflow, which excludes one mechanism for violating the bound but does not prove it. The bound may grow with the radius, as C(rho) with int^infinity d rho/(rho (1 + C(rho)^2)) = infinity, and this growth condition is sharp at the scalar level on the scale C(rho) = (log rho)^p: exponents p <= 1/2 exclude positive mass, while for p in (1/2, 1] exact scalar solutions with bounded drift saturate uniformly (Proposition 26(i)). On the physical solution the window errors can be made to vanish at every fixed normalized radius along a slow diagonal, but this gives no relation between the box sizes and the radii where the circulation is nearly maximal. Long-box means also vanish identically for axially localized fields, which a blow-up of a finite-energy solution may produce. For those, Z-integrated time means obey the same radial equation, but the critical value 2 nu degrades to (2 - alpha) nu when the Z-integrated weighted mass grows like the radius to the power alpha, as it does when the axial extent of the superlevel sets grows at that rate (Proposition 26(g)). Three further items reformulate the averaged target. A critical decay, at large radii, of the averaged fluctuation of the axial velocity about an axially uniform flow implies the flux bound (Proposition 26(j)). The averaged meridional and swirl energy budgets close exactly, the total centrifugal work satisfies W(infinity) = -nu D_theta(infinity) <= 0 and the averaged swirl dissipation is finite, but the pressure work and the meridional transport have no sign, and an explicit kinematic field satisfies every averaged budget while violating the flux bound (Proposition 26(k)). Long-box means are expectations under invariant measures on the hull of the limit, so positive mass means that some invariant measure charges nonconstant fields; for an ergodic such measure the flux bound becomes a bound on the negative second moment int_{k != 0} k^{-2} d sigma_{Q,R}(k) of the axial spectral measure of Q_R = d_Z Phi_R, after projecting Phi_R off its axially invariant component (a condition at low axial frequency), and limits on whose hull every invariant measure is trivial, which include uniformly axially localized structures, are outside the averaged route altogether (Proposition 26(l)).

## 4. Limitations and open questions

1. The decisive open step is an exclusion of both branches of Theorem 13. Possible sufficient approaches in branch (I) include a property of the Case-1 limits not shared by every member of LL (or a Liouville theorem for LL with Q <= C_*; a Liouville theorem for eternal members would exclude an eternal limit selected by Proposition 15 if its viscosity is positive, not an arbitrary prescribed viscous subsequence); in branch (II) they include a backward-decay estimate for the inviscid gradient-scale limits as in Proposition 12, a critical bound N(s_n) = o(1/kappa_n) as in Proposition 14, or another rigidity mechanism for ancient swirl-free Euler flows with |V|, |grad V| <= 1, |grad^2 V| <= C_*. None of these was obtained.
2. (A) involves global historical suprema whose maximizers need not lie near the swirl records. Theorem 13 shows that in branch (I) the normalized numbers m_n, d_n, h_n are bounded, and in branch (II) the gradient maximizers leave every swirl-record observation cylinder; in neither case was (A) localized to the record cylinders, and passing (A) with its constant to the swirl-record limit U was not justified.
3. Theorem 9 is about the global gradient; it does not place large gradients at the swirl records and does not use (K) or (TAIL). It rules out only one growth regime.
4. In branch (I) the swirl of the gradient-record limit has a uniform but nonexplicit lower bound on a fixed spacetime ball (from compactness and [KNSS, Theorem 5.2]); the amplitude and location of the original swirl record are not retained, because the historical swirl maximum may be attained far from the gradient record.
5. The cited results [CIV], [CIV2], [CSTY2], [SS], [KNSS], [LZ] (Theorems 1.1 and 1.4), [LZ2], [LZZ], [LRZ1], [LRZ2], [LP], [QZ], [Se20] (Theorem 2.1, Proposition 1.4, sec. 3) were used as stated in the sources (statements checked in the arXiv texts where available); their proofs were not re-derived. The epsilon-regularity lemma [SS, Lemma 1.2] is used in the form quoted in [CIV]; the original was not retrieved. [Pan] and [ZZ] were not retrieved and are used only as remarks. Proposition 20(c) uses [Se11, Proposition 1.1] only as it is quoted in [Se20, sec. 3]; that note was not retrieved, and the use is qualitative (its bound must involve pressure information, so only finiteness for one rescaled solution is used). [Se20, Lemma 2.2] as printed has a sign misprint and a misplaced axis condition and is not used; Proposition 20(d) uses only [LZ, Theorem 1.1].
6. Questions that would advance the problem: (i) a Liouville theorem for bounded ancient axisymmetric NS solutions with bounded circulation and a quantitative shape bound sup|W| sup|grad^2 W| <= C_* sup|grad W|^2; (ii) whether Type II gradient growth under (A) forces windows, or the weaker decay of Proposition 12, along some sequence; (iii) a Liouville theorem for ancient swirl-free axisymmetric Euler flows with |V|, |grad V| <= 1 and bounded Hessian under backward decay assumptions weaker than those of Proposition 12; (iv) a localization of (A) to record cylinders; (v) a critical a priori bound, under (A), for the potential-vorticity mass ||omega^theta/r||_{L^1(dx)} or for the time integral of ||d_z(q^2)||_{L^1(dx)}; (vi) a restriction on the space-time distribution of the inflow (u^r)^- q^2, whose total is infinite in Case 1, strong enough to close one of the functionals of Proposition 16 on the record cylinders; (vii) for some constant c_0, a bound sup_{tau <= 0} ||(psi_U - c_0 R/2) e_theta||_{BMO(R^3)} < infinity for the meridional vector potential of the swirl-record limit U, derived from the fixed trajectory (Proposition 19(b)); (viii) an upper bound, uniform as t -> T_*, for the local critical mass int_{C_rho(z_*)} |omega^theta/r| dx on one cylinder around a singular point (Proposition 20(c)); (ix) a late-time bound -r u^r <= 2 - beta_0 near the axis on the set where Gamma > Gamma_0, for some beta_0 > 0 and Gamma_0 < G_0 (Proposition 21(c)); (x) a lower bound, near the record heights and on time average along axial trajectories, for the axial pressure curvature on the axis, together with a bound on the viscous term of Proposition 22(c); (xi) for some fixed R_0 >= 1 and every finite L, a bound -r u^r <= 2, for all sufficiently large indices n of the subsequence defining U (the first index may depend on L), on the window {R_0 r_n < r <= L r_n, |z - z_n| <= L r_n, t_n - L^2 T_n <= t <= t_n} intersected with {Gamma > gamma_n (A - m)} (Proposition 23(c)), for which the weighted critical vorticity bound calQ_{omega^theta(t)} <= 4 at those points, and by Proposition 25(a) only where moreover d_r Gamma > 0, would suffice (Proposition 25(c)); posed for the whole class LL this bound is equivalent to the Liouville problem, so information that distinguishes the physical limits from unrestricted members of LL is one possible route; (xii) propagation of a signed circulation loss along backward particle paths beyond the record window, or a sign for the pairing int q^2 phi_z against a record-adapted weight (Proposition 24); (xiii) averaged saturation of the circulation of a Case-1 limit for some family of long boxes, together with a critical one-sided outflow bound R U^R <= C at large radii (Proposition 26(d)), or nonzero weighted mass at one radius together with weighted mean inflow at most 2 nu at all large radii (Proposition 26(e)); by the models of Proposition 26(f), (h) this needs more than the linear averaged identities, the averaged momentum balances and the sign of the averaged Dirichlet energy; one sufficient addition is a critical bound on the oscillation of the axial flux through disks in a box family with positive mass (Proposition 26(i)), which the scalar equation alone does not give (the first construction of Proposition 26(i)); these alternatives are not claimed to be exhaustive; (xiv) a uniform bound, at one fixed scale delta, for the uniformly local energy N_{n,delta} of the rescaled record data (sec. 3.12, time remaining to the singularity) outside branch (I) of Theorem 13, which with [KMT, Corollary 1.2] gives regularity on a fixed normalized forward window around the records; this excludes Case 1 only if the window extends past the singular time (sigma_n < gamma_n s_{2,n}; the guaranteed interval is open) and covers a singular point (within a fixed multiple of r_n of the records; convergence a_n -> x_* does not bound |a_n - x_*|/r_n); in branch (I) the bound holds, the normalized data are globally bounded and [KMT, Corollary 1.3] gives a window global in space; but that window proves sigma_n >= gamma_n s_2, a lower bound on the time remaining of the same kind as the restart bound, and an incompatible strict upper bound would be needed for this line to give a contradiction; outside branch (I) neither the temporal comparison nor the spatial coverage has been established; (xv) a property of the Case-1 limits that selects or excludes one branch of Proposition 23(f), for example equality in Proposition 23(g)(vi). (xvi) an exclusion of atoms on the axis for the terminal energy measure of an axisymmetric solution with bounded circulation under (A); by Proposition 11(vii) this would leave in branch (II) only limits along records with M^5/D^3 -> 0, and atoms are excluded by ||u(t)||_infty = o((T_* - t)^{-3/5}) but by no estimate derived here (for general Leray-Hopf solutions the absence of atoms at a first singular time is, as far as we know, not established; [LS] gives it under L^q L^p conditions, among them Type I in time); the Euler limits alone do not exclude the finite-energy alternative if, as suggested in research answer res31 (not checked here), smooth translating vortex rings with bounded Hessian are members of the class of Proposition 8(c) for C_* at least their shape ratio; (xvii) along branch-(II) records with M^5/D^3 >= epsilon_0, in the notation of Proposition 18(f), choose c_n -> 0 such that the normalized exterior integral kappa_n int_a^{s_n} int_{|eta| > lambda_n, r >= c_n l_n} |d_z(q^2)| dx dt tends to 0; a bound kappa_n int_a^{s_n} int_{|eta| > lambda_n, r < c_n l_n} |d_z(q^2)| dx dt -> 0 for this same sequence, or such a bound with a fixed c > 0 in place of c_n, or a bound o(1) for the adjoint pairing int int q_{V_n}^2 d_Z phi_n of Proposition 18(c) (whose absolute value is at least a fixed positive constant for large n), would exclude these records (the smallness of the inner integral for some unrelated sequence c_n -> 0 holds automatically and excludes nothing). Research answer res35 (its kinematic part is not reproduced or checked here) describes a smooth kinematic family, not a Navier-Stokes solution, of thin swirl cells at radius of order 1/S along a near-axis tube for which the energy, dissipation, int q^4 dx and L^infty budgets used in this report leave room for an inner source of order 1/kappa_n. So, for compatible fixed profile constants, these magnitude budgets alone do not force the required inner smallness; the kinematic family does not satisfy the evolution equations, does not involve the actual adjoint, and is not shown to meet all necessary conditions simultaneously; (xviii) a Liouville theorem for LL together with a tightness property of the inner source of (xvii): research answer res36 (sketch, checked by the author only in outline) shows that if, at points of the inner high-level source where |d_z(q^2)| >= beta S^{1/2} H^{3/2}, the quantity M(t) (S(t)/H(t))^{1/2} stays bounded, then the rescalings at length (S(t)/H(t))^{1/2} have subsequential limits in LL (by Proposition 18(g) these points lie at rescaled distance at most (2 sqrt6/beta)^{1/2} from the axis and the rescaled source there is bounded below); so if LL is empty, the inner bound of (xvii) would follow from: for every epsilon > 0 there are beta, K > 0 such that the normalized inner source outside the set where |d_z(q^2)| >= beta S^{1/2} H^{3/2} and M (S/H)^{1/2} <= K is eventually at most epsilon. That answer also describes smooth kinematic fields, not Navier-Stokes solutions, compatible with the magnitude budgets, in which the inner source sits at r = o((S/H)^{1/2}), or has vanishing amplitude in these units, or sits in regions of unbounded normalized strain; and the Burgers vortex, which has bounded strain, circulation and Hessian but zero source, shows that bounded strain alone does not place a limit in LL; (xix) along branch-(II) records, in the notation of Proposition 18(h), a bound int_{a_n}^{s_n} int_{lambda'_n/2 < |eta| < lambda'_n, r < c_n B_n l_n} q^4 dx dt = o(D_n M_n/B_n) for some sequence c_n -> 0 with e_n = o(c_n^4 B_n^3 epsilon_n); by Proposition 18(h) this would exclude these records. Research answer res37 (checked by the author by hand, the cutoff estimates only in outline) disproves the specific uniform instantaneous estimate stated below under the spatial bounds of this report. With R^2 = r^2 + z^2, q = e^{-R^2/2} and a meridional field b^r = -r phi_z, b^z = 2 phi + r phi_r with -Delta_5 phi = d_z e^{-R^2}, one has f := -Delta_5 phi = d_z(q^2) = -2 z e^{-R^2} and Delta_5 f = (4 R^2 - 14) f; for the data alpha times this field (cut off far away, which does not change E = {|f| > 1/4}) the potential vorticity is alpha f and the source alpha^2 f, so E = {|alpha f| > alpha/4} and the instantaneous rate of Q_{alpha/4} is int_E (alpha^2 + alpha (4 R^2 - 14)) |f| dx >= alpha (alpha - 14) int_E |f| dx > 0 for alpha = 28. About L/h disjoint copies rescaled to size h = c_cell delta^{14/25} along an axial interval of length L = delta^{11/25}, together with a fixed swirl-free compact core profile v rescaled to size delta^{12/25} with velocity of order delta^{-18/25}, give smooth finite-energy data with the bounds E_0, G, M, D, C_* D^2/M of this report, kappa of order delta^{6/25}, epsilon fixed, S/M of order delta^{4/25} and the gradient maximizer at bounded distance from the axis in units M/D, for which the rate of Q_lambda at lambda = c_lambda D^2/(B M) (with c_cell^3 = alpha B/(4 c_lambda X_v), X_v = ||grad v||_infty^2/||v||_infty) is at least of order delta^{-31/25} while ||grad u||_2^2/(B^2 epsilon kappa) is of order delta^{-30/25}. So no estimate d/dt Q_lambda <= C ||grad u||_2^2/(B^2 epsilon kappa) at such levels holds for all data with these bounds. These are different data at different scales, not one solution; the time-integrated balance of Proposition 18(h) avoids this obstruction at the cost of the q^4 term, which the kinematic family of res35 described in (xvii) matches in order of magnitude (int q^4 dx of order delta^{-73/25} over times of order delta, against D M of order delta^{-48/25}). Another sufficient condition, by the corrected balance of Proposition 18(h), is int_a^{s_n} int (u^r)^- r q^4 dx dt = o(D_n M_n/B_n) for some fixed a; research answer res38 (described in the key estimates of Proposition 18(h)) rules out a scale-uniform instantaneous bound of this weighted inflow, or of the derivative of the corrected functional, by C (D M/(B^4 epsilon)) ||grad u||_2^2 under the listed spatial bounds.
