Research answer res39 (the following checked by the author by hand) extends the instantaneous obstruction to int Psi_lambda(eta) dx + mu int r^2 q^4 f(eta/lambda) dx, for every fixed mu > 0 and fixed nonzero f >= 0 in C_c^2((1, 2)), choosing v_* in (1, 2) with f(v_*) > 0 and beta > 0, and freezing lambda = (beta/v_*) h^{-3}: replacing Phi by (Z - beta R^2/8) zeta(|y|), for which -Delta_5 Phi = beta and V = (-R, 2Z - beta R^2/2) on the unit ball, the same scalings give eta = beta h^{-3}, so eta/lambda = v_*, on the whole swirl support, the source term of the band balance vanishes (d_z eta = 0 there), and the cutoff contributes no extra term since q^4 d_z(q^2) = d_z(q^6)/3; the derivative is again of order sigma^{37} against sigma^{36}. These are instantaneous data from different solutions. Their intrinsic strain time is h^2/sigma, and the initial inflow rate multiplied by this time is O(sigma^{-2} D M/B); no persistence or upper-duration estimate is proved here, so this construction does not disprove the time-integrated condition along the fixed solution. Research answer res41 (checked by the author by hand, except the uniform short-time bounds for its family of cells) adds the following. With W = int r^2 q^4 dx and I_pm = int (u^r/r)^pm r^2 q^4 dx, the identity for W in Proposition 18(h) reads 6 int_a^s I_- dt = W(s) - W(a) + 12 int_a^s int r^2 q^2 |grad q|^2 dx dt + 6 int_a^s I_+ dt, so the sufficient condition above is equivalent to bounds o(D_n M_n/B_n) for the three nonnegative terms W(s_n), 12 int int r^2 q^2 |grad q|^2 and 6 int I_+, none of which was derived (the bound W <= G^2 int q^2 dx is false, since W scales like lambda^3 and int q^2 dx like lambda under u -> lambda u(lambda .)). Since W' <= 6 ||(u^r/r)^-||_infty W, Gronwall's inequality and the lower bound of Proposition 18(h) give the necessary strain exposure int_a^{s_n} ||(u^r/r)^-(t)||_infty dt >= (1/6) log(3 c_h D_n M_n/(B_n W(a))) for large n when W(a) > 0. The Burgers vortex u^r = -A r, u^z = 2 A z, Gamma = gamma (1 - e^{-A r^2/2}) shows that viscous diffusion of the circulation can cancel the inviscid growth of q under persistent compression (along the trajectories L Gamma/Gamma tends to -2A while -2 u^r/r = 2A). An exact short evolution of N disjoint compression cells of the type above, carrying swirl of relative size kappa = N^{-1/2} and rescaled to viscosity one by u(x, t) = kappa^{-5} U(x/kappa^4, t/kappa^9), has bounded energy and circulation, D/M^2 of order kappa, S/M = O(kappa), time-integrated dissipation O(kappa) and time-integrated inflow of order M D; it rules out any bound of the inflow by (D M/B) F(D/M^2, S/M, int ||grad u||_2^2 dt) with F -> 0, but it starts with W already of order M D and has no record or witness structure, so it does not disprove the condition along the fixed solution.
7. Information that the limit forgets was tested in quantitative form. In the normalized units of the swirl records (length r_n, velocity gamma_n/r_n, viscosity 1/gamma_n) the energy is at most E_0/(gamma_n^2 r_n) and the dissipation over the whole history at most E_0/(2 gamma_n r_n). Writing calE_n for the supremum of the normalized energy over the normalized past [-calL_n, 0], calL_n = t_n/T_n, and calD_n for the normalized dissipation over that interval, the energy inequality gives calE_n calD_n/(calL_n nu_n^4) <= E_0^2/(2 t_n) for the product that controls small critical data; this bound does not tend to zero with r_n, and it is neither an equality nor a lower bound on the actual product. At normalized distance delta/r_n from the rescaled singular set the normalized velocity is at most C_delta r_n/gamma_n (local energy, interpolation and epsilon-regularity), but the exterior circulation is not small. Tested quantitative versions of the Liouville arguments of [KNSS], [LZZ], [LRZ1], [LRZ2], [LZ] and of a forced potential-vorticity argument give deteriorating input bounds, sometimes only a nonvanishing bound independent of r_n; no uniform quantitative version of every cited Liouville argument is established here. Along a branch-(I) subsequence, with P_kappa a fixed smooth real radial low-pass Fourier multiplier and Q_kappa = I - P_kappa, for a sufficiently small fixed c > 0 the high-pass part at the record frequency satisfies ||Q_{c/r_n} u(t_n)||_2^2 >= c gamma_n^2 r_n (the low-pass swirl at the record is at most C r_n kappa M(t_n) <= S_n/2 with kappa = c/r_n, and D(t_n) <= C S_n/r_n spreads the remaining amplitude over a ball of radius comparable to r_n), while the commutator bound for the energy transfer into these frequencies gives only o(1) over the history; the missing factor r_n is the supercriticality of the energy. The commutator estimate is not specific to axisymmetry; the stated lower bound uses the swirl record and branch (I). For a kinematic history satisfying the numerical constraints, (A), finite energy and integrable dissipation, put l_n = r_n^{3/4}; at the record tops the scaled meridional quantities A_b and E_b are of order (rho/r_n)^2 for r_n << rho <= l_n, and for l_n <~ rho -> 0 the construction gives lower bounds of order rho^{-2/3} (the rho^{-2/3} asymptotics at all small radii is a statement at the terminal top), so pigeonholing the numerical bounds does not produce good scales. A scalar history with M = 2S, D = 2S^2, H = 4S^3 satisfies (A) with waiting times between levels that are unbounded after division by the record time T_n, so forward growth gives no contradiction at the scalar level. The model histories and their verification are not supplied here; they are reported illustrations from a research answer. Verification: research answer res17, checked by the author by hand and with a script for the exponents; the model histories are kinematic or scalar, not Navier-Stokes solutions. Two further kinds of fixed-trajectory information were tested in branch (I) (research answer res40, checked by the author by hand, except the uniform Sobolev bounds for the family of data in the second obstruction below, for which a scaling argument is given there). First, let X_s be the backward diffusion of the circulation equation started at a record point x_n at time t_n (radial drift -u^r - 1/R, axial drift -u^z, noise sqrt2 in each coordinate, killed at the axis), and Y_s = sigma Gamma(X_s, t_n - s) with sigma = sgn Gamma(x_n, t_n) and Y = 0 after killing. Y is a bounded martingale with quadratic variation 2 |grad Gamma|^2 ds, so 2 E int_0^{(t_n - t_0) ^ zeta} |grad Gamma(X_s, t_n - s)|^2 ds <= G^2 - gamma_n^2 (zeta the killing time), additively over disjoint backward windows under the same terminal record, and by optional stopping the path keeps Y > g/2 on the whole backward interval with probability at least g/(2G - g) whenever gamma_n >= g. In the uniformly viscous scenario of sec. 3.7 with M <= C_0 S and D <= C_1 S^2 one may take g = 1/C_0; for geometric records S_k = S_0 lambda^k the ancestor radius R at time t_k satisfies R S_k >= (g/(2 C_1))^{1/2} on that event, and the expected number of k with R <= L/S_k at time t_k (alive) is at most 1/p, p = Phi(-(L + C_0 theta)/(2 theta)^{1/2}), theta = (lambda^2 - 1)/(4 C_1), Phi the standard normal distribution function. An exclusion would follow from divergence, along the terminal records n, of the cumulative mixing E_n sum_k int_{I_k} |grad Gamma(X_s, t_n - s)|^2 ds over the disjoint backward windows I_k = [t_n - t_k, t_n - t_k + theta/S_k^2], or if, for some fixed L < infinity, limsup_{n -> infinity} sum_k P_n{zeta > t_n - t_k, R_{t_n - t_k} <= L/S_k} = infinity, where the sum runs over the earlier geometric records and, for each n, all probabilities use the diffusion started at the same terminal record (x_n, t_n); a uniform positive lower bound on an increasing number of these probabilities would suffice. Neither was proved. Second, two obstructions. On r > a the exact steady field u^r = -beta/r, u^z = 0, Gamma = G_e (1 - (a/r)^{beta - 2}), beta > 2 (the field of Proposition 23(b)) has outward dyadic survival losses (a/r_j)^{beta-2} (1 - 2^{2-beta})/(1 - (a/r_{j+1})^{beta-2}) and circulation-variance charges G_e^2 (1 - (a/r_j)^{beta-2}) (a/r_j)^{beta-2} (1 - 2^{2-beta}), r_j = 2^j r_0, both summable although -r u^r = beta > 2. And a smooth decaying finite-energy datum, consisting of a compact core (u^theta = r e^{(1 - r^2 - z^2)/2} near the origin, meridional field (-8r, 16z) there, so that u^theta has its maximum 1 at (1, 0) with d_t u^theta = 4 there) and disjoint pure-swirl tori with circulation 4 f((r - R_j)/w_j) f(z/w_j), R_j = 2^{4j+8}, w_j = 2^j, f a smooth cutoff equal to 1 on [-1/2, 1/2] (energies O(2^{-2j})), has sup_x Gamma(x, t) = 4 at every time of its smooth existence interval, by the backward representation on the plateaus of the tori. The exterior example blocks a uniform per-annulus charge under the stated exterior hypotheses. The datum blocks a strictly positive decrement of the global circulation supremum at every genuine small swirl first crossing under finite energy and the short-time norm bounds; it supplies no singular time, no unbounded record chain and no Case-1 witnesses, so a decrement estimate that uses this additional singular history remains open.
8. Steady members of LL were tested separately. For a bounded smooth steady member the total head Phi = P + |U|^2/2 (the pressure is Lipschitz, since its gradient nu Delta U - U.grad U is bounded) satisfies nu Delta Phi - U.grad Phi = nu |omega|^2, where in axisymmetric form |omega|^2 = (d_Z U^theta)^2 + (omega^theta)^2 + (d_R U^theta + U^theta/R)^2, and for a steady Case-1 normalized limit the record conditions give |omega(e_1)|^2 >= 1. Hence a steady member whose head attains its supremum on R^3, for instance one with limsup_{|x| -> infinity} Phi <= Phi(e_1), is constant (strong maximum principle for nu Delta - U.grad, then omega = 0 and U is a bounded harmonic field), which contradicts the record. The attainment is not known: the swirl maximum gives no extremum of P + |b|^2/2, a bounded subharmonic function may approach its supremum only at infinity, and unbounded superlevel components carry an outer flux of unknown sign. The circulation equation with the scalar Case-1 facts does not exclude steady members: with a = 1 + R^2 and D = a^2 + 4Z^2, the smooth divergence-free field with stream function psi = 6 nu R^2 Z a/D (U^R = -psi_Z/R, U^Z = psi_R/R) and U^theta = 2R/D^{1/2} solves b.grad Gamma = nu L Gamma exactly, has U^theta <= 1 with equality only at e_1, Gamma = 2R^2/D^{1/2} < 2 with d_R Gamma > 0 and Gamma(R, 0) -> 2, -U^R(e_1) = 3 nu and -R U^R(R, 0) -> 6 nu, but its meridional vorticity residual R_eta = b.grad eta - nu Delta_5 eta - d_Z(q^2) satisfies d_Z R_eta(e_1) = 648 nu^2 + 2, so it is not a Navier-Stokes field. Integration of the steady eta equation over Z in [-H, H] gives an exact identity whose limit H -> infinity needs an integrability that bounded U, grad U and Gamma do not give, and whose transport term int U^R eta dZ has no sign. Case-1 limits are not known to be steady or time periodic: a steady limit would follow, for example, from r_n S_n^{-2} sup |d_t u| -> 0 on compact rescaled sets, which is not established, and no strict Lyapunov functional on LL is known that would make the supports of invariant measures steady. Among the steady Liouville theorems, [LZZ, Theorem 1.3 and Remark 1.4] (Gamma in L^infinity_t L^p_x for some finite p, or Gamma -> 0 uniformly, also in the ancient case), [CW] (steady solutions with the decay assumptions stated there, under Gamma -> 0 at infinity, Gamma in L^q for some 2 <= q < infinity, or the one-sided bound u^r >= -1/r at viscosity one) and the slab, periodic and D-solution results [BGWX], [WY] do not cover bounded steady solutions with bounded circulation alone. Verification: research answer res19, checked by the author by hand and with a script (independent code); literature statements checked at abstract level, [LZZ] in the full text.
9. Mechanisms of other types were tested at their first step (research answer res42, checked by the author by hand, the Gaussian integrals with a script). (a) Topology of the meridional flow. A swirl record is not a meridional stagnation point (u^r < 0 there), and the axial Galilean transformation U_c(r, z, t) = U(r, z - ct, t) + c e_z, which preserves the Navier-Stokes equations, axisymmetry, the circulation bound, the swirl maxima and the record inequality, removes every meridional stagnation point of a bounded ancient solution when c > sup |U^z| (it does not preserve finite energy). The listed properties alone, allowing their finite constants to change, therefore cannot force meridional stagnation in the chosen frame. A stagnation-based argument for the fixed physical solution could instead use additional information selecting its distinguished frame. (b) Backward Gaussian monotonicity. At viscosity one, for F in C^2 with F(0) = 0, an axis point x_*, s = t_* - t, X = x - x_* and the three-dimensional backward heat kernel G_3 = (4 pi s)^{-3/2} e^{-|X|^2/(4s)}, the quantity E_F = int F(Gamma) G_3 dx satisfies E_F' = -int F''(Gamma) |grad Gamma|^2 G_3 dx - (2s)^{-1} int F(Gamma)(2 + X.b) G_3 dx, because the dx-adjoint of L is Delta_5 (the axis term vanishes since F(0) = 0) and d_t G_3 + Delta_5 G_3 = -G_3/s. The compression term X.b has no sign: for the finite-energy datum Gamma = r^2 e^{(1 - r^2 - z^2)/2}, b^r = -Psi_z/r, b^z = Psi_r/r with Psi = 80 sqrt2 r^2 z e^{-5(r^2 + z^2)/4}, x_* = 0, s = 1 and F(Gamma) = Gamma^2 one has E_F'/E_F = 2/5 > 0 at the initial time, while the swirl maximum (equal to 1, at (1, 0)) increases. (c) Comparison. The order Gamma_1 <= Gamma_2, eta_1 <= eta_2 is not preserved: for smooth finite-energy data with equal circulations, b_1 = 0, eta_1 = 0 and eta_2 = h, where h is nonzero, smooth, compactly supported, axisymmetric and nonnegative, with support strictly below a point p with Gamma_r(p) > 0 and Gamma_z(p) = 0, the Biot-Savart law gives u_2^r(p) > 0, so d_t(Gamma_2 - Gamma_1)(p) = -u_2^r(p) Gamma_r(p) < 0 at the initial time. (d) Renormalization dynamics. Uniformly over all finite constants and positive viscosities, terminal historical swirl-maximum attainment gives no additional Liouville hypothesis: every member of LL has a translation-closure representative attaining its swirl supremum, which can then be normalized as in Theorem 3. This does not preserve prescribed constants or establish realization by the fixed physical trajectory. In abstract compact dynamics attainment does not give a nontrivial invariant measure (the hull of t -> sech^2(t + s), s real, together with 0 has delta_0 as its only invariant probability measure), so this route needs a nontrivial recurrence theorem specific to Navier-Stokes; none is established here. (e) Lyapunov functionals for the Euler limits of branch (II). Since V(t) -> -V(-t) maps Euler solutions to Euler solutions, no autonomous functional invariant under V -> -V can be strictly decreasing along all Euler flows; a time-directed quantity would be needed. None of (a) to (e) excludes either branch.

## 5. Verification

The report combines author derivations and results supplied in research answers. Scripts check the specified identities, constants and examples; compactness, comparison, exhaustion, removability and subsequent extensions require separate analytic verification. The scripts check symbolic identities and illustrative examples with sympy, and some elementary lemmas numerically; the numerical checks are sanity checks only. Independent verification consists of adversarial audit rounds and research consultations conducted with a second model (GPT-6, run through Codex). Results supplied by an audit or a research consultation were checked by the author as stated below before inclusion. The Status line of each numbered item names its rounds. The audit records, the research consultations (cited as res1, res2, and so on) and the scripts are kept with the working files and are not part of this report. Forty-six adversarial audit rounds (rounds 1 to 19, round 21 with two parts, rounds 22 to 47; forty-seven audit files) and one research consultation (round 20) are described below.

- Rounds 1 to 3. Round 1 covered the extraction, the reduction, the barrier and Route B; round 2 covered Route A, the pressure Hessian and Proposition 8; round 3 covered Theorem 9. The main named results were retained after repairs; incorrect auxiliary claims were repaired: the compactness order in the extraction, the scope of the reduction, the envelope in Route B, a Fourier sign and a running-maximum step in Theorem 7, the scope of Proposition 8, the running-minimum selection, notation and the far-field citation in Theorem 9, and the numerical excursion check.
- Round 4 covered the Type II analysis (Proposition 11 and the window discussion), the 5D identities and the literature notes. It found a wrong spatial normalization of the Gaussian example (now b = sqrt(e/2), valid for C_* >= sqrt2 e); a wrong claim that ||q||_{L^4(dx)} is supercritical and unbounded under (A) (it is subcritical, and the audit supplied ||q||_{L^4(dx)}^4 <= 4 E_0 H^2); a proof of window rigidity that needed an unproved comparison of M (replaced by the volume argument of Proposition 12, proposed by the audit); an incorrect description of the mechanism of [QZ]; and overstatements (equivalence of windows with decay, eternal rather than ancient limits, a missing planar alternative, overly broad literature and reduction statements). All were corrected.
- Round 5 covered Theorem 13 and the corrected Propositions 11 and 12. It found that the restart delays in the uniform local regularity step of Theorem 2 did not fit the step intervals (repaired with delays 2^{-j-2}), that the constant C_c of Theorem 13(b) had been shown to exist only for the fixed solution (a class version was supplied), a wrong quantifier in the statement relating branch (I) to the Liouville problem, a false sentence about single-profile solutions and an unproved conclusion in the heuristic jet discussion. It supplied the stronger bound on m_n in branch (II) and the uniform swirl witness. All were corrected or withdrawn. Research answers supplied the M-comparison in Theorem 13(e), Proposition 14 and its balance remarks, the Duhamel and q^2 bounds of sec. 3.6, the power-law histories of sec. 3.12 and the swirl-level eternal limits of Proposition 15; the gradient-level version of Proposition 15 is the author's adaptation.
- Round 6 covered Theorem 13(e) and Propositions 14 and 15. It found no wrong constant, one dropped hypothesis (the finiteness of N at an initial time in the mass balance, which finite energy does not imply) and several statements of scope that were too broad (the selection in Proposition 15 is existential; the regularity-criterion remark concerns global hypotheses only; several remarks now say "would suffice" instead of "would need"). All were corrected. Research answers supplied the o(D^{1/2}) bound for N, the backward adjoint identity, and the frozen-field and log-slow obstructions of Proposition 18. Propositions 16, 17 and 18(a) are the author's.
- Round 7 covered Propositions 16 to 18. It found the identities and constants correct and one gap: the unconditional finiteness of Y_p holds only for p >= 2 (a counterexample for 3/2 < p < 2 uses disjoint translates with slowly decaying amplitudes; the statement was restricted). It improved the stream-function threshold of Proposition 16 from 0.4748 to the values P_p, which increase to (8/3)^{1/2} = 1.632993..., by keeping the axial dissipation; it removed the hypothesis that N be finite at the initial time from Proposition 18(c), restricted the scalar-history obstruction to windows of normalized length O(1/kappa) and corrected one sufficiency statement worded as a necessity. Research answers supplied the pointwise inflow bound and the source-sign identity of Proposition 18(e), and the exact form of the coupled meridional energy identity, which agrees with a script
- Round 8 covered Proposition 19. It found the main statements correct (with an added identification argument at the initial time) and several defects in the remarks: ambiguous window quantifiers in the proposed closures; an unjustified necessity of signed cancellation in the balance of N (a stationary scalar counterexample was supplied and the statement withdrawn); a stale numerical threshold; and the global statement limsup N = infinity presented as new although it follows from [LZ, Theorem 1.4]. All were corrected. It supplied the divergence of the integrated absolute source of N (Proposition 19(d)) and the kinematic obstruction of sec. 3.11. Proposition 19 and Proposition 20(a)-(c) are otherwise the author's.
- Round 9 covered Proposition 20. It found (a)-(c) correct, made the constant in (b) explicit (152), repaired a ball-versus-cylinder step in (c) and two time-domain details in the existence of singular points, found that the first proof of (d) relied on [Se20, Lemma 2.2], which is misprinted as stated, and supplied the present proof of (d) through [LZ, Theorem 1.1], which also strengthens it; it corrected overstatements about the pressure quantity and the swirl energy in the remarks. The many-scale statement (e) is from research answer res6; its version at the singular top was not accepted. Propositions 21 and 22 are the author's, with identities checked by scripts; parts of Proposition 22(a), (b) were also derived independently in research answer res7.
- Round 10 covered Proposition 21. It found the Harnack chain, the barrier bound and the weighted-record argument correct, and supplied the strict inequality in (a), the extended range Gamma_0 < G_0 and the quantitative compression criterion in (c), and the exclusion of meridional bounds on cylinders reaching the axis in (b) (given in this report in outline only). It corrected several interpretations: the two hypotheses of the gain lemma fail jointly, not separately; the ancient-limit escape does not locate large meridional speeds at circulation maxima; the fixed-radius flux bound gives continuity in time (the defect is only at shrinking radii); the weight obstruction holds for fixed radial weights only; the averaging statement for nonlinear transforms needs Phi' > 0 on the range. The repairs were checked with a script
- Round 11 covered Proposition 22. It found the pressure source, the axis formula, the record inequalities, the Riccati equations and the Burgers formulas correct, and one error: the principal-value formula for the axial curvature of the swirl pressure omitted a local term 2c^2/5 (an explicit Gaussian example has curvature 2/5 while every spherical shell integral vanishes; a research answer derived the same term independently). The no-sign conclusion is unaffected. It corrected overstatements in the reading of the Burgers model (which also fails finite energy, the tail and a bounded canonical pressure, and for which no universal distance from the record follows), restricted the averaged pressure statements to trajectory averages, added the endpoint restriction t > 0 in the swirl-pressure lemma, and supplied the bound d_n^2 >= 2 + 6 beta_n^2/gamma_n^2 and further signed identities at the record.
- Round 12 covered Proposition 23 (the author's; a script). It found the deficit comparison, the far-field theorem (including the comparison on the irregular near-maximal set and the order of the two limits) and the physical corollary correct, and noted that the Liouville consequence is the case b = 2 nu/R of Theorem 5. It found that the sample constants used an inadmissible value of A; that whole-space sharpness of 2 nu is not established (the line sink is singular on the axis); that a claim that Case-1 limits violate near-axis circulation moduli was false; that several literature entries were misdescribed (which theorem of [LZ] uses the drift decomposition, the swirl-potential form of [LZ2], the extra hypotheses of [LZZ], of Pan and Li [PL] and of [CIV], and the scaling of the quantity of [Se22]); that a sufficient condition was called an equivalence; and that the discussion of the near-maximal set drew causal conclusions about the origin of the inflow and a vorticity-smallness implication that do not follow (it supplied the zero-vorticity counterexample of sec. 3.16). All were corrected. The Feller form of Proposition 23(b) was added later and checked symbolically and by the author.
- Proposition 24 is from research answer res8; the author checked each part, and selected identities and one-sample examples symbolically; the small-dissipation target proposed there was shown false by the author (Proposition 24(e)). Round 13 audited the whole report for overclaims and consistency, and Proposition 24 in detail. It found the principal formulas of Proposition 24 and the dissipation lower bound correct, and found omitted hypotheses (Propositions 10, 16, 18(b), 20(e), 21(b), and the sign convention in Theorem 2), an invalid rounded stream-function threshold (1.633 exceeds the limit (8/3)^{1/2}), a slightly too large Burgers cylinder constant, and statements stronger than the evidence (that every critical functional has one unsigned term, that the total inflow could not be restricted, that different targets are the same missing implication, that exclusions must use particular listed information, and sufficiency statements worded as equivalences or necessities). All were corrected; the audit's common status paragraph is used at the top.
- Proposition 25 is from research answer res9; its identities were rechecked by the author and symbolically; the existence of the exhaustion limit in 25(d) was read, not rewritten. Round 14 covered it. It found the comparison arguments, the refinement (a), the flux and Biot-Savart bounds, the Morrey example (with constants rechecked by the audit), both models and the failure of the vorticity equation correct. It found a normalization constant in the Feller scale function that is exact only for base point e, an inapplicable citation of Theorem 5 for the prescribed drift (replaced by Proposition 23(b)), a reliance on sampled signs for the barriers (replaced by an analytic proof), missing quantifiers and a wrong symmetry argument in the limit-level equivalence (the reflection W^# replaces the negation of Gamma), a claim that a proof must use information that does not pass to the limit, a claim that the meridional field is close to a swirl-free solution in the near-maximal set (replaced by a small-residual statement), and sufficiency statements worded as reductions. All were corrected. Proposition 25(f) is from research answer res10; the exact slender residual and the Landau flow with potential swirl were rechecked symbolically, and the profile classification was read and partly rechecked.
- Proposition 26(a)-(d) are the author's. Round 15 covered Proposition 25(f) and Proposition 26(a)-(d). It found the identities, the exterior solution and the representation formula correct. The profile classification needed its class (odd, nonzero vorticity, finite slope at k = 0) and global formulas for the explicit profile. The comparison with [LRZ2] was misstated: the averaged hypothesis is weaker, so the result is a stronger necessary condition. A claim about the deficit in every large box was wrong, because a Banach limit is not a liminf, and was replaced. The audit supplied the stronger lower bounds for the averaged deficit now in 26(d), rechecked by the author.
- Round 16 covered Proposition 26(e) and the repaired 26(d). It found the deficit bounds, the subsequence argument, the convex-weight identity and its representation correct, extended the threshold theorem to the endpoint 2 nu with vanishing at every radius, and supplied the gap statement and the weighted outflow statement of 26(e) (rechecked by the author). It found that incompressibility was missing from the inherited hypotheses and gave the counterexample quoted in the statement of Proposition 26; that a pointwise corollary was already covered by Proposition 23(b); that exchanging a Banach limit with an improper integral needs a finite-interval argument followed by Tonelli's theorem; that A^2 - Gamma^2 need not be nonnegative; and that the deficit bound is asymptotic at each fixed radius rather than exact in each finite box. All were corrected.
- Proposition 26(f) is from research answer res11; its explicit formulas were rechecked symbolically and numerically. The scripts check algebra and selected constants only; the barrier inequalities, the exhaustion and the removability of the axis for the first field rest on the analytic arguments of that answer and of round 17. Round 17 covered Proposition 26(e), (f). It found the gap statement, the weighted outflow statement and the scalar construction correct. It found false the claim that the second explicit field of 26(f) satisfies all averaged identities, since the convex identity for the square of the circulation fails, and found that the conclusion drawn from it, that further progress must use the unaveraged equations, did not follow. It also required a C^1 rather than smooth window weight, a sufficient rather than necessary reading of the window-size condition, centres fixed before each diagonal stage, an explicit vorticity-equation hypothesis for the averaged vorticity identity, and the restriction of a positivity remark to weights with f(A) > 0. All were corrected.
- Round 18 covered Proposition 26(g) and the level-set part of 26(h), which are the author's. It found the vanishing of localized means, the growth argument, the two-level moment identities (for every admissible weight) and the periodic estimate correct; it found the Z-integrated balance unproved as written (repaired by explicit trace hypotheses), the level measure in need of a construction (supplied by a thin-strip estimate), and the pointwise flux-ratio limit and the claimed necessity of a lower bound for the level-set energy not justified. These were corrected. Proposition 26(g) was reviewed in round 18; its integrated balance is conditional on the explicit trace hypotheses. The momentum part of 26(h) is from research answer res12: the averaged radial, angular and axial momentum identities, the vanishing axis value of the averaged meridional vorticity and the leading-order behaviour of the periodic field were rechecked by the author (with a script for the averaged balances and the Wirtinger bound); the pressure bounds of the periodic field were checked only by that answer's script.
- Round 19 covered Proposition 26(i). The theorem is correct after an indexing repair (D(s_k) >= lambda^{k-1} D_1); the audit supplied the sharper inequality dD/ds >= D^2/(2 G'^2 (1 + C^2)), so the theorem holds whenever int^infinity dr/(r (1 + C(r)^2)) = infinity, with no monotonicity, while an arbitrary unbounded sequence of good radii does not suffice in the scalar class (explicit saturating scalar example). A remark needs theta >= 0; the fixed-data remark is correct with the signed time boundary (only the earlier time is adverse) and with errors compared in consistent viscosity units. For the first construction of 26(i) (from research answer res13; a script, whose error constants were read, not rechecked) the flux is exactly MM[Phi_R^2] = (nu^2/2) R^{9/4}, and exponents 0 < alpha < 1/2 give saturating scalar examples with flux of order nu R^{1+alpha}; the level inequality of that answer needs a supremum bound on Phi, which is stronger than the mean-square hypothesis.
- Round 20, the research consultation (checked by the author), confirmed the growth criterion with 1 + C^2, constructed an exact saturating scalar solution with C(rho) = log rho (main-term identity checked symbolically, a script; error bounds read), gave the dip example showing that C^2 alone is not enough, computed the meridional vorticity residual of that solution (of order nu^2/(R log^3 R), checked symbolically, the order of the axial damping), and wrote the box inequality with smooth axial weights. The available upper bounds for inventory and mean Dirichlet energy have the same order; these are not two-sided estimates of the actual terms. It derived no improvement of the flux bound from the coupled system.
- Round 21 had two parts. The first audited the scaling remark, the long-pattern calculation and the sharpness results of Proposition 26(i). It found the residual formulas, the linearization, the jump identity, the general-p construction for each fixed p in (1/2, 1], the dip example and the logarithmic residual correct, and repaired an unstated assumption on the axial derivative of the circulation in the scaling test, a flux size that holds only at marginal balance, a missing integrability hypothesis in the jump identity, and two interpretations stated more strongly than the estimates allow. It also proved, independently of research answer res15, that bounded half-line solutions of the long-pattern equation are constant; the author rechecked this proof, with a symbolic check in a script The scaling calculation and long-pattern analysis were reviewed in round 21, with the scope restrictions stated above. The extension for each fixed p in (1/2, 1], including the required corrector estimates, was reviewed in round 21; no uniformity as p down 1/2 is asserted. The second part was a standalone review of the whole report, with a verdict on every numbered item. It found incorrect formulas (among them the integrating factor attributed to [Sh]), missing hypotheses, incomplete proofs and contradictory verification statements. The formula, notation and scope corrections were incorporated, and the proofs and key estimates appended to the numbered items respond to its list of missing arguments; several requested standalone proofs remain incomplete, and the items concerned say so in their text.
- Round 22 was a standalone review of the revised report, with a verdict on every numbered item and a disposition of every round-21 finding. It confirmed the status label of the report and found that most round-21 repairs had been applied. It found a missing Navier-Stokes hypothesis, missing definitions and a missing pressure normalization in Proposition 26(k), an incorrect gauge step in the tanh example there, overstatements in sec. 4(7) (the energy product, the scope of the quantitative Liouville remark, the Fourier lower bound outside branch (I), and the kinematic history), imprecise interpretations in Proposition 26(l) and in the opening, a normalization ambiguity in sec. 4(8), and stale incompleteness notices in Theorems 2 and 9 and Propositions 8 and 15. It supplied the short arguments now included in Theorem 5, Theorem 9 case (P), Proposition 19(a) (positive times only; the initial-time identification was removed), Proposition 20(e) (the record-scale dissipation statement), Proposition 21(a), (b) and Proposition 22(a) (boundary terms). The author checked these arguments, and checked the formulas of the tanh example (divergence, circulation residual, the axial derivative of the meridional vorticity residual at (L, 0)) and the dissipation scaling of Proposition 20(e) symbolically in a script; all corrections were incorporated, but the change of variables then written in Proposition 20(e) combined a record-centred ball with an axis-centred integrand (the script checks the prefactor and the time interval only); round 23 found this, and it is corrected. Its verdicts INCOMPLETE as a whole for Propositions 16, 18, 25 and 26 (global justification of the weighted integrations, the adjoint construction, the profile classification, some scalar constructions and the model pressure claims) stand, and the Status lines of those items say so. The literature statements of sec. 3.9 were checked against the abstracts and, for [Hou1] and [HWY], against the texts.
- Round 23 audited the changes made after round 22, compared line by line with the version reviewed there: Proposition 23(f), the literature and exponent paragraphs of sec. 3.9, the entries of sec. 3.12 on the minimal-circulation element and on unique continuation, and sec. 5. It found Proposition 23(f) correct, with the derivative statement to be read for each fixed order at least one (now stated), and the enstrophy remark and every exponent relation of sec. 3.9 correct (it checked in the text of [Se20] that the Type I definition used there needs only one of three scaled quantities to be bounded). It found two literal errors: the change of variables in Proposition 20(e) (corrected; the bound and its conclusions survive), and the sentence that equality of exponents does not give the uniform constant in (A), which is false under genuine two-sided power bounds (replaced). It required a definition of the class in the minimal-element entry of sec. 3.12, the explicit kinematic example, narrower unique-continuation wording, an expanded restart argument for D <= C M^2, the description of the tracked point of [Hou1], and a separate verification record for Proposition 23(f). It found one wrong assertion: Proposition 23(g)(vi) said that equality would require near-maximizers in a fixed compact set; such compactness is sufficient, not necessary, and in Case 1 it is impossible; the sentence was replaced, with the consequence of equality. It found that the entry of sec. 3.12 on the time remaining to the singularity overlooked that Theorem 13(d) gives the ratio bound, hence a uniform N_{n,delta}, in branch (I), and it asked for the time units and hypotheses of [KMT, Corollary 1.2], the formula of the remote-bump family and the corresponding qualification of question (xiv) in sec. 4(6); all were incorporated. The entry of sec. 3.12 on weighted energies of (J, eta) was added after round 24 from research answer res24; the author checked the counterexample with a script (divergence, stream-potential relation, four integrals, transport identity and derivative); round 25 audited it.
- Round 25 audited the changes made after round 24, compared range by range with the version reviewed there: the repairs of Proposition 23(f), (g), the rewritten entry of sec. 3.12 on the time remaining to the singularity, the helicity entry, the new entry on weighted energies of (J, eta), sec. 4(6)(xiv) and sec. 5. It found the repairs correct up to two conventions (the value 0 of the generalized inverse calR on an empty set, and the condition 0 <= chi <= 1 in the cutoff test of (g)(vi)); it confirmed the rescaling, the statement, units and hypotheses of [KMT, Corollary 1.2] (Definition 3.2 and the factor delta^{-1} in N_delta, checked in the text), the remote-bump estimates for instantaneous data, the restart bound, the helicity integration and the computation for the core V_0, and it rederived independently the (J, eta) system, the transport term and the four Gaussian integrals. It found one inaccurate qualification: in branch (I) the normalized data are globally bounded, so [KMT, Corollary 1.3] gives a regularity window global in space, and locating a singular point is not an additional obstacle there; the entry of sec. 3.12 and question (xiv) were corrected accordingly, together with the instantaneous scope of the remote-bump data, the positivity and integrability conditions of the (J, eta) entry and the description of the script coverage. - Round 28 audited the changes made after round 27, compared range by range with the version reviewed there. - Round 39 audited these additions. The referee rederived the truncated balance with its axis-loss coefficient 4 pi, the finiteness of Q_lambda at positive times, the source bound by Y_2(a) + 3 I_in(a, b), the lower bound pi^2 c_0 rho^2/(4 kappa_n) and the volume estimate, kappa_n S(s_n) -> infinity without N(a) < infinity, the constants c_eta and c_Q, the exterior estimate with X_n l_n^4 = epsilon_n kappa_n, the radius bound R_0 <= R_1 + C(C_*) E_0 D_n^3/M_n^5 of Proposition 18(c) and the res35 kinematic exponents, and found them correct. It found one gap, in the sufficient condition of sec. 4(6)(xvii): smallness of the inner integral for some sequence c_n -> 0 is automatic (dominated convergence for each fixed n), so the condition must refer to a sequence for which the exterior integral is small, or to a fixed c; and the adjoint pairing is bounded above by a negative constant, so its absolute value is what is bounded below. Both were repaired, together with the time-based diagonal of the key estimates of Proposition 18(f), the positive starting time a in Proposition 18(c), the wording of the round-38 bullet and the description of the res35 kinematic family. After round 39 the author added Proposition 18(g) (from research answer res36, checked by hand), the extension of Proposition 18(f) to B_n^2 epsilon_n >= epsilon_0, and sec. 4(6)(xviii).
- Round 40 audited the round-39 repairs and these additions. The referee checked the axial Galilean invariances and the record inequality -r u^r >= 1, the backward Gaussian identity with the sign of its axis term, the Gaussian integrals E, D/E = 13/10 and B/E and the value E_F'/E_F = 2/5, the increase of the swirl maximum of the datum, the Biot-Savart sign in the comparison example, the invariant measure of the sech^2 hull, the closure representatives of members of LL, and the Euler reversal argument. It required three repairs of wording in sec. 4(9), all made with the referee's wording: in (a) the conclusion is restricted to the listed properties, in (c) h is required to be nonzero, and in (d) the statement on LL carries its uniformity qualification and the need for a recurrence theorem is stated without a literature claim. It confirmed the status label and the stated first missing implication.
- Round 47, a final check, audited the round-46 repairs, the round-46 bullet and the counts. The referee found the repairs in (c) and (d) reproduced exactly and correct, rederived the record inequality, the Biot-Savart sign, the bounds on the radii of near-maximizers and the hull statement, confirmed the counts through round 46 and found no other change. It found one deviation: in (a) the report had written that a stagnation-based argument would have to use additional information selecting the distinguished frame, a necessity claim beyond the established insufficiency of the listed properties, where the referee had proposed that it could instead use such information; the referee's wording was restored, which also makes the attribution of the round-46 bullet accurate. This restoration reproduces the referee's sentence and has not been audited again. It confirmed the status label, the opening first missing implication and the closing paragraph of sec. 1.3, within the scope of the changes reviewed.

An audit is evidence, not a proof of correctness.

## 6. References

[BGWX] J. Bang, C. Gui, Y. Wang, C. Xie, Liouville-type theorems for steady solutions to the Navier-Stokes system in a slab, arXiv:2205.13259v4 (abstract read).

[BKM] J. T. Beale, T. Kato, A. Majda, Remarks on the breakdown of smooth solutions for the 3-D Euler equations, Comm. Math. Phys. 94 (1984), 61-66 (used in its standard Navier-Stokes form: int_0^T ||omega||_infty dt < infinity implies regularity).

[CH] J. Chen, T. Y. Hou, Stable nearly self-similar blowup of the 2D Boussinesq and 3D Euler equations with smooth data I: Analysis, arXiv:2210.07191 (2022) (abstract and metadata read).

[CIV] P. Constantin, M. Ignatova, V. Vicol, Regularity for axisymmetric Navier-Stokes with an Euler length, arXiv:2609.20762v2 (2026).

[CIV2] P. Constantin, M. Ignatova, V. Vicol, Regularity of asymptotically axisymmetric solutions to the 3D Navier-Stokes equations with analytic forcing, arXiv:2609.20803v2 (2026).

[CKN] L. Caffarelli, R. Kohn, L. Nirenberg, Partial regularity of suitable weak solutions of the Navier-Stokes equations, Comm. Pure Appl. Math. 35 (1982), 771-831.
